MuJoCo-compatible, state-solvable manipulation task sources

Survey for scaling continual-learning distillation from 5 to 10–100 tasks · generated 2026-07-11 by a 110-agent web survey (6 domain sweeps → per-candidate verified deep-dives → completeness critic) · mjlab / MuJoCo-Warp target · constraint: tasks solvable from proprioception + object poses (no vision), manipulation primitives (push / pull / pick / place / open / close / rotate / insert / press / slide / stack)

Executive summary — the path to 50–100 state-solvable tasks

Every entry below was deep-dived by a dedicated agent that verified the repo, paper, task list, state-observation situation, license, and a concrete mjlab porting path. Bottom line: reaching 50–100 vision-free Franka tasks in mjlab is realistic, via a layered strategy:

  1. Port Meta-World MT50 (top pick) — 50 tuned, state-solvable tabletop tasks with dense rewards, plain MJCF + STL assets, MIT license, and the strongest CL precedent (Continual World CW10/CW20 + many follow-ups). Effort: build one shared EE-control pipeline in mjlab (~1–2 wk), then ~0.5–1 day/task. Caveats: Sawyer robot (retarget to Franka via EE-space actions), ~30–35 truly distinct skills out of 50, no official GPU port.
  2. Adopt LIBERO's task set / precedent (top pick) — 130 Franka tasks on MuJoCo (robosuite 1.4) with privileged object poses on by default, plus a BDDL generator for unlimited more; it is THE benchmark of 2023–26 continual-imitation papers (LOTUS, TAIL, M2Distill), so using it makes our results directly comparable. Caveats: sparse success predicates only (we must author dense rewards for RL teachers) and a CPU robosuite stack (port scenes to mjlab or run teachers in-place).
  3. Generate tasks from asset libraries — MolmoSpaces (48k manipulable MJCF objects + 42M pre-computed grasps + 22 articulated categories, Apache-2.0, MuJoCo-native, Franka FR3), PartNet-Mobility (2.3k articulated URDF objects → per-instance open/close/turn tasks), RoboCasa (2.5k+ objects, kitchen fixtures, MJCF). This is the scaling lever past ~50 tasks: "open X / rotate Y / push Z" instantiated over hundreds of objects, all state-solvable since geometry and joint states are known.
  4. Native quick wins already in our stack — MuJoCo Playground manipulation suite (MJX/warp-native), Franka Kitchen (7 sub-goals), dm_control manipulation, robosuite/MimicGen task designs, Adroit hands if we ever want dexterous.
  5. Mine design catalogs — ManiSkill's ~20 primitive-geom tabletop tasks ship open dense-reward functions (Apache-2.0) that transplant to mjlab with zero asset dependency; RLBench's 100+ task designs are a menu to re-author (its assets/license cannot be ported).
LayerSourceState-solvable tasksEffort
Ready suiteMeta-World MT5050 (~30–35 distinct skills)port: 1–2 wk pipeline + 0.5–1 d/task
CL-standard suiteLIBERO (spatial/object/goal/90/10)130 (+ generator)reward authoring + robosuite→mjlab port
Asset-driven generationMolmoSpaces / PartNet-Mobility / RoboCasa100s (procedural)1 generator per primitive family
Native / catalogsPlayground, Kitchen, ManiSkill recipes, RLBench designs~30–60low per task, uneven quality

Suggested first milestone: mjlab EE-control pipeline + 10-task set mixing ported Meta-World tasks with our existing 5, using the Continual World CW10 ordering ideas; then scale to MT50-in-mjlab and asset-generated articulation tasks. Also noteworthy: CompoSuite (256 compositional MuJoCo tasks — robot × object × obstacle × objective) is the largest single MuJoCo task grid and made for exactly this kind of study; see its card for caveats.

Top pick (2)

Use directly: many state-solvable tasks, MuJoCo-native or well-trodden port path, CL precedent.

Top pick

LIBERO

paper · code

130-task language-annotated Franka tabletop lifelong-learning benchmark on robosuite/MuJoCo, with BDDL task specs, a procedural task generator, and 130x50 human demos.

LIBERO: Benchmarking Knowledge Transfer for Lifelong Robot Learning (NeurIPS 2023 Datasets & Benchmarks)

Verdict: Conceptually the closest benchmark to your agenda (it IS a Franka lifelong-learning benchmark on MuJoCo) with 130 tasks, MJCF assets, and full privileged state (object poses + proprio) exposed via robosuite observables. Not top-pick for two verified reasons: (1) tasks have only sparse BDDL success predicates — no shaped rewards — so every task needs a hand-written (or predicate-templated) reward for your state-based RL teachers; (2) it runs on CPU robosuite 1.4 (old MuJoCo bindings, gym 0.25, numpy 1.22 pins), so you cannot use it in-place with mjlab/mujoco-warp GPU-parallel envs — you must port scenes. The port is concrete and the assets/tasks are exactly what you need, but it is real per-scene work rather than plug-and-play.

Full details
Simulatorrobosuite 1.4.0 (MuJoCo physics, native MJCF; CPU-only, no MJX/warp). Pins: bddl==1.0.1, robomimic 0.2.0, gym 0.25.2, numpy 1.22.4 — an aging CPU stack, incompatible in-process with a modern mujoco-warp env.
# Tasks130 total, verified on repo/paper: LIBERO-Spatial (10), LIBERO-Object (10), LIBERO-Goal (10), LIBERO-100 = LIBERO-90 (90, pretraining) + LIBERO-10/Long (10, long-horizon eval). Procedural BDDL pipeline can generate "in principle infinitely many" more.
Example tasksLIBERO-Spatial: "pick up the black bowl between the plate and the ramekin and place it on the plate" (10 spatial rearrangements of identical objects). LIBERO-Object: "pick up the alphabet soup and place it in the basket" (10 different objects, same scene). LIBERO-Goal: "open the middle drawer of the cabinet", "put the bowl on the stove", "turn on the stove", "put the wine bottle on the rack". LIBERO-10 (long-horizon): "put both the alphabet soup and the tomato sauce in the basket", "turn on the stove and put the moka pot on it".
Primitivespick, place (on/in), open/close (drawers, cabinets, microwave), turn on/off (stove/faucet knobs — press/rotate), put-in-container (basket, tray, caddy, bowl-on-plate/stove), plus long-horizon 2-3-step compositions in LIBERO-10/90. Heavy skew toward pick-and-place in the Spatial/Object suites (10 spatial variants of the same bowl pick-place; 10 object variants of pick-X-into-basket); primitive diversity mostly lives in LIBERO-Goal and LIBERO-90.
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (single arm, OSC controller, 7D relative EE actions + gripper). Matches our existing mjlab Franka setup exactly.
State observationsYes — full privileged state. bddl_base_domain.py enables robot0_joint_pos, eef_pos/eef_quat, and with use_object_obs=True generates per-object sensors: {obj}_pos, {obj}_quat, and relative {obj}_to_eef_pos/quat; articulated objects expose joint states via object_states_dict. Success is checked by BDDL predicates (on/in/open/close/turnon/turnoff) over these states, so all tasks are geometrically state-checkable. Caveat: published results are almost all vision-based IL from the demos; vision-free RL-from-state solving has NOT been demonstrated in the literature (the community explicitly avoids the sparse-reward RL setting), so state-solvability is architecturally guaranteed but empirically unproven — you would be the first to shape rewards per task.
Action spacerobosuite OSC_POSE by default: 7-dim (6-DoF delta EE pose + 1 gripper), single Franka Panda; code also handles a 4-dim OSC_POSITION-style variant. Demos are recorded in this action space.
AssetsMJCF XML + STL/OBJ meshes + textures under libero/libero/assets/: articulated_objects (drawers, cabinets, microwave, stove — jointed MJCF), scenes (kitchen/living-room/study tabletops), stable_hope_objects (NVIDIA HOPE groceries), stable_scanned_objects (Google-Scanned-style), turbosquid_objects, textures. Directly loadable by MuJoCo — this asset library alone is a task-generation substrate. Plus 130x50 human teleop demos (HDF5, on HuggingFace) if you ever want IL baselines or grasp priors.
LicenseCode: MIT. Demonstration datasets: CC BY 4.0. Gotcha: asset provenance is mixed — HOPE and scanned objects are permissive (CC BY 4.0), but the turbosquid_objects folder contains TurboSquid-sourced meshes whose redistribution terms are murkier; fine for research use, check before re-releasing assets.
CL track recordExtensive — it is THE lifelong robot-manipulation benchmark. The paper itself (NeurIPS 2023 D&B) benchmarks EWC, ER, PackNet + sequential/multitask baselines (shipped in repo). Used by TAIL (ICLR 2024 adapter-based lifelong), LOTUS (ICRA 2024 continual skill discovery), M2Distill (multi-modal distillation for lifelong IL — directly adjacent to your distillation framing), LiMoDE (2026 mixture-of-dynamic-experts), and dozens of VLA papers (OpenVLA, SRPO etc.) use it as the standard eval.
Port to mjlabConcrete path: (1) instantiate each BDDL task in robosuite once and dump the composed model via env.sim.model.get_xml() to get a self-contained MJCF per task; (2) load that MJCF in mjlab/mujoco-warp (assets are plain MJCF + STL/OBJ meshes + PNG textures — MuJoCo-native, no conversion); (3) re-implement reset randomization from the BDDL init-region spec and translate the small predicate vocabulary (on/in/open/close/turnon/turnoff) into reusable mjlab success/reward terms — this is templatable, not per-task bespoke; (4) write shaped reward templates per primitive family (reach-grasp-lift-place, joint-angle-target for open/close/turn). Estimate: ~1-2 weeks for the pipeline + first scene family, then hours per additional task since the ~20 scenes and predicates repeat. Main risks: old robosuite MJCF idioms (actuator/equality setup) needing minor edits for MuJoCo 3.x/warp, and replacing robosuite's OSC_POSE controller with your own Cartesian/joint controller.
Scale potentialHigh. 130 ready-made tasks; realistically 30-60 distinct-enough tasks for your purposes after de-duplicating the near-identical Spatial/Object variants (LIBERO-90 spans ~20 scenes with genuinely different goals). Beyond that, the BDDL generator + the object/scene/predicate library lets you mint arbitrary new pick/place/open/close/turn tasks cheaply — BDDL files are short declarative text (objects, init regions, goal predicates), so 100+ generated tasks is realistic once the mjlab predicate-to-reward templates exist.
Gotchas1) No shaped rewards anywhere — success is sparse BDDL predicates; the community explicitly avoids RL on LIBERO for this reason, so budget per-primitive reward engineering. 2) CPU robosuite — no GPU-parallel envs; direct use contradicts your mjlab throughput model, hence port-not-adopt. 3) Prior-sweep claim "65k demos" is roughly right (130 tasks x 50 demos = 6,500 trajectories; ~65k was likely a miscount or counts frames-ish — treat as 6.5k demos). 4) Spatial/Object suites are 10x near-duplicates of one skill each — nominal 130 overstates skill diversity. 5) Frozen 2022-era dependency pins make even standalone installation mildly painful on modern Python. 6) TurboSquid asset licensing if you redistribute. 7) Demos replay reliably only with the pinned robosuite/controller version — irrelevant if you train RL teachers, relevant if you want to reuse demos.
Repo activity~2,000+ stars, ~440 forks. Maintenance is light/slow — small commit count, dependency pins frozen at 2022-2023 versions (robosuite 1.4, transformers 4.21); community activity is high (issues, forks, VLA ecosystem) but upstream development has largely stopped.
Merged duplicate assessments (1)
  • [top-pick] Only candidate that hits all four hard requirements simultaneously: MuJoCo-native (robosuite 1.4, no mujoco-py/Isaac), 130 verified tasks + a BDDL/PDDL generator for unlimited more, ground-truth object-pose observations enabled by default (use_object_obs=True) so every task is state-solvable in principle, and it is THE benchmark that 2023-2026 continual-imitation papers (LOTUS, TAIL, M2Distill, SPECI) actually evaluate on — using it makes results directly comparable. Caveat keeping it from being friction-free: the stock envs are CPU robosuite with runtime-composed scenes, so a GPU mjlab port means exporting flattened MJCF scenes and reimplementing BDDL success predicates (moderate, not trivial, effort); no published work demonstrates state-based RL solving of LIBERO tasks, so scripted-solver/RL feasibility per task must be validated (tasks are quasi-static pick/place/open/press, so risk is low).
Top pick

Meta-World / Meta-World+ (MT50)

paper · code

Canonical 50-task Sawyer tabletop manipulation benchmark (Farama-maintained, MuJoCo 3.x, Gymnasium API); Meta-World+ (NeurIPS 2025 D&B) is the cleaned, reproducible re-release with controllable task sets.

Meta-World: A Benchmark and Evaluation for Multi-Task and Meta Reinforcement Learning (CoRL 2019); Meta-World+: An Improved, Standardized, RL Benchmark (NeurIPS 2025 Datasets & Benchmarks)

Verdict: Verified: 50 distinct, fully state-solvable tabletop manipulation tasks, MuJoCo-native (mujoco==3.3.0, plain MJCF assets), MIT license, actively maintained by Farama (v3.1.1 released June 2026), dense shaped rewards already tuned for SAC/PPO, and the single strongest continual-learning precedent (Continual World CW10/CW20, NeurIPS 2021, plus many follow-ups). The only real friction is that the robot is a Sawyer (not Franka) and there is no official MJX/warp GPU port — so mjlab integration means re-implementing envs in the manager API, which is mechanical, not conceptual, work.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (modern bindings, pinned mujoco==3.3.0; gymnasium>=1.1 API). CPU-stepped, single-env — NOT MJX or mujoco-warp. Control via a mocap body welded to the Sawyer end-effector (Cartesian delta control). Action space Box(-1,1,(4,)): dx,dy,dz + gripper, identical across all tasks.
# Tasks50 distinct tasks (verified against metaworld.farama.org task-descriptions page). Modes: MT1/MT10/MT50 and ML1/ML10/ML45, plus v3 user-defined custom task sets. Each task has parametric randomization of object/goal positions. Examples: reach, push, pick-place, press-button(-top/-wall), open/close drawer/door/window/box, lock/unlock door, faucet on/off, turn dial, pull lever, insert peg side, unplug peg, slide plate, shelf place, bin pick, sweep(-into-hole), hammer, assemble/disassemble nut, stick-push/pull, coffee button/push-mug/pull-mug, pick-out-of-hole, soccer, basketball.
Example tasksreach, push, pick-place, door-open, door-close, door-lock/unlock, drawer-open, drawer-close, window-open/close, button-press (4 variants incl. wall/top), faucet-open/close, dial-turn, lever-pull, peg-insert-side, peg-unplug-side, hammer, assembly/disassembly (nut on peg), shelf-place, bin-picking, sweep, sweep-into-hole, coffee-push/pull/button, plate-slide (4 variants), stick-push, stick-pull, basketball, soccer, box-close, hand-insert, pick-out-of-hole
Primitivespush, pull, pick-place, reach, press (button/handle, incl. wall-obstructed variants), open/close (door, drawer, window, box), rotate/turn (dial, faucet, door lock), insert (peg, hand), unplug, slide (plate), sweep, stack-like place (shelf, bins), hammer, lever-pull, tool-use (stick push/pull), basketball/soccer dynamic placing
RobotsSawyer (Rethink Robotics) 7-DoF arm with two-finger gripper — single robot for all tasks. NOT Franka; EE-space control makes the arm largely interchangeable, but a port to your Franka setup needs workspace/gripper retargeting.
State observations39-dim flat state vector, identical across all 50 tasks: current frame [ee xyz (0-2), gripper openness (3), obj1 pos (4-6) + quat (7-10), obj2 pos (11-13) + quat (14-17)] = 18-dim, + the previous timestep's same 18-dim block, + 3-dim goal position = 39. Unused slots are zero-padded placeholders (the criticized padding; Meta-World+ documents/standardizes this). No vision anywhere — the benchmark is state-based by design, and thousands of papers demonstrate solving all 50 tasks from this state obs (SAC/PPO baselines in the original CoRL 2019 paper and Meta-World+). Object geometry is fixed per task (poses randomized), so proprioception + object poses fully determine the task.
Action space4-dim Box(-1,1): 3D end-effector delta position (mocap-driven EE position control) + 1 gripper effort. No orientation control — top-down-ish grasps only.
AssetsMJCF XML scenes + mesh/texture files (metaworld/assets: objects/, sawyer_xyz/, scene/, textures/), MIT-licensed, loadable in any modern MuJoCo (incl. MJX/warp after minor cleanup). Rich object set: drawers, doors (revolving + lock), windows, faucet, dial, lever, handles, coffee machine, shelf, bins, box-with-lid, pegs, nut/peg assembly, hammer+screw, plates/cabinet, mugs, pucks, balls, stick. The dense state-based reward functions are equally reusable assets.
LicenseMIT (verified in pyproject/repo)
CL track recordStrongest CL precedent of any manipulation suite. Continual World (NeurIPS 2021, arXiv:2105.10919, github.com/awarelab/continual_world) built CW10/CW20 task sequences directly on Meta-World (CW20 = CW10 twice, 1M steps/task), benchmarking EWC, PackNet, replay, etc. Many follow-ups reuse it: Self-Composing Policies for Scalable Continual RL (ICML 2024), Lifelong RL with Modulating Masks (TMLR 2023), ClonEx-SAC, and numerous CL-via-distillation papers. Your distillation setup has direct, citable baselines and task orderings.
Port to mjlabTwo viable paths. (A) Use as-is for CPU rollouts: pip install, works today with mujoco 3.3 — zero effort but no GPU-parallel envs, mismatched with mjlab training throughput. (B) Port into mjlab (recommended): assets are plain MJCF + STL, rewards are readable Python; build one shared Sawyer-EE-control (or retarget to your Franka + IK/Cartesian action) scene pipeline in the manager API, then port tasks one by one. The heavy lift is the shared pipeline (~1-2 weeks: EE-delta control, contact-rich objects under mujoco-warp, reward transcription + verification against reference returns); after that each task is roughly 0.5-1 day (scene XML merge, reward port, success criterion, RL sanity run). Watch for warp/MJX solver differences on contact-heavy tasks (hammer, assembly, peg-insert). Realistic estimate: 10 tasks in ~3-4 weeks, 20+ in ~6-8 weeks of focused work. Note some third-party MJX ports of Meta-World subsets exist (unofficial, quality varies) — worth a scan before writing from scratch.
Scale potential50 state-solvable tasks out of the box — directly hits your 20-50 target without generation tricks. Each task also has parametric goal/object randomization (ML1-style) if you want intra-task variation. Beyond 50: task variants (wall/no-wall, side/top approaches already show the pattern) and object swaps could stretch to ~70-80, but 50 curated, reward-tuned tasks is the realistic and sufficient number. For a first CL scale-up, the Continual World CW10 subset (window-open, drawer-close, hammer, faucet-close, push-wall, handle-press-side, push-back, stick-pull, shelf-place, peg-unplug-side) is a pre-vetted difficulty-balanced 10-task sequence.
Gotchas1) Robot is Sawyer, not Franka — either keep Sawyer in mjlab or retarget to Franka (EE-space actions make this feasible but grasp widths/workspace need tuning). 2) 4-DoF action space (no EE orientation) — simpler than your current tasks if you use full Cartesian control. 3) Historical reward inconsistencies across v1/v2 versions confounded literature comparisons — use v3/Meta-World+ only, and don't mix numbers with older papers. 4) 39-dim obs contains zero-padded placeholder slots and the previous timestep frame — quirky but consistent. 5) mujoco pinned to ==3.3.0, may conflict with mjlab's mujoco-warp pin if you install side-by-side (use separate venv or port assets). 6) No official GPU (MJX/warp) version — CPU-only unless you port. 7) Some tasks are near-duplicates (button-press x4, plate-slide x4), so 'distinct skills' is closer to ~30-35 than 50.
Repo activity~1.8k stars, ~345 forks; actively maintained — v3.1.1 released June 2026, Python 3.10-3.13, docs at metaworld.farama.org. Meta-World+ (2025, arXiv:2505.11289) is the Farama-blessed standardization fixing historical version/reward inconsistencies.
Merged duplicate assessments (2)
  • [top-pick] Exactly what the requirements ask for: 50 distinct MuJoCo-native manipulation-primitive tasks, all solvable from a shared 39-dim low-level state (proven by hundreds of state-based RL papers), MIT-licensed plain-MJCF assets, actively maintained by Farama (v3.1.1, June 2026), and it is THE de-facto continual-RL task source (Continual World CW10/CW20 and many successors). Only real work is adapting Sawyer+mocap-EE-control tasks to your Franka mjlab setup, or simply using it as-is for CPU teacher training.
  • [top-pick] Best single-source match for all hard requirements: 50 genuinely distinct, primitive-centric tabletop manipulation tasks, all demonstrably solvable from a fixed 39-dim low-level state vector (state obs is the default mode — vision not required), MuJoCo-native (pinned mujoco==3.3.0, MJCF assets), MIT licensed, actively maintained by Farama (v3.1.1 June 2026), and it is THE canonical continual-RL substrate (Continual World CW10/CW20 and many follow-ups built on it). Caveats that make it a port rather than a drop-in for mjlab: Sawyer embodiment (not Franka), mocap-based 4D end-effector delta control, and CPU MuJoCo stepping (no MJX/warp GPU parallelism) — so you vendor scenes+rewards into mjlab rather than import the package.

Strong (17)

High value with one significant caveat (port effort, reward authoring, or generator-not-suite).

Strong

CALVIN

paper · code

Language-conditioned long-horizon play benchmark: Franka at a playtable (drawer, slider, button, switch, lightbulb, LED, 3 colored blocks) in 4 texture/layout variants, with 34 atomic tasks whose success is detected purely from simulator state.

CALVIN: A Benchmark for Language-Conditioned Policy Learning for Long-Horizon Robot Manipulation Tasks (IEEE RA-L 2022, Best Paper Award)

Verdict: Best effort-to-task ratio of any single-scene candidate: one playtable port yields ~34 verified state-defined tasks (all success predicates are pure state checks on a 24-dim ground-truth scene vector), MIT-licensed URDF/OBJ assets, and the tasks look like natural extensions of the existing 5-task suite. Not top-pick because it is PyBullet-native (real porting work: articulated table URDF, scripted LED/lightbulb logic, and reimplementing 34 success predicates in mjlab), provides NO reward functions (only sparse success predicates — you must design dense rewards per task for RL teachers), and has no continual-learning track record.

Full details
SimulatorPyBullet (calvin_env), 30 Hz control, EGL GPU rendering. No official or community MuJoCo port found.
# Tasks34 atomic tasks (verified in calvin_models/conf/callbacks/rollout/tasks/new_playtable_tasks.yaml), each instantiable in 4 scene variants (A/B/C/D differing in textures and fixture layout). Tasks are composable into 5-step chains for the official long-horizon eval, but the atomic set is 34.
Example tasksopen_drawer, close_drawer, move_slider_left, move_slider_right, push_red_block_right, rotate_blue_block_left, lift_pink_block_table, lift_red_block_drawer, lift_blue_block_slider, place_in_slider, place_in_drawer, stack_block, unstack_block, turn_on_lightbulb (via switch), turn_on_led (via button), push_into_drawer
Primitivespush (6: red/blue/pink block left/right), rotate (6: block left/right), lift/pick (9: 3 blocks x from table/slider-shelf/drawer), place (2: in slider, in drawer), open/close drawer (2), slide door left/right (2), press/toggle (4: lightbulb switch on/off, LED button on/off), stack/unstack (2), push_into_drawer (1)
RobotsFranka Emika Panda with parallel-jaw gripper (single robot, fixed at desk). Action spaces: absolute cartesian pose, relative cartesian displacement, or joint actions; continuous gripper, 30 Hz.
State observationsFully state-observable. scene_obs is a documented 24-dim float32 vector: slider joint (1), drawer joint (1), button state (1), switch state (1), lightbulb on/off (1), green-LED on/off (1), plus 3 blocks x 6D pose (xyz + euler) = 18. robot_obs is 15-dim: TCP pos (3) + TCP euler (3) + gripper width (1) + 7 joint angles + gripper action (1). Every task's success predicate in the yaml is defined on these state values (joint thresholds, block displacement/rotation/height, light booleans) — no vision needed for success detection. Caveat: CALVIN's own baselines are vision-based imitation from play data; nobody has published state-based RL solving of these tasks, but the tasks are geometrically simple (blocks, 1-DoF fixtures) and clearly RL-solvable from state, closely matching your existing push/button/door/drawer/lift tasks.
Action space7D: TCP xyz + euler + gripper (absolute or relative/normalized), or joint-space; matches your current cartesian/joint Franka setup.
AssetsURDF + OBJ meshes + textures (calvin_env/data/): 4 playtable variants, blocks, franka_panda, plane. Plain formats, trivially reusable; the articulated playtable (drawer + slider + button + switch in one desk) is the valuable asset. Also ships ~24h teleop play dataset (useless for your state-RL pipeline; language annotations ignorable as planned).
LicenseMIT (both mees/calvin and mees/calvin_env)
CL track recordNo notable continual-learning usage found — CALVIN is used almost exclusively for language-conditioned imitation / VLA evaluation (ABC→D generalization, 5-task chains). The robot-CL community standard is LIBERO, not CALVIN. You would be pioneering its use as a CL task suite (fine — you only want the tasks/scene, but no prior CL baselines exist on it).
Port to mjlabAssets are plain URDF + OBJ meshes + textures (calvin_env/data: calvin_table_A..D/{urdf,meshes,textures}, blocks/, franka_panda/, plane/). Concrete path: (1) convert one calvin_table URDF to MJCF (MuJoCo's URDF import or obj2mjcf; the table has drawer + slider prismatic joints, button and switch — standard 1-DoF joints); (2) swap in your existing mjlab Franka instead of theirs; (3) reimplement the 34 success predicates from the yaml/tasks.py as mjlab termination/success terms (mechanical translation of state thresholds); (4) design dense rewards per task family (CALVIN ships none — biggest hidden cost); (5) lightbulb/LED are scripted visual logic in Python, not physics — replicate as a state flag toggled by switch/button joint thresholds. Estimate: 1-2 weeks for scene port + predicates for one variant; +2-4 weeks to get RL teachers training well across all task families. Variants B-D are mostly texture/layout swaps.
Scale potential~30 net-new state-solvable tasks from one scene (34 minus overlap with your existing open-drawer/push/button/lift). Multiply by 4 scene variants for domain variation (not really distinct tasks). Beyond that it caps out — to reach 50-100 you would procedurally vary blocks (colors, sizes, positions) and directions, plausibly reaching 50-60 task instances, but CALVIN alone will not give 100 qualitatively distinct skills.
Gotchas(1) No reward functions anywhere — only sparse success predicates in yaml/tasks.py; dense reward design for ~8 task families is the real cost, not the asset port. (2) Lightbulb/LED are Python-scripted visual effects, not physics — must reimplement as state flags. (3) Success predicates are written against PyBullet joint/pose conventions; translate thresholds carefully (slider 0-0.56 m, drawer 0-0.24 m, rotation in degrees of yaw). (4) Several 'tasks' are near-duplicates (same skill, different block color/direction) — 34 tasks is ~10-12 distinct skills; do not oversell diversity. (5) The 2M-frame teleop dataset and language annotations are irrelevant to your pipeline. (6) calvin_env is effectively unmaintained (Python 3.8, tacto submodule) — use it only as reference, do not build on it. (7) Prior sweep info checks out: simulator=PyBullet, 34 tasks, MIT, links resolve.
Repo activitymees/calvin: 956 stars, 124 forks, last push Sep 2025. mees/calvin_env: 17 stars, last push Jan 2024 — env itself is low-maintenance/frozen. Repo pins Python 3.8; install is dated.
Strong

CompoSuite

paper · code

256 compositional robosuite/MuJoCo manipulation tasks ({IIWA,Jaco,Kinova3,Panda} x {Box,Dumbbell,Plate,Hollowbox} x {None,GoalWall,ObjectDoor,ObjectWall} x {PickPlace,Push,Shelf,Trashcan}), all trained/solved from low-level state, designed for multi-task and lifelong RL.

CompoSuite: A Compositional Reinforcement Learning Benchmark (Mendez, Hussing, Gummadi, Eaton), CoLLAs 2022

Verdict: Verified: 256 fully state-based MuJoCo (robosuite 1.4, native mujoco bindings) manipulation tasks, MIT-licensed, built by a lifelong-RL group explicitly for multi-task/lifelong RL, with 64 Panda-only tasks immediately relevant to us. Not top-pick for two reasons: (1) skill diversity is combinatorial, not primitive-diverse — only 4 underlying objectives (PickPlace, Push, Shelf, Trashcan), all pick/push variants, so the 64 Panda tasks are context variations of ~4 skills; (2) it runs on CPU robosuite, so using it inside GPU-parallel mjlab requires exporting the composed MJCF scenes and reimplementing the staged shaped rewards (moderate, not trivial, effort).

Full details
Simulatorrobosuite 1.4.0 on native MuJoCo python bindings (free mujoco >= 2.3, no mujoco-py). CPU-based, Gym-wrapped; NOT MJX/warp GPU-parallel. Python <= 3.11.
# Tasks256 verified in code (itertools.product of 4x4x4x4 in composuite/env/main.py). 64 per single arm (e.g. all-Panda via sample_tasks(experiment_type='smallscale', smallscale_elem='Panda')). Prior sweep info was accurate.
Example tasksPanda_Box_None_PickPlace; Panda_Dumbbell_GoalWall_Push (push dumbbell to goal behind a wall); Panda_Plate_ObjectDoor_Shelf (carry plate through door frame onto shelf); Panda_Hollowbox_ObjectWall_Trashcan (lift hollow box over wall, drop into trashcan); IIWA_Box_GoalWall_PickPlace; etc. — every (robot, object, obstacle, objective) tuple.
PrimitivesOnly 4 objectives: PickPlace (pick object, place in bin), Push (slide object to goal), Shelf (place object onto a shelf), Trashcan (drop object into a trashcan). Obstacle axis adds avoidance behavior (reach over a goal wall, through a door frame, over an object wall). So effective primitives = pick, place, push, insert-into-container, place-on-shelf, obstacle-avoiding reach. No open/close/rotate/turn/press/stack.
RobotsIIWA (KUKA), Jaco (Kinova), Kinova3 (Gen3), Panda (Franka) — all standard robosuite arm models with default grippers. Panda subset = 64 tasks.
State observationsFully state-based by default (use_camera_obs=False). Flat obs = robot proprio-state (joint pos sin/cos, joint vel, eef pos/quat, gripper qpos/qvel) + object-state (obj_pos, obj_quat, obj_to_eef_pos, obj_to_eef_quat) + goal-state (goal_pos, goal_quat, goal_to_eef pos/quat, obj_to_goal) + obstacle-state (obstacle pos/quat, obstacle_to_eef) + optional 16-dim multi-hot task-ID (4x one-hot per axis, use_task_id_obs flag). Companion offline-dataset paper (arXiv 2307.07091, RLJ 2024) reports ~93-dim obs, 8-dim action (joint-position controller; OSC position/pose also available). Vision-free solving demonstrated: CoLLAs-22 paper trains PPO from state on all 256 tasks; pretrained models in CompoSuite-Data repo.
Action space8-dim by default: 7 joint-position deltas + gripper (JOINT_POSITION controller). OSC_POSITION and OSC_POSE controllers also selectable via make(controller=...). Horizon 500 steps, shaped rewards default (sparse optional via reward_shaping=False).
AssetsPlain MJCF XML objects and obstacles (composuite/assets/objects/*.xml) + PNG textures; arenas built on robosuite arena MJCF. Fully MuJoCo-native, trivially reusable; whole composed scene exportable via sim.model.get_xml(). MIT license.
LicenseMIT (verified in repo LICENSE)
CL track recordExplicitly designed for lifelong/compositional RL (Lifelong-ML group, published at CoLLAs 2022). Used in: the CoLLAs-22 paper itself (multi-task + zero-shot compositional generalization with compositional PPO); Hussing et al., 'Robotic Manipulation Datasets for Offline Compositional RL' (arXiv 2307.07091, RLJ 2024; 4 datasets x 256M transitions from the 256 tasks — directly reusable for distillation); 'Distributed Continual Learning' (arXiv 2405.17466) uses CompoSuite for continual RL experiments; Mendez & Eaton's lifelong compositional RL line of work. Caveat: most published usage is multi-task/zero-shot-composition rather than strictly sequential CL protocols.
Port to mjlabAssets are trivially MuJoCo: objects/obstacles are plain MJCF XMLs in composuite/assets/objects (dumbbell.xml, plate.xml, hollowbox.xml, custombox.xml, goal_wall.xml, object_door_frame.xml, object_wall.xml) plus 4 arena classes; robosuite composes the full scene XML at runtime, so you can instantiate each task once and dump env.sim.model.get_xml() to get a standalone MJCF per task for mjlab/mujoco-warp. What must be rewritten: the staged shaped reward functions (4 subtask files, compositional_env.py ~638 lines, readable) and success criteria, plus randomized object placement, as mjlab manager terms. Estimate: ~3-7 days for the 16 Panda scene variants of one objective, ~1-2 weeks for all 64 Panda tasks, since rewards/observations follow one shared template across objectives. Alternative zero-port path: run CompoSuite as-is on CPU robosuite for distillation data (slower, no GPU parallelism).
Scale potential64 state-solvable Panda tasks out of the box (256 if multiple arms count as distinct tasks, which for CL distillation they arguably do since dynamics/kinematics differ). Realistically 20-64 tasks for our pipeline. Further generation is possible by extending axes (new objects are one MJCF each; new obstacles likewise), but genuinely NEW skills (open/turn/press) are not covered — diversity is contextual, not skill-level. Combining CompoSuite's 64 with our existing 5 skill-diverse tasks is a natural fit.
Gotchas(1) Task diversity is combinatorial: 64 Panda tasks share ~4 skills, so CL forgetting dynamics may be milder/more transfer-heavy than skill-diverse suites — arguably a feature for compositional studies but weaker as a '64 distinct skills' claim. (2) CPU robosuite: no GPU-parallel rollout; direct use bottlenecks mjlab-style training, so plan on the MJCF-export + reward-reimplementation port. (3) Objectives Push and PickPlace overlap with our existing push-cuboid/lift-cube tasks. (4) Pinned old stack: gym (not gymnasium), robosuite==1.4.0, Python<=3.11; example training scripts need Python 3.6-3.7 (Spinning Up) — ignore those, use only the env. (5) The 'None' obstacle appears twice in AVAILABLE_OBSTACLES (None and 'None'); sample_tasks correctly uses 4 obstacles. (6) Multi-hot task-ID obs (16-dim) is needed to distinguish tasks in multi-task training since some contexts look identical from state.
Repo activity~51 stars, 4 forks. Low activity: last commit 2024-01-27 ("changed to robosuite 1.4 compatibility to enable free mujoco"). Small, clean codebase (~1500 LOC core), effectively finished/maintenance mode.
Strong

Continual World (CW10/CW20)

paper · code

Ordered 10/20-task continual-RL sequences over Meta-World Sawyer manipulation tasks with forward-transfer/forgetting metrics and 7 CL baselines; the de-facto standard eval protocol for continual RL.

Continual World: A Robotic Benchmark For Continual Reinforcement Learning (Wolczyk, Zajac, Pascanu, Kucinski, Milos; NeurIPS 2021)

Verdict: The canonical continual-RL benchmark, fully state-based and MuJoCo-native via Meta-World, so it matches every hard requirement in principle. Not top-pick for direct use because: (a) the CW repo itself is stale TF2 code with no LICENSE file, pinned to a 2020 metaworld commit and mujoco-py<2.1 — you would not run it as-is; (b) the robot is a Sawyer with mocap end-effector control, not Franka, so mjlab integration means re-targeting tasks or wrapping CPU envs; (c) CW10 is only 10 distinct tasks — the real scaling asset is the underlying Meta-World-50 (MIT, actively maintained v3, native mujoco bindings), with CW10/CW20 providing the community-standard ordering and forgetting/forward-transfer metrics to report against. Adopt the CW10 sequence definition + metrics, source tasks from modern Meta-World.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo. Original repo pins mujoco-py>=2.0,<2.1 and a 2020 metaworld commit (0875192, v1-era envs). Modern route: Farama Meta-World v3 (June 2026 release) uses native mujoco bindings + Gymnasium. No MJX/warp version exists; envs are CPU MuJoCo.
# Tasks10 distinct (CW10); CW20 = CW10 concatenated with itself twice (verified in tasks.py: TASK_SEQS["CW20"] = CW10 + CW10). Drawn from Meta-World's 50-task pool, so up to 50 distinct available. Paper also uses smaller triplet sequences for method development (not in the main tasks.py sequence dict).
Example tasksCW10 exact list (verified from continualworld/tasks.py): hammer-v1, push-wall-v1, faucet-close-v1, push-back-v1, stick-pull-v1, handle-press-side-v1, push-v1, shelf-place-v1, window-close-v1, peg-unplug-side-v1.
PrimitivesCW10 covers: hammer (strike), push, push-against-wall, push-back, faucet-close (rotate), stick-pull (tool-use pull), handle-press-side (press), shelf-place (pick-and-place), window-close (slide), peg-unplug-side (extract/pull). Broader Meta-World-50 adds pick, place, open/close drawer+door+box, insert, turn dial, press button, sweep, stack-adjacent skills.
RobotsSawyer 7-DoF arm with 2-finger gripper; action is 4-dim end-effector delta-xyz + gripper via MuJoCo mocap. Not Franka — but because control is task-space delta, tasks conceptually retarget to a Franka with an IK/differential-IK action term (which mjlab-style managers support).
State observationsFully state-based, vision-free by construction — this is how all CW/Meta-World results are produced. CW uses the 12-dim Meta-World v1-era obs (hand xyz, object position(s), goal position; envs.py sets MW_OBS_LEN=12, masks goal dims 9:12 to +/-inf in hidden-goal mode) plus an appended one-hot task id (OneHotAdder wrapper). Modern Meta-World v2/v3 obs is 39-dim: gripper pose+openness, two object pos+quat, previous-step copy, goal xyz. SAC solves all CW10 tasks from this state vector (paper reports per-task success rates), so state-solvability is demonstrated, not just plausible.
Action space4-dim continuous: end-effector delta position (3) + gripper open/close (1), applied through mocap body; all 50 tasks share this identical action space (a key reason CW works for continual learning with one shared policy head).
AssetsTask scenes are MJCF XML with mostly primitive geoms plus some STL meshes (Sawyer arm, task objects like hammer/faucet/window), bundled in Meta-World (MIT). Rewards/success criteria are pure-Python functions of state — directly translatable to mjlab reward terms. CW repo itself adds only sequence definitions, wrappers, and TF2 SAC + 7 CL methods (EWC, PackNet, etc.).
Licensecontinual_world repo: NO license file (GitHub API reports license: none) — code legally all-rights-reserved, though the task-sequence definition (a list of 10 names) is trivially reproducible. Meta-World (the actual tasks/assets): MIT.
CL track recordThe canonical continual-RL benchmark. Origin: Wolczyk et al., NeurIPS 2021. Used by ClonEx-SAC / "Disentangling transfer in continual RL" (NeurIPS 2022), CompoNet (ICML 2024), modulating-masks continual RL, Learning-to-Modulate (NeurIPS 2023), and most 2022-2026 continual-RL papers that report forward transfer/forgetting on a fixed sequence. Reporting CW10 metrics gives immediate comparability to ~all prior continual-RL work.
Port to mjlabTwo concrete paths. (1) Low effort, CPU: wrap modern Meta-World v3 (Gymnasium, native mujoco, MIT) envs directly and just adopt the CW10 order + metrics — days of work, but no GPU-parallel envs, which conflicts with mjlab's MJX/warp workflow. (2) mjlab-native port: load Meta-World MJCF scenes in mujoco-warp (assets load fine; some STL meshes and mocap-based control need adaptation — replace mocap with an mjlab IK/task-space action term on Franka or import the Sawyer), then re-implement each task's reward/success from Meta-World's Python reward functions as mjlab reward/termination terms. Realistic estimate: ~0.5-1.5 days per task after the first 2-3 establish the pattern, so ~1-2 weeks for CW10, ~4-6 weeks for all 50. Main risks: contact-heavy tasks (hammer, stick-pull, peg-unplug) needing solver tuning under warp, and Sawyer-to-Franka retargeting changing task difficulty vs published numbers.
Scale potential10 distinct tasks from CW10 (CW20 adds no new tasks — it is CW10 repeated). Underlying Meta-World pool: 50 distinct state-solvable tasks with shared robot/action space, giving a realistic ceiling of ~45-50 usable tasks (a few, e.g. basketball/soccer, are gimmicky but still state-solvable). Parametric goal/object-position randomization (random_init_all) gives intra-task variation but not new tasks; getting to 100 would require combining with another asset source.
Gotchas(1) CW pins a 2020 metaworld commit with v1-era 12-dim obs and "-v1" task names; modern Meta-World is v3 with 39-dim obs and slightly changed reward shaping — published CW numbers are NOT directly comparable if you use v3. (2) No license on the CW repo itself. (3) Robot is Sawyer, not Franka — retargeting to your Franka stack changes dynamics and success rates. (4) Mocap-based end-effector control does not translate 1:1 to MJX/warp; needs an IK action term. (5) One-hot task id in the obs bakes in task-aware CL — if you want task-agnostic distillation, drop the wrapper deliberately. (6) CW's headline metrics (forward transfer, forgetting) assume the fixed 1M-step-per-task budget; deviating breaks comparability.
Repo activitycontinual_world: 124 stars, 20 forks, 7 open issues, last push 2024-01-09 — effectively archived/stale. Metaworld: ~1.8k stars, actively maintained by Farama (979 commits, release June 2026).
Strong

Franka Kitchen / Relay Policy Learning (adept_envs)

paper · code: Original: https://github.com/google-research/relay-policy-learning (ARCHIVED Apr 2026, read-only). Maintained: https://github.com/Farama-Foundation/Gymnasium-Robotics (envs/franka_kitchen + envs/assets/kitchen_franka).

The original 9-DoF Franka kitchen: 7 articulated appliances in one MuJoCo scene, composable into multi-stage goals, with 566 human teleop demos and a strong continual/skill-learning literature.

Relay Policy Learning: Solving Long-Horizon Tasks via Imitation and Reinforcement Learning (Gupta et al., CoRL 2019)

Verdict: Cheap, near-perfect fit in quality but limited in quantity: MuJoCo-native modular MJCF assets on the exact robot (Franka), fully state-solvable (59-D pure state obs, no vision), proven in continual-imitation papers (IsCiL NeurIPS'24 'Evolving Kitchen'). But it yields only 7 atomic sub-tasks (~14 with open/close reversals) that overlap heavily with your existing open-door/drawer/button tasks — it gets you from 5 to ~12-15 tasks, not to 50-100. Combinatorial 4-stage sequences multiply goals, not skills. Use it as an asset+task pack alongside a larger suite, not as the scaling backbone.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo. Original repo uses legacy mujoco-py; maintained Gymnasium-Robotics port uses the modern `mujoco` python bindings (v1.4.2, Jan 2026). No MJX/warp version exists, but the scene is pure MJCF+STL so it loads in modern MuJoCo directly.
# Tasks7 atomic sub-tasks (verified): microwave door open, kettle move to top-left burner, light switch, slide cabinet open, hinge cabinet open, bottom-burner knob turn, top-burner knob turn. ~14 with close/reverse variants. Combinatorially many 3-4-stage sequences (D4RL kitchen-complete/-partial/-mixed use fixed 4-subtask goals; P(7,4)=840 ordered sequences possible).
Example taskskitchen_relax-v1 / FrankaKitchen-v1 with tasks_to_complete=['microwave'], ['kettle'], ['light switch'], ['slide cabinet'], ['hinge cabinet'], ['bottom burner'], ['top burner']; D4RL kitchen-complete-v0 (microwave+kettle+light+slider), kitchen-partial-v0, kitchen-mixed-v0.
Primitivesopen (hinge door, slide door, microwave door), turn/rotate (2 burner knobs), press/flip (light switch), pick-and-place (kettle onto burner), plus close variants. No insert/stack/push-object primitives.
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (7-DoF arm + 2-DoF gripper = 9 DoF) — your exact robot.
State observationsFully state-based, vision-free by construction: 59-D obs = 9 robot joint pos + 9 joint vel + 15 object joint pos (knob angles, door hinge/slide angles, light switch, kettle 7-D free-body pose) + 15 object vels (+ small uniform noise; goal config appended in goal-conditioned variants). Success = joint-config within 0.3 norm of per-task target. Original RPL paper and all D4RL kitchen baselines solve it from this state — vision never required.
Action space9-D continuous Box(-1,1): 7 arm + 2 gripper joint velocity commands converted to position targets (Gymnasium-Robotics); original adept_envs used position control. Easy to re-actuate for your cartesian/joint controllers in mjlab.
AssetsModular MJCF: one asset.xml + chain.xml pair per appliance (microwave, kettle, hingecabinet, slidecabinet, oven/knobs, counters, backwall) + STL meshes, in gymnasium_robotics/envs/assets/kitchen_franka/. Appliances are drop-in composable into any MuJoCo scene — ideal for mixing into your mjlab scenes individually. Also 566 human teleop demo trajectories (kitchen_demos_multitask.zip) if you ever want BC seeds.
LicenseCode: Apache-2.0 (original) / MIT (Gymnasium-Robotics). Kitchen appliance assets: Apache-2.0 (Copyright 2019 The DSuite Authors); Franka assets carry their own LICENSE.md (Franka mesh redistribution terms — check before shipping meshes, or swap in the MuJoCo Menagerie Panda you already use).
CL track recordYes. IsCiL (NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2410.22658) builds its 'Evolving Kitchen' continual-imitation stream on Franka Kitchen; the SPiRL/skill-extraction lineage (EXTRACT arXiv:2406.17768, and other continual task-adaptation works) uses it heavily. It is a standard long-horizon/skill-transfer testbed, so kitchen results are legible to reviewers.
Port to mjlabLow (days, not weeks). Path: copy the per-appliance asset/chain MJCF pairs + STL meshes into mjlab scenes (they load in modern MuJoCo as-is; Gymnasium-Robotics already runs them on current bindings), attach your existing Menagerie Panda instead of the bundled franka_assets, wire each appliance joint into your manager-based reward/termination terms (success = joint angle threshold, same pattern as your open_door/open_cabinet tasks). Main work items: MJX/warp compatibility pass on collision geometry (convex-hull STLs, contact pair pruning for the full kitchen scene — or just spawn one appliance per task, which sidesteps this entirely) and re-tuning dense rewards since original envs are sparse.
Scale potentialRealistically ~7 atomic tasks, ~12-15 with close/reverse and per-knob variants (4 knobs exist in the model though the env scores only 2) — of which 3-4 duplicate your existing open-door/drawer/button skills. Multi-stage sequences give unbounded GOAL diversity (840 ordered 4-stage combos) which is useful for long-horizon CL streams, but not distinct skills. Cannot alone reach 20+ distinct tasks; treat it as +7-10 tasks and a reusable articulated-appliance asset pack.
Repo activityOriginal: 137 stars, last push May 2020, archived. Gymnasium-Robotics: 939 stars, 136 forks, actively maintained (v1.4.2 Jan 2026, Farama Foundation).
Strong

Isaac Lab manipulation env zoo (task designs + assets)

paper · code

NVIDIA's GPU-parallel RL framework whose manager-based manipulation env definitions (lift, stack, open-cabinet, in-hand reorient, Factory/FORGE/AutoMate assembly) are the most directly translatable task specs for mjlab, even though its PhysX/USD runtime is excluded.

Orbit: A Unified Simulation Framework for Interactive Robot Learning Environments (IEEE RA-L 2023); newer: Isaac Lab: A GPU-Accelerated Simulation Framework for Multi-Modal Robot Learning (arXiv 2511.04831)

Verdict: Not usable as a runtime (PhysX/USD, excluded), but as a TASK-DESIGN source it is the cheapest port available: mjlab clones Isaac Lab's manager API, so its manager-based env configs (obs/reward/termination/randomization terms, all Python, BSD-3) can be near-mechanically translated. All manipulation envs are trained from low-level state by default. Caveats keep it from top-pick: only ~9 distinct manager-based manipulation families (reach/lift/stack/cabinet/pick-place/place/inhand/dexsuite/deploy) — much of the "50+ envs" count is robot/controller variants; the big scale (AutoMate 100 assemblies) is a single primitive (insertion) over geometry variation, and contact-rich Factory-style tasks (nut threading, gear meshing) rely on PhysX SDF contact that will not port faithfully to MuJoCo convex-hull contact without SDF-plugin work. Main assets are USD on NVIDIA Nucleus (hard direction: no USD-to-MJCF tool), but the key meshes exist elsewhere as OBJ/URDF (IsaacGymEnvs Factory assets, AutoMate meshes, Menagerie robots).

Full details
SimulatorIsaac Sim / PhysX (GPU-parallel). Excluded as runtime per requirements; evaluated purely as task-spec + asset source. Isaac Lab ships MJCF-to-USD converters but nothing in the reverse (USD-to-MJCF) direction.
# Tasks~50+ registered manipulation env IDs, but many are robot/controller variants. Distinct designs: 9 manager-based manipulation families (reach, lift, stack, cabinet open, pick_place, place, inhand reorient, dexsuite, deploy) + direct-workflow contact-rich suite: Factory (peg insert, gear mesh, nut-bolt thread), FORGE (same 3 + force sensing/randomization), AutoMate (2 env types over 100 assembly geometries). Realistic count of distinct portable task designs: ~12-15, plus 100 insertion-geometry variants.
Example tasksIsaac-Lift-Cube-Franka-v0 (lift cube to sampled pose), Isaac-Stack-Cube-Franka-v0 (stack blue/red/green cubes), Isaac-Open-Drawer-Franka-v0 (grasp handle, open Sektion cabinet drawer), Isaac-Reach-Franka/UR10, Isaac-Repose-Cube-Allegro-v0 (in-hand reorient), Isaac-Factory-PegInsert/GearMesh/NutThread-Direct-v0, Isaac-Forge-PegInsert-Direct-v0, AutoMate specialist/generalist assembly over 100 plug-socket geometries, GR-1/G1 humanoid pick-place.
Primitivesreach, lift/pick, place, stack (3 cubes), open (cabinet drawer via handle grasp), in-hand rotate/reorient (Allegro/Shadow), insert (peg-in-hole, plug-socket x100 AutoMate geometries), mesh/turn (gear meshing), screw/thread (nut-on-bolt), pick-and-place (humanoid GR-1/G1 variants)
RobotsFranka Panda (primary), UR10/UR10e, Allegro hand, Shadow hand, OpenArm (uni/bimanual), humanoids GR-1 and Unitree G1; Franka and hands all have MuJoCo Menagerie equivalents.
State observationsManager-based manipulation envs are state-based by default and RL-solved (PPO) from state: joint positions/velocities (proprioception), object pose in robot/world frame, goal/target pose command, previous actions; cabinet adds handle pose and gripper-to-handle relative terms. Vision is opt-in only (a few camera variants gated behind --enable_cameras, e.g. Shadow-hand vision). Factory/FORGE/AutoMate use privileged state (peg/hole poses, EE pose/wrench). So essentially ALL tasks are demonstrated vision-free — matches requirement 1 exactly. Dims vary per env (roughly 20-60 for arm tasks).
Action spaceJoint-position, IK/task-space (differential IK), and operational-space controller variants per task family; Factory/FORGE use task-space pose actions; hands use joint-position. mjlab's action-manager pattern maps 1:1.
AssetsNative format is USD (much of it streamed from NVIDIA Nucleus under NVIDIA Omniverse asset EULA — not redistributable-friendly and no USD-to-MJCF converter exists). BUT the useful subset is available in portable form: IsaacGymEnvs (archived Apr 2026, BSD-3) has Factory URDF+OBJ assets and the Sektion cabinet; AutoMate provides 100 preprocessed watertight OBJ assembly pairs (chamfered + unchamfered, derived from Fusion 360 Gallery / Assemble-Them-All); table-top tasks use primitives; robots via MuJoCo Menagerie. Treat USD as reference-only, source meshes from the older repos.
LicenseIsaac Lab code: BSD-3-Clause (isaaclab_mimic ext: Apache-2.0). Isaac Sim itself proprietary. Nucleus-hosted USD assets under NVIDIA Omniverse asset license (restrictive). IsaacGymEnvs Factory URDF/OBJ assets: BSD-3. AutoMate meshes derive from Fusion 360 Gallery dataset (research-friendly, check dataset terms). Some connector CADs never released for licensing reasons.
CL track recordEssentially none. Continual-learning policy-distillation work (e.g. "Continual Policy Distillation from Distributed RL Teachers", arXiv 2601.22475) uses MetaWorld MT25, not Isaac Lab; no notable CL paper builds on Isaac Lab manipulation envs. Its value here is task design, not CL precedent.
Port to mjlabCheapest design port of any candidate for the manager-based families: re-author each env's ObservationCfg/RewardCfg/EventCfg in mjlab's cloned manager API (you already did this pattern for open_cabinet/lift). Assets: lift/stack/reach/place use primitive cubes + Franka (MuJoCo Menagerie) — near-zero asset work; cabinet uses the Sektion cabinet whose URDF lives in the archived isaac-sim/IsaacGymEnvs repo (BSD-3, OBJ meshes) — URDF-to-MJCF via mujoco compile, ~1 day; Factory assets also in IsaacGymEnvs as URDF+OBJ; AutoMate ships 100 watertight OBJ mesh pairs (plug/socket). Effort: ~0.5-1 day per non-assembly task; assembly tasks 1-2 weeks of contact tuning (need convex decomposition or MuJoCo SDF plugin; nut-threading/gear-meshing may not be faithfully simulable in MJX/warp). Note connector CAD files (BNC/USB/etc.) were never released due to licensing.
Scale potentialRealistically ~10-12 distinct state-solvable tasks ported cheaply (reach, lift, place, stack, open drawer, in-hand reorient, peg insert, gear mesh, nut thread, plus your existing 5 overlap partially), then +20-100 insertion tasks by sweeping AutoMate geometries if peg-in-hole contact works in mujoco-warp. Gets you to 15-20 diverse tasks; 50-100 only via geometric variation of insertion, not skill diversity.
Gotchas(1) The "~15-25 tasks" prior estimate conflates variants with designs — only ~9 manager-based manipulation families exist; headline diversity is thinner than env-ID counts suggest. (2) Contact-rich Factory tasks depend on PhysX SDF collision + Gauss-Seidel solver; MuJoCo convex-hull contact will not reproduce nut-threading/gear-meshing without SDF-plugin or heavy convex decomposition, and MJX/warp support for SDF is limited — budget risk here. (3) Primary USD assets are license-encumbered and there is no USD-to-MJCF path; always source from IsaacGymEnvs/AutoMate/Menagerie instead. (4) Several families (pick_place, dexsuite, deploy) target humanoids/dex hands, not your Franka setup. (5) Some tasks (lift, open cabinet) duplicate tasks you already have.
Repo activity7.7k stars, 3.8k forks, very active (latest release v3.0.0-beta2.patch1, July 2, 2026; 300+ open PRs). IsaacGymEnvs (asset source) archived/read-only since Apr 2026 but still downloadable.
Strong

ManiSkill2 / ManiSkill3 (task-recipe source)

paper · code

GPU-parallel SAPIEN manipulation benchmark whose table-top task family (PickCube, StackCube, PegInsertionSide, PushT, PlugCharger, ...) is a proven, dense-reward, state-solvable task catalog ideal for reimplementation in mjlab.

ManiSkill3: GPU Parallelized Robotics Simulation and Rendering for Generalizable Embodied AI (RSS 2025); ManiSkill2: A Unified Benchmark for Generalizable Manipulation Skills (ICLR 2023)

Verdict: Runtime is SAPIEN/PhysX (excluded), so it cannot be used directly — but it is the single best source of PROVEN state-solvable task designs to reimplement in mjlab. Roughly 15-20 of the ~22 table-top gripper tasks use only primitive shapes (cube, peg, T-block, sphere, charger) with open-source Apache-2.0 dense-reward functions and published state-based PPO results, i.e. you can transplant the task+reward design into mjlab with zero asset dependency. The PartNet-Mobility path (TurnFaucet/OpenCabinet over hundreds of instances) adds scale but needs a URDF-to-MJCF conversion pipeline. Not top-pick only because every task requires reimplementation rather than a trivial port; not assets-only because the task designs and reward code are the most valuable part, not the meshes.

Full details
SimulatorSAPIEN (PhysX 5, GPU-parallelized). NOT MuJoCo — excluded as a runtime per constraints. ManiSkill3 also supports heterogeneous per-env scenes and 30k+ FPS visual collection, but none of that transfers.
# Tasks~35-40 built-in environments in ManiSkill3 docs across categories: 22 table-top 2-finger-gripper tasks (the relevant set), 3 mobile manipulation (OpenCabinetDrawer/Door over many PartNet-Mobility cabinets, RoboCasaKitchen), 4 humanoid, plus control/quadruped/dexterous/drawing/digital-twin categories. Of the 22 table-top tasks, ~16 have dense rewards. Object-variant tasks (PickSingleYCB ~70 YCB objects, OpenCabinet* over PartNet-Mobility, AssemblingKits 20 shapes) multiply this into hundreds of instances.
Example tasksPickCube-v1, StackCube-v1, StackPyramid-v1, PushCube-v1, PullCube-v1, PokeCube-v1, PushT-v1, PlaceSphere-v1, RollBall-v1, LiftPegUpright-v1, PegInsertionSide-v1, PlugCharger-v1, PullCubeTool-v1, PickSingleYCB-v1, PickClutterYCB-v1, AssemblingKits-v1, TurnFaucet-v1, TwoRobotPickCube-v1, OpenCabinetDrawer-v1, OpenCabinetDoor-v1.
Primitivespush (PushCube, PushT, PokeCube), pull (PullCube, PullCubeTool), pick (PickCube, PickSingleYCB, PickClutterYCB), place (PlaceSphere), stack (StackCube, StackPyramid), insert (PegInsertionSide, PlugCharger, AssemblingKits), lift/reorient (LiftPegUpright), open (OpenCabinetDoor, OpenCabinetDrawer), turn/rotate (TurnFaucet, RotateValve, in-hand rotate), roll (RollBall), transport (humanoid TransportBox). Excellent primitive coverage, zero language-variation fluff.
RobotsPanda (default for table-top — matches your Franka setup), Fetch (mobile manip), SO100, WidowX AI, xArm6, Unitree G1/H1 humanoids, trifinger/dexterous hands, quadrupeds. Task code is largely robot-agnostic via an agent abstraction.
State observationsExplicit 'state' obs mode (flat Box) = agent proprioception (qpos, qvel, controller state) + task-specific _get_obs_extra (TCP pose, object poses, goal positions). No vision required. State solvability is demonstrated, not just claimed: official PPO state baselines solve PickCube to ~100% in ~1 minute of training; PegInsertionSide solved from state with tuned PPO configs (1024 envs); official wandb baseline reports exist. Rigid-body tasks all support state mode (only soft-body MS2 tasks do not). Dims vary per task (~30-60 for table-top tasks).
Action spaceMultiple controllers per robot: delta joint position (default for RL baselines), joint velocity, delta end-effector pose (pd_ee_delta_pose), and more; gripper as binary/position control. Maps cleanly onto your existing joint-space and cartesian mjlab controllers.
AssetsTask scenes: mostly procedurally-built primitive shapes (no asset files). Object datasets: YCB (~70 objects, meshes as .glb/OBJ), PartNet-Mobility (~2,300 articulated objects, URDF + OBJ meshes + textures — the key reusable library for open/turn/pull tasks), AssemblingKits shapes. URDF is directly compilable by MuJoCo; meshes need convex decomposition for contacts. ManiSkill packages assets as downloads via its data CLI.
LicenseCode and all rigid-body environments: Apache-2.0 (verified via GitHub API). Assets: CC BY-NC 4.0 per the README (non-commercial; fine for research use, attribution required). PartNet-Mobility originally distributed under SAPIEN's terms-of-use (registration, non-commercial research) — check before redistributing converted MJCF assets publicly.
CL track recordWeak. No prominent continual-learning paper uses ManiSkill as its CL benchmark — the CL manipulation literature standardized on Meta-World/Continual World and LIBERO (e.g. 'Continual Policy Distillation from Distributed RL Teachers', arXiv:2601.22475, uses Meta-World; LIBERO NeurIPS 2023 is vision-centric). Related-but-not-CL: 'State-to-Visual DAgger' (arXiv:2412.13662) trains state-based teachers on ManiSkill tasks and distills them, validating exactly your teacher-training recipe. Using ManiSkill-derived tasks for CL would be a mild novelty point, not a comparability point.
Port to mjlabTwo-tier path. Tier 1 (low effort, high value): reimplement the ~15 primitive-shape table-top tasks (PushCube, PullCube, PokeCube, PickCube, StackCube, StackPyramid, PushT, PlaceSphere, RollBall, LiftPegUpright, PegInsertionSide, PullCubeTool, PlugCharger...) directly in mjlab's manager API — scenes are boxes/cylinders/spheres you author as MJCF, and the dense reward + success functions are readable Apache-2.0 Python you translate ~0.5-1 day per task given your existing 5-task template; expect ~2-4 weeks for 15 tasks including reward tuning under MuJoCo contact dynamics. Tier 2 (moderate): TurnFaucet/OpenCabinetDrawer/Door over PartNet-Mobility — MuJoCo compiles URDF natively but you need a conversion pipeline (mesh cleanup + CoACD convex decomposition for collision, joint limit/damping retuning); ~1-2 weeks to build the pipeline, then each articulated instance is nearly free.
Scale potentialRealistically 15-20 distinct state-solvable Franka tasks from Tier 1 alone (primitive shapes, no assets), reaching your 10-20 goal. Tier 2 unlocks the 50-100 regime: TurnFaucet has ~60 faucet models and OpenCabinet has hundreds of PartNet-Mobility cabinets, so open/close/turn task instances can be generated per articulated object. Combined ceiling: dozens of task families x hundreds of object instances.
Gotchas(1) SAPIEN/PhysX contact behavior differs from MuJoCo — dense rewards tuned there (esp. insertion tasks) will need retuning; PegInsertionSide took 250M steps even natively, expect insertion to be the hard tail. (2) ~6 of the 22 table-top tasks are sparse-reward only in MS3 (AssemblingKits, PlugCharger, TurnFaucet, StackPyramid, PickClutterYCB) — MS2 had dense rewards for some (e.g. TurnFaucet), so pull those from the MS2 git history. (3) Prior-sweep claim of '30-50 task families' is inflated for your purposes: the manipulation-relevant set is ~25. (4) PartNet-Mobility meshes are artist meshes — collision quality after CoACD varies per instance; expect to curate a working subset. (5) Repo org moved (haosulab -> mani-skill); pin the new URL. (6) Asset license CC BY-NC forbids commercial use and complicates public redistribution of converted MJCF.
Repo activity3,090 stars; very active — last push 2026-06-24, 125 open issues, backed by the Hao Su lab / ManiSkill org. One gotcha: repo moved from haosulab/ to mani-skill/ org (old URL redirects).
Merged duplicate assessments (1)
  • [usable] Not MuJoCo and scenes are Python/SAPIEN-API (no scene files to convert), so it fails the simulator requirement as a direct dependency. But it is the single best REFERENCE library of GPU-parallel, state-solvable, dense-reward manipulation primitives: ~15-20 tabletop tasks use only primitive geoms and have open-source dense reward functions that port to mjlab in <1 day each, and PartNet-Mobility URDFs give hundreds of articulated-object instances loadable into MuJoCo. Treat it as a task-recipe + reward-design + asset quarry, not a platform.
Strong

MimicGen (environments + data generator)

paper · code

Automated demonstration-generation system over robosuite/MuJoCo with 12 task families (~26 sim variants) spanning contact-rich and long-horizon manipulation, plus 48K+ released demos.

MimicGen: A Data Generation System for Scalable Robot Learning using Human Demonstrations (CoRL 2023)

Verdict: 12 genuinely distinct, state-solvable MuJoCo/robosuite manipulation tasks plus a demo-generation multiplier (10 human demos -> 1000s of trajectories) that could replace RL teachers for distillation. Not top-pick because: pinned to old robosuite (<1.5) + MuJoCo 2.3.2 (CPU, not MJX/warp), so mjlab use means per-task manual porting; variants (D0/D1/D2) are reset-distribution widenings, not new skills, so real scale is ~12-20 tasks, not 50+; envs have sparse success checks only (no dense rewards for RL teachers); NVIDIA non-commercial license on code/assets.

Full details
Simulatorrobosuite on MuJoCo classic — pinned to robosuite pre-v1.5 commit (v1.4.1 branch OK, "does not support robosuite v1.5+") and MuJoCo 2.3.2 (docs explicitly warn other MuJoCo versions are problematic). Paper's 4 Factory tasks used Isaac Gym but are NOT in the released code. No MJX/warp support.
# Tasks12 robosuite task families / ~26 sim variants (D0/D1/D2 are broader reset distributions of the same task, not distinct skills); project page also shows a later Mobile Kitchen D0 and 2 real-world tasks. Paper claims 18 tasks total incl. 4 Isaac Gym Factory tasks not released.
Example tasksStack (cube stacking), StackThree, Square (nut onto square peg), Threading (needle through hole), Coffee (pod into machine + close lid), ThreePieceAssembly (sequential insertion), HammerCleanup (open drawer, place hammer, close), MugCleanup, Kitchen (multi-stage stove/pot/cabinet), NutAssembly, PickPlace (4 objects to bins), CoffeePreparation (long-horizon: mug, pod, machine)
Primitivespick, place, stack (Stack, StackThree, PickPlace), precise insert (Square/NutAssembly peg insertion, Threading needle-through-hole, ThreePieceAssembly), press/slide/articulated open-close (Coffee machine lid, HammerCleanup drawer, Kitchen stove/cabinet), long-horizon multi-stage (Kitchen, CoffeePreparation, MugCleanup)
RobotsFranka Panda default; datasets also cover Sawyer, IIWA, UR5e (robot-transfer dataset, 16K demos). robosuite controllers: OSC pose / joint.
State observationsYes, state-solvable. robosuite provides ground-truth low-dim obs: proprioception (joint pos/vel, EEF pose, gripper) + object poses; follow-up work reports ~37-dim state / 7-dim action vectors. Released robomimic-format HDF5 datasets include low-dim obs and full simulator states (re-renderable), and the MimicGen paper trains low-dim (non-vision) BC agents successfully — vision is optional, not required.
Action space7-dim: 6-DoF delta end-effector pose (OSC) + 1 gripper; joint-space controllers also available via robosuite
AssetsMJCF XML scenes + STL/OBJ meshes bundled in the repo (robosuite conventions), directly loadable in modern MuJoCo with minor XML fixes; datasets are robomimic HDF5 (obs, actions, sim states) hosted on Hugging Face. Code/assets: NVIDIA non-commercial license; datasets: CC-BY-4.0.
LicenseCode + environments/assets: NVIDIA License (research/evaluation only, non-commercial; only NVIDIA may use commercially). Datasets: CC-BY 4.0. robosuite itself is MIT.
CL track recordNot a standard CL benchmark. Some continual-imitation papers touch it: PPL "Think Small, Act Big" (arXiv:2504.00420) uses MimicGen tasks (e.g. MugCleanup); LOTUS (arXiv:2311.02058) and SPECI (arXiv:2504.15561) are continual imitation works but primarily on LIBERO, which is the dominant robosuite CL benchmark. No CL-via-policy-distillation paper found using MimicGen directly.
Port to mjlabTwo paths. (a) Run as-is in robosuite/MuJoCo-2.3.2 CPU to generate distillation demos, train mjlab students on ported scenes — low effort for data, but obs/action spaces must be matched. (b) Port tasks to mjlab: scenes are plain MJCF+meshes so asset porting is easy (~1 day/task to rebuild scene, success check, and manager-based rewards), but precise-insertion tasks (Square, Threading, ThreePieceAssembly) are contact-rich with non-convex meshes — a known pain point for MJX/warp contact approximation; expect tuning or convex-decomposition work. Realistic estimate: 1-3 days per task family for the easy ones (Stack, PickPlace, MugCleanup, HammerCleanup), 3-7 days for insertion tasks; ~4-8 weeks for all 12. Also note envs ship sparse success only — dense rewards for RL teachers must be authored, or skip RL teachers and distill from MimicGen-generated demos.
Scale potentialRealistically 12-20 distinct tasks: 12 families, plus splitting long-horizon tasks (Kitchen, CoffeePreparation, HammerCleanup) into subtask stages, plus MugCleanup's 12-object variants (O1/O2 object datasets). D0/D1/D2 variants add randomization breadth, not skill diversity. Not a route to 50-100 tasks by itself — but the data-generation algorithm (object-centric segment transforms) is reusable in mjlab to multiply demos across any task set, which is arguably its biggest value for distillation-based CL.
Gotchas1) Hard-pinned old stack: MuJoCo 2.3.2 + robosuite pre-v1.5 + specific robomimic/robosuite_task_zoo commits — clashes with modern MuJoCo installs, use a separate venv. 2) NVIDIA non-commercial license covers code AND task assets (fine for research, blocks commercial reuse); only the datasets are CC-BY. 3) The 4 Factory tasks and real-world tasks from the paper are not in the release — released sim count is 12, matching the prior sweep. 4) No dense rewards — imitation-only benchmark. 5) Data generation is not guaranteed-success: generated trajectories are filtered by success check, with per-task success rates sometimes low for precise tasks, so generation can be slow. 6) HammerCleanup/Kitchen require the extra robosuite_task_zoo dependency.
Repo activity617 stars, 105 forks; releases v1.0.0/v1.0.1 (latest release only migrated datasets to Hugging Face). Effectively maintenance mode — pinned old deps, no robosuite v1.5+ support.
Strong

MolmoSpaces / MS-Bench (Ai2)

paper · code

Ai2's fully open embodied-AI ecosystem (230k scenes, 130k objects, 42M grasps) with MS-Bench, an 8-task atomic nav/manipulation benchmark running natively in MuJoCo on a Franka FR3 — few task types, but the strongest open MJCF asset+grasp library for procedurally generating manipulation tasks.

MolmoSpaces: A Large-Scale Open Ecosystem for Robot Navigation and Manipulation (2026; companion: MolmoBot, arXiv:2603.16861)

Verdict: MuJoCo-native, permissively licensed, and the asset+grasp ecosystem (48k manipulable objects, 42M pre-computed stable grasps, articulated furniture) is a genuine task GENERATOR that can reach 20-100 state-solvable task variants. Held back from top-pick because: only ~7 distinct manipulation primitives (one of the 8 tasks is navigate-to), no demonstrated state-based RL (all published baselines are vision/VLA; experts are scripted planners), scenes are house-scale so a fixed-base-Franka tabletop carve-out is required, and it runs on plain CPU MuJoCo — porting to GPU-parallel mjlab means rebuilding envs and dealing with heavy Objaverse collision meshes. Also brand-new (Feb 2026): zero continual-learning track record.

Full details
SimulatorPlain MuJoCo (CPU, ParallelRolloutRunner workers; classic + Filament renderers). Installing/running the benchmark and data generation is currently supported ONLY in MuJoCo. Assets also ship for Isaac Sim (USD) and ManiSkill; community Isaac Lab-Arena beta. NOT MJX/mujoco-warp — no GPU-parallel physics; optional GPU acceleration is for planning (cuRobo) only.
# Tasks8 base tasks in MS-Bench v1 (verified from paper: navigate-to, pick, pick-and-place, pick-and-place-next-to, pick-and-place-color, open, close, open-door). ~6-7 are manipulation-relevant; open-door needs the mobile RB-Y1. MS-Bench v2 ("extended benchmark for atomic tasks") exists in the repo but adds variations, not new primitive categories. Each task type spans thousands of object/scene instantiations via JSON episode specs.
Example tasksMS-Bench: navigate-to, pick, pick-and-place, pick-and-place-next-to, pick-and-place-color, open, close, open-door — instantiated across diverse scenes/objects with systematic variation in object properties, layout, dynamics. Derivable for you: open-microwave, close-fridge, open-dresser-drawer, pick-mug-place-on-shelf, etc.
Primitivespick, place (into/on receptacle, next-to, color-disambiguated), open, close (cabinets, drawers, refrigerators, microwaves — success = >=15% articulation), door push/pull (mobile), navigate. Missing from your wishlist: insert, turn/rotate, press, slide, stack — you would build those yourself from the asset library.
RobotsFranka FR3 (+ Droid gripper, Robotiq 2F-85 v4) — primary manipulation arm; Rainbow Robotics RB-Y1 (mobile/whole-body); RUM/CAP gripper (NYU GRAIL), I2RT. FR3 is close enough to your Panda that grasp annotations and task setups transfer with minor retuning.
State observationsPrivileged low-level state IS exposed: paper lists joint states, end-effector and base poses, and "task-state information (object and articulation states)" plus task annotations and planner signals alongside RGB/depth. Exact dims not documented. Vision-free SOLVING is implicitly demonstrated: expert data generation uses scripted/motion-planned policies driven by privileged state and 42M pre-computed grasp annotations (no vision), so every benchmark task is solvable from state by construction. However, no state-based RL or low-dim policy baselines are published — you would train those yourself.
Action spaceAbsolute/relative joint-position commands for arms (gravity-compensated stiffness control); absolute/relative base poses for mobile platforms; gripper open/close.
AssetsMJCF + USDA. Objects: ~2k hand-crafted THOR + ~129k converted Objaverse (48k manipulable, 42M stable grasp annotations). Scenes: 120 iTHOR hand-crafted + ~110k ProcTHOR + Holodeck LLM-generated (~230-340k total). Distributed via HuggingFace (allenai/molmospaces) and public R2 buckets with an asset-manager/versioning system. Grasp-generation and house-generation pipelines included. Highly reusable standalone.
LicenseApache 2.0 (code), CC BY 4.0 (most assets/data), ODC-BY 1.0 (Objaverse-derived subsets). All permissive; verified on GitHub README and HF dataset card.
CL track recordNone found. Paper is from Feb 2026 and no continual-learning or policy-distillation papers using MolmoSpaces/MS-Bench surfaced in search; CL+distillation works to date (e.g. arXiv:2601.22475) still use Meta-World. You would be first — fine for a contribution claim, but no established CL protocol/splits exist.
Port to mjlabAssets are plain MJCF (+USDA for Isaac), so object/furniture import into mjlab is straightforward. Realistic path: (1) pull manipulable objects + articulated assets (cabinets, drawers, doors, microwaves, fridges) as MJCF, (2) build tabletop/countertop crops around a fixed-base Franka in mjlab rather than loading whole houses, (3) reuse their per-object stable-grasp annotations for success criteria/reward shaping, (4) write mjlab reward/termination managers per primitive. Effort: days to ~2 weeks for a first batch of 10-20 tasks. Main risk: Objaverse-converted collision geometry may be too heavy for mujoco-warp GPU-parallel stepping — expect to filter for simple objects or re-run convex decomposition. Their env/task Python code (built on CPU MuJoCo, JSON episode specs) does not transfer directly.
Scale potentialHigh as a generator, moderate in skill diversity. From 48k manipulable objects + thousands of articulated furniture pieces you can mint 20-100 mjlab tasks easily (pick-mug, pick-bottle, open-drawer, open-cabinet, open-microwave, close-fridge, place-X-on-Y, place-next-to...), each with randomized instances. But these are variants of ~7 primitives; if your CL study needs genuinely distinct SKILLS (insert, turn, press, stack), MolmoSpaces adds objects, not new primitives — combine with e.g. Meta-World-style tasks for skill breadth.
Gotchas(1) Prior sweep overstated task count: it is 8 base tasks (7 manipulation), not "8 tasks x thousands of combos" as distinct tasks — the thousands are episode samples of the same task type. (2) MuJoCo-native means CPU MuJoCo, not MJX/warp — no GPU-parallel training envs out of the box; mjlab port is asset-level, not code-level. (3) House-scale scenes: fixed-base FR3 in kitchens/rooms, not a tabletop rig — must carve reachable workspaces. (4) Objaverse mesh collision complexity may hurt mujoco-warp throughput. (5) Benchmark is vision/VLA-oriented (prompting, camera occlusion studies); state-based training is supported by the obs API but undocumented — verify the task-state observation actually covers all articulation DOFs before committing. (6) Large downloads (hundreds of GB for full asset sets); use the asset manager to pull subsets.
Repo activity~396 stars; active: v0.2.0 released June 12, 2026; Policy Zoo repo and public leaderboards maintained; docs at allenai.github.io/molmospaces.
Merged duplicate assessments (2)
  • [strong] Not a large task suite (only 8 benchmark tasks, and its bench is vision/zero-shot-eval oriented), so it fails "many ready-made tasks" — but it is the single best MJCF-native asset+annotation bank found: 48K manipulable objects with 42M validated grasps and 22 articulated categories with explicit joint type/axis/range annotations, permissive licenses. For a team that already writes its own mjlab tasks, this trivially generates 20-100+ state-solvable pick/place/open/close tasks. Downgrade from top-pick because zero ready tasks in your framework, env code is CPU-MuJoCo (not warp/MJX), warp-compatibility of collision meshes is unverified, and no continual-learning track record (too new).
  • [strong] Verified MuJoCo-native (benchmark runs ONLY in MuJoCo), Franka FR3, MJCF assets, Apache-2.0 — a near-perfect stack match. But the prior-sweep framing holds: only ~7 manipulation-relevant atomic task types (not 10+ distinct primitives), and it is an imitation/VLA evaluation benchmark (vision+qpos protocol, JSON episode specs), not a set of RL-ready reward-defined envs. Its real value for you is as a task GENERATOR: 48k manipulable MJCF objects + 42M precomputed 6-DoF grasps + articulated fixtures let you procedurally mint dozens-to-hundreds of state-solvable pick/place/open/close task variants in mjlab. The scripted experts (IK + privileged object poses + grasp DB) prove tasks are solvable from low-level state. Not top-pick because you must write your own reward functions and port assets into mujoco-warp-friendly scenes (full household scenes are too heavy for GPU-parallel sim); not assets-only because the task definitions, grasp annotations, and scripted-planner logic are directly reusable, not just meshes.
Strong

MuJoCo Playground (manipulation)

paper · code

DeepMind's GPU-parallel MJX/MuJoCo-Warp RL environment suite; the manipulation subset is ~10 state-based envs (Franka pick/cabinet, Robotiq push, Aloha bimanual, LEAP/Aero in-hand) and is the exact stack the user's current 5-task CL setup is forked from.

MuJoCo Playground (arXiv 2502.08844, 2025)

Verdict: It is the native substrate the user already builds on (5 custom CL tasks live in this local fork), and everything is state-solvable and MuJoCo-native — but upstream offers only ~10 manipulation tasks, of which only ~5 are new to the user (2 Aloha bimanual, 2-3 dexterous-hand, 1 Robotiq push). It cannot by itself deliver 20+ Franka-arm tasks; its value going forward is the registration framework, reward-shaping patterns, and the Aloha/LEAP envs, with new tasks to be authored by hand or imported from other asset suites.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo MJX (JAX) and MuJoCo Warp (selectable backend, `--impl warp`) — same stack as mjlab. Optional pixel rendering via Madrona/MJWarp batch renderer for one env; all others pure state.
# Tasks10 manipulation envs upstream (verified in main-branch registry): AlohaHandOver, AlohaSinglePegInsertion, PandaPickCube, PandaPickCubeOrientation, PandaPickCubeCartesian, PandaOpenCabinet, PandaRobotiqPushCube, LeapCubeReorient, LeapCubeRotateZAxis, AeroCubeRotateZAxis. The vendored copy at /media/cvlab/EXTDRIVE/sudhir/continual_learning/mujoco_playground registers 14 because the user already ADDED PandaOpenDoor, PandaPushButton, PandaPegInHole, PandaPickDevice, PandaOpenCabinetCartesian — i.e. 4-5 of the project's current tasks are homegrown extensions of this suite, not upstream.
