The full Playground has ~40+ envs but the rest are locomotion (quadruped/humanoid/dm_control), not manipulation.
Example tasksPandaPickCube / PandaPickCubeOrientation (lift cube to target pose), PandaPickCubeCartesian (EE-space control, optional vision), PandaOpenCabinet (pull drawer/cabinet handle), PandaRobotiqPushCube (non-prehensile push to goal), AlohaHandOver (bimanual pass object between arms), AlohaSinglePegInsertion (bimanual peg-into-socket), LeapCubeReorient (in-hand cube to target orientation), LeapCubeRotateZAxis / AeroCubeRotateZAxis (continuous in-hand rotation).
Primitivespick, pick+orient, place/handover (bimanual), push (non-prehensile), open (cabinet/drawer), insert (peg-in-hole, bimanual), in-hand reorient, in-hand rotate. Missing upstream: press, turn, slide, stack, close (user already added button-press and door-open themselves).
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (+ Robotiq 2F-85 variant), Aloha (bimanual ViperX 300), LEAP Hand, Aero Hand; locomotion suite has many more (Unitree, Berkeley Humanoid, etc.) via MuJoCo Menagerie.
State observationsAll manipulation envs are pure low-level state; NO env requires vision (PandaPickCubeCartesian has an optional Madrona vision mode upstream; the vendored copy strips it). Verified in source: PandaPickCube obs = robot qpos/qvel (9+9), obj pos/quat/rot-mat, gripper pose, obj-gripper and target-obj deltas, ctrl error (~60-dim flat vector). AlohaHandOver obs = full qpos/qvel + both gripper poses + box top/bottom sites + target delta. LeapCubeRotateZ/Reorient use asymmetric actor-critic: actor gets noisy 32-dim state (joint angles, qpos-error history, cube pos/quat) stacked over history; critic gets privileged exact state. Leap policies were deployed sim-to-real from state (paper), so vision-free solving is demonstrated for every env.
Action spacePanda envs: 8-dim joint-position deltas (7 arm + gripper); PandaPickCubeCartesian: low-dim Cartesian deltas; PandaRobotiqPushCube: arm + Robotiq gripper; Aloha: 14-dim bimanual joint targets; LEAP/Aero hand: 16-dim hand joint targets. Heterogeneous across the new envs — a real complication for a single distilled student policy.
AssetsMJCF (MuJoCo XML) scenes in-repo; robot models pulled automatically from MuJoCo Menagerie (MJCF + STL/OBJ meshes). Fully reusable in any MuJoCo/mjlab pipeline; this is the cleanest possible asset format for the user's stack.
LicenseApache-2.0 (repo); Menagerie robot assets individually licensed (mostly Apache-2.0/BSD, all permissive).
CL track recordNo published continual-learning papers found using Playground's manipulation envs as a CL benchmark (searched July 2026). Recent CL-via-distillation work (e.g. arXiv:2601.22475, Continual Policy Distillation from Distributed RL Teachers) benchmarks on Meta-World instead. The user's own in-progress CL-distillation project on this fork appears to be the first.
Port to mjlabEffectively zero for MJX/Playground itself — already vendored and running in this project. Porting individual envs into mjlab's Isaac-Lab-style manager API means rewriting reward/obs terms as manager terms and reusing the MJCF scenes as-is (the user has already done this pattern 5 times); estimate ~0.5-1 day per env. Leap-hand envs additionally need the LEAP hand model from mujoco_menagerie (auto-downloaded by mjx_env.ensure_menagerie_exists) and a 16-dim hand action space added to mjlab. Aloha envs need the bimanual ALOHA model (also menagerie) and a 14-dim dual-arm action space.
Scale potentialLow as a task source: only ~5-6 envs beyond what the project already has, and only PandaRobotiqPushCube shares the (near-)Franka embodiment; Leap/Aero/Aloha require new action spaces. Realistic extraction: 6-8 new state-solvable tasks, reaching ~11-13 total — not 20+. Its real scaling value is as a GENERATION substrate: the MJCF scene templates + register_environment API + the user's demonstrated pattern of adding envs (door, button, peg-in-hole already added locally) make procedurally generated Franka task variants (object shapes, articulation types, goal families) the plausible route to 20-50 tasks, but that is authoring work, not extraction.
Gotchas(1) Prior-sweep claim of "~10 manipulation envs" is correct upstream, but 4-5 of the project's current 5 tasks are LOCAL additions in the vendored copy — do not double-count them as new. (2) Leap envs use dict observations with asymmetric actor/critic (noisy state history + privileged state) and domain randomizers — different training plumbing than the flat-vector Panda envs. (3) Heterogeneous action spaces (8 vs 14 vs 16 dim) break a single shared student head for continual distillation. (4) Upstream PandaPickCubeCartesian defaults to a vision variant (Madrona renderer) — use the state config. (5) menagerie assets are downloaded at env-load time (ensure_menagerie_exists), which needs network on first run — a known cluster gotcha. (6) The vendored copy has diverged from upstream (v0.2.0 added AeroCubeRotateZAxis, warp backend); syncing may conflict with local edits to pick.py/open_cabinet.py.
Repo activity2.1k stars, 332 forks; actively maintained — v0.2.0 released March 2026, ongoing commits (new Aero hand env added since the paper).
Merged duplicate assessments (2)
  • [usable] Zero porting cost (it is literally the stack this project already vendors and builds its 5 tasks on) and all envs are state-solvable, but the marginal gain is only ~5-6 new envs (2-3 dexterous-hand, 2 Aloha bimanual, 1 Robotiq push), most on DIFFERENT embodiments/action spaces than the Franka. It cannot by itself get you to 20+ Franka manipulation tasks — it is a small high-quality top-up, not a scaling path.
  • [strong] Perfect simulator/API fit (MJX + MuJoCo-Warp native, Apache-2.0, pure state obs, GPU-parallel — it is literally the codebase this project already forked), but the upstream manipulation suite is small: 10 registered manipulation envs, of which only ~7 are arm-based and ~4-5 overlap the existing 5 CL tasks. It cannot by itself yield 20-100 tasks; it is the ideal substrate/reference-implementation source, not a task-scale source. Not top-pick purely on scale grounds.

OGBench manipspace suite

paper · code

ICLR-2025 offline goal-conditioned RL benchmark whose manipspace suite offers UR5e cube/scene/puzzle manipulation in plain MuJoCo with state obs, portable MJCF assets, and scripted oracle policies.

OGBench: Benchmarking Offline Goal-Conditioned RL (ICLR 2025)

Verdict: Meets every hard requirement: pure-MuJoCo MJCF assets (MIT), verified state-based observations, exactly our primitive vocabulary (pick/place/stack/open/close/press/lock), plus scripted oracle expert policies usable directly as distillation teachers. Not top-pick because primitive yield is ~10-20 atomic tasks (the ~50 named tasks are combinatorial goal mixes of few primitives), rewards are sparse success-only (dense rewards must be authored for online RL), and there is no CL track record.

Full details
SimulatorPlain MuJoCo (>=3.1.6), CPU, Gymnasium API; NOT MJX/Warp — GPU-parallel port needed for mjlab, but MJCF scenes are MJX-friendly (Menagerie UR5e/Robotiq + simple primitive-geom objects).
# Tasks~10 env variants (cube-single/double/triple/quadruple/octuple, scene, puzzle-3x3/4x4/4x5/4x6) x 5 eval goals each = ~50 named singletask variants; but only ~10-20 distinct atomic primitive tasks (pick, place, stack, open/close drawer, open/close window, press button, lock/unlock).
Example tasksscene task1_open (open drawer + window); scene task4_put_in_drawer (unlock, open drawer, put cube in, close); cube-double stacking/swap pick-and-place; puzzle-3x3 (press button sequence to reach Lights-Out goal); scene task5_rearrange_hard (cube into drawer + window + button unlocking, up to 8 atomic behaviors).
Primitivespick, place, stack (cubes), open/close (drawer, window), press (buttons), lock/unlock (button toggles gating drawer/window), rearrange; puzzle = sequenced button presses (Lights-Out).
RobotsUR5e arm + Robotiq 2F-85 gripper (MuJoCo Menagerie descriptions). Different from our Franka, but object/task assets are robot-agnostic and can drop into our Franka scene.
State observationsVerified in manipspace_env.py: arm joint pos/vel, EE position + yaw, gripper opening, gripper contact force, plus per-object info (cube xyz+quat+yaw; button binary state + joint pos/vel; drawer/window slide joint pos/vel). Normalized. Pixels (64x64) optional, not required.
Action space5-D continuous in [-1,1]: delta EE xyz (+/-0.05 m), delta yaw (+/-0.3 rad), gripper open/close; DiffIK controller maps Cartesian targets to UR5e joints; 50 Hz control, 2 ms physics.
AssetsNative MJCF XMLs in ogbench/manipspace/descriptions/: cube.xml, drawer.xml, window.xml, buttons.xml (+ inner/outer variants), floor_wall.xml; metaworld/-derived models; robotiq_2f85/ and universal_robots_ur5e/ meshes. All MIT, trivially portable.
LicenseMIT
CL track recordNone found. OGBench is used heavily for offline GCRL (HIQL/CRL/QRL baselines) but no continual/lifelong-learning papers using manipspace surfaced in search. Would be a novel CL substrate.
Port to mjlabModerate (days per task family). MJCF objects (drawer, window, buttons, cubes) drop into our mjlab Franka scene directly; must author dense rewards (envs give sparse 0/1 success only) and replace the Python-side DiffIK UR5e controller with our Franka Cartesian/joint control. Alternatively keep UR5e via Menagerie in mjlab. Scripted markov/plan oracles could be replayed in original envs to distill teachers without any RL.
Scale potentialAdds ~10-20 clean state-solvable primitive tasks (2-4x our current 5), and combinatorial goal composition (multi-cube arrangements, scene goal combos, puzzle configurations) can generate 50+ task instances from the same primitive set if long-horizon tasks are acceptable. Not by itself a path to 100 diverse primitives.
Gotchas1) Offline-first design: no dense rewards, online RL from scratch on these envs is untested; 2) DiffIK controller is Python/CPU per-step — needs Warp/JAX reimplementation for GPU-parallel envs; 3) embodiment mismatch (UR5e vs our Franka); 4) many named tasks are long-horizon composites, inflating apparent task count; puzzle variants are all the same press primitive; 5) cube-octuple/puzzle-4x6 likely intractable from scratch with sparse reward.
Repo activity~435+ stars per fetched page; active, well-maintained reference benchmark (pip package ogbench), widely used in 2025-2026 offline-GCRL papers.
Strong

PartNet-Mobility (SAPIEN asset library)

paper · code: Dataset portal: https://sapien.ucsd.edu/browse (resolves; download gated behind registration). Reference code: https://github.com/haosulab/SAPIEN. Helper: https://github.com/r-pad/partnet_mobility_utils. No official MuJoCo/MJCF conversion repo exists — verified by search; you'd build the converter.

2,346 part-annotated articulated 3D objects (46 categories: cabinets, doors, faucets, microwaves, switches, knobs...) with joint/motion annotations as URDF+OBJ — the standard raw material from which open/close/rotate/press task suites (RoboGen, GAPartNet, PartManip, UniDoorManip) are generated.

SAPIEN: A SimulAted Part-based Interactive ENvironment (CVPR 2020); dataset descends from PartNet (CVPR 2019) + ShapeNet

Verdict: Best single scaling lever for your open/close/turn/press primitives: ~2,347 articulated objects (46 categories, ~14k annotated joints: ~1.9k revolute + ~7.6k prismatic) that directly instantiate hundreds of variants of your existing open-door/open-drawer/push-button envs. Not top-pick because it is NOT MuJoCo-native and the port is real work (per-asset convex decomposition via CoACD, scale/mass/damping normalization, curation of broken assets), the license is non-commercial with registration-gated download, and it has essentially no continual-learning track record — it's a task generator, not a benchmark, so you must write the success/reward templates and do the asset QA yourself.

Full details
SimulatorNone (pure asset dataset: URDF + meshes). Reference runtime is SAPIEN/PhysX; heavily used via ManiSkill. MuJoCo can load URDF directly, and recent work (e.g. Infinigen-Articulated, arXiv:2505.10755) prepares PartNet-Mobility-style articulated assets for MuJoCo RL using CoACD convex decomposition — so a MuJoCo path is proven, just not shipped.
# Tasks0 as shipped (it is a dataset, not a benchmark). Realistic yield: one reward template x per-joint instances = 100s of candidate tasks; after curation expect a comfortable 30-100 solid tasks (ManiSkill1 built ~66 cabinet-door + ~60 cabinet-drawer environments plus TurnFaucet variants from exactly this dataset, which is the best evidence of achievable scale).
Example tasksGenerated per-instance tasks: open cabinet door #1000/…, open drawer of StorageFurniture instance, open microwave/oven/refrigerator/washing-machine door, open laptop lid, open trash-can lid, turn faucet handle, rotate valve/knob, flip light switch, press dispenser button, slide table drawer, close any of the above. (ManiSkill1's OpenCabinetDoor/OpenCabinetDrawer treat each object instance as a separate task variant — the template you'd replicate.)
Primitivesopen/close (doors, drawers, lids, laptops, boxes, ovens, microwaves, refrigerators, washing machines, trash cans), turn/rotate (faucets, knobs, valves, kettle lids), press/toggle (switches, buttons on remotes/dispensers), slide (prismatic drawers, sliding lids), pull (handles), plus pick/push of small articulated items (scissors, pliers, USB caps, bottles).
RobotsNone included — bring your own (your Franka). Downstream uses: floating gripper (Where2Act), single-arm Panda (ManiSkill2 TurnFaucet), mobile Fetch/dual-arm (ManiSkill OpenCabinet*).
State observationsNothing shipped (dataset), but state-solvability is well demonstrated downstream: ManiSkill's OpenCabinetDrawer/OpenCabinetDoor/TurnFaucet built on these assets expose state obs = robot proprioception + target link/handle pose + articulation joint qpos/qvel, with success = joint qpos past threshold (e.g. drawer 90% open, velocities small); RL from state works (ManiSkill papers, DrS, state-to-visual DAgger). For your mjlab port the obs is exactly what you already use for open-door/open-drawer: proprio + handle site pose + hinge/slide joint position — dims identical across instances, so one policy head per task family. Every URDF carries joint type, axis, and limits, so dense rewards (joint progress + handle reaching) are template-able. No vision needed anywhere.
Action spaceN/A for the dataset; whatever your env defines (your existing joint/cartesian Franka action space carries over unchanged).
AssetsPer object: mobility.urdf (kinematic tree, joint types/axes/limits), OBJ meshes + textures, semantics.txt labeling each link (e.g. 'rotation_door', 'translation_drawer', 'handle'). ~2,347 objects / 46 categories. Highly reusable, but raw: meshes are non-convex and non-watertight (need CoACD/VHACD for MuJoCo collisions), scales are not always metric, and dynamics (mass/damping/friction) are placeholder — expect per-category normalization plus dropping ~10-30% broken/exotic instances. r-pad/partnet_mobility_utils (github.com/r-pad/partnet_mobility_utils) helps parse the dataset without SAPIEN.
LicenseCustom academic terms of use (sapien.ucsd.edu/about): non-commercial research/education only, registration with academic email required for download (manual verification for non-.edu emails per SAPIEN issue #283); redistribution not permitted — you likely cannot ship converted assets publicly, only conversion scripts. SAPIEN code repo itself has a non-standard ('Other') license. Unofficial HuggingFace mirrors exist but inherit the same restrictions.
CL track recordEssentially none as a CL suite. Searches found no continual/lifelong-learning paper using PartNet-Mobility as its task sequence; CL manipulation work (LOTUS, CoLLAs'23 Caccia et al., LIBERO-based papers) uses other suites. PartNet-Mobility's downstream use is generalization across object instances (ManiSkill's train/test object splits), not sequential task learning. You would be defining the CL protocol yourself — which also means no baseline numbers to compare against.
Port to mjlabConcrete path: (1) register + download; (2) select 5-10 categories (StorageFurniture, Door, Faucet, Microwave, Oven, Refrigerator, Switch, Laptop, Box, Kettle); (3) per asset: CoACD convex decomposition of collision meshes, normalize scale to metric + set masses/damping/friction from a per-category table, emit MJCF (MuJoCo loads URDF directly, but you'll want native MJCF for sites on handles, actuator-free articulations, and mujoco-warp/MJX compatibility — note MJX/warp prefer modest convex-hull counts, so cap CoACD parts per body); (4) one env template per primitive with success = joint qpos threshold, reward = reach-handle + joint-progress (you already have exactly this for door/drawer); (5) manual QA pass culling bad instances. Estimate: 1-2 weeks for the pipeline + first 20-30 curated tasks; the marginal task after that is nearly free. Main risks: collision quality around thin handles (CoACD tuning), MJX contact-count blowup with many-part decompositions, and untuned joint dynamics making some instances degenerate.
Scale potentialVery high — the strongest of any candidate for your 20-100 goal. ManiSkill1 alone extracted ~125+ per-instance cabinet tasks from this dataset; with open/close x doors/drawers/lids + turn-faucet/valve + press-switch templates, 50-100 curated state-solvable tasks is realistic, and 20-30 is nearly guaranteed. Caveat: tasks within a family are highly correlated (same primitive, different geometry), so for CL diversity you should count task *families* (~8-12) times instances, and decide whether per-instance counts as a 'task' in your protocol.
Gotchas(1) Not a benchmark: zero shipped tasks/rewards/robots — all env engineering is on you. (2) Download is registration-gated (academic email; manual review otherwise) and terms forbid redistribution, so converted MJCF assets probably can't be released with your paper's code — plan to release the conversion script instead. (3) Raw asset quality is uneven: non-metric scales, placeholder inertials/damping, non-watertight meshes, occasional wrong joint limits — budget a curation pass. (4) Convex decomposition of thin handles is the known failure mode; policies can't grasp handles whose collision hulls are blobs. (5) MJX/mujoco-warp performance degrades with many collision primitives per scene — keep decompositions coarse and use one object per env. (6) Instance diversity is geometric, not skill-level: 60 drawer tasks are one skill; don't oversell task count in a CL paper. (7) The sweep's github link (sapien.ucsd.edu/browse) is the dataset portal, not a repo — correct as verified.
Repo activityhaosulab/SAPIEN: ~793 stars, last pushed 2026-06-01 (active). The dataset itself is hosted on sapien.ucsd.edu, stable since 2020 and very widely used (ManiSkill, Where2Act, GAPartNet, UMPNet, FlowBot3D all build on it).
Merged duplicate assessments (1)
  • [assets-only] It is a task-free asset dataset, not a benchmark: 0 tasks, no rewards, no env code. But as an asset source it is the canonical one — 2,346 articulated objects (14K+ annotated movable parts) with URDF kinematics that map directly onto our existing task templates (open door/drawer, push button, turn knob/faucet). Every task you generate from it is trivially state-solvable (object joint qpos + handle/part frames come free from the kinematic annotation). Cost: registration-gated download, non-commercial ShapeNet-style license restricting redistribution of converted assets, and mandatory collision-mesh cleanup (convex decomposition) per object before MuJoCo will simulate drawers/handles correctly. Use it to scale our 5 tasks to 20-100 by instancing task families over curated objects; do not expect any ready-made benchmark scaffolding.
Strong

RoboCasa / RoboCasa365

paper · code

Large-scale robosuite/MuJoCo kitchen benchmark: 365 tasks (65 atomic manipulation primitives + 300 composite) across 2,500 procedurally generated kitchens with 3,200+ objects and demo datasets.

RoboCasa: Large-Scale Simulation of Everyday Tasks for Generalist Robots (RSS 2024); updated as RoboCasa365: A Large-Scale Simulation Framework for Training and Benchmarking Generalist Robots (ICLR 2026)

Verdict: MuJoCo-native (robosuite/MJCF) with 65 atomic primitive tasks that exactly match your taxonomy and expose ground-truth object-pose state — but it is CPU-MuJoCo, imitation-first with sparse-only rewards, and full kitchen scenes are too heavy for mjlab GPU parallelism, so the realistic use is extracting fixtures+objects into minimal mjlab scenes and writing dense rewards per task. Not top-pick because zero published state-based RL results and moderate per-task porting work; far more than assets-only because task logic/success predicates are reusable Python on MJCF.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo via robosuite v1.5 (CPU, single-env; MJCF-native). NOT MJX/mujoco-warp — no GPU-parallel envs out of the box. Default embodiment PandaOmron (Franka arm on mobile base); v1.0 supports custom robot composition.
# TasksRoboCasa (RSS 2024): 100 tasks (25 atomic + LLM-guided composites). RoboCasa365 (ICLR 2026, arXiv 2603.04356, repo v1.0 Feb 18 2026): 365 tasks = 65 atomic + 300 composite (60 kitchen activities, 2–15+ subtasks each). Prior sweep's "8 fixture-interaction families" claim confirmed.
Example tasksAtomic: PnPCounterToCab, PnPCounterToSink, PnPCounterToMicrowave, OpenSingleDoor, CloseDoubleDoor, OpenDrawer, CloseDrawer, TurnOnStove (knob twist), TurnOnSinkFaucet, TurnSinkSpout, TurnOnMicrowave (button press), CoffeeSetupMug (insertion), CoffeePressButton, plus v1.0 additions for oven, toaster, toaster oven, electric kettle, stand mixer, blender, NavigateKitchen. Composite: StackCans, FillKettle, PlaceLidToBoil, GatherTableware, etc.
Primitives10 macro skill sets over 14 fixture types: pick-and-place (counter/cabinet/sink/microwave/stove/oven...), open/close doors (single/double/hinged), open/close drawers, turn knobs (stove), turn levers/faucets (sink, incl. spout rotation), press buttons (coffee machine, microwave, toaster, kettle, blender, stand mixer), insertion (mug into coffee machine, bread into toaster), navigation. Composite tasks chain these.
RobotsFranka Panda arm on Omron mobile base (PandaOmron) is the default; v1.0 adds custom robot composition (arm/gripper/base/torso modules). Atomic manipulation tasks mostly usable with fixed base; some tasks (navigate, cross-kitchen pick-place) require the mobile base.
State observationsYes — verified in robocasa/environments/kitchen/kitchen.py: base Kitchen env registers ground-truth observables for ALL task objects (absolute pos+quat and eef-relative pose) plus eef pose, mobile-base pose, gripper state; robosuite low_dim extraction (dataset_states_to_obs.py) is supported. HOWEVER: no dense rewards (reward = float(_check_success()), sparse binary only) and no published vision-free/state-based RL solving of these tasks — the benchmark is teleop+MimicGen imitation-first with RGB policies. State-solvability is plausible for the atomic tasks (they are short-horizon primitives with scripted success predicates) but you would write dense rewards yourself.
Action spaceComposite controller: arm (OSC/IK cartesian or joint), gripper, mobile base, torso — body_part_ordering ["right","right_gripper","base","torso"] for PandaOmron. Configurable per robosuite v1.5 controller configs.
AssetsAll MJCF + meshes (MuJoCo-native): 2,500 kitchen scenes (procedural floor plans, 10+ layouts x styles), 3,200+ objects (150+ categories; Objaverse-sourced + AI-generated), 14 articulated fixture types (cabinets, drawers, microwave, oven, stove with knobs, sink with faucet/lever/spout, coffee machine, toaster, kettle, blender, stand mixer, dishwasher, fridge). ~10 GB download. Assets CC BY 4.0, directly loadable in MuJoCo — highly reusable. Also 600h human + 1,600h synthetic (MimicGen) demos.
LicenseCode: MIT (LICENSE file verified, with Apache-2.0 notice for vendored MuJoCo parts). Assets/datasets: CC BY 4.0. No restrictions on porting.
CL track recordYes — RoboCasa365 (ICLR 2026, arXiv:2603.04356) itself defines a lifelong-learning benchmark track (sequential acquisition of tasks while retaining prior ones) and reports it as an open challenge; that track is imitation-learning-based, not RL/distillation. The original RoboCasa has been widely used for generalist/foundation-model training (e.g., GR00T N1) but I found no third-party continual-RL papers on it.
Port to mjlabDo NOT try to run whole RoboCasa kitchens in mjlab — full scenes have thousands of geoms/meshes and articulated fixtures, hostile to mujoco-warp batching. Realistic path (matches how you built your current 5 tasks): (1) extract individual fixture MJCFs (cabinet, drawer, microwave, stove knob, faucet lever, coffee machine, toaster...) + object meshes into minimal single-fixture scenes with your Franka; (2) reimplement each atomic task's success predicate (short Python in robocasa, easy to translate) as an mjlab manager-based env; (3) write dense rewards yourself (RoboCasa has none). Estimate ~0.5-1.5 days per task after a 2-3 day one-time asset-conversion/collision-simplification pipeline (convex decomposition of Objaverse meshes is the main pain). robosuite-side validation is trivial since it is already MuJoCo.
Scale potentialRealistically 20-50 tasks: the 65 atomic tasks reduce to ~15-20 distinct fixture-interaction templates, each multiplied by object category (150+) and fixture-style variation — an effective task generator for your regime. Composite tasks are long-horizon and demo-oriented; mostly out of scope for state-based RL teachers. The fixture library alone (14 articulated appliance types) directly yields 25+ open/close/turn/press/insert tasks beyond your current 5.
Gotchas(1) Sparse rewards only — every task needs a hand-written dense reward for RL teachers; no one has published state-based RL on these tasks. (2) Default embodiment is a mobile-base Panda; some atomic tasks (navigate, far-side pick-place) assume base motion. (3) Prior sweep numbers were stale: it is now 365 tasks/65 atomic, not 100/25. (4) Objaverse/AI-generated meshes have heavy non-convex collision geometry — needs decomposition/simplification for mujoco-warp. (5) robosuite master-branch dependency and Python 3.11 pin; ~10 GB asset download. (6) The lifelong-learning track is imitation-based — its demo datasets (600h human + 1600h MimicGen) could alternatively serve as distillation targets if you relax the RL-teacher requirement.
Repo activity~1.5k stars, 223 forks; actively maintained — v1.0 "RoboCasa365" release Feb 2026, docs at robocasa.ai, LeRobot integration on HuggingFace.
Merged duplicate assessments (2)
  • [strong] Best-in-class MuJoCo-native asset bank (clean hand-modeled articulated fixture MJCFs + 3,200 object MJCFs) whose 65 atomic tasks map almost 1:1 onto your existing 5 task types, and every env exposes ground-truth object-pose state observables. Not top-pick only because it is an imitation-learning benchmark: rewards are sparse success checks (you must write dense rewards per task for RL teachers), envs run in classic CPU robosuite (not GPU-parallel — each task must be re-implemented in mjlab's manager API), and the canonical robot is a mobile-base Franka in a full kitchen scene, so you extract fixtures into tabletop Franka scenes rather than "use directly".
  • [strong] Largest MuJoCo-native manipulation task set available (65 atomic + 300 composite, verified in code: 77 atomic classes / 301 composite files), MIT-licensed MJCF+OBJ assets, ground-truth object-pose observables built in, and an explicit lifelong-learning benchmark track. Not top-pick because: it is CPU robosuite (no GPU batching — incompatible with mjlab's mujoco-warp parallelism without a port), all baselines are vision+BC-from-demos (no dense rewards exist, so state-based RL requires writing rewards per task yourself), and many tasks assume a Franka on an Omron mobile base (only 145/365 doable fixed-base). Best used as a task-design + asset quarry: extract 20-40 fixed-base atomic tasks (fixtures are clean standalone MJCF) into mjlab rather than running the framework directly.
Strong

RoboDesk

paper · code

Single Franka-desk MuJoCo scene from Google Research (Kannan, Hafner, Finn, Erhan, 2021) with 9 benchmark / ~22 code-level manipulation tasks (buttons, drawer, slide, stack, lift, place-in-bin/shelf), all with state-based rewards and a full state observation dict.

No paper — released 2021 as code + blog by Kannan, Hafner, Finn, Erhan; cited as "Kannan et al., 2021 (RoboDesk)" pointing at the GitHub repo.

Verdict: MuJoCo-native (dm_control MJCF), same Franka arm as our mjlab tasks, full low-level state in the obs dict, and rewards computed purely from state — so every task is state-solvable by construction. Real usable task count is ~12 core / ~22 total parameterized tasks (not just the 9 headline tasks), which cleanly gets us from 5 to ~25 tasks. Not a top-pick because it caps out there: one fixed desk scene, no procedural task generation, no path to 50-100 tasks, minimal CL track record, and the repo is unmaintained (68 stars, last touched ~2023).

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo via dm_control (`from dm_control import mujoco`; `mujoco.Physics.from_xml_path('assets/desk.xml')`). Pure MJCF + meshes, no mujoco_py. Straightforwardly loadable in modern mujoco/MJX/mujoco-warp (scene is simple: arm + desk + blocks/ball/drawer/slide/buttons).
# Tasks9 official benchmark tasks; code defines 12 "core" tasks and ~21-22 total via parameterized reward functions (8 reward methods x object/color variants). Prior-sweep claim of "9 tasks" undersells it by ~2x.
Example tasksopen_slide, open_drawer, push_green/push_blue/push_red (buttons), stack, upright_block_off_table, flat_block_in_bin, flat_block_in_shelf, lift_upright_block, lift_ball, flat_block_off_table; extras: ball_in_bin, ball_in_shelf, upright_block_in_bin, upright_block_in_shelf, ball_off_table, lift_flat_block.
Primitivespush (buttons, blocks off table), pull/open (drawer), slide/open (sliding door), pick/lift (block, ball), stack, place (in bin, in shelf), reorient (upright/flat block). No insert, turn-knob, or screw primitives.
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (same platform as our existing 5 mjlab tasks) — 9D arm+gripper qpos.
State observationsObs dict: qpos_robot (9), qvel_robot (9), end_effector xyz (3), qpos_objects (26), qvel_objects (26), plus 64x64x3 image. README says "for the standard benchmark, only the image should be used" — a convention, not a constraint: all rewards/success criteria are computed from state (distances, drawer/slide joint positions, containment/height checks), so vision-free solving is trivially supported and MMBench-style work uses it with state obs. ~73D state total; no camera needed.
Action space5D continuous Box(-1,1): end-effector XYZ deltas (x0.01), wrist rotation delta (x0.02), gripper (x0.02), applied via IK to the Franka joints. In an mjlab port we would replace this with our own joint/IK action manager.
AssetsSingle desk.xml MJCF scene + mesh files (derived from prior open-source Franka releases), packaged inside the pip package/repo. Directly reusable in any MuJoCo pipeline; the desk fixture (drawer, sliding door, 3 buttons, bin, shelf) is a nice ready-made articulated tabletop that complements our existing cabinet/door assets.
LicenseApache-2.0 (verified on repo).
CL track recordThin but nonzero: used in "Continual Visual Reinforcement Learning with a Life-Long World Model" (ECML PKDD, Springer 2025) and as a domain in the MMBench 200-task multitask world-model benchmark (arXiv 2511.19584, state obs). It is NOT a standard CL benchmark like Continual World/Meta-World; CORA does not use it. Mostly known from Hafner-line world-model work (e.g., Director).
Port to mjlabLow-moderate, and unusually clean for us: (1) load desk.xml directly in mujoco/mujoco-warp — dm_control MJCF is plain MuJoCo XML; verify it compiles under MJX/warp constraints (mesh collisions, equality constraints on drawer/slide joints may need convex-decomposition or primitive approximations for warp); (2) wrap the scene in mjlab's manager API — one scene config plus ~8 reward-manager implementations transcribed from robodesk.py's state-based reward functions (they are short distance/threshold computations); (3) swap their 5D IK action space for our existing Franka action manager. Estimate: ~2-4 days for scene port + first task, then <1 day per additional task since they share the scene. Biggest risk: making the articulated desk (drawer + slide + buttons) warp-friendly.
Scale potential~22 tasks out of the box; realistically ~25-30 with easy manual variants (per-color button/block combinations, goal-region variants). One fixed scene and a small object set (2 blocks, 1 ball, drawer, slide, 3 buttons) means no procedural generation path — this is a +15-20-task booster that pairs well with our current 5 tasks (same robot, tabletop setting), not a route to 50-100. Combine with a larger suite (e.g., Meta-World-style) for the 50+ regime.
Gotchas(1) The "9 tasks" figure everywhere refers to the official benchmark subset; the code's task registry has 12 core + extras — read robodesk.py, not the README table, when enumerating. (2) README's "only the image should be used" is benchmark etiquette for the vision setting; ignore it — full state is exposed and rewards are state-computed. (3) qpos_objects is 26D raw MuJoCo qpos (includes free-joint quaternions + articulation joints), not a tidy per-object pose list — you will re-derive per-object poses in mjlab anyway. (4) Depends on dm_control-era rendering; for mjlab you take only the XML/meshes and reward logic, not their env wrapper. (5) Unmaintained repo — pin versions; one known rendering/egl issue in old installs. (6) Dense rewards are shaped for their 5D IK action space; may need re-tuning for our controllers.
Repo activity~68 stars, 2 open issues (from 2021), effectively unmaintained since release (last meaningful update ~2021, page metadata 2023). Stable but frozen; expect to own any fixes ourselves.
Strong

RoboHive

paper · code

Unified MuJoCo robot-learning ecosystem whose Franka Kitchen (FK1) suite alone gives 20 dense-reward, state-based manipulation subtasks on our exact Franka embodiment, plus portable MJCF appliance assets.

RoboHive: A Unified Framework for Robot Learning (NeurIPS 2023 Datasets & Benchmarks)

Verdict: Verified ~30 distinct state-based Franka-relevant manipulation tasks (20 kitchen + ~6 arms, all MuJoCo-native MJCF, Apache-2.0) on exactly our embodiment, with dense rewards and state-solvability demonstrated via NPG baselines. Not top-pick because the framework itself (CPU MuJoCo + gym API) cannot be adopted into GPU-parallel mjlab — every task must be re-implemented in the manager API against the imported MJCF assets — mujoco-warp compatibility of the mesh-heavy kitchen scene is unverified, and scaling beyond ~30 relevant tasks requires manual authoring. The kitchen appliance assets (microwave, hinged/sliding cabinets, stove knobs, light switch, kettle) are the single best drop-in source for our open/close/turn/slide/press primitive expansion.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (native python bindings, MuJoCo 3.1.3 as of v0.7; CPU-stepped gym/gymnasium envs — no MJX/warp support in-framework)
# TasksFranka-relevant state-based: ~26-30 distinct (FK1 kitchen 20 + arms 6). Registered env ids far higher (60 kitchen ids across Fixed/Random/visual variants; 39 hands ids; ~50 Myo musculoskeletal; claws/quadrupeds/TCDM extras) — README's "100+" counts variants and non-manipulation suites.
Example tasksFK1_MicroOpenFixed-v4, FK1_MicroCloseFixed-v4, FK1_Knob1OnFixed-v4 (x4 knobs, on/off), FK1_RdoorOpen/Close, FK1_LdoorOpen/Close, FK1_SdoorOpen/Close (slide), FK1_LightOn/Off, FK1_Stove1KettleFixed-v4, FrankaPushRandom-v0, FrankaPickPlaceRandom-v0, FrankaReachRandom-v0, door-v1/hammer-v1/pen-v1/relocate-v1 (Adroit)
Primitivesopen, close, slide, turn (knobs), press/flip (light switch), reach, push, pick-place, relocate, hammer, in-hand reorient (pen, baoding)
RobotsFranka Panda (kitchen + arms), Fetch, Sawyer (assets), Adroit/Shadow Hand, D'Manus, TriFinger, Robotiq gripper, MyoSuite musculoskeletal models, quadrupeds
State observationsConfirmed pure-state by default: obs assembled from obs_keys dicts, e.g. arms PickPlace = qp_robot, qp_object, qv_robot, qv_object, object_err, target_err (~30-40 dims); kitchen tasks = robot qpos/qvel + appliance joint states + goal-error terms. Visual variants are SEPARATE env ids (suffix _v2d/_vr3m*/_vrrl*) that explicitly suppress state obs to avoid oracle leakage — i.e., the maintainers treat state as the privileged oracle. State-solvability demonstrated: NPG baselines and expert demos ship for kitchen/arms/hands/myo (v0.6 release notes).
Action spaceJoint-space position/torque control via gym API (Franka 9-DOF incl. gripper in arms suite; kitchen uses 9-DOF Franka). No built-in Cartesian/IK controller for RL envs; teleop controllers (spacemouse/Xbox/keyboard) added in v0.7.
AssetsMJCF (XML + meshes) organized as 13 git submodules under simhive: franka_sim, furniture_sim (kitchen: microwave, hinged+sliding cabinets, 4-knob stove, light switch, kettle), YCB_sim, object_sim, Adroit, fetch_sim, sawyer_sim, myo_sim, etc. All plain MJCF, load in modern MuJoCo — highly reusable independently of the framework.
LicenseApache-2.0 (main repo; asset submodules by same author, generally Apache-2.0 — spot-check per submodule before redistribution)
CL track recordNot a canonical CL benchmark itself, but its Franka Kitchen tasks are the backbone of multi-task/lifelong distillation work: PolyTask (arXiv:2310.08573) distills kitchen subtasks into a unified policy; kitchen is standard in D4RL offline RL and Relay Policy Learning lineage; EXTRACT (arXiv:2406.17768) uses it for transferable-skill learning. Directly analogous to our policy-distillation CL setup.
Port to mjlabAsset-level port, not framework adoption: (1) vendor furniture_sim/franka_sim MJCF into mjlab scenes — kitchen XML loads in MuJoCo 3.x already; (2) verify mujoco-warp steps the mesh-heavy kitchen scene (main risk: collision mesh count/contact settings; may need convex-decomposition or primitive-collision simplification); (3) re-implement each subtask as mjlab manager-API rewards/terminations/obs — the RoboHive reward dicts (dense distance+bonus terms) translate almost line-for-line. Estimate: ~1 week for scene import + warp validation, then ~0.5-1 day per task; 20 kitchen tasks in ~3-4 weeks total.
Scale potentialRealistically 20-30 state-solvable Franka tasks near-term (20 kitchen + push/pick-place/reach variants). The kitchen scene supports combinatorial task generation (any appliance-state -> goal-state pair, multi-stage sequences a la RPL's 4-subtask chains), plausibly 40-60 generated tasks. YCB_sim/object_sim submodules add object diversity for pick/push/place task generation beyond that. Getting to 50-100 requires our own procedural task authoring on top of these assets.
Gotchas(1) Task count inflation: "100+ envs" counts visual-encoder variants and Myo/locomotion suites; Franka-relevant distinct tasks ~26-30. (2) Assets live in git submodules — plain clone is empty; need --recursive. (3) Framework is CPU-MuJoCo gym envs — useless for our GPU-parallel training, assets/reward-specs only. (4) Kitchen scene mujoco-warp compatibility unverified (mesh-heavy appliances). (5) FK1 kitchen robot is welded 9-DOF Franka with its own controller config — action-space conventions differ from mjlab defaults. (6) v4 kitchen envs differ from the older D4RL/gymnasium-robotics FrankaKitchen — results not directly comparable. (7) Repo maintenance slowed since Mar 2024.
Repo activity629 stars, 97 forks, 647 commits; last release v0.7 Mar 2024 (MuJoCo 3.1.3, Gymnasium) — maintenance has slowed, effectively stable/low-activity as of mid-2026
Strong

robosuite (+ robomimic / MimicGen envs)

paper · code

Modular MuJoCo manipulation framework with ~9-14 benchmark environments (single- and two-arm), the de-facto MJCF substrate under robomimic, MimicGen, LIBERO and RoboCasa; all core tasks proven solvable from proprio + object-state vectors.

robosuite: A Modular Simulation Framework and Benchmark for Robot Learning (Zhu et al., 2020); companion: robomimic "What Matters in Learning from Offline Human Demonstrations" (arXiv:2108.03298)

Verdict: MuJoCo-native, MIT-licensed, actively maintained (v1.5.2, Dec 2025), and every core task is demonstrably solvable from low-level state (robosuite's own SAC benchmarks and robomimic's low-dim BC results both use proprio + object-pose vectors, no vision). But by itself it only yields ~9 base tasks (~15-20 with PickPlace/NutAssembly single-object variants), short of the 20+ goal, and the envs are CPU-MuJoCo Python classes that must be re-implemented (not just re-loaded) in mjlab's manager API. Its real value is as the highest-quality MJCF asset/task-design substrate, which the MimicGen/LIBERO/RoboCasa ecosystem extends to 100+ tasks. Use it as the backbone plus ecosystem tasks rather than as a standalone suite.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (classic CPU MuJoCo via official python bindings since v1.4; robosuite v1.5.2 latest, Dec 2025). NOT MJX/mujoco-warp — no GPU-parallel stepping out of the box. v1.5 adds Isaac Sim photorealistic rendering (rendering only, physics stays MuJoCo).
# Tasks9 canonical benchmark tasks; 12 manipulation env files in repo (adds ToolHang, TwoArmTransport). With built-in single-object variants (PickPlace: Milk/Bread/Cereal/Can; NutAssembly: Square/Round) ~15-20 distinct trainable tasks. robomimic curates demos + low-dim configs for 5 of them (Lift, Can, Square, Transport, ToolHang).
Example tasksLift (raise cube), Stack (cube on cube), PickPlace{Milk,Bread,Cereal,Can} (bin-to-bin), NutAssembly{Square,Round} (nut onto peg), Door (open with handle/latch), Wipe (clean markers off table), ToolHang (insert hook, hang wrench — high precision), TwoArmLift (bimanual pot lift), TwoArmPegInHole, TwoArmHandover, TwoArmTransport.
Primitiveslift, stack (2 and 3 blocks), pick-place (bins, 4 objects), insert/thread (needle threading, nut-onto-peg, square peg), multi-part assembly (ThreePieceAssembly), press/rotate (coffee machine lid, pod insertion), open/close (drawer in HammerCleanup/MugCleanup, kitchen cabinet), door opening, wipe/slide (TableWipe), pour-adjacent serving (Kitchen: stove, pot, button), handover, peg-in-hole (two-arm)
RobotsPanda, Sawyer, UR5e, Kinova3, Jaco, IIWA, Baxter; v1.5 adds mobile manipulators (e.g. Tiago, Google Robot) and humanoids (GR-1 etc.), plus composable custom robots. Multiple grippers per arm.
State observationsExplicit low-dim state path, vision-free by construction: set use_camera_obs=False. Obs dict has `robot0_proprio-state` (joint pos as sin/cos, joint vel, EE pose, gripper finger pos/vel; ~32-40 dims for a Panda) plus task-specific `object-state` (object poses, handle positions, gripper-to-object relative vectors; ~10-40 dims per task). Vision-free solving is demonstrated twice over: robosuite's official SAC benchmarking uses exactly these proprio+object observations, and robomimic (arXiv:2108.03298) reports near-100% BC success on low-dim Lift/Can and strong results on Square/Transport/ToolHang from state only.
Action spacePluggable controllers: joint velocity/torque, IK, operational-space control (OSC pose/position), and v1.5 whole-body control; typically 7-DoF arm + 1 gripper dim. Matches your existing Cartesian/OSC setup well.
AssetsAll MJCF: arenas (table, bins, wipe surface), object models (XML + STL/OBJ meshes and textures), robot and gripper MJCF. Procedural object classes (Box/Cylinder/composite objects) generate MJCF programmatically — reusable as a task-generation library. robomimic ships HDF5 demo datasets (proprietary format but plain HDF5) for 5 tasks in both low-dim and image variants.
Licenserobosuite: MIT (portions Apache-2.0 from MuJoCo) — verified in LICENSE file. MimicGen code/envs: NVIDIA Source Code License — non-commercial research/evaluation only (verified); datasets CC-BY 4.0. Mirror exists (BerkeleyAutomation/mirage_mimicgen_environments).
CL track recordYes, mainly via its derivatives: LIBERO (NeurIPS 2023, arXiv:2306.03310) is an explicit lifelong/continual-learning benchmark built directly on robosuite; LOTUS (continual imitation learning via skill discovery, arXiv:2311.02058) uses robosuite/LIBERO envs. robomimic tasks are standard in multi-task/transfer IL work. Note: the recent continual-policy-distillation paper (arXiv:2601.22475) uses Meta-World, not robosuite — plain robosuite itself is less common as a raw CL suite than Meta-World/Continual World, largely because of its small base task count.
Port to mjlabAssets: trivial — everything is MJCF (arenas, objects, Panda/gripper models), directly loadable in mujoco/mujoco-warp; MIT license permits vendoring. Env logic: robosuite composes scenes procedurally in Python and computes rewards/resets on CPU, so each task needs a rewrite in mjlab's manager API (scene MJCF export via env.sim.model, then reimplement reward terms, success criteria, and randomized resets as warp/JAX-friendly terms). Comparable to what you already did for your 5 tasks: roughly 0.5-1.5 days per task; Lift/Stack/Door/PickPlace variants are easy, ToolHang and Wipe (soft-contact reward with many markers) are the hard ones. Two-arm tasks need a dual-arm scene but no exotic MuJoCo features. Realistic estimate: 2-4 weeks for ~12-15 ported tasks.
Scale potentialStandalone: ~15-20 state-solvable tasks (base + object variants + goal/pose randomization variants you define). Via its ecosystem the ceiling is much higher: MimicGen adds ~12 further robosuite-MJCF tasks (Coffee, Threading, ThreePieceAssembly, MugCleanup, Kitchen, etc., each with D0/D1/D2 difficulty variants), LIBERO adds 130 language-annotated tasks (procedurally generated from PDDL over robosuite scenes — the scene/goal machinery, not the language, is the useful part), RoboCasa ~100 kitchen tasks on robosuite v1.5. Realistically 30-50 state-solvable single-arm tasks extractable from robosuite+MimicGen assets alone.
Gotchas1) Env stepping is CPU MuJoCo with heavy Python object-oriented scene composition — nothing runs on GPU as-is; you port task *designs* and assets, not code. 2) Scenes are generated procedurally at runtime, so you must dump the composed MJCF (env.sim.model) per task rather than finding static scene XMLs in the repo. 3) Wipe's reward depends on ~100 contact-sensed markers — awkward in a warp/vector setting. 4) ToolHang and NutAssembly need tight-tolerance mesh collisions; verify mujoco-warp contact fidelity before committing. 5) robomimic demo datasets were collected with OSC controllers at specific control frequencies — replaying them in a re-implemented mjlab env will not match exactly (fine for RL-teacher + distillation, a problem only if you wanted the demos themselves). 6) v1.5 refactored controller/robot APIs; older third-party task code (some MimicGen versions, LIBERO) pins robosuite v1.4.x.
Repo activity~2.5k stars, 732 forks, 1,274 commits; actively maintained — v1.5 (Oct 2024) added humanoids/mobile manipulators and Isaac Sim rendering, latest release v1.5.2 (Dec 2025).
Merged duplicate assessments (1)
  • [strong] MuJoCo-native, MIT-licensed substrate with ~40 distinct state-solvable task configs (robosuite core 9-10 + MimicGen 12 families x D0-D2 = 26+ variants), all with explicit object-pose observables and proven low-dim BC/RL solvability (robomimic low_dim pipeline). Not top-pick because: (a) it is classic CPU MuJoCo, not MJX/warp — porting to mjlab means extracting MJCF scenes and re-implementing rewards/resets in the manager API per task (procedurally-composed XML, Python-side reward logic); (b) MimicGen env code is NVIDIA Source Code License (non-commercial research only, fine for academia but viral into derived code); (c) high-precision tasks (nut threading, 3-piece assembly) rely on mesh-mesh contact that needs validation under mujoco-warp. Assets and task designs are excellent; expect ~2-4 weeks to port 15-20 tasks.
Strong

RoboVerse / MetaSim

paper · code

Unified multi-simulator robot-learning platform (MetaSim) that re-hosts 200+ tasks from RLBench, LIBERO, ManiSkill, Meta-World, robosuite, CALVIN etc. under one config format, with MJCF/URDF/USD asset conversions and state-based success checkers — best used as a task/asset extraction source for mjlab, not a training platform.

RoboVerse: Towards a Unified Platform, Dataset and Benchmark for Scalable and Generalizable Robot Learning (RSS 2025 per repo README; prior sweep's "CoRL 2025" is wrong)

Verdict: The single richest verified source of MuJoCo-ready (MJCF) manipulation task definitions + state-based success criteria at scale (66 RLBench tasks alone with per-object MJCF conversions on HuggingFace), Apache-2.0, very active, and it already has a documented, parity-tested integration with mjlab itself. Not top-pick because (a) most re-hosted tasks ship demo trajectories + sparse success checkers, NOT dense rewards — you must write reward shaping per task to train RL teachers from state; (b) MuJoCo handler is CPU-based (MJX handler explicitly "not fully supported yet"), so you'd extract assets/tasks into mjlab rather than train inside RoboVerse; (c) physics fidelity of auto-converted RLBench/CoppeliaSim assets is unvalidated per task; (d) zero continual-learning track record.

Full details
SimulatorSimulator-agnostic via MetaSim handlers: mujoco (CPU, most mature non-Isaac backend), mujoco-mjx (WIP, "not fully supported yet"), isaacsim/isaacgym/isaaclab, sapien2/3, pybullet, genesis. Primary paper results use IsaacSim. Plus a passthrough + partial physics-port integration of mjlab (mujoco-warp) itself.
# Tasks~200-270 registered tasks across packs (verified in repo): RLBench 68, LIBERO-90 68, LIBERO 16, ManiSkill ~30 (subset with dense rewards), SimplerEnv ~33, pick_place ~14, CALVIN ~8, robosuite 6, Meta-World (native passthrough), HumanoidBench 19, plus dm_control/gym_robotics/mujoco_playground/myosuite passthroughs. Manipulation-from-state relevant core: ~150-180.
Example tasksrlbench: close_box, close_drawer, close_jar, close_microwave, open_door, open_drawer, open_oven, open_window, open_wine_bottle, insert_onto_square_peg, plug_charger_in_power_supply, lamp_on/lamp_off, change_clock, lift_numbered_block, pick_and_lift, place_shape_in_shape_sorter, meat_on_grill, hockey; maniskill: pick_cube, push_cube, pull_cube, stack_cube, poke_cube, peg_insertion_side, plug_charger, place_sphere, lift_peg_upright, roll_ball; libero: libero_pick_{alphabet_soup,ketchup,milk,...}; metaworld/calvin/robosuite packs.
Primitivespush, pick, place, lift, stack, open/close (box, door, drawer, fridge, grill, jar, laptop, microwave, oven, window, wine bottle), insert (peg, square peg, shape sorter), plug (charger), press/turn (lamp on/off, buttons, change clock/channel), pull, poke, roll, pour, hang, screw, slide — RLBench pack alone covers nearly every primitive on the wishlist.
State observationsFull privileged state exposed as batched torch TensorState: per-robot joint_pos/joint_vel (+ pos targets), per-body and per-object 13-dim root_state (pos 3 + quat 4 + lin vel 3 + ang vel 3), joint_pos/joint_vel for articulated objects; dims task-dependent (Franka arm ~18-20 proprio + 13/object + articulation joints). Success checkers are pure state functions (JointPosChecker joint-angle thresholds, PositionShiftChecker lift-height etc.), so every task is evaluable vision-free. State-based RL demonstrated in-repo (CleanRL PPO/SAC/TD3, e.g. ppo.py --task reach_origin --sim mjx --num_envs 2048), but only tasks with ported dense rewards (ManiSkill native suite, reach) have been RL-trained; RLBench/LIBERO ports are demo-replay + sparse-success, no dense rewards shipped.
Action spaceJoint-position targets by default (MetaSim action helper reads only dof_pos_target; vel/effort targets dropped with a warning — backend-dependent). Franka 7-DoF + gripper for manipulation packs; EE/IK control available via curobo third_party integration in some packs.
AssetsPer-object triplet conversions: MJCF + URDF + USD for every task object (verified on HF dataset RoboVerseOrg/roboverse_data, ~78k files, e.g. assets/rlbench/close_box/box_base/mjcf/box_base_unique.mjcf), plus demo trajectories (pkl.gz, ~2k/task for RLBench, v2 format) usable for distillation targets or reset-state distributions. Robots as MJCF (franka, panda variants, G1, Go1, YAM). Highly reusable standalone — the MJCF conversions are exactly what mjlab consumes. Caveat: upstream asset licenses vary by source benchmark (RLBench assets are research-only licensed upstream) even though RoboVerse code is Apache-2.0.
LicenseApache-2.0 (RoboVerse and MetaSim code). Converted assets inherit upstream benchmark licenses — RLBench-derived assets are research-only; verify per source before any non-academic use.
CL track recordNo continual-learning paper found that uses RoboVerse/MetaSim as its benchmark (searched July 2026). Its re-hosted LIBERO tasks are the de-facto CL-manipulation standard, but CL papers (LOTUS, M2Distill, etc.) use LIBERO natively. A 2026 continual policy-distillation paper (arXiv:2601.22475) uses Meta-World, not RoboVerse. You would be first — fine, since you'd cite the source benchmarks anyway.
Port to mjlabUnusually concrete: RoboVerse already integrates mjlab (roboverse_pack/tasks/mjlab + docs/source/dataset_benchmark/integrations/mjlab.md, with physics-parity tables vs raw mujoco: cartpole bitwise, G1 machine-epsilon, contact-heavy tasks show residuals). Recommended direction is the reverse of theirs: extract per-task MJCF assets (verified present on HF: assets/rlbench/<task>/<obj>/mjcf/*.mjcf), initial states (from shipped demo pkl.gz trajs), and checker thresholds into mjlab manager-based envs. Per task: drop MJCF into scene, write termination from the checker spec (mechanical, ~30 min), write dense reward shaping (the real cost, ~0.5-1 day/task for reach-grasp-actuate style shaping like your existing 5 tasks). Budget ~2-4 weeks for a first batch of 15-20 RLBench articulated-object tasks; contact-heavy tasks need per-task physics validation since assets are auto-converted from CoppeliaSim.
Scale potentialRealistically 20-60 state-solvable tasks: 66 RLBench tasks have MJCF assets + state checkers today (expect ~2/3 to survive physics-quality triage and admit tractable reward shaping); ManiSkill dense suite adds ~10-15 with rewards already written (portable logic); LIBERO-90 adds dozens more pick/place/open variants if you accept BDDL-derived scenes. 100+ only if you also mine their asset pipeline for generated variants.
Gotchas1) No dense rewards for most tasks — RLBench/LIBERO ports are demo-replay + sparse checker; you write reward shaping per task (checkers give you the success spec for free). 2) MuJoCo handler is CPU; MJX handler officially immature — don't plan to train GPU-parallel inside RoboVerse; extract into mjlab instead. 3) Auto-converted CoppeliaSim→MJCF assets: their own mjlab parity report shows contact-heavy tasks diverge (lift_cube_yam qpos Δ≈1.6 rad over 200 steps, attributed to contact-exclude semantics) — validate physics per task. 4) Initial states come from demo trajectory files (deterministic/demo-derived), so you must add your own reset randomization (their DenseRLResetMixin shows the pattern). 5) Repo restructured recently (roboverse_pack + separate MetaSim pip package) — docs/wiki task counts lag the code. 6) Upstream asset licenses (RLBench research-only) despite Apache-2.0 code.
Repo activity1,778 stars, 157 forks, 63 open issues; last push 2026-06-29 (MetaSim repo pushed 2026-07-03); HF data repo updated 2026-06-08, 17k downloads. Actively developed, recent major restructure into roboverse_pack/MetaSim split.
Strong

scene_synthesizer (NVIDIA SRL)

paper · code

Simulator-agnostic Python library (trimesh-based) for procedurally generating articulated manipulation scenes — parametric kitchen furniture plus placement logic — exported to URDF/USD; a scene/task generator, not a task suite.

scene_synthesizer: A Python Library for Procedural Scene Generation in Robot Manipulation (JOSS 2025, Eppner, Murali, Garrett, O'Flaherty, Hermans, Yang, Fox — NVIDIA Research)

Verdict: Not a benchmark — zero tasks, no envs, no rewards, no robots — but verified as an excellent task GENERATOR for exactly our niche: ~30 parametric articulated assets (cabinets/drawers/doors/knobs/levers/dishwashers/microwaves/fridges/handwheels) with fully known analytic geometry and joint states, exportable to URDF that MuJoCo loads natively (the JOSS paper's own teaser shows scenes simulated in MuJoCo). It directly multiplies our existing open-door/open-drawer/press-button task family to 20-50 state-solvable tasks with one generic mjlab env per primitive. Downgraded from top-pick because ALL env/reward/task-definition work is on us and there is no MJCF export (URDF-only articulation path, with the usual MuJoCo-URDF conversion friction), and it has never been used for RL policy training or continual learning.

Full details
SimulatorNone built in; simulator-agnostic. Scene representation is trimesh; exports URDF/USD consumed by MuJoCo, PyBullet, Isaac Sim/Lab (paper teaser shows all four). No physics, no stepping, no RL API of its own.
# Tasks0 predefined tasks (verified in source: no envs/ or tasks/ modules exist). What exists: 30 procedural asset classes + 6 procedural kitchen layouts (kitchen_galley, kitchen_island, kitchen_l_shaped, kitchen_peninsula, kitchen_single_wall, kitchen_u_shaped), each infinitely parameterizable.
Example tasksNone shipped; realistic derived tasks: open drawer k of an N-drawer cabinet with randomized compartment layout (RecursivelyPartitionedCabinetAsset generates random layouts), open hinged cabinet door (left/right/top), open dishwasher/microwave/fridge/oven door, turn stove knob, flip lever/safety switch, turn handwheel, open wall-cabinet, pick mug/plate/glass from shelf or dish rack and place on counter, put object into bin/open box, open sink-cabinet under-counter door.
PrimitivesAffordances implied by the assets: open/close (drawers, cabinet doors, dishwasher, microwave, fridge, wall cabinet, range/oven), turn/rotate (KnobAsset, HandWheelAsset), press/flip (LeverSwitchAsset, SafetySwitchAsset), pick/place/stack (Mug, Plate, Glass, Bowl, Bin, OpenBox on tables/shelves/countertops via support-surface labeling), slide. Prior-sweep claim of ~30 procedural assets is CORRECT: verified exactly 30 asset classes in procedural_assets.py (BaseCabinet, Bin, Bowl, Cabinet, CabinetDoor, Cage, CNCMachine, CubbyShelf, DishRack, Dishwasher, Glass, Handle, HandWheel, KitchenIsland, Knob, LeverSwitch, Microwave, Mug, OpenBox, Plate, Range, RangeHood, RecursivelyPartitionedCabinet, Refrigerator, SafetySwitch, Shelf, SinkCabinet, Table, TableWithBars, WallCabinet).
RobotsNone included. Robots are external — you load your own Franka MJCF in mjlab and drop the exported furniture scene next to it (the library can also import robot URDF/MJCF into a scene for layout purposes only).
State observationsN/A as shipped — no observations because no environments. However this is the best possible case for state-based RL: every scene is generated from explicit parameters, so exact object poses, joint axes, joint limits, handle positions, and support-surface frames are programmatically queryable at generation time (users set collision geometry, mass, friction, joint stiffness/damping/limits). Geometry is clean parametric primitives/boxes, not scanned meshes — stable contacts, no vision needed anywhere. Tasks built on it would be trivially vision-free (e.g. obs = arm proprio + drawer joint pos + handle pose), matching how our current mjlab open-door/open-drawer envs work.
Assets30 parametric procedural asset classes (pure Python + trimesh, no mesh files needed for the procedural ones) plus file import of OBJ/STL/PLY/GLTF/DAE and articulated URDF/MJCF (via dm_control)/USD. Export verified in source: URDF, USD/USDA, GLB, GLTF, OBJ, STL, PLY, JSON/dict (exchange/export.py, exchange/urdf.py, exchange/usd_export.py). NO MJCF export. URDF export preserves articulations (joints with limits, and physical properties). Highly reusable: assets are seeded/deterministic and parametric (width/height/depth/compartment_mask/compartment_types), so you can emit thousands of distinct furniture instances.
LicenseApache-2.0 (verified LICENSE file in repo).
CL track recordNo continual-learning usage found (searched). Track record is synthetic-data generation for NVIDIA papers, per the JOSS paper itself: Motion Policy Networks (Fishman 2022), CabiNet neural collision checker (Murali 2023), M2T2 pick-and-place (Yuan 2023), Optimus visuomotor policies (Dalal 2023), RoboPoint VLM fine-tuning (Yuan 2024), and planning-based data-gen (Garrett 2024). Notably none of these trained RL policies in the generated scenes — mostly motion-planning/perception data generation.
Port to mjlabConcrete path: (1) pip install scene-synthesizer; (2) generate cabinet/appliance instance with fixed seed; (3) scene.export('asset.urdf') — articulations, joint limits, damping, and collision geoms are preserved; (4) load in MuJoCo, either via MuJoCo's native URDF compiler or a one-time URDF-to-MJCF conversion (mujoco.MjModel.from_xml_path on URDF, then mj_saveLastXML to get MJCF for mjlab), or import through mjlab's asset pipeline. Gotchas at this step: URDF has no free joints for loose objects (export furniture and graspables separately), MuJoCo's URDF importer ignores some visual materials, and you must sanity-check inertials on thin panels. Effort estimate: ~2-4 days for a robust generate-export-load pipeline plus one generic 'ArticulatedObjectEnv' in mjlab parameterized by (asset XML, target joint id, goal joint value); each additional task after that is hours, since our existing open-door/open-drawer reward code transfers almost verbatim.
Scale potentialHigh. ~12-15 distinct articulated-interaction task TYPES (drawer, hinged door left/right/up, dishwasher, microwave, fridge, oven, knob, lever, safety switch, handwheel, wall cabinet, sink cabinet) x unlimited seeded geometry instances, plus pick/place-from-shelf/rack tasks with the small-object assets. Realistically 20-50 well-separated state-solvable tasks by discretizing (asset class x handle type x joint direction x size bucket), or an effectively unbounded task distribution for procedural continual learning — arguably its most interesting use for us: a task STREAM generator rather than a fixed suite. Cleaner contact geometry than PartNet-Mobility scans, as the prior sweep claimed.
Gotchas(1) It is a library, not a benchmark: every env, reward, success criterion, and reset distribution is our work — the '0 tasks' sweep claim is confirmed. (2) No MJCF export; the URDF route works (paper demonstrates MuJoCo sim) but expect per-asset-class debugging of collision margins, inertials, and joint damping after conversion. (3) MJCF IMPORT needs dm_control as an extra dependency. (4) USD export pulls OpenUSD; stick to URDF to stay light. (5) Procedural assets are mostly kitchen-domain; task diversity beyond articulated-furniture + tabletop pick/place is limited (no tools, no insertion/peg tasks, no deformables). (6) Exported scenes are static furniture — dynamic graspable objects should be exported/loaded separately so they get free joints in MuJoCo. (7) Prior sweep said 'imports MJCF' as if symmetric with export — correct for import, wrong to assume MJCF export exists.
Repo activity~226 stars, 22 forks; 14 releases, latest v1.15.0 (June 27, 2025); actively maintained by NVIDIA Research (Eppner et al.), Python 98.6%.

Usable (10)

Worth mining for a subset of tasks, task designs, or tooling.

Usable

Adroit / DAPG hand tasks (AdroitHand* in Gymnasium-Robotics)

paper · code: Original: https://github.com/aravindr93/hand_dapg (with mjrl + mj_envs). Maintained port: https://github.com/Farama-Foundation/Gymnasium-Robotics. Both verified live.

Four classic dexterous-manipulation tasks (door, hammer, pen, relocate) for a 24-DoF Shadow-hand-on-arm, MuJoCo-native and state-observed, from the DAPG (RSS 2018) paper; maintained today in Farama's Gymnasium-Robotics.

Learning Complex Dexterous Manipulation with Deep Reinforcement Learning and Demonstrations (DAPG), RSS 2018 — Rajeswaran et al.

Verdict: MuJoCo-native, fully state-based, well-understood tasks with a maintained MIT-licensed port — but only 4 tasks and a completely different embodiment (24-30 DoF Shadow hand on arm, not a Franka). It cannot contribute to the 20-100 task scaling goal, and mixing embodiments complicates a shared distilled policy. Worth adding only if you explicitly want a dexterous-hand add-on set; otherwise skip.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (native). Original hand_dapg/mj_envs used old mujoco-py; the maintained Gymnasium-Robotics port uses the official DeepMind mujoco Python bindings with standard MJCF XML assets. Also available in RoboHive and as D4RL offline datasets.
# Tasks4 (verified on robotics.farama.org): AdroitHandDoor-v1, AdroitHandHammer-v1, AdroitHandPen-v1, AdroitHandRelocate-v1, each with a Sparse-reward variant (same task, so still 4 distinct tasks).
Example tasksAdroitHandDoor (unlatch and swing open a door), AdroitHandHammer (pick up hammer, drive nail into board), AdroitHandPen (in-hand reorient pen to target pose), AdroitHandRelocate (grasp ball, move to target position).
Primitivesopen (door with latch), hammer/strike (nail into board), in-hand rotate/reorient (pen to goal orientation), pick-and-place/relocate (ball to target). All dexterous whole-hand skills, not parallel-gripper primitives.
RobotsAdroit platform: 24-DoF Shadow Dexterous Hand mounted on a free/pseudo arm (up to 30 actuated DoF total). No Franka or parallel-jaw gripper.
State observationsPure low-level state, no vision anywhere. Verified Relocate: 39-dim Box (30 joint angles of arm+wrist+fingers, plus palm-to-ball, palm-to-target, ball-to-target 3D deltas). Door ~39-dim, Pen ~45-dim, Hammer ~46-dim, same pattern (joints + object/target poses/deltas). Action space: 30-dim absolute joint-position targets in [-1,1]. State-based solving is the canonical setting: DAPG (NPG+25 human demos) solves all 4; shaped-reward pure RL solves door/pen/hammer, relocate is hard without demos. D4RL provides offline state-based datasets (human/cloned/expert) for all 4.
Action space30-dim continuous Box(-1,1), absolute angular position targets for hand+arm joints (position control, tendon-actuated fingers).
AssetsMJCF XML scenes + meshes for the Adroit hand, door, hammer/nail board, pen, ball. Reusable in any MuJoCo pipeline; hand model itself could seed other dexterous tasks. Original repo also ships 25 human demos per task (mocap-glove collected) and pretrained policies; D4RL/Minari host offline datasets.
LicenseOriginal hand_dapg: Apache-2.0. Gymnasium-Robotics port: MIT. Both permissive.
CL track recordNo established continual-learning track record. Adroit's main usage is demo-accelerated RL (DAPG) and offline RL (D4RL, where door/hammer/pen/relocate are standard). I could not verify any well-known CL/lifelong paper using it as a task sequence (Continual World / CORA etc. use Meta-World, Atari, MiniHack instead); with only 4 tasks it is too small for CL sequences anyway.
Port to mjlabLow-to-moderate. Assets are plain MJCF XML in Gymnasium-Robotics (MIT); scenes load in current MuJoCo directly. Porting to mjlab = wrapping 4 scenes in the manager API and re-implementing the (published, simple) dense rewards: roughly days, not weeks. Caveats: (a) tendon-driven Shadow hand + many finger contacts — verify MJX/mujoco-warp handles the tendon actuation and contact count at acceptable GPU throughput (this is the real risk, not the assets); (b) new embodiment means new action/obs spec alongside your Franka tasks.
Scale potentialVery limited. Hard ceiling of 4 tasks; sparse variants are not new tasks. You could procedurally vary objects (pen shapes, relocate objects, door handle types) for maybe 8-15 variants, but they remain 4 skill families on a non-Franka embodiment. Not a path to 20+ tasks.
Gotchas(1) Different embodiment from your Franka tasks — a shared distilled policy would need per-embodiment heads or padded action spaces. (2) Tendon actuation + dense finger-object contacts: confirm mujoco-warp/MJX throughput before committing; classic Adroit was CPU MuJoCo. (3) Relocate is essentially unsolvable by shaped pure RL — plan on the bundled human demos or D4RL expert data. (4) Original repo is frozen and mujoco-py-based; use the Farama port, not hand_dapg, as the porting source. (5) Prior-sweep claims all verified accurate (4 tasks, MuJoCo-native, maintained Farama port).
Repo activityhand_dapg: ~321 stars, 14 commits, effectively frozen (research artifact, old mujoco-py). Gymnasium-Robotics: ~939 stars, 136 forks, actively maintained (release 1.4.2, Jan 2026, 15 releases).
Usable

dm_control manipulation suite

paper · code

DeepMind's Jaco-arm manipulation tasks (reach, lift, place, stack, reassemble) built on dm_control composer with procedurally composable snap-together Duplo bricks; every task has a privileged-state "features" variant.

dm_control: Software and Tasks for Continuous Control (Tunyasuvunakool et al., Software Impacts 2020)

Verdict: MuJoCo-native with explicit state-only ("features") variants of every task, so all hard requirements pass — but there are only 13 state tasks spanning ~5 skill families (reach/lift/place/stack/reassemble), the arm is a Kinova Jaco (not Franka) with joint-velocity control, bricks are contact-heavy (~49 geoms each) for MJX/warp scaling, and it has essentially no continual-learning track record. Useful as a +5-10 task family (especially the snap-brick generator pattern and Duplo/cradle assets re-mounted on our Franka), not as the backbone for 50-100 tasks.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (dm_control composer / PyMJCF). Pure MJCF models — loads in vanilla MuJoCo; no MJX/mujoco-warp port exists, but assets are directly loadable.
# Tasks25 registered environments = 13 "features" (state) + 12 "vision" variants; ~8 distinct task types. Verified by cloning the repo and enumerating @registry.add decorators in dm_control/manipulation/{reach,lift,place,bricks}.py.
Example tasksreach_duplo_features, reach_site_features, lift_brick_features, lift_large_box_features, place_brick_features, place_cradle_features, stack_2_bricks_features, stack_2_bricks_moveable_base_features, stack_3_bricks_features, stack_3_bricks_random_order_features, stack_2_of_3_bricks_random_order_features, reassemble_3_bricks_fixed_order_features, reassemble_5_bricks_random_order_features
Primitivesreach, lift, place (on pedestal / into cradle), stack (2 and 3 bricks, fixed/random order, moveable base), reassemble (disassemble+restack 3/5 bricks). No push/open/close/insert/turn/press beyond the stud-insertion inherent in stacking.
RobotsKinova Jaco 2 (6-DOF arm + 3-finger hand), velocity actuators (integrated velocity control, ±0.628 rad/s large joints / ±0.838 rad/s small joints; gains are acknowledged placeholders, not system-identified). Paper claims modular design "permits other arms with minimal changes" — true at the Composer-API level, moot for mjlab since you'd reimplement anyway.
State observationsExplicitly vision-free: PERFECT_FEATURES setting (shared/observations.py) enables arm proprio (joints_pos, joints_vel, joints_torque), hand proprio (joints_pos/vel, pinch_site_pos, pinch_site_rmat) and full privileged prop state per free object (position, orientation quat, linear_velocity, angular_velocity) plus task-specific target observables (e.g. target site pos, desired stack order as hinted translucent bricks). The features/vision split is the whole design: features variants disable cameras entirely. Dim varies by task (~40-90 for 6-DoF Jaco + 1-5 bricks). Solvability from state was DeepMind's intended benchmark mode (D4PG/DMPO baselines in the paper trained on features variants).
Action space9-D joint velocity commands (6 arm velocity actuators with firmware-like velocity limits, jaco_arm.py:102-121, + 3 finger actuators). No Cartesian controller provided.
AssetsMJCF XML + STL meshes throughout: Kinova Jaco arm/hand (third_party/kinova, BSD-style Kinova license permitting redistribution), Duplo 2x4 brick (pure primitive geoms, no meshes — trivially portable, includes an autotune.py for stud snap-fit friction), cradle/pedestal/primitive props. All directly usable in any MuJoCo pipeline including mjlab.
LicenseApache 2.0 (repo and Duplo assets); Kinova meshes under a permissive Kinova Robotics BSD-style redistribution license in third_party/kinova/LICENSE.
CL track recordEssentially none for the manipulation module. The dm_control *suite* (walker/cheetah) appears in some CL papers, but I found no continual-learning paper using the Jaco manipulation tasks; CL manipulation work overwhelmingly uses Meta-World (Continual World) or LIBERO. Main users of these tasks are DeepMind RL papers (D4PG/DMPO baselines in the dm_control tech report, Acme examples).
Port to mjlabLow-to-medium. Assets are plain MJCF + STL (third_party/kinova/*.xml, entities/props/duplo/duplo2x4.xml, primitive props) — they load in stock MuJoCo today. Two porting paths: (a) run tasks as-is under dm_control (CPU, composer API — clashes with mjlab's GPU-parallel manager API); (b) extract the Duplo brick + cradle + pedestal MJCF props, mount the tasks on our Franka scene in mjlab and reimplement the ~400 lines of reward logic (stacking reward = stud-to-hole distance in bricks.py:416-460) — roughly 1-2 days per task family, ~1-2 weeks for the full set. Caveat: each Duplo has ~49 primitive geoms (8 stud capsule clusters + hole boxes); 3-5 bricks means hundreds of potential contacts, which will stress MJX/mujoco-warp broadphase at 1000s of parallel envs — may need simplified 1-stud bricks.
Scale potentialDirect: 13 state tasks (~8 after deduplicating obs variants), of which reach x2 are trivial. Generator: the _stack/_reassemble factories are parameterized (num_bricks, randomize_order, moveable_base, target_height, colors) so 20-40 stacking/reassembly variants are mechanically generatable — but they are all one skill family (pick-and-insert), so effective skill diversity tops out around 10 tasks. Not a path to 50-100 diverse tasks on its own; best as one contributor plus an asset/generator pattern to copy.
Gotchas(1) Prior sweep said ~12 tasks; actual count is 25 registered envs / 13 state tasks — vision variants inflate the list. (2) Jaco arm + joint-velocity actions, not Franka + Cartesian — cross-embodiment mismatch with our existing 5 tasks unless tasks are rebuilt around Franka. (3) Duplo bricks are contact-rich (~49 geoms/brick); stacking/reassembly rely on fine stud-hole snap contacts that may misbehave or be slow in MJX/mujoco-warp at scale. (4) Composer's Python-callback reward/initializer logic cannot run inside JIT-compiled GPU stepping — rewards must be reimplemented in mjlab regardless. (5) Rewards are shaped but stacking tasks were hard even for D4PG (millions of steps); reassemble_5 was unsolved in the original report.
Repo activity~4.6k stars; actively maintained by Google DeepMind — latest release 1.0.43 (June 2026, upgraded to MuJoCo 3.10.0), 40 releases total. Manipulation module itself is stable/frozen (essentially unchanged since 2020).
Merged duplicate assessments (1)
  • [usable] MuJoCo-native, Apache-2.0, with genuinely state-solvable "_features" variants by design (privileged prop poses in obs) — but only 13 task families with narrow primitive coverage (reach/lift/place/stack/reassemble, no doors/drawers/buttons/insertion), built on dm_control Composer (CPU MuJoCo + per-step Python callbacks) which cannot run under mjlab's GPU-parallel MuJoCo-Warp — every task must be reimplemented in the manager API. Jaco arm, not Franka, so props/tasks need re-hosting. Zero continual-learning track record. Best value: the snap-together Duplo brick model and the stack/reassemble task designs as a source of ~5-10 new task specs, not a drop-in 20+ task suite.
Usable

Fetch Block Construction (richardrl, rlkit-relational lineage)

paper · code

Fork of OpenAI gym Fetch robotics envs adding a single programmatic multi-block construction env (stack N blocks into towers/pyramids/multitowers) from the ICRA 2020 relational-RL paper; valuable as a task-design recipe, not as importable code or assets.

Towards Practical Multi-Object Manipulation using Relational Reinforcement Learning (ICRA 2020)

Verdict: Not a multi-task benchmark — it is ONE task family (goal-conditioned multi-block stacking) with combinatorial variants. Code is dead (2020, mujoco-py/MuJoCo 1.5, committed mjkey.txt) and has NO license on the code, so nothing should be copied verbatim anyway. The genuinely useful part is the task DESIGN: N-block singletower/pyramid/multitower goal sampling + incremental per-block reward, all state-solvable, which is ~300 lines you can cleanly re-implement on your Franka in mjlab to add ~5-15 stack/pick-place tasks of graded difficulty. Assets are worthless to you (Fetch robot STLs; blocks are programmatic 5cm boxes).

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo 1.5/2.0 via mujoco-py (old OpenAI gym GoalEnv API; an mjkey.txt license file is committed in the repo root). NOT modern mujoco bindings, NOT MJX/Warp. Physics content itself is trivial to reproduce in modern MuJoCo.
# Tasks1 task family. Registered gym IDs number in the thousands (24 block-counts x 4 rewards x 3 obs types x 2 render sizes x 2 stack_only x 4 goal cases) but these are hyperparameter variants, not distinct tasks. Distinct-for-CL variants: num_blocks 1-17 (hard assert at 17 for collision-free init; paper used up to 6) x 3 goal cases (Singletower/Pyramid/Multitower) = realistically ~10-20 usable difficulty-graded variants, of which maybe 5-10 are meaningfully different tasks.
Example tasksFetchBlockConstruction_2Blocks_IncrementalReward_DictstateObs_42Rendersize_TrueStackonly_SingletowerCase-v1 (stack 2 blocks into a tower); ..._6Blocks_..._SingletowerCase (6-block tower, paper flagship, 75% success); ..._PyramidCase (arrange blocks in pyramid); ..._MultitowerCase (several shorter towers); ..._AllCase (mixed goal sampling); stack_only=False also samples in-air/tabletop place goals per block.
Primitivespick, place, stack (multi-object, goal-conditioned). Also inherits standard Fetch reach/push/slide/pick-and-place envs from the gym-robotics fork. No open/close/rotate/insert/press.
RobotsFetch mobile manipulator (7-DoF arm, parallel gripper, mocap-welded EE with fixed downward orientation); ShadowHand envs inherited from gym fork but unused for block construction.
State observationsFully state-based, verified in construction.py _get_obs(): grip_pos(3) + gripper finger state(2) + grip_velp(3) + gripper_vel(2), then per block: pos(3), rel_pos to gripper(3), euler rot(3), linear vel(3), angular vel(3) = 12/block; goal = concatenated 3D target positions per block + gripper. obs_type options: dictstate/np/dictimage (image optional, not required). Paper solved up to 6-block stacking from this state obs (75% success) using GNN policy + curriculum over block count — solvable but sparse-reward stacking beyond ~3 blocks needed relational architecture + curriculum; 'incremental' per-block reward eases this.
