Action space4D: EE position delta (3, scaled by 0.05, mocap-driven, fixed EE orientation quaternion) + symmetric gripper open/close (1). Verified in fetch_env._set_action; n_actions=4 hardcoded. README's 'rotation control' claim is NOT in the block-construction action path.
AssetsOnly robot assets are Fetch/ShadowHand STLs + MJCF (irrelevant for your Franka setup). Task objects are procedurally generated: identical 5cm box geoms with free joints, colored materials, sphere target sites — zero asset value; trivially recreated in mjlab.
LicenseCode: NO license file — GitHub API reports license: null, so the code is technically all-rights-reserved (it is itself a fork of MIT-licensed OpenAI gym robotics, muddying provenance). Assets: envs/robotics/assets/LICENSE.md covers Fetch model (from fetchrobotics/fetch_ros, adapted by OpenAI) and ShadowHand (Apache 2.0, Vikash Kumar). Safest path: re-implement logic from the paper/spec, use nothing verbatim.
CL track recordNone as a CL benchmark. Used for curriculum learning over object count (train 1..N blocks) and relational/GNN generalization studies (e.g. arXiv:2201.13388 Compositional Multi-Object RL); zero-shot generalization to more blocks is the headline result, not continual learning.
Port to mjlabLow-to-moderate (1-3 days): do NOT port the code (old mujoco-py API, gym GoalEnv, mocap-welded Fetch arm, no code license). Instead re-implement in mjlab: blocks are programmatic free-joint box geoms (generate_xml is ~50 lines), goal sampling for singletower/pyramid/multitower is ~100 lines, incremental/sparse reward is ~50 lines. Drops straight onto your existing Franka lift-cube scene.
Scale potentialAdds roughly 5-15 tasks to your suite: stack-2/3/4/5/6, pyramid-3/6, multitower-4/6, N-block pick-and-place-to-goal. Graded difficulty is a plus for curricula/CL, but it will not get you to 50-100 tasks — all variants share one primitive family and transfer heavily between each other (which may itself be interesting for CL forgetting studies).
Gotchas1) No code license — cannot legally copy code; re-implement instead. 2) Requires legacy mujoco-py + MuJoCo 1.5 key (mjkey.txt committed); will not run against modern mujoco without a rewrite. 3) Sparse-reward stacking >3 blocks is very hard for plain MLP policies — paper needed attention-GNN + curriculum; your per-task distillation teachers may struggle on stack-4+ without the incremental reward. 4) Registered ID explosion (thousands) is cosmetic; do not count it as tasks. 5) num_blocks capped at 17 by collision-free init assert, 24 by registration, ~6 by what was actually demonstrated. 6) Gymnasium-Robotics (Farama) has a maintained MIT-licensed modern-MuJoCo Fetch stack that is a better code starting point if you want the Fetch lineage at all.
Repo activity14 stars, 9 forks, ~7 commits; created Nov 2019, last push Jul 2020 — dead/unmaintained.
Usable

Gymnasium-Robotics (Fetch + Adroit + Shadow Hand + Franka Kitchen)

paper · code

Farama-maintained MuJoCo goal-conditioned suites: 4 Fetch arm tasks, 4 Adroit hand tasks, 7 Shadow-Hand reorientation variants, and the 7-subgoal Franka Kitchen — all state-based by default.

Multi-Goal RL (Fetch/Hand): Plappert et al. 2018; Adroit: DAPG, Rajeswaran et al. RSS 2018; Kitchen: Relay Policy Learning, Gupta et al. CoRL 2019 — all as claimed in the sweep

Verdict: Everything checks out: MuJoCo-native, MIT, actively maintained (v1.4.2 Jan 2026), and every manipulation env is solvable from pure state (that is the default obs; no vision anywhere). But the real distinct-manipulation-task count is only ~13-16 spread across THREE different embodiments (Fetch gripper arm, Adroit/Shadow Hand, Franka), so for a single-Franka mjlab CL curriculum the directly usable core is Franka Kitchen (7 composable subgoals, Franka-native MJCF — high value, overlaps our open-door/open-drawer/press themes) plus the 4 Fetch task DESIGNS re-implemented on Franka. It cannot get us to 20+ tasks alone; it is a quality booster (battle-tested rewards, Minari/D4RL demo datasets), not a scaling solution. The hand suites are a different embodiment and irrelevant to a Franka arm curriculum.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (new DeepMind python bindings, mujoco>=3.x; NOT MJX/warp, but plain MJCF so MJX-liftable). Actively maintained by Farama.
# Tasks~15-16 distinct manipulation tasks (verified on robotics.farama.org): Fetch 4 (Reach, Push, Slide, PickAndPlace), Adroit 4 (Door, Hammer, Pen, Relocate), Shadow Hand 7 (Reach + Manipulate Block/Egg/Pen, each with TouchSensors variants — really 3 core reorientation tasks), Franka Kitchen 1 env with 7 combinable subgoals (bottom burner, top burner, light switch, slide cabinet, hinge cabinet, microwave, kettle) → 7 single-goal tasks or up to 127 subgoal-subset sequences. Maze/MaMuJoCo envs also in repo but are locomotion, not manipulation.
Example tasksFetchPush-v4, FetchSlide-v4, FetchPickAndPlace-v4, AdroitHandDoor-v1, AdroitHandHammer-v1, AdroitHandPen-v1, AdroitHandRelocate-v1, HandManipulateBlockRotateXYZ-v1, FrankaKitchen-v1 with tasks_to_complete=['microwave','kettle','light switch','slide cabinet']
Primitivesreach, push, slide, pick-place (Fetch); open door, hammer nail, rotate pen, relocate ball (Adroit); in-hand reorientation (Shadow Hand); turn knob, flip switch, slide/hinge cabinet open, open microwave, pick-place kettle (Kitchen)
RobotsFetch Mobile Manipulator (7-DoF arm, 2-finger gripper, mocap EE control), Shadow Dexterous Hand (24 DoF), Adroit (Shadow Hand + 4-DoF arm, 28-dim action), Franka Emika Panda (9-DoF joint-velocity control in Kitchen)
State observationsFully state-based by default — vision is never required and vision-free solving is the published baseline (HER for Fetch/Hand, DAPG for Adroit, RPL for Kitchen). Verified dims: FetchPickAndPlace obs=25 (EE pos/vel, gripper joints, block pos/rot/vel, relative pos) + 3-dim desired/achieved goal dicts (GoalEnv API); FetchReach=10. AdroitDoor obs=39 flat Box (27 joint angles, palm xyz, handle xyz, palm-to-handle vector, latch angle, hinge state/vel); other Adroit 39-46. Shadow Hand ~61 obs + 7-dim goal (object pose quaternion). Kitchen obs=59 (9 robot qpos + 9 qvel + 21 object poses + 20 object vels) with per-task goal dicts. Rewards: sparse and dense variants for Fetch/Adroit; Kitchen sparse (count of completed subgoals, 0.3-norm threshold).
Action spaceFetch: Box(4) Cartesian EE delta + gripper, 25 Hz; Adroit: Box(28) absolute joint positions; Shadow Hand: Box(20) joint positions; Kitchen: Box(9) joint velocities (7 arm + 2 gripper) — Kitchen matches our Franka action convention closely
AssetsMJCF (MuJoCo XML) throughout + STL/mesh files, bundled in the pip package / repo. Kitchen assets derive from Google relay-policy-learning (Apache-2.0 lineage); Fetch/Hand models from OpenAI Gym robotics (MIT). Directly loadable in mujoco 3.x, hence MJX/mjlab-compatible after scene surgery.
LicenseMIT
CL track recordModerate, real but secondary to Meta-World/Continual-World. Franka Kitchen (via D4RL) is a standard multitask/long-horizon benchmark (Relay Policy Learning, arXiv:1910.11956) and appears in continual/offline-CL work (e.g. TSN-Affinity continual offline RL 2026 uses D4RL Adroit/Kitchen; LOTUS continual imitation uses kitchen-style scenes); Fetch tasks appear in HER-lineage CRL papers and the panda-gym CL suite copies its 3 task designs. CORA (arXiv:2110.10067) chose other domains; the canonical CRL manipulation benchmark remains Continual World (arXiv:2105.10919) on Meta-World. Sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.10919, https://arxiv.org/pdf/2110.10067, https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.25898
Port to mjlabLow-to-moderate, but selective. (1) Franka Kitchen: best target — Franka 9-DoF arm+gripper in MJCF with kitchen furniture assets (microwave, cabinets, burner knobs, switch, kettle); copy the kitchen MJCF/meshes into an mjlab scene, attach our existing Franka, write 7 manager-based reward/termination terms keyed on the documented joint indices — roughly 1-2 weeks for all 7. MJX/warp caveat: check hinge/slide contact-heavy kitchen collisions for warp performance, but geometry is simple primitives+meshes. (2) Fetch: the robot is a Fetch with mocap-driven Cartesian EE control — do NOT port the robot; re-implement the 4 task designs (esp. Slide, which we lack) on our Franka in days each; rewards are 5-line distance functions. (3) Adroit/Shadow Hand: different embodiment (24-DoF hand), skip for a Franka curriculum; MJCF is portable if we ever want hands.
Scale potentialRealistically +8-12 tasks for a Franka curriculum: 7 Kitchen subgoals as separate tasks (plus goal-subset combinations, e.g. 21 pairs, for CL sequence variety) + 3-4 Fetch-style tasks re-implemented (slide/push/pick-place with air goals/reach). Not a path to 50-100 on its own — no procedural task generation and no large object library. Its Minari/D4RL demo datasets (kitchen-complete/partial/mixed, adroit human demos) are a bonus for distillation baselines.
Gotchas(1) GoalEnv dict obs (observation/desired_goal/achieved_goal) needs flattening for our distillation pipeline. (2) Fetch uses mocap welds for EE control — mocap is awkward under MJX/warp; re-implement control rather than port. (3) Shadow/Adroit are a different embodiment — a shared Franka policy head cannot absorb them. (4) Kitchen success = joint-config norm threshold 0.3, a somewhat loose criterion; check it matches our success definitions. (5) Repo counts inflate: MaMuJoCo + mazes are locomotion; TouchSensors variants duplicate tasks. (6) Not GPU-vectorized — task/asset value only, not a training harness for us.
Repo activity939 stars, 136 forks, 481 commits; latest release v1.4.2 (Jan 2, 2026) — actively maintained by Farama Foundation
Usable

obj2mjcf + CoACD (tooling)

paper · code

CLI that splits composite OBJ files by material, runs CoACD collision-aware convex decomposition, and emits ready-to-load MJCF — the standard mesh-to-MuJoCo conversion path.

CoACD: Approximate Convex Decomposition for 3D Meshes with Collision-Aware Concavity and Tree Search (SIGGRAPH 2022). obj2mjcf itself has no paper.

Verdict: Not a benchmark — contributes zero tasks by itself — but it is the de-facto standard, verified-working pipeline for getting arbitrary OBJ meshes (Objaverse, PartNet-Mobility parts, RoboSuite/ManiSkill assets, custom CAD) into MuJoCo with sane collision geometry. Keep it as core infrastructure for porting other candidates' assets into mjlab; do not count it as a task source.

Full details
SimulatorTooling for MuJoCo (outputs plain MJCF+OBJ, loadable in vanilla MuJoCo, MJX, mujoco-warp/mjlab)
# Tasks0 (tooling — provides no tasks or environments)
Example tasksn/a (no tasks). Example use: convert a YCB mug OBJ into MJCF with ~8 convex collision hulls, drop into an mjlab lift/handover task.
PrimitivesNone directly; enables any primitive by making arbitrary object meshes physically simulable (graspable/pushable/stackable with accurate convex collision hulls).
Robotsn/a — object/mesh tooling only, robot-agnostic; works alongside any Franka MJCF (e.g. Menagerie panda already used in mjlab)
State observationsn/a — no environments or observations. Relevant indirectly: CoACD produces convex hull sets, so imported objects get well-defined poses/geometry usable as low-level state features in state-based policies.
AssetsConsumes Wavefront OBJ (+MTL); emits per-material OBJ submeshes + MJCF with materials/meshes/geoms prefilled; CoACD collision hulls as OBJ. Fully reusable, sim-agnostic mesh outputs. Requires MuJoCo >= 2.1.2.
LicenseBoth MIT (obj2mjcf and CoACD)
CL track recordNone as a benchmark (it isn't one). Widely used as infrastructure across MuJoCo robotics work (e.g. MuJoCo Menagerie asset prep); CoACD is used by 210+ downstream projects including ManiSkill2's asset pipeline. No continual-learning paper uses it as a task suite.
Port to mjlabZero porting needed — it IS the porting tool. pip install obj2mjcf coacd; per-object conversion is one CLI call (--decompose flag runs CoACD) yielding an MJCF you attach to a scene. For articulated URDF assets, MuJoCo's native URDF compiler handles kinematics while obj2mjcf handles visual/collision meshes. Effort: minutes per object, trivially scriptable for batch conversion of hundreds of objects. Gotcha-level effort only (units/scale, watertightness, hull count tuning).
Scale potentialAs an enabler: high. Batch-converting object libraries (Objaverse subsets, YCB, PartNet-Mobility link meshes) could supply objects for dozens-to-hundreds of procedurally generated pick/push/stack/insert task variants in mjlab. By itself: zero tasks.
Gotchas(1) OBJ-only input — GLB/STL/DAE assets (common in Objaverse/URDF suites) need a pre-conversion step (trimesh/Blender). (2) CoACD parameters (threshold, max hulls) need per-object-category tuning; too many hulls slows GPU-parallel mujoco-warp contact processing — hull count directly hits mjlab throughput. (3) Watch mesh scale/units; CoACD's new real-metric mode (2026) helps but older recipes assume normalized meshes. (4) obj2mjcf does not handle articulation — kinematic structure of articulated assets (doors, drawers) must come from URDF conversion or hand-written MJCF. (5) obj2mjcf's last release is Mar 2024; MJCF it emits may need minor touch-ups for newest MuJoCo attributes.
Repo activityobj2mjcf: 367 stars, 24 releases, last release Mar 2024 (mature/stable rather than dead — small scope, feature-complete). CoACD: 1.1k stars, 148 forks, used by 210+ projects, actively maintained (Apr 2026 update).
Usable

Ravens (Transporter Networks)

paper · code

10 PyBullet tabletop rearrangement tasks (CoRL 2020, Google) with scripted ground-truth-pose oracles and partial-credit rewards; vision-first benchmark whose tasks are nonetheless fully solvable from privileged state.

Transporter Networks: Rearranging the Visual World for Robotic Manipulation (CoRL 2020)

Verdict: All 10 tasks are provably state-solvable (scripted oracles act purely on ground-truth 6D poses), but the framework is PyBullet with a UR5e + suction cup and a high-level SE(2) two-pose pick/place action primitive — nothing about the env code transfers to mjlab's low-level Franka control. Value is the task DESIGNS and URDF/OBJ assets (~8 rigid tasks after dropping rope/sweeping deformables), each of which must be re-implemented from scratch. Good idea quarry, mediocre scaling engine, no CL precedent.

Full details
SimulatorPyBullet (confirmed). No MuJoCo version exists. UR5e arm with suction-cup end effector (spatula for sweeping-piles), 0.5x1 m tabletop.
# TasksExactly 10 (verified from README): block-insertion, place-red-in-green, towers-of-hanoi, align-box-corner, stack-block-pyramid, palletizing-boxes, assembling-kits, packing-boxes, manipulating-rope, sweeping-piles. CLIPort (follow-up repo) adds ~10 language-conditioned variants on the same engine. Only 8 are rigid-body (rope and sweeping-piles are deformable/particle).
Example tasksblock-insertion (place L-shaped block into fixture), assembling-kits (insert 5 random shapes into matching holes), palletizing-boxes (stack boxes onto pallet in layers), packing-boxes (fill container with random-size boxes), towers-of-hanoi (multi-step sequencing), stack-block-pyramid (6 blocks into 3-2-1 pyramid), align-box-corner (rotate/align box to corner marker), sweeping-piles (push granular pile into zone with spatula).
Primitivesinsert, place-into, stack, align, palletize, pack, kit/insert-shape, sweep/push, rope manipulation. Strong overlap with our target primitives; notably adds insert/pack/palletize/stack which we lack. Note: task-level action space is itself a "pick-and-place primitive" (SE(2) pick pose + place pose), not joint control.
RobotsUR5e with suction gripper (spatula for sweeping). Not Franka; suction-to-parallel-gripper retargeting is the main design change when porting.
State observationsDefault obs is RGB-D + camera params, BUT env info exposes ground-truth 6D poses, sizes, and colors of all objects, and every task ships a scripted oracle that solves it using ONLY these ground-truth poses — direct proof all 10 tasks are solvable from low-level state, no vision needed. No fixed-dim state vector is provided out of the box (object count varies per task/episode), so we would define our own state obs when re-implementing.
Action spaceTwo-pose motion primitives: action = (T_pick, T_place) in SE(2) (top-down); the env executes scripted arm motions. No joint-level or Cartesian velocity control exposed — we must supply our own low-level control when porting.
AssetsURDF-based assets + OBJ meshes, verified in repo (ravens/environments/assets: block, bowl, box, container, corner, hanoi, insertion, kitting (20 shapes), pallet, stacking, ur5, zone, plus cable/cloth/bags for deformables). Plain URDF/OBJ = trivially portable to MuJoCo/MJCF. Assets are simple/clean and reusable.
LicenseApache-2.0 (verified via GitHub API)
CL track recordEssentially none. No continual-learning paper found that uses Ravens as its task sequence; CL manipulation work standardizes on Meta-World/Continual-World (CW10/CW20) and LIBERO instead. Ravens has multi-task (Transporter multi-task, CLIPort) and lifelong-adjacent follow-ups, but not sequential-CL benchmarking.
Port to mjlabNo env code ports: PyBullet + suction + SE(2) primitive actions are all incompatible with mjlab low-level control. Concrete path: (1) take task specs + URDF/OBJ assets (MuJoCo loads URDF; obj meshes fine; kitting = 20 mesh shapes); (2) re-implement each task in mjlab manager API with Franka + parallel gripper (replaces suction — changes graspability of flat boxes/shapes, may need mesh tweaks or a weld-constraint suction hack); (3) write dense/partial-credit rewards from scratch (Ravens rewards are pose-matching, easy to replicate). Estimate ~1-3 days per rigid task for an experienced person, ~8 tasks => 2-4 weeks. Deformable tasks: skip (not MJX-friendly).
Scale potentialModest. 8 rigid task designs directly. assembling-kits is a generator (random subsets of 20 shapes + hole boards) and packing/palletizing randomize box counts/sizes, so parametric variants can yield 20-40 nominal tasks, but they collapse to ~8 distinct skills — variants share one primitive (e.g. all kitting = insertion). Best used to add 5-8 genuinely new skills (insert, pack, palletize, stack, kit) to our suite, not to reach 50-100.
Gotchas(1) Prior sweep note confirmed: assembling-kits is a task generator but all variants are one insertion skill. (2) Suction gripper: many tasks (palletizing, packing flat boxes, kit shapes) are easy for suction but hard/different for a Franka parallel gripper — expect reward/asset redesign, not just a port. (3) Two deformable tasks (rope, sweeping-piles) plus the DeformableRavens extension (cloth/bags) are particle-based and poor fits for MJX/mujoco-warp. (4) SE(2) top-down assumption bakes into task design; success thresholds are planar pose matches. (5) Repo archived, TF2-era code, Python deps stale — treat as spec+assets, not software.
Repo activity631 stars; archived/read-only (last push 2024-07-30 = archival); effectively unmaintained since ~2021. CLIPort repo (cliport/cliport) is the maintained-ish descendant.
Usable

RLBench (task-design catalog)

paper · code

~100-task Franka tabletop manipulation benchmark on CoppeliaSim/PyRep, demo-driven with sparse rewards; heavily used by 2024-25 skill-incremental CL papers but locked into a non-portable binary asset format and a restrictive academic license.

RLBench: The Robot Learning Benchmark & Learning Environment (RA-L 2020)

Verdict: Wrong simulator with no viable porting path: tasks live in binary CoppeliaSim .ttm scene files (not URDF/MJCF), the license is Imperial College non-commercial with explicit no-transfer/no-sublicense terms (so even the assets cannot legally be redistributed/ported), meshes are scraped from TurboSquid/CGTrader/Free3D with murky provenance, and tasks ship only sparse success detectors (no dense rewards), so 'porting' a task to mjlab equals re-authoring scene + logic + reward from scratch. Its real value to you is the task TAXONOMY: use its ~100-task list as a checklist for designing your own 20-50 mjlab tasks — several of your existing tasks (push_button, open_door, open_drawer, lift/pick) already mirror RLBench tasks.

Full details
SimulatorCoppeliaSim v4.1 + PyRep (V-REP successor). NOT MuJoCo; no MuJoCo/MJX port exists anywhere (verified by search — MuJoCo Playground, ManiSkill3, loco-mujoco none include RLBench tasks).
# Tasks~100 hand-designed tasks (repo tasks/ dir has 100+ .py task files; paper advertises 100 tasks with 900+ parametric variations, e.g. stack_blocks has color/count variations). Community forks (RLBench-18, PerAct-18, Colosseum) use 18-20 task subsets.
Example taskspush_button, open_door, close_door, open_drawer/close_drawer, open_fridge, open_jar, pick_and_lift, pick_up_cup, stack_blocks, stack_cups, place_cups, put_knife_in_knife_block, put_groceries_in_cupboard, pour_from_cup_to_cup, screw_nail, basketball_in_hoop, block_pyramid, empty_container, setup_chess, play_jenga, reach_target, close_box, change_channel.
PrimitivesBroad primitive coverage: reach, push (button/block), pick-and-lift, place, stack (blocks/cups), open/close (door, drawer, box, fridge, jar, microwave), pour, insert (knife in block, put groceries in cupboard), screw (nail, jar lid), turn (tap), slide, press, plus long-horizon composites (setup_chess, empty_container, block_pyramid, play_jenga).
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (7-DoF + parallel gripper) only, on a fixed tabletop scene.
State observationsLow-dim obs exists and is per-task: proprioception (joint pos/vel/forces, gripper pose + open amount + finger touch) plus task_low_dim_state containing the poses of task-relevant objects. So YES, most tasks are geometrically solvable from privileged state in principle, and CL papers (IsCiL, PCSIL) train state/hybrid policies on it. Caveats: (a) the repo docs themselves warn against relying on low-dim state; (b) rewards are SPARSE success-only — no dense shaped rewards ship with tasks, so state-based RL from scratch is known to be very hard here (most usage is imitation learning from the built-in motion-planner demos, which DO give you unlimited scripted expert trajectories from state).
Action spaceMultiple modes: joint velocity/position/torque, end-effector pose (with built-in motion-planner 'plan' mode used for waypoint demos), gripper open/close.
AssetsPer-task binary CoppeliaSim .ttm model file (scene + demo waypoints) + Python task logic. Meshes originally sourced from TurboSquid, CGTrader, Free3D, Thingiverse, Cadnav — third-party provenance, not clean redistributable URDF/MJCF/OBJ libraries. .ttm is a proprietary binary format; meshes can technically be exported from CoppeliaSim one by one, but license forbids redistribution.
LicenseCustom Imperial College London non-commercial academic license: research use only, no commercial use, no rent/lend/transfer/sub-license, 10-year term; small BSD-3 components excepted. This blocks porting-and-redistributing assets.
CL track recordYes, strong recent track record: Policy-Compatible Skill Incremental Learning via Lazy Learning Interface (NeurIPS 2025, arXiv:2509.20612) builds skill-incremental sequences from RLBench; IsCiL: Incremental Learning of Retrievable Skills (NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2410.22658) uses it for continual task adaptation; SPECI (arXiv:2504.15561) does hierarchical continual imitation on manipulation. These mostly use imitation from RLBench's scripted demos, not RL, and typically 10-20 task subsets.
Port to mjlabNo automated path. Assets are binary CoppeliaSim .ttm scenes; meshes can only be exported manually via the CoppeliaSim GUI/PyRep, and the underlying models come from TurboSquid/CGTrader/Free3D/Thingiverse/Cadnav, so redistributing extracted meshes is legally dubious. Task logic (success conditions, variations) is plain readable Python against PyRep — that transfers as a spec. Realistic path: treat RLBench as a specification and re-implement tasks in mjlab with your own MJCF assets (your existing drawer/door/button assets already cover several). Estimate 0.5-2 days per simple single-stage task including shaped-reward design; multi-stage tasks substantially more. 20 tasks ~ 3-6 weeks of engineering.
Scale potentialAs a direct source: 0 tasks (nothing portable). As a design checklist: excellent — its 100-task taxonomy maps ~30-50 tasks onto simple-geometry scenes (buttons, drawers, doors, lids, blocks, cups, taps, sliders) that you could re-implement natively in mjlab with primitive shapes, no exotic meshes needed, and dense rewards of your own design. Your existing 5 tasks already overlap with 4 RLBench tasks, confirming the checklist role.
Gotchas(1) CoppeliaSim dependency is CPU-bound single-threaded and famously painful headless — irrelevant anyway since you would not run it. (2) Prior-sweep claim '100+ tasks' verified true, but the '900+ variations' are mostly color/count/language templates, not distinct skills. (3) Docs explicitly discourage low-dim-state policies (objects leaving workspace cause state-distribution issues). (4) Sparse-reward-only design means RLBench task definitions do not give you the shaped rewards you need for PPO-style teacher training in mjlab. (5) License bars any redistribution of derived assets — do not copy meshes.
Repo activity~1.8k stars, ~318 forks, ~93 open issues. Maintenance has slowed (still pinned to CoppeliaSim 4.1.0, 2020-era); ecosystem activity lives in downstream derivatives (PerAct, Colosseum, GemBench).
Merged duplicate assessments (1)
  • [usable] Best-in-class task DIVERSITY catalog (100+ Franka tabletop tasks) but wrong simulator (CoppeliaSim/PyRep), proprietary binary .ttm assets built from third-party marketplace meshes (TurboSquid/CGTrader etc.) that are legally murky to extract and redistribute, and a custom non-commercial license. Nothing is directly runnable in mjlab and no MuJoCo port exists. Use it as a task-design catalog: re-implement 10-30 of its simpler single-stage tasks (open/close/press/turn/slide/stack/pick) with your own primitives and free assets. Not assets-only (the assets are precisely the un-portable part) and not strong/top-pick (zero MuJoCo compatibility, no CL track record, sparse rewards mean shaped rewards must be written per task anyway).
Usable

RoboGen / GenSim (generative task creation)

paper · code: GenSim: https://github.com/liruiw/GenSim (resolves, 350 stars, MIT). GenSim2: https://github.com/GenSim2/GenSim2 (resolves, 85 stars, 6 forks, 11 commits, MIT). RoboGen: https://github.com/Genesis-Embodied-AI/RoboGen (resolves, ~1.2k stars, 109 forks, 27 commits, Apache-2.0).

LLM task-generation trio: GenSim2 is the useful part — ~44 verified Franka articulated-object tasks (SAPIEN, URDF assets, privileged-keypoint solvers) whose task specs and objects you could re-host in mjlab; GenSim v1 (Ravens suction pick-place) and RoboGen (open-ended PyBullet) are poor fits.

GenSim: Generating Robotic Simulation Tasks via Large Language Models (ICLR 2024, spotlight); GenSim2: Scaling Robot Data Generation with Multi-modal and Reasoning LLMs (CoRL 2024); RoboGen: Towards Unleashing Infinite Data for Automated Robot Learning via Generative Simulation (ICML 2024)

Verdict: Not a benchmark but a task-generation blueprint: PyBullet-only, dormant since May 2024, no MuJoCo code, heavy suction-gripper use, and generated rewards need manual vetting. However, its recipe (LLM task proposal + PartNet-Mobility scene retrieval + reward codegen) is exactly the mechanism to scale mjlab to 20-100 state-solvable tasks, and its 48 shipped task specs are a ready-made task list. Use the idea + asset pipeline, not the code.

Full details
SimulatorGenSim: PyBullet (Ravens/CLIPort codebase, UR5 + suction). GenSim2: SAPIEN (Franka Panda, pd_joint_pos / pd_ee_pose / joint_vel controllers). Neither is MuJoCo; EXCLUDE-by-simulator rule applies unless assets ported.
# TasksGenSim: 165 generated task .py files verified in cliport/generated_tasks/ (paper: "expand benchmark 10x to 100+"). GenSim2: 59 task files verified in repo — 15 origin + 22 primitive + 22 long-horizon; paper claims "up to 100 articulated tasks with 200 objects" (not all in repo). 40 kPAM solver configs present.
Example tasksGenSim2 primitive: OpenDrawer, PushDrawerClose, OpenBox, CloseBox, OpenSafe, CloseSafe, OpenLaptop, CloseMicrowave, OpenRefrigeratorDoor, PressToasterLever, RotateOvenKnob, RotateMicrowaveDoor, TurnOnFaucet, TurnOffFaucet, PushBox, SwingDoorOpen, SwingBucketHandle, LiftBucketUpright, RelocateSuitcase, MoveBagForward. Long-horizon: PlaceCrackerBoxIntoDrawer, InsertOrangeIntoMicrowave, SecureGoldInSafe, StoreLemonInRefrigerator. GenSim v1: build-house, build-car, align-box-corner, block-insertion, ball-in-bowl-obstacle-course.
PrimitivesGenSim2 covers exactly our primitives: open/close (box, laptop, drawer, safe, microwave, refrigerator, suitcase), push (box, drawer-close, oven-close, toaster), rotate (microwave door, oven knob), press (toaster lever), turn (faucet on/off), swing (door, bucket handle, suitcase lid), lift/relocate (bucket, suitcase, bag), reach, plus long-horizon place-X-into-Y compositions. GenSim v1: only top-down pick-place/stack/insert arrangements (build-house, align-boxes, block-insertion).
RobotsGenSim: UR5 with suction end-effector (Ravens). GenSim2: Franka Panda (franka_description meshes, panda_pinch/panda_bucket URDF+SRDF variants, mobile-panda single/dual-arm URDFs also present).
State observationsGenSim2 supports obs_mode="state": base get_oracle_state() returns robot state (15-dim: proprioception + TCP); rewards in every task are computed from privileged sim state (TCP position, object position, articulation joint openness fraction) — see e.g. gensim2/env/task/primitive_tasks/open_drawer.py. Demos are generated by the kPAM planner from ground-truth keypoints stored per asset (keypoints.json), and DrQv2 RL solver configs exist — so all tasks are solvable from low-level state; the headline PPT policy is point-cloud-based but that is a distillation choice, not a task requirement. Caveat: the shipped 15-dim state obs is robot-only; you would add object pose/openness to obs (trivial, the getters exist). GenSim v1 tasks are solved by scripted oracles from ground-truth poses (state-solvable) but the action space is a discrete pick-pose/place-pose primitive, not continuous control.
Action spaceGenSim: high-level top-down pick-and-place primitive (pick pose, place pose) executed by scripted motion — incompatible with our low-level control setting. GenSim2: standard continuous control — pd_joint_pos, pd_joint_vel, pd_joint_pos_vel, pd_ee_pose controllers on the Franka + gripper.
AssetsGenSim2: PartNet-Mobility-style articulated objects — 35 categories (drawer, door, faucet, microwave, oven, refrigerator, safe, laptop, box, bucket, suitcase, toaster, stapler, switch, window, dishwasher, washing_machine, toilet, coffee_machine, bottle_cap...), each instance = mobility.urdf + textured/decomposed OBJ meshes + semantics.txt + per-task keypoints.json annotations; plus YCB-style rigid objects and Franka URDFs. Plain URDF+OBJ = straightforwardly convertible to MJCF. GenSim v1: simple Ravens URDF primitives (blocks, bowls, kits) — low value.
LicenseBoth repos MIT (verified via GitHub API). GOTCHA: the bundled articulated assets derive from PartNet-Mobility/SAPIEN, which has its own research-use license requiring registration — the MIT tag on the repo does not cleanly cover the meshes; fine for research use.
CL track recordNone found. No continual/lifelong-learning paper uses RoboGen tasks as a CL benchmark; CL work in this space uses LIBERO, Continual World, or RoboCasa365 instead. RoboGen's own 'continuously acquires skills' framing is open-ended generation, not CL evaluation.
Port to mjlabDo NOT port the PyBullet code. Concrete path: (1) convert selected PartNet-Mobility URDFs to MJCF (MuJoCo loads URDF; known community converters exist; expect per-asset joint/collision fixups); (2) re-implement RoboGen's task-proposal + scene-config + reward-codegen prompts targeting mjlab's manager API (its rewards call simple primitives like get_position/get_joint_state — easy to mirror as mjlab reward terms); (3) replace suction with a weld-constraint gripper hack or restrict to fingered-gripper tasks; (4) drop OMPL subtask motion-planning in favor of GPU-RL per subtask or full task. Estimate: 1-2 weeks for the asset+prompt pipeline plus ~0.5-1 day of vetting/tuning per accepted task (10-20% of generated rewards are broken and long-horizon tasks need decomposition).
Scale potentialRealistically 20-40 state-solvable Franka tasks from GenSim2's shipped set alone (44 tasks, some near-duplicates like open_drawer vs your existing tasks); ~100+ if you also vary object instances (paper used 200 objects across categories — same task template x many PartNet-Mobility instances is the cheap multiplier, e.g. open_X for 20 different articulated categories). The LLM-generation pipeline itself is reusable conceptually in mjlab (task proposal -> reward code -> verify with RL), which is the 'generate rather than adopt' upside, but that's a research project, not a drop-in.
Gotchas(1) GenSim2's headline '100 tasks' is a pipeline-capacity claim; the repo ships ~44 curated tasks — plan around 44, not 100. (2) Nothing is MuJoCo-runnable out of the box; you adopt task specs + assets, not code. (3) PartNet-Mobility asset licensing is separate from the MIT repo license (academic-use registration). (4) Generating NEW tasks requires OpenAI GPT-4/GPT-4V API and human rejection-sampling — the 'infinite tasks' story has a human-in-the-loop cost. (5) GenSim2 repo is near-dead (11 commits); expect to own any fixes. (6) Several GenSim2 tasks overlap your existing 5 (open drawer/door, push) — dedupe when counting new tasks. (7) SAPIEN articulation conventions (joint limits, damping) differ from MuJoCo; articulated objects will need per-instance dynamics retuning to be RL-solvable. (8) RoboGen's LLM-written rewards frequently produce degenerate behaviors — treat its generated tasks as proposals needing manual verification.
Repo activityGenSim ~350 stars, moderately maintained. GenSim2 only 85 stars / 11 commits / 0 open issues — essentially a frozen paper-code dump, expect no upstream support. RoboGen ~1.2k stars but only 27 commits — also a frozen release; its promised Genesis integration never fully materialized in this repo.
Merged duplicate assessments (2)
  • [usable] Neither is MuJoCo-native and neither can be used directly: GenSim v1 is Ravens/PyBullet with a top-down suction pick-place ACTION PRIMITIVE (not low-level Franka control) — effectively reject for our setting. GenSim2 is the real value: ~60 concrete Franka manipulation task definitions (open/close/push/rotate/press/swing on 35 articulated PartNet-Mobility categories) with dense state-based rewards and per-asset keypoint annotations, all assets plain URDF+OBJ that port to MJCF. Use it as a task-recipe + asset + LLM-generation-methodology source for mjlab, not as a benchmark to adopt. No CL track record, and per-task porting work is nontrivial (SAPIEN env API, kPAM planner rewrite), which keeps it below strong/top-pick.
  • [usable] The three systems are very unequal. GenSim (v1) is effectively a REJECT for you: it extends Ravens/CLIPort (UR5 + suction, top-down pick/place pose action primitives, Transporter-style vision policies) — wrong robot, wrong action space, tasks don't map to Franka joint/Cartesian control. RoboGen is ASSETS/IDEA-ONLY: open-ended LLM pipeline, no fixed benchmark, auto-generated rewards of uneven quality, PyBullet. GenSim2 is the genuinely USABLE core: ~44 concrete human-verified Franka tabletop tasks (22 primitive + 22 long-horizon) on articulated PartNet-Mobility-style URDF objects, all solved by privileged-state kPAM keypoint/motion-planning solvers (i.e., state-solvable by construction), MIT-licensed, with a pipeline claimed to reach ~100 tasks / 200 objects. But it is SAPIEN-native with only 85 stars / 11 commits, so you'd be adopting its task specs + assets and reimplementing envs in mjlab, not running it as-is. Not top-pick/strong because nothing here is MuJoCo-native, the port is real work, and there is zero continual-learning track record.
Usable

RoboPianist

paper · code

Bimanual Shadow Hands playing 150 piano songs in MuJoCo/dm_control; huge state-solvable task count but one primitive, wrong robot, archived repo.

RoboPianist: Dexterous Piano Playing with Deep Reinforcement Learning (CoRL 2023)

Verdict: Genuinely MuJoCo-native with 150+ state-solvable tasks and confirmed vision-free training (DroQ from state), so it technically clears requirements 1, 2 and 4 — but it fails requirement 3 badly: every task is the same primitive (timed key-pressing) on a bimanual Shadow-Hand embodiment, with zero overlap with our Franka push/pick/open/press suite. Add the archived (read-only) repo, PIG-dataset licensing friction for the main 150 songs, ~5M-steps-per-song training cost with fingering-based reward shaping, and no continual-learning track record. Usable only if the goal is raw CL sequence length on a separate embodiment; not a source of manipulation-primitive diversity for mjlab.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (native MJCF, dm_control-based; Shadow Hand models from MuJoCo Menagerie + bundled full-size digital piano model). Not MJX/warp out of the box.
# TasksVerified in suite/__init__.py: 150 (Repertoire-150, PIG MIDI dataset) + 12 (Etude-12) + a handful of debug songs ≈ 162+ named envs; load() also accepts arbitrary MIDI files, so nominally unbounded.
Example tasksRoboPianist-repertoire-150-{SongName}-v0 (150 PIG-dataset pieces), RoboPianist-etude-12-{SongName}-v0 (e.g. easier etudes), RoboPianist-debug-TwinkleTwinkleLittleStar-v0.
PrimitivesEffectively ONE primitive: precisely timed multi-finger key pressing (plus sustain pedal). Tasks differ in note sequence/tempo/difficulty, not in skill type. No push/pick/place/open/insert.
RobotsTwo Shadow Dexterous Hands (left+right) with forearms, mounted over an 88-key piano. No arm/Franka. Hand MJCFs come from MuJoCo Menagerie (Apache-2.0).
State observationsFully state-based and vision-free solving is demonstrated: proprioception (2x22 hand/forearm joint angles), exteroceptive piano key states (88 keys + sustain), and goal piano-roll lookahead (88 x (L+1) horizon of upcoming notes) — order ~1k dims depending on lookahead. Trained with DroQ (SAC variant) MLPs at 20 Hz control, 44-dim target-joint-angle actions, 5M steps/song; no vision anywhere in the main results (a pixel wrapper exists but is optional). Fingering labels from the PIG dataset enter via reward shaping and matter for solvability.
Action space44-dim continuous: 22 target joint angles per hand fed to proportional-position actuators (plus sustain pedal); 20 Hz control.
AssetsMJCF only: piano model (88 hinge-jointed keys) bundled in repo, Shadow Hands from MuJoCo Menagerie — both trivially loadable in plain MuJoCo/mujoco-warp. Tasks themselves are MIDI files + fingering annotations, not scene assets. Asset reusability for a Franka manipulation suite is essentially zero.
LicenseCode: Apache-2.0. Shadow Hand assets: Menagerie Apache-2.0. BUT the PIG dataset (the 150 repertoire MIDIs + fingering labels) cannot be redistributed and must be requested separately (research-use application) — the flagship task set is not freely bundled.
CL track recordNo continual-learning papers found using RoboPianist (searched; results surface Continual World / CORA instead). Its citations are in high-dim RL (e.g. DroQ-style scaling work) and imitation (PianoMime), positioned by the authors for multi-task/zero-shot research, but nobody appears to have run a CL song sequence on it.
Port to mjlabModerate-high (2-4 weeks): MJCF scene composes cleanly (Menagerie hands + bundled piano), but you must reimplement in mjlab's manager API the MIDI parsing/goal piano-roll machinery, the fingering-shaped reward, and the timed-goal episode logic (currently dm_control Tasks). GPU-parallel (warp/MJX) feasibility is unproven: 88 hinged keys + 2 dexterous hands is contact-heavy, and each song is a different goal sequence rather than a different scene (good: one compiled model serves all tasks). Also expect high per-task training cost (paper: 5M steps DroQ per song with shaped rewards).
Scale potentialNominally unbounded (any MIDI), and 150 curated tasks exist today — easily 20-100+ CL tasks of one skill. Practical ceiling: songs beyond PIG lack fingering labels, and the paper shows fingering-based shaping is important for solvability, so scaling past 150 needs automatic fingering generation. All tasks share one primitive, so 'more tasks' means longer CL sequences, not broader skills.
Gotchas(1) Repo archived Jan 2026 — read-only, pinned to older MuJoCo/dm_control versions. (2) PIG dataset (the 150 songs + fingerings) is not redistributable; requires a research-use application, so the headline benchmark is gated. (3) Single primitive and Shadow-Hand embodiment — incompatible with a Franka primitive-diversity CL suite; would be a separate embodiment track. (4) Expensive tasks: ~5M steps/song with careful reward shaping; success is graded by F1 on key presses, not binary success. (5) Prior sweep's framing was accurate: great for CL sequence length, weak for primitive diversity.
Repo activity736 stars, 62 forks, 6 releases; repository archived Jan 2026, no further maintenance.
Usable

robosuite-task-zoo (ARISE-Initiative)

paper · code

Four community robosuite (MuJoCo) tabletop environments from the BUDS paper — ToolUse, HammerPlace, Kitchen, MultitaskKitchen (3 goals x 3 init variants) — long-horizon Panda manipulation with full low-dim state observables.

Bottom-Up Skill Discovery from Unsegmented Demonstrations for Long-Horizon Robot Manipulation (BUDS), Zhu, Stone, Zhu — IEEE RA-L 2022

Verdict: Only ~6 distinct tasks (not a real zoo), unmaintained since Oct 2022 and pinned to old robosuite, but assets are clean MIT-licensed MJCF+STL, tasks are fully state-solvable, and decomposing the long-horizon kitchen tasks yields 6-10 primitive subtasks that port easily to mjlab. Marginal add given heavy overlap with your existing open/close/press tasks; treat mainly as an asset+task-idea source, not a scaling path to 50-100 tasks.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo via robosuite (built for robosuite ~1.3/1.4 era, old mujoco-py generation; issue #3 shows texture-name incompatibility with newer robosuite). Assets themselves are plain MJCF, portable to modern MuJoCo/MJX/mjlab.
# Tasks4 environments; ~6 distinct tasks (ToolUse, HammerPlace, Kitchen, + 3 MultitaskKitchen goals; the advertised 9 MultitaskKitchen task_ids are 3 goals x 3 initial-state variants). Decomposed into primitives: ~6-10 subtasks.
Example tasksHammerPlace/ToolUse: place hammer into cabinet then close cabinet. Kitchen: put bread in pot, press button to turn stove on, cook, turn stove off, move pot to serving region. MultitaskKitchen task 2: close cabinet, turn off stove, place object in pot, move pot to serving region.
Primitivespick/place (hammer, ingredient, pot), open/close sliding cabinet, press button (stove on/off), tool use, place-in-region serving. Long-horizon compositions of 3-5 primitives each; overlaps your existing push-button/open-cabinet/lift primitives.
RobotsPanda (single-arm; robosuite supports swapping arms). 100 demos per single task, 360 for multitask (Panda), from the BUDS release.
State observationsYes, fully state-solvable: per-object pos/quat, obj-to-eef relative pos/quat, gripper binary contact (force threshold), eef force norm, 16-step force/torque ring buffer, plus robosuite proprioception (joint pos, eef pose, gripper). Images available but not required. Rewards are sparse success conditions (geometric containment + button state checks) readable from sim state.
Action spacerobosuite standard: OSC pose / joint-velocity / joint-position controllers with gripper open/close; envs are controller-agnostic (SingleArmEnv), so compatible with your Cartesian or joint-space mjlab setups.
AssetsSmall custom assets per env under robosuite_task_zoo/models/{tool_use,hammer_place,kitchen,multitask_kitchen}: MJCF XMLs (cabinet.xml, stove.xml, button.xml, serving_region.xml) + one STL mesh (burnerplate.stl) + Python robosuite object wrappers. Gotcha: references 4 texture names (Orange, Apple, Melon, Cake) removed from newer robosuite.
LicenseMIT
CL track recordIndirect. Built for BUDS skill discovery; reused by MimicGen-adjacent work (mirage_mimicgen_environments) and UT-Austin follow-ups. These envs are the direct precursors of LIBERO (same authors), which is the actual lifelong/continual benchmark — the zoo itself has no established CL benchmark usage.
Port to mjlabLow-to-medium per task: assets are a handful of small MJCF XMLs + 1 STL (trivial to drop into mjlab scenes), but env logic (staged success conditions, observables) must be rewritten in mjlab's manager API anyway since the repo targets old robosuite and won't run against modern stacks. Estimate ~1 day per environment given your existing Franka scene infrastructure; texture references need replacement.
Scale potentialLow as a scaling source: caps at ~6-10 tasks even after primitive decomposition, with substantial overlap with your current 5 (button press, cabinet open/close). Useful to add 3-6 genuinely new tasks (pot/stove/serving-region manipulation, hammer-in-cabinet). For 50-100 tasks look to its successor LIBERO or RoboCasa instead.
Gotchas(1) README calls it a community zoo but it only ever accumulated 4 envs from one paper; (2) MultitaskKitchen's 9 task_ids are only 3 distinct goals; (3) texture-name incompatibility with robosuite >=1.4 (issue #3, closed unresolved); (4) environments.md copy-pastes the same one-line description for all 4 envs — docs are sloppy; (5) unmaintained since 2022, 1 open issue, 0 PRs.
Repo activity37 stars, 20 forks, 31 commits, no releases; last push 2022-10-04 — effectively unmaintained for ~4 years.

Assets only (25)

The suite itself does not fit, but its objects/scenes/robots are reusable.

Assets only

Articulate-Anything

paper · code

VLM actor-critic system (UPenn, Eaton lab) that turns text/images/video into articulated-object URDFs via mesh retrieval from PartNet-Mobility plus joint-synthesis-as-Python-code; an asset generator, not a task suite.

Articulate-Anything: Automatic Modeling of Articulated Objects via a Vision-Language Foundation Model (ICLR 2025)

Verdict: It is an asset-generation pipeline, not a benchmark: zero tasks, zero environments, zero reward functions in the repo. Its only value to us is producing articulated URDF objects (doors, drawers, laptops, faucets, etc.) that we would then wrap in our own mjlab tasks. Even for that role it is heavyweight: it needs a VLM API key per generation, its mesh-retrieval mode depends on PartNet-Mobility (whose own SAPIEN terms-of-use apply, so outputs are not automatically license-clean despite the MIT code license), and every generated URDF still needs manual quality checking plus URDF-to-MJCF conversion with convex decomposition. For scaling to 10-50 articulated open/close/turn tasks, directly mining PartNet-Mobility (or an existing MJCF conversion of it) gets the same assets with less machinery; Articulate-Anything only wins if we need novel categories from our own photos/videos.

Full details
SimulatorSimulator-agnostic URDF output; repo demos use PyBullet and SAPIEN utilities. No MuJoCo support or mention anywhere in repo/paper. MuJoCo can compile URDF directly, so porting is mechanical but not turnkey (mesh decomposition, joint/contact tuning).
# Tasks0 (verified: repo README lists only text/image/video/masked reconstruction of objects; no RL envs, rewards, or task definitions). The paper demonstrates training a few manipulation policies on generated assets as a downstream showcase, but that code/task list is not released.
Example tasksNo tasks exist. Hypothetical tasks we would author from its outputs: open generated laptop lid, open dishwasher door, pull generated drawer, turn generated faucet handle, press generated switch — i.e., variants of our existing open-door/open-drawer with new asset instances.
PrimitivesNone defined. Generated assets naturally afford articulated primitives we could author ourselves: open/close (doors, drawers, boxes, laptops, ovens), turn/rotate (knobs, faucets, valves), press (buttons, switches), slide (drawers, sliding lids).
RobotsNone. User supplies the robot; paper demos apparently used a Franka in SAPIEN for the policy showcase but this is not in the repo.
State observationsN/A — no environments, so no observation spec exists. If we wrap its URDFs in mjlab, every task would be trivially state-solvable: articulated objects expose joint positions/velocities (qpos of hinge/slide joints) plus base pose, exactly like our existing open-door/open-drawer tasks. Nothing about the assets requires vision.
Action spaceN/A — no robot or controller included; objects only.
AssetsOutput is URDF + retrieved meshes (OBJ) with joints synthesized as Python code compiled via odio_urdf. Mesh source is PartNet-Mobility (must be downloaded separately from sapien.ucsd.edu; preprocessed copy on HuggingFace). Also works from in-the-wild images/video (mesh retrieval, not mesh generation). Reusable in MuJoCo after URDF import + convex decomposition (e.g., obj2mjcf/CoACD).
LicenseRepo code: MIT (badge in README; GitHub API reports license=null, i.e., no detected LICENSE file — minor red flag). Assets: PartNet-Mobility meshes carry SAPIEN's own terms of use (registration, academic use), so generated objects built from retrieved PartNet-Mobility parts inherit those terms; the sweep's "license-clean regeneration" claim only holds if you supply your own meshes.
CL track recordNo continual-learning paper uses it as a benchmark (searched; nothing found). Amusingly it comes from Eric Eaton's lifelong-learning lab at UPenn, but the paper itself is about asset generation, and follow-ups (URDF-Anything+, Articraft) are also generation methods, not CL benchmarks.
Port to mjlabPer asset: run the VLM pipeline (needs Gemini/GPT/Claude API key + PartNet-Mobility download) to get a URDF, then load into MuJoCo via its native URDF compiler or convert to MJCF; run CoACD/obj2mjcf convex decomposition for manipulable contact geometry; sanity-check joint axes/limits (VLM output is ~75% correct on PartNet-Mobility reconstruction, so ~1 in 4 assets needs manual fixing). Then author the mjlab task (reward, resets, success) from scratch. Estimate: 1-2 days to stand up the conversion toolchain, then hours per new task family — but note the same toolchain applied directly to raw PartNet-Mobility gives identical assets without the VLM step.
Scale potentialAs a task source it is indirect: it could supply dozens-to-hundreds of articulated object instances (PartNet-Mobility has ~2.3k objects/46 categories), enabling maybe 10-30 distinct open/close/turn/press task families with per-instance variation — all state-solvable. But every task must be hand-authored in mjlab, and the tool adds value over raw PartNet-Mobility only for novel categories or objects captured from our own photos/video.
Gotchas1) Zero tasks despite paper mentioning policy training — that showcase code is not released. 2) "Actor-critic" in the title is a VLM propose-critique loop, not RL. 3) Requires paid VLM API calls per generated object. 4) PartNet-Mobility must be separately downloaded and its terms accepted; generated assets are derivatives of it in retrieval mode. 5) No LICENSE file detected by GitHub despite MIT badge. 6) ~75% articulation success means silent joint errors in a quarter of outputs — need automated joint-motion validation before using assets in training. 7) Repo is near-dormant (14 commits, last push July 2025).
Repo activity200 stars, 20 forks, 5 open issues; created 2024-12-10, last push 2025-07-08 (about a year ago as of now); only ~14 commits on main, no releases. Small, lightly maintained research-code repo.
Assets only

Bi-DexHands (PKU-MARL DexterousHands)

paper · code

~20 bimanual Shadow-Hand manipulation tasks (hand-over, catch, door open/close, bottle cap, scissors, pen cap, switch, swing cup, turn button, grasp-and-place, block stack, re-orientation) in Isaac Gym, GPU-parallel (30-40k FPS on one 3090), with YCB+SAPIEN object variants.

Towards Human-Level Bimanual Dexterous Manipulation with Reinforcement Learning (Bi-DexHands), NeurIPS 2022 Datasets & Benchmarks

Verdict: Isaac Gym Preview 3/4 only (hard-excluded engine, now deprecated by NVIDIA) and the embodiment is two 24-DoF Shadow Hands — a total mismatch with our Franka-arm mjlab stack. Porting any single task means rewriting physics setup, resets, and dense rewards on a bimanual dexterous platform where mujoco-warp contact simulation is far more expensive; that is a research project, not a task import. What IS reusable: its MJCF/URDF object assets (door, bottle+cap, scissors, pen, kettle, switch, cup, blocks) and its well-documented catalog of ~20 task designs with per-task reward formulas, which we can re-implement as Franka tasks in mjlab. No continual-learning track record. If bimanual dexterous tasks are ever wanted, the newer MuJoCo-native DexJoCo (arXiv 2605.16257) is the better starting point than porting this.

Full details
SimulatorNVIDIA Isaac Gym Preview Release 3/4 ONLY (deprecated engine; no Isaac Lab migration in repo, no MuJoCo support). Verified from README: "We currently support the Preview Release 3/4 version of IsaacGym."
# TasksREADME front page lists 16 environments; paper (NeurIPS 2022 D&B) claims 20 tasks; docs add variants (DoorCloseOutward, TwoCatchUnderarm, BlockStack, Re-Orientation, Kettle, Switch, GraspAndPlace). Realistic count: ~20 distinct tasks, plus multi-task/meta splits (MT1/MT4/MT20, ML1/ML4/ML20 style) and thousands of object swaps from YCB/SAPIEN (>2,000 objects).
Example tasksShadowHandOver (throw object between hands), ShadowHandCatchUnderarm/Over2Underarm/Abreast/TwoCatchUnderarm, ShadowHandLiftUnderarm (lift pot with both hands), ShadowHandDoorOpenInward/OpenOutward/CloseInward/CloseOutward, ShadowHandBottleCap (unscrew), ShadowHandPushBlock, ShadowHandOpenScissors, ShadowHandOpenPenCap, ShadowHandSwingCup, ShadowHandTurnButton, ShadowHandGraspAndPlace, ShadowHandBlockStack, ShadowHandReOrientation, ShadowHandKettle (pour).
Primitiveshand-over/throw, catch (underarm, over-to-underarm, abreast, two-object), lift, push (block), open/close (door inward/outward), unscrew (bottle cap), open scissors, open pen cap, swing (cup), turn/press (button, switch), grasp-and-place, stack (blocks), in-hand re-orientation, pour (kettle)
RobotsTwo Shadow Dexterous Hands (24 DoF each, 20 actuated), optionally other hands via docs ("Change the type of dexterous hand"); floating/mounted hands, no arm.
State observationsFull privileged state, vision-free solving is the DEFAULT and is demonstrated: all benchmark results in the paper are state-based PPO/MAPPO/HAPPO. Obs per task is roughly 400-dim (e.g. ShadowHandOver full state ~398): per-hand 24 joint pos/vel/force, fingertip poses/velocities/forces, previous actions, object pose+velocity, goal pose. Point-cloud/depth-camera obs are an optional add-on, not required. So yes, every task is solvable from low-level state — but the state/action spaces are dexterous-hand-specific, not transferable to our Franka observation design.
Action spacePer hand: 20 actuated finger joints + (in most tasks) 6-DoF hand base motion = 26 per hand, 52 total continuous; treated as single-agent, multi-agent (hand=agent), or per-finger agents.
Assetsassets/ contains mjcf/ (Shadow Hand XML, OpenAI-gym-derived — genuinely MuJoCo-format), urdf/ (task objects: door, bottle, scissors, pen, kettle, cup, switch, blocks + meshes), textures/. Objects also drawn from YCB and SAPIEN (>2,000 objects). MJCF hand + URDF objects are the reusable part; Apache-2.0 so freely reusable.
LicenseApache-2.0 (repo LICENSE); note Isaac Gym itself has a proprietary NVIDIA license, another reason the engine is dead weight.
CL track recordNo continual-learning usage found. The paper itself benchmarks multi-task RL and meta-RL (MT/ML splits) and offline RL, and the repo is widely used in MARL papers (HAPPO/HARL, MARL robustness studies), but web search surfaced no CL/lifelong-learning paper adopting Bi-DexHands as its task sequence.
Port to mjlabHIGH. Positive: the Shadow Hand model in the repo is already MJCF (assets/mjcf, derived from the OpenAI Gym shadow-hand XML), objects are URDF/MJCF with meshes, and MuJoCo Menagerie has a canonical Shadow Hand — so ASSET port is easy. Negative: all task logic (Isaac Gym tensor API resets, rewards, randomization) must be rewritten per task in mjlab's manager API; bimanual 48-DoF contact-rich control in mujoco-warp is drastically slower and harder to tune than our Franka tasks; dynamic tasks (throw/catch) are sensitive to Isaac Gym physics quirks and may not reproduce. Estimate: 1-2 weeks per task for an experienced person just to get training working, on a new embodiment orthogonal to our current 5-task Franka suite.
Scale potentialAs a direct source: 0 tasks without a bimanual-hand porting project. As a design catalog for our Franka suite: ~8-12 re-implementable task IDEAS (bottle cap unscrew, scissors, pen cap, switch, swing/pour cup, kettle, grasp-and-place, block stack, re-orient) plus its door/bottle/scissors/kettle URDF+MJCF assets convertible to MuJoCo. Object-swap scaling (YCB/SAPIEN) is a technique we can copy independently.
Gotchas(1) Prior-sweep claim of "20 tasks" is paper-level; the maintained README lists 16 — some paper tasks are variants. (2) "Thousands of objects" applies to object-swap variants of a few tasks, not thousands of distinct skills. (3) Isaac Gym Preview is discontinued; repo never migrated, so even Isaac users can't easily run it today. (4) Dynamic throw/catch tasks likely won't transfer numerically to MuJoCo contact dynamics. (5) A MuJoCo-native successor exists — DexJoCo (arXiv 2605.16257, uses MuJoCo Menagerie assets, bimanual tasks); evaluate that instead if bimanual dexterity ever becomes a goal. (6) The docs' MuJoCo mention is only about d4rl-style offline data collection, not engine support.
Repo activity~1.1k stars, 133 forks, 224 commits, 36 open issues, no active PRs; effectively unmaintained (still pinned to deprecated Isaac Gym Preview 3/4, no updates for Isaac Lab).
Assets only

BiGym

paper · code

Demo-driven mobile bimanual manipulation benchmark: 40 sparse-reward home/kitchen tasks with a floating-base Unitree H1 + Robotiq grippers, MuJoCo-native with clean MJCF articulated-furniture assets.

BiGym: A Demo-Driven Mobile Bi-Manual Manipulation Benchmark (CoRL 2024)

Verdict: Fails the requirements as a task suite: bimanual mobile Unitree-H1 embodiment (not fixed Franka), sparse binary rewards designed for demo-driven IL, and only 3 of 40 tasks actually implement the privileged object-pose observation hook — the rest are vision-only out of the box. But its ~45 clean MJCF scenes with 378 mesh assets (articulated dishwasher with door+2 trays, drawers, base/wall cabinets, kitchen set, plates/mugs/cutlery/saucepan/sandwich/groceries) load directly in MuJoCo/mjlab and are an excellent source for generating 15-25 Franka-reachable household tasks with your own dense rewards and state obs.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (native, plain Python + dm_control-style prop/body wrappers; no MJX/warp GPU parallelism — single-env CPU stepping)
# Tasks40 (verified by counting concrete env classes in bigym/envs/: 13 dishwasher tasks, 8 cupboard/drawer, 8 pick&place, 3 manipulation, 2 move-plates, 2 groceries, 3 reach, plus StackBlocks)
Example tasksDishwasherOpen/Close/OpenTrays/CloseTrays, DishwasherLoad/UnloadPlates/Cups/Cutlery(+Long), DrawerTopOpen/Close, DrawersAllOpen/Close, WallCupboardOpen/Close, CupboardsOpenAll/CloseAll, MovePlate, MoveTwoPlates, PutCups, TakeCups, StoreBox, PickBox, SaucepanToHob, StoreKitchenware, ToastSandwich, FlipSandwich, RemoveSandwich, FlipCup, FlipCutlery, StackBlocks, GroceriesStoreLower/Upper, ReachTarget/Single/Dual
Primitivesreach, pick, place, open, close (dishwasher door/trays, drawers, wall cupboards), load/unload (plates, cups, cutlery into racks/trays), flip (cup, cutlery, sandwich), stack (blocks), store (boxes, groceries, kitchenware), move/transfer (plates between racks)
RobotsUnitree H1 humanoid with Robotiq 2F-85 grippers (H1 and H1FineManipulation configs); repo also contains configs/XMLs for Google Robot and Hello Robot Stretch (from MuJoCo Menagerie)
State observationsDefault proprioception only: joint pos+vel, gripper opening (0-1), floating-base pose + accumulated base actions. ObservationConfig has a privileged_information flag routing to per-task _get_task_privileged_obs(), but ONLY 3/40 tasks implement it (ReachTarget: 3D target pos; ReachTargetDual: 2x3D; MovePlate: rack 7D pose + plate pos; MoveTwoPlates returns empty dict). No task provides full object-pose state vectors; vision-free solving is NOT demonstrated in the paper (all baselines are demo-driven IL/RL: ACT, diffusion policy, DrQ-v2+demos). Adding object poses yourself is straightforward (every prop is a MuJoCo body with get_pose()), but rewards are sparse float(success) with no dense shaping, so state-RL from scratch needs reward engineering too.
Action spaceConfigurable ActionModes: JointPositionActionMode (absolute or delta, optionally with floating-base pelvis DOFs) or TorqueActionMode; dual Robotiq 2F-85 grippers; legs are kinematically animated, base moves via actuated pelvis DOFs
AssetsClean MJCF XMLs (45) + ~378 OBJ/STL meshes under bigym/envs/xmls/props/: articulated dishwasher (door + 2 sliding trays), base/wall cabinets and drawers (base_cabinet_600, wall_cabinet_600, open_shelf_600), kitchen set, tables, plates, mugs, cutlery + tray, dish drainer, pan/saucepan/spatula, sandwich, boxes, cube, groceries. Directly loadable in any MuJoCo pipeline including mjlab; robot XMLs from MuJoCo Menagerie. This is the most reusable part of the repo.
LicenseCode: Apache-2.0. Assets: mixed — CC0 and CC BY 4.0 mostly, but at least one CC BY-NC 4.0 (Sketchfab box asset); see 3D_MODELS_ATTRIBUTION.md. Fine for research use; avoid the NC assets if commercial constraints matter.
CL track recordNone found. Web search turned up no continual-learning papers using BiGym; CL policy-distillation work (e.g. arXiv:2601.22475) uses Meta-World MT25 instead. BiGym citations are demo-driven IL/RL papers (ACT/diffusion baselines, RL-with-action-sequence arXiv:2411.12155).
Port to mjlabTasks themselves don't port (bimanual mobile humanoid, sparse rewards). Realistic path: harvest the MJCF prop/furniture XMLs (dishwasher, drawers, cabinets, kitchenware) — they are self-contained MuJoCo XMLs with local mesh assets, so dropping them into an mjlab Franka scene is roughly copy-XML + attach to your scene + write reward/termination managers per task, i.e. similar effort to your existing open_door/open_drawer tasks: ~0.5-1 day per new task after a 1-2 day one-time asset-integration pass. No format conversion needed at all (already MJCF), which is the main advantage over ManiSkill/SAPIEN sources.
Scale potentialAs a benchmark: 0 directly usable tasks. As an asset library: realistically 15-25 Franka state-solvable tasks — dishwasher door/tray open+close (4), drawer/cupboard open+close variants (6-8), load/unload plate/cup/cutlery into racks (6, may need reachability tweaks), pick-place mug/box/saucepan/groceries (4-6), flip cup/cutlery, stack blocks (3). Combined with your existing 5 tasks this plausibly gets you to the 20-30 range, but not to 50-100 on its own.
Gotchas1) The prior sweep's 'borderline only because of sparse rewards, not observability' claim is WRONG for out-of-box use: 36/40 tasks expose NO object state — you must write privileged obs yourself. 2) Canonical repo moved to NeuracoreAI/bigym; chernyadev/bigym shows 0 stars and may lag. 3) CPU single-env MuJoCo — no GPU parallelism; you'd only use assets, not the env loop. 4) One CC BY-NC 4.0 asset (box). 5) H1 legs are animated (kinematic), not physically simulated — scenes assume humanoid-height counters/cabinets, so a table-mounted Franka may need scene rescaling/repositioning for reachability. 6) Demos are keyed to specific action modes and the H1 embodiment — useless for Franka distillation teachers.
Repo activity224 stars, 31 forks (NeuracoreAI/bigym); last push 2026-07-01, recent maintenance commits (e.g. safetensors pin fix May 2026) — moderately active, maintained by Neuracore
Assets only

DexMimicGen (NVlabs) — robosuite bimanual/dexterous task suite

paper · code

ICRA 2025 automated demo-generation system + 9 bimanual/dexterous robosuite-MuJoCo environments (Panda pairs and GR-1 humanoid) used to synthesize 21K demos from 60 human demos for imitation learning.

DexMimicGen: Automated Data Generation for Bimanual Dexterous Manipulation via Imitation Learning (ICRA 2025)

Verdict: All 9 tasks are BIMANUAL (two Panda arms or GR-1 humanoid), 6 of 9 use multi-finger dexterous hands — a hard embodiment mismatch with our single-Franka mjlab setup. Rewards are sparse success-only (built for IL from human demos, not RL), so every task would need reward engineering AND a bimanual scene port. Only ~9 tasks total, so no scaling story. The MJCF object assets (needle/ring-tripod, three-piece assembly, coffee machine, drawer, tray, pouring cups) are MuJoCo-native and could seed a handful of new single-arm mjlab tasks, but the NVIDIA non-commercial license taints anything derived from the code. The single-arm ancestor MimicGen (12 Panda tasks, also robosuite/MuJoCo) is a strictly better candidate for our requirements.

Full details
Simulatorrobosuite (MuJoCo physics). Standard MJCF XML scenes + STL/OBJ meshes; requires latest robosuite and a special 'dexmimicgen' branch of robomimic for training. Not MJX/Warp — CPU MuJoCo via robosuite.
# Tasks9 exactly (verified against environments/ dir): TwoArmThreading, TwoArmThreePieceAssembly, TwoArmTransport, TwoArmBoxCleanup, TwoArmDrawerCleanup, TwoArmLiftTray, TwoArmPouring, TwoArmCoffee, TwoArmCanSort (plus a shared base class two_arm_dexmg_env.py).
Example tasksTwoArmThreading: one arm holds a ring tripod while the other threads a needle through the ring (success = needle-ring distance &lt; ring radius). TwoArmDrawerCleanup: dexterous hands open a drawer and place an object inside. TwoArmCoffee (GR-1): insert pod and operate coffee machine. TwoArmCanSort (GR-1): sort cans into bins — also their real-robot demo (90% real success).
Primitivesinsert/thread (Threading, Piece Assembly), pick-place/pack (Box Cleanup, Transport, Can Sort), open/close articulated (Drawer Cleanup), lift (Tray Lift), pour (Pouring), press/insert (Coffee). Good primitive coverage but every one requires bimanual coordination.
RobotsBimanual Panda + parallel-jaw grippers (Threading, Piece Assembly, Transport); bimanual Panda + dexterous hands (Box Cleanup, Drawer Cleanup, Tray Lift); Fourier GR-1 humanoid + dexterous hands (Pouring, Coffee, Can Sort). No single-arm configuration.
State observationsrobosuite-standard: envs expose use_object_obs=True ground-truth object pose observables plus proprioception (eef pose, gripper/hand joint states), so low-level-state training is mechanically possible. HOWEVER the paper only ever trained image-based BC policies; state-solvability of the dexterous-hand tasks is unproven, and rewards are sparse (success check only, e.g. needle-in-ring distance test), so RL teachers would need hand-written dense rewards.
Action spacePer-arm OSC / joint controllers via robosuite; dexterous-hand tasks add finger joint actions (high-DOF). Demo generation transforms 6-DoF eef trajectories per subtask.
AssetsMJCF object models in-repo: NeedleObject, RingTripodObject, three-piece assembly parts, coffee machine, articulated drawer, tray, cans/bins, pouring cups; TableArena scenes. Datasets: HDF5 demos on HuggingFace (CC-BY 4.0). Object XMLs are MuJoCo-native and individually portable.
LicenseCode: NVIDIA Source Code License — NON-COMMERCIAL only, redistribution must carry the license, patent-retaliation termination clause. Datasets: CC-BY 4.0. Fine for academic research; restrictive for anything commercial.
CL track recordNone. Used for imitation-learning data generation (BC-RNN, GR00T N1 pretraining), never as a continual-learning benchmark. Success rates in-paper are for image BC on generated demos (e.g. Drawer Cleanup 76%, Can Sort 97.3%, Threading 69.3%).
Port to mjlabHigh. Assets are MJCF+meshes so mechanically portable to mjlab, but tasks assume two arms (mjlab suite is single Franka) — porting means redesigning each task as single-arm, writing dense rewards from scratch, and for 6/9 tasks either porting a dexterous hand (many DOF, mesh-heavy collisions, expensive in MuJoCo-Warp/MJX GPU batches) or re-rigging for a parallel gripper. Realistically 1-3 salvageable object sets, not turnkey tasks.
Scale potentialLow for us: 9 tasks, no procedural task generator (DexMimicGen scales DEMOS per task, not the number of tasks). Even a perfect port adds &lt;9 tasks. Does not help reach 50-100 tasks.
Gotchas(1) Repo README undersells content — env list only verified by inspecting dexmimicgen/environments/. (2) Paper trained ONLY image-based policies; 'state-solvable' is untested, and rewards are sparse success checks, so RL-teacher training needs custom dense rewards. (3) Needs a forked 'dexmimicgen' branch of robomimic. (4) NC license contaminates derived code, though datasets are CC-BY. (5) The original single-arm MimicGen (NVlabs/mimicgen, 12 Panda robosuite tasks incl. Coffee/Threading/Assembly variants) matches our single-Franka + primitives + MuJoCo requirements far better and should be evaluated instead — DexMimicGen is essentially its bimanual extension.
Repo activity261 stars, 23 forks; created 2025-02-25, last push 2025-12-06 (verified via GitHub API) — maintained but modest adoption; used downstream by NVIDIA GR00T N1.
Assets only

DISCOVERSE

paper · code

3DGS-photorealistic Real2Sim2Real layer on plain CPU MuJoCo for imitation-learning demo generation (ACT/DP/RDT), with ~22 scripted tabletop/mobile tasks on AirBot Play and MMK2 robots.

DISCOVERSE: Efficient Robot Simulation in Complex High-Fidelity Environments (IROS 2025)

Verdict: MuJoCo-native and MIT-licensed, but it is a vision/3DGS imitation-learning data-generation framework, not an RL benchmark: only ~22 scripted-demo tasks on AirBot Play / MMK2 robots (not Franka), with no reward functions, no gym-style env API, no defined state observation, no GPU-parallel physics (plain CPU MuJoCo; the parallelism is multiprocess rendering), and zero continual-learning track record. The only transferable value is a small set of MIT-licensed MJCF/mesh tabletop assets (drawer, laptop, cabinet, cups, fruit, blocks) that could seed ~8-12 mjlab tasks, most of which overlap primitives we already have.

Full details
SimulatorPlain MuJoCo (CPU, single-env) + 3D Gaussian Splatting renderer; NOT MJX/MuJoCo-Warp; no GPU-parallel physics (Taichi GPU only for LiDAR sim); multiprocessing used for demo collection
# Tasks~22 verified in repo: 12 AirBot Play tabletop (examples/tasks_airbot_play/), 9 MMK2 mobile dual-arm (examples/tasks_mmk2/ + models/mjcf/tasks_mmk2/), 1 hand-arm (build_tower). README/paper do not claim more.
Example taskspick_jujube, place_coffeecup, stack_block, block_bridge_place, push_mouse, open_drawer, close_laptop, cover_cup, place_kiwi_fruit (AirBot); cabinet_door_open, drawer_open, box_pick, coffeecup_plate, pan_pick, pour_tea (MMK2); build_tower (dex hand)
Primitivespick, place, push, stack, open (drawer, cabinet door), close (laptop), cover/insert (cover_cup, block_bridge_place, peg-in-hole config), pour (pour_tea MJCF)
RobotsTasks built for AirBot Play (6-DoF arm+gripper) and MMK2 (mobile dual-arm); robot models also shipped for panda, ur5e, iiwa14, xarm7, arx_l5/x5, piper, rm65, Leap Hand, Inspire hand-arm, RM2 car, Skyrover — but no tasks defined on Franka
State observationsNo defined state-observation vector or RL API. Tasks expose check_success() from body/site poses and scripted experts read mj_data directly, so all tasks are state-solvable in principle, but obs/reward/termination would be authored from scratch in mjlab.
Action spaceJoint-position targets via position actuators; scripted experts compute them with analytic IK (AirbotPlayIK) or mink IK; also impedance/force-control examples. No Cartesian RL action wrapper provided.
AssetsMJCF scenes + OBJ meshes for tabletop objects (jujube, kiwi, coffeecup, plate, blocks, laptop, drawer, cabinet, pan, mouse), robot MJCFs/URDFs, plus 3DGS .ply appearance models auto-downloaded from HuggingFace (irrelevant for state-based use). MJCF+mesh assets are directly portable to mjlab.
LicenseMIT (verified via GitHub API spdx_id)
CL track recordNone. No continual-learning papers or benchmarks use DISCOVERSE; its evaluation is zero-shot sim2real imitation learning (vs MuJoCo, RoboTwin, SplatSim baselines).
Port to mjlabMedium per task: MJCF scenes/objects load in MuJoCo-Warp with minor edits (asset paths, actuators are AirBot Play position-controlled), but every task needs a rewrite as an mjlab manager env — swap robot to Franka, write rewards, success, randomization, obs. Roughly comparable to authoring a new mjlab task with free object meshes.
Scale potentialLow-moderate: yields ~8-12 additional state-solvable Franka tasks after de-duplicating against our existing 5 (its open_drawer/drawer_open/push overlap ours). Its Real2Sim asset pipeline (mesh2mjcf, AIGC/Rodin object generation tooling) is more interesting for asset creation than the benchmark itself. Does not get us to 50-100 tasks.
GotchasREADME emphasis on "massively parallel" refers to sensor/demo rendering, not GPU physics. Tasks are demo-recording scripts with Chinese comments, tightly coupled to AirBot Play kinematics and the 3DGS renderer config. Some assets (scenes) exist only as 3DGS point clouds with simple collision proxies. Success thresholds are simple distance checks. Panda support is model-only.
Repo activity470 stars, 58 forks; created 2024-12; actively maintained (last push 2026-03-06)
