DoorGym

paper · code

Procedural door-opening generator: 3 doorknob classes (pull/lever/round) x ~3,000 randomized knob instances each, composed with randomized doors/physics into MuJoCo MJCF worlds; one primitive, massive instance diversity.

DoorGym: A Scalable Door Opening Environment And Baseline Agent (Urakami et al., NeurIPS 2019 Deep RL Workshop)

Verdict: It is a single task family (door opening), not a multi-task suite: 3 knob types x door/physics randomization yields thousands of INSTANCES of one primitive, at most ~4-6 distinct tasks for a CL curriculum (pull door, push door, turn lever, rotate round knob, hooked vs gripper variants) — and you already have open-door and open-drawer. The code stack is dead (MuJoCo 2.0 + mujoco-py + gym 2019 API, robots are Baxter/BLUE/floating hook, not Franka), so you would not run it directly. What IS valuable: the 3x ~3,000-knob MJCF+mesh dataset (BSD-3, links verified live) and the world_generator pattern as a template for procedurally scaling one primitive into many instances — directly relevant to your task-generation ambitions.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo 2.00 via mujoco-py + old gym.envs.mujoco.MujocoEnv API; optional Unity3D plugin for photorealistic vision rendering (not needed for state-based use). NOT maintained for MuJoCo 3.x/MJX.
# Tasks1 task family (open door). Concretely: 3 knob types (pull, lever, round) x ~3,000 procedurally generated knob instances each (~9K instances) x door randomization x 6 robot configs (floating hook/gripper, mobile hook/gripper, BLUE arm, Baxter). As distinct CL tasks: ~3-6, not 10+.
Example tasksopen pull-knob door with floating hook; open lever-knob door with BLUE gripper arm; open round-knob door (grasp, rotate knob past latch, pull) with mobile-base gripper; push-open unlatched door; same tasks across 3,000 randomized knob geometries per type.
Primitivespull (pull-knob door), turn+pull (lever knob), rotate+pull (round knob), push (door push variant); reach/grasp implicit. All within the open-door family.
RobotsUC Berkeley BLUE arm (hook or gripper end-effector, fixed or mobile base), floating hook, floating gripper, Baxter (single/both arms). No Franka — you would use your own.
State observationsVision-free by default and demonstrated in the paper. doorenv.py _get_obs() default branch = concat(get_robot_joints(), get_dist_vec()) — robot joint positions/velocities plus the 3D end-effector-to-knob vector taken from MuJoCo ground truth (~2*nn+3 dims; nn=6-16 depending on robot, e.g. ~17D for floating hook). visionnet_input/vision_obs are optional flags that replace ground-truth knob position with a CNN estimate. Paper PPO/SAC baselines trained on state: pull knobs up to ~95% success, lever moderate, round knobs hardest (near 0-20%) — so 'solvable from state' holds for pull/lever, round is genuinely hard, not obs-limited.
Action spaceJoint torques by default (actuator ctrlrange Box); optional position control (pos_control) and IK control flags. 6-16 DoF depending on robot config.
AssetsDoorknob dataset: 3 tar.gz releases (pull 0.75 GB, lever 0.77 GB, round 1.24 GB), ~3,000 procedurally generated knobs each, as MJCF/XML + mesh files; world_generator.py composes knob+door+robot into standalone MuJoCo world XMLs. Download links verified working (GitHub release v1.0). Highly reusable — plain MJCF/STL.
LicenseBSD 3-Clause (copyright Panasonic) — verified in repo LICENSE file; GitHub API labels it 'other' but the text is standard BSD-3. Vendored a2c_ppo_acktr/rlkit carry their own MIT licenses. Knob dataset released under the same repo.
CL track recordNo continual-learning usage found. Searched; it appears in door-manipulation follow-ups (e.g. cited by UniDoorManip, arXiv:2403.02604) and sim2real/domain-randomization work, but no CL/lifelong-learning paper uses it as a benchmark.
Port to mjlabDo not port the code; harvest assets + the generation recipe. Path: (1) download knob tar.gz datasets (verified live on GitHub releases, 0.75-1.24 GB each; MJCF+STL meshes), (2) run or reimplement world_generator.py to emit door+knob subtrees WITHOUT their robots, (3) attach the door subtree to your mjlab Franka scene like your existing mjx_door.xml. MJCF from MuJoCo 2.0 loads in MuJoCo 3.x with minor fixes (compiler/actuator attribute deprecations); main real work is collision/contact tuning for MJX/mujoco-warp (mesh knobs may need convex decomposition or primitive approximation for GPU sim) and reward/success definitions in your manager API. Estimate: 3-7 days for a lever+pull+round doorknob trio of mjlab tasks with instance randomization; mesh-heavy knobs on warp/MJX is the risk item.
Scale potentialAs distinct tasks: ~3-6 (pull door, push door, lever knob, round knob, +locked/unlatched variants). As instance-level diversity within your existing open-door task: effectively unlimited (9K knobs x door pose/size/physics randomization). Will not get you to 20+ tasks alone; its main strategic value is as a proven template for procedurally exploding one primitive into thousands of instances, which you could replicate for drawers/buttons/valves.
Gotchas(1) Prior-sweep claim of '3K+ knobs' is per type — ~9K total across pull/lever/round; still one task family. (2) Code requires deprecated MuJoCo 2.0 license-key era mujoco-py — treat code as reference only. (3) Round-knob opening had near-zero baseline success even from state; budget for reward shaping if you include it. (4) Generated worlds bundle robot+door in one XML — you must strip the robot subtree to import doors into mjlab. (5) Mesh-based knobs (thousands of unique STLs) may be slow/fragile in MJX/mujoco-warp contact handling; consider convex decomposition or reducing knob set. (6) Vision pipeline needs the separate DoorGym-Unity plugin — irrelevant for your state-based use but do not expect the in-repo vision path to work today.
Repo activity121 stars, 22 forks; last push 2022-07-28, substantive development ended late 2019. Effectively unmaintained.
Assets only

Factory / IndustReal (IsaacGymEnvs assembly suite)

paper · code

NVIDIA's NIST-Task-Board-1-derived contact-rich assembly suite (Factory RSS 2022 + IndustReal RSS 2023) in Isaac Gym: nut-bolt pick/place/screw and peg/gear insertion with a Franka, built on 60 simulation-ready part models.

Factory: Fast Contact for Robotic Assembly (RSS 2022); IndustReal: Transferring Contact-Rich Assembly Tasks from Simulation to Reality (RSS 2023); scale successor: AutoMate: Specialist and Generalist Assembly Policies over Diverse Geometries (RSS 2024)

Verdict: The runtime is Isaac Gym (hard-excluded, and the repo was archived April 2026 / read-only). Only ~7 defined envs exist, all in one primitive family (pick/place/insert/screw), so it adds skill depth, not skill diversity. Tasks ARE fully state-solvable (low-dim proprio + object poses, no vision), and the 60 OBJ+URDF part models under BSD-3 are best-in-class 'insert' assets — but the physics that makes these tasks work (SDF collision, sub-mm clearances, threaded nut-bolt contact) is exactly what MJX/mujoco-warp is weakest at: MuJoCo's SDF plugin is CPU-only and not supported in mujoco-warp/MJX, so a faithful GPU-parallel port to mjlab is a research project, not an integration task. Harvest the meshes for a handful of coarse-clearance peg/gear insertion tasks; do not adopt the suite.

Full details
SimulatorIsaac Gym (preview) with Factory's custom SDF-based contact pipeline — NOT MuJoCo. IsaacGymEnvs repo is archived (read-only since 2026-04-14). Successors (IndustRealLib/AutoMate) target Isaac Gym/Isaac Lab, all excluded runtimes.
# Tasks7 defined envs, not ~10: Factory = FactoryTaskNutBoltPick, NutBoltPlace, NutBoltScrew (trained policies) + NutBoltInsertion and Gears as unsolved templates ("open area of research"); IndustReal = IndustRealTaskPegsInsert, IndustRealTaskGearsInsert. Instance-level expansion over the 60 part models (nut/bolt sizes, round/rect pegs x3 sizes, 3 gears, BNC/D-sub/USB/RJ45/NEMA connectors) yields dozens of variants of the same few skills.
Example tasksNutBoltPick (grasp M16 nut), NutBoltPlace (place nut on bolt shank), NutBoltScrew (thread nut down bolt), PegsInsert (round/rectangular pegs x3 sizes into holes), GearsInsert (3-gear train onto shafts), connector mating templates (BNC, D-sub, USB, RJ45, NEMA 2/3-prong)
Primitivespick, place, insert (peg-in-hole, gear-on-shaft, connector mating), screw/fasten (nut-on-bolt). Essentially one primitive family: precision insertion/fastening. No push/open/slide/stack diversity.
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (corrected/de-dissipated URDF in IndustReal); task-space impedance / 7 controller variants in Factory
State observationsFully state-based, vision-free — verified. E.g. FactoryTaskNutBoltPick uses numObservations=20: arm joint angles, fingertip-midpoint pose (pos+quat) and velocities, nut/bolt object poses; Screw/Insert variants add goal pose and pose deltas (~24-32 dims). IndustReal likewise trains from proprioception + (noisy) object pose, with vision only used for real-world pose estimation. All published successful policies were trained purely from this privileged low-dim state.
Action spaceTask-space impedance control (delta EEF pose targets) via Factory's controller suite (7 controllers incl. OSC, task-space impedance); gripper open/close. Maps cleanly onto mjlab's Franka action managers.
Assets60 simulation-ready part models (Factory paper claim, verified): OBJ meshes + URDF + YAML physical constants in IsaacGymEnvs; industrealkit adds ~20 3D-printable STLs + Onshape CAD (no URDF/MJCF). Highly reusable geometry; AutoMate extends to 100 interpenetration-free assemblies with realistic clearances (OBJ+URDF). All plain mesh formats, straightforward to wrap in MJCF.
LicenseIsaacGymEnvs (code + Factory assets): BSD-3-Clause (NVIDIA, verified from LICENSE.txt). industrealkit: has a LICENSE file but raw LICENSE.txt 404'd — terms unverified, check before redistribution. Isaac Gym runtime itself is under a proprietary NVIDIA EULA, but you don't need it for assets.
CL track recordNo continual-learning usage found. Searches for CL/lifelong/policy-distillation papers using Factory or IndustReal returned nothing; the CL literature clusters around Meta-World, Continual World, and LIBERO. You would be first — which also means no CL baselines to compare against on these tasks.
Port to mjlabAssets: OBJ meshes + URDF for every part ship inside IsaacGymEnvs (BSD-3) — URDF-to-MJCF via MuJoCo's compiler is trivial. Physics is the blocker: (1) coarse-clearance peg/gear insertion could run in mjlab today via convex decomposition (obj2mjcf/CoACD), ~1-2 weeks for 5-10 tasks with widened clearances; (2) faithful sub-mm insertion and any threaded nut-bolt screwing needs SDF collision — MuJoCo 3.x has an SDF plugin (with actual nut/bolt demo geoms in google-deepmind/mujoco plugin/sdf), but it is CPU-only and unsupported in MJX/mujoco-warp, killing GPU-parallel training in mjlab. Faithful port = months of contact-engineering research.
Scale potentialRealistically 10-20 mjlab tasks from instance variation at relaxed clearances (pegs: 2 shapes x 3 sizes, 3 gears, ~6 connector types, nut pick/place per size), all within the single insert/place primitive family. AutoMate's 100 assemblies could push to 50-100 insertion instances, but MuJoCo-warp contact fidelity at their sub-mm clearances is unproven, and 100 variants of "insert" arguably do not constitute 100 tasks for CL purposes. Nut-bolt screwing is effectively unportable to GPU MuJoCo today.
Gotchas(1) IsaacGymEnvs archived — no support, and Isaac Gym preview no longer installs cleanly on modern CUDA; treat as asset quarry only. (2) 2 of the 5 Factory "tasks" are untrained templates, so the prior-sweep "~10 envs" figure was inflated; real trained-task count is 5. (3) The whole suite's value depends on SDF contact at 0.1-1 mm clearances; MuJoCo's SDF plugin (which even ships nut/bolt demos) is CPU-only and incompatible with MJX/mujoco-warp — your GPU-parallel requirement and the suite's physics requirement are in direct tension. (4) industrealkit has no URDF/MJCF, only STL/OBJ/CAD; and its license terms could not be verified. (5) Screwing tasks additionally rely on Isaac Gym-specific contact reduction and small substeps; expect divergent dynamics even if geometry ports.
Repo activityIsaacGymEnvs ~2k+ stars but archived April 2026, zero future maintenance; NVIDIA's effort moved to Isaac Lab (Forge/AutoMate/MatchMaker line). industrealkit is a small static asset repo.
Assets only

fancy_gym (ALR lab, KIT)

paper · code

KIT ALR lab's MuJoCo env collection with movement-primitive (DMP/ProMP/ProDMP) wrappers; one strong Panda box-pushing task, a few dynamic-hitting tasks, several locomotion variants, plus pass-through wrappers for Meta-World/dm_control.

No dedicated paper (confirmed); envs introduced/used across ALR publications (ProDMP, BBRL, TCE, 2022-2024).

Verdict: Only ONE genuine Franka manipulation task (BoxPushing with orientation goal — a well-tuned, contact-rich, harder variant of our push-cuboid) plus a few dynamic-hitting tasks (table tennis, beer pong, air hockey); the rest are locomotion-jump variants of Gym classics. Its multiplicity is reward-shaping variants and MP wrappers, not distinct skills, so it can never yield 10-20 new tasks. Worth harvesting the box_pushing MJCF + reward design directly into mjlab; nothing more.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (native MJCF, Gymnasium API); wrapped external suites (Meta-World, dm_control) are also MuJoCo. Pure Python, torque control via mujoco bindings — no MJX/warp, CPU step-based.
# Tasks~10 custom MuJoCo envs, but only ~4 are manipulation-ish (BoxPushing, TableTennis, BeerPong, AirHockey) and only BoxPushing is a Franka arm-manipulation task. BoxPushing has 5 reward variants (Dense, TemporalSparse, TemporalSpatialSparse, etc.) of the same skill. MiniGolf claimed in prior sweep NOT found in master. Remaining envs are locomotion (ant/hopper/cheetah/walker jump-throw variants) + a reacher.
Example tasksfancy/BoxPushingDense-v0, fancy/BoxPushingTemporalSparse-v0, fancy/BoxPushingTemporalSpatialSparse-v0, fancy/TableTennis4D-v0, fancy/TableTennisWind-v0, fancy/BeerPong-v0, fancy/HopperJump-v0, fancy/AntJump-v0, plus MP-wrapped ids like fancy_ProDMP/BoxPushingDense-v0.
Primitivespush (with target orientation — the standout), hit/strike (table tennis, beer pong, air hockey), reach. No pick, place, open, close, insert, press, stack.
RobotsFranka Panda (BoxPushing, TableTennis, BeerPong — 7-DoF torque control), KUKA iiwa (air hockey challenge envs), plus standard Gym locomotion bodies.
State observationsFully state-based, no vision anywhere. BoxPushing: 28-D = 7 joint pos + 7 joint vel + box pos(3) + box quat(4) + target pos(3) + target quat(4); action = 7-D normalized joint torques. All tasks demonstrated solvable from state in ALR's episodic-RL papers (ProDMP/BBRL/TCE trained from these obs).
Action space7-D continuous normalized joint torques for Panda tasks; MP-wrapped variants expose movement-primitive weight vectors (DMP/ProMP/ProDMP black-box episodic actions).
AssetsNative MuJoCo MJCF XMLs (e.g. box_pushing.xml with Panda + rod + box) bundled in-repo, MIT-licensed — directly reusable in mjlab with zero format conversion.
LicenseMIT
CL track recordNo continual-learning usage found — web search surfaces no CL paper using fancy_gym; its citation footprint is episodic RL / movement-primitive papers from the ALR lab itself.
Port to mjlabTrivial for BoxPushing: native MJCF (box_pushing.xml with Panda, rod end-effector, box) loads directly in MuJoCo/MJX/mujoco-warp; re-implement the ~150-line reward (joint-limit penalty, TCP-box distance, box-goal pos+rot error, energy cost) as mjlab manager terms. Estimate 1-2 days for one polished mjlab task including randomization (box init pose + target pose >=0.3m apart). Table tennis/beer pong would need ball-dynamics tuning under MJX and are dynamic tasks, less aligned with our primitives.
Scale potentialVery low as a suite: at most 2-4 new tasks (box-push-to-pose, maybe push-with-orientation as a distinct harder task; table tennis/beer pong are off-primitive). Reward variants and MP wrappers do not count as distinct tasks. Cannot get anywhere near 20 tasks from this resource.
GotchasPrior-sweep claim of "MiniGolf" not present in master (possibly only in the Andrewllab/fancy_gymnasium fork). Task count is inflated by reward variants and MP wrappers of the same underlying skills. Torque-level control at 1kHz-ish sim with bias compensation differs from our likely Cartesian/joint-position action space, so reward transfer is easy but the exact difficulty calibration may shift. CPU-only Gymnasium envs — no GPU parallelism; you port the task, not the runtime.
Repo activity~43 stars, 18 forks; last release v0.3.0 (Mar 2024); low activity since — effectively maintenance mode. Docs at alrhub.github.io/fancy_gym.

FurnitureBench / FurnitureSim

paper · code

Real+sim (Isaac Gym) benchmark of 8-9 long-horizon furniture-assembly tasks with a Franka; valuable insert/screw assets, but not a source of many state-solvable RL tasks.

FurnitureBench: Reproducible Real-World Benchmark for Long-Horizon Complex Manipulation (RSS 2023)

Verdict: Runtime is Isaac Gym (excluded) and depends on Factory's SDF contact solver for the tight-tolerance insert/screw physics that define the benchmark — exactly the part that does not port cheaply to MuJoCo/mjlab. Only 9 tasks (really 8 furniture + 1 subtask), all long-horizon; state-based RL from scratch does NOT solve them — the only high-success state result (ResiP, 98% on one_leg) is residual RL on top of a BC policy trained from 50 demos, so these are not drop-in teacher tasks for a distillation pipeline. The MIT-licensed URDF+OBJ part meshes are genuinely useful for building insert/screw/align primitives your suite lacks, and the same lab's MuJoCo-native predecessor (clvrai/furniture, 60+ MJCF furniture models, MIT) is arguably the better asset source for mjlab. Not a task-suite pick; mine it for parts.

Full details
SimulatorFurnitureSim = Isaac Gym preview + NVIDIA Factory-style SDF collision (needed for tight peg-in-hole and screwing). No MuJoCo support in this repo. NOTE: the predecessor "IKEA Furniture Assembly Environment" (github.com/clvrai/furniture, ICRA'21) is MuJoCo 2.0/robosuite-based with 60+ furniture models in MJCF — a separate, older codebase.
# Tasks9 furniture IDs: lamp, square_table, desk, drawer, cabinet, round_table, stool, chair, one_leg (one_leg = attach a single square_table leg, the standard easy subtask). So effectively 8 full assemblies + 1 canonical subtask; paper decomposes them into ~30-60 skill phases but those are annotations, not packaged environments — you would have to build subtask envs (init states + per-phase success checks) yourself.
Example tasksone_leg (insert+screw one leg into square table top), square_table (attach 4 legs: pick, align, insert, screw x4), lamp (base + bulb screw + shade), drawer (box + sliding drawer), cabinet, desk, round_table, stool, chair.
Primitivespick/grasp, reorient/align, insert (peg-in-hole, tight tolerance), screw (repeated gripper rotation), push/slide parts on table. Insert and screw are the primitives your current 5-task suite lacks.
RobotsFranka Emika Panda only (matches your mjlab setup).
State observationsYes, privileged state exists: FurnitureSimState-v0 and FurnitureSimFull-v0 expose robot_state (ee_pos, ee_quat, ee_pos_vel, ee_ori_vel, gripper_width; ~14D) plus parts_poses = concatenated 7D pose (pos+quat) per furniture part (e.g. square_table has 5 parts -> 35D; dims vary per furniture). Action: 8D delta-EE pose + gripper. Vision-free solving demonstrated only partially: ResiP reports 98% on one_leg with a state-based BC+residual-RL policy (50 demos); TRANSIC also uses state in Isaac Gym re-implementations. Full 8-part assemblies from pure state RL: no published success. On the real benchmark, AprilTag part poses are used for reproducibility, not as agent obs.
Action space8D: 3D EE delta position + 4D EE delta rotation (quaternion) + 1D gripper, normalized to [-1,1], 10Hz control on a Franka Panda (Osc/diffik controllers in sim).
Assetsfurniture_bench/assets/furniture: URDF part descriptions + OBJ meshes (parts are simple 3D-printable rigid shapes, also released as CAD for printing); franka_description_ros URDFs; MIT license, so freely reusable. URDF+OBJ converts to MJCF easily (mujoco compiles URDF directly, or obj2mjcf), BUT sim relies on SDF collision for the tight insert/screw fits — in MuJoCo you'd use convex decomposition (coacd) or MuJoCo's native SDF plugin and likely loosen hole tolerances. Predecessor repo clvrai/furniture already has 60+ furniture models as MuJoCo MJCF (MIT).
LicenseMIT (both furniture-bench and clvrai/furniture).
CL track recordEssentially none for continual learning. Searches for CL/lifelong papers using FurnitureBench come up empty; CL manipulation papers standardize on Continual World/Meta-World and LIBERO instead. FurnitureBench's citation trail is imitation learning + residual RL (JUICER, ResiP/From-Imitation-to-Refinement, TRANSIC, diffusion-policy works). You would be the first to use it for CL — which also means no CL baselines exist on it.
Port to mjlabConcrete path: (1) convert URDF+OBJ parts to MJCF (mujoco URDF import / obj2mjcf + coacd convex decomposition) — days; (2) rebuild per-subtask envs in mjlab (grasp-leg, align, insert, screw as separate tasks with dense rewards and relaxed tolerances) — the real work, since no subtask envs ship; (3) tight insert/screw contact fidelity is the risk: Factory SDF contacts have no cheap mujoco-warp equivalent (MuJoCo SDF plugin exists but not in mjx/warp), so expect to widen holes ~1-2mm and approximate screwing (e.g. weld-on-alignment or hinge+equality constraint). Realistic estimate: 2-4 weeks for a first batch of 5-10 insert/align subtasks; full assemblies with faithful screwing are out of reach in mjlab. Alternative: lift MJCF models straight from clvrai/furniture (already MuJoCo) and modernize them.
Scale potentialFrom furniture-bench proper: ~15-30 hand-built subtask envs (per-part grasp/align/insert, per-leg screw variants across 8 furniture models) — but each is manual env-engineering, not extraction. Adding the predecessor's 60+ MuJoCo furniture models could template-generate more insert/attach tasks. Realistic near-term yield of state-solvable RL tasks: ~10-20 insertion/alignment tasks with loosened tolerances; screwing tasks are questionable in mjx/warp. Not a 50-100-task engine.
Gotchas(1) Prior sweep's "9 assembly tasks decomposable into 30-60 skill segments" is roughly right, but the segments are dataset annotations, not runnable envs. (2) The benchmark is BC-oriented: it ships 200+ hours of teleop demos; nobody has solved full assemblies with RL, state-based or otherwise — one_leg is the only well-solved task and it needed BC+residual RL. (3) FurnitureSim requires Isaac Gym Preview 4 (deprecated NVIDIA download) — even reproducing baselines is painful in 2026. (4) Screw physics depend on Factory SDF contacts; naive MuJoCo port will jam or tunnel. (5) TRANSIC (transic-robot/transic-envs) already re-implemented FurnitureBench subtasks in IsaacGym with state obs — useful reference for subtask decomposition even though its runtime is excluded. (6) parts_poses dims vary per furniture, complicating a shared multi-task state space.
Repo activity~234 stars, 76 commits, 11 open issues; maintenance is slow (RSS 2023 artifact, no releases). Predecessor clvrai/furniture: ~564 stars, effectively unmaintained.
Assets only

GAPartNet

paper · code

Part-centric re-annotation of PartNet-Mobility + AKB-48: 8,489 actionable part instances (handles, buttons, knobs, lids, doors, drawers, sliders) on 1,166 objects with per-part canonical pose labels — a task-generation asset library, not a task suite.

GAPartNet: Cross-Category Domain-Generalizable Object Perception and Manipulation via Generalizable and Actionable Parts (CVPR 2023 Highlight)

Verdict: Ships ZERO ready-made tasks and no MuJoCo code — the repo is a perception dataset (part segmentation/pose) plus IsaacGym heuristic interaction demos. But the part-level metadata (9 actionable part classes with canonical 7-DoF part poses + URDF joint parameters on 1,166 objects) is exactly the annotation layer needed to procedurally emit 'open drawer / press button / turn knob / lift lid' Franka tasks in mjlab. High-value raw material, meaningful engineering to turn into tasks (URDF→MJCF conversion, collision decomposition, reward templating). Non-commercial license and form-gated download are acceptable for research but note them.

Full details
SimulatorDataset (URDF assets usable in any sim). Official interaction demos use IsaacGym (reimplemented April 2024, in repo's manipulation/ folder and companion geng-haoran/GAPartNet_env). No MuJoCo code provided; underlying PartNet-Mobility URDFs load in MuJoCo after mesh processing.
# Tasks0 defined RL tasks. The manipulation/ folder contains heuristic (non-learned) part-pose-based demos: open drawer/articulated part (run_arti_open) and free arm control. Task potential comes from 8,489 annotated actionable parts, not from shipped tasks.
Example tasksAuto-generated (not shipped): 'pull slider-drawer of storage furniture 45841 to 80% extension', 'open hinge door of microwave to 60 deg', 'press slider button on remote/machine', 'turn hinge knob on oven', 'lift hinge lid of box', 'grasp round handle and pull'. Repo's own demo: run_arti_open (open drawer/door via handle in IsaacGym).
PrimitivesImplied by the 9 GAPart classes: pull/push drawer (slider drawer), open/close door (hinge door), press (slider button), turn (hinge knob), lift/open lid (hinge + slider lid), grasp handle (line/round fixed handle, hinge handle). Covers open, close, press, turn, pull, slide, lift — strong overlap with your existing 5-task set.
RobotsFranka (used in repo demos and real-world experiments; also KINOVA in real world). Object assets are robot-agnostic — drop into your existing mjlab Franka scenes.
State observationsNo env, so no fixed obs vector. In a generated MuJoCo task the privileged state is complete: robot proprioception + the articulated object's joint position/velocity (from URDF joints, e.g. drawer extension, door angle) + annotated part pose (7-DoF oriented bbox per actionable part in canonical frame). The paper itself shows tasks are solvable from part pose alone — their manipulation is a pose-based HEURISTIC (no vision policy needed at execution once pose is known), which is direct evidence of state-solvability. Reward shaping is trivial: target joint value + gripper-to-handle distance.
Action spaceN/A (no shipped envs). Repo demos drive a Franka in IsaacGym via pose-based heuristics; in mjlab you define your own (joint-space or Cartesian, matching your current 5 tasks).
AssetsURDF articulated objects (from PartNet-Mobility, OBJ meshes, per-link visual+collision) + AKB-48 real-scanned objects; GAPartNet adds uniform JSON annotations: per-part semantic class (9 classes), canonical 7-DoF part pose / oriented bbox, actionability. Also pre-rendered point clouds (not needed for state-based RL). Reusable after URDF→MJCF conversion with convex decomposition.
LicenseCC BY-NC 4.0 (non-commercial), dataset access gated behind a Google Form + terms acceptance and citation requirement. Underlying PartNet-Mobility also requires SAPIEN account/terms.
CL track recordNone found. No continual-learning paper uses GAPartNet as a task suite (searches surface Continual World/Meta-World and navigation benchmarks instead). Using it for CL task generation would be novel.
Port to mjlabConcrete path: (1) obtain dataset via Google Form (or pull raw PartNet-Mobility from SAPIEN downloads and use GAPartNet's annotation JSONs for part/handle labels); (2) convert each URDF to MJCF — MuJoCo's URDF importer plus obj2mjcf/CoACD convex decomposition of the OBJ meshes (per-link multi-mesh, non-convex handles need decomposition for graspability); (3) auto-generate mjlab manager-based env per actionable part: spawn object on/near table, reward = joint-progress + reach-handle terms from annotation. Effort: ~1-2 weeks for a robust conversion+generation pipeline, then per-object QA (mass/damping/contact tuning, culling broken meshes) at maybe 10-30 min/object for the curated subset. mujoco-warp/MJX caveat: many convex hulls per object raise contact costs — cap decomposition pieces and simplify collision geometry.
Scale potentialVery high in principle (8,489 parts), realistically 20-100 solid tasks: pick ~5-15 clean instances each of drawer-open, door-open, button-press, knob-turn, lid-open, handle-pull across distinct object categories (StorageFurniture, Microwave, Oven, Box, Dishwasher, Table, etc.). Instance variation within a part class can also serve as task variants. Comfortably clears your 10-20 goal and can stretch toward 50+ with QA effort.
Gotchas(1) Form-gated download and CC BY-NC license — plan access early, no commercial use. (2) It is a perception dataset: all task/reward/scene logic is on you; the 'hundreds of tasks' exist only after you build a generation pipeline. (3) PartNet-Mobility meshes are non-convex and sometimes broken — convex decomposition and per-object physics QA are the real cost, and heavy decompositions hurt MJX/mujoco-warp throughput. (4) IsaacGym demo code is excluded per your requirements; only annotations+assets carry over. (5) AKB-48-sourced objects may be harder to obtain/convert than the PartNet-Mobility subset — the PartNet-Mobility subset alone is likely sufficient. (6) Repo dormant since Oct 2024 — expect no upstream support.
Repo activity161 stars, ~last push Oct 2024 (effectively dormant since late 2024). Companion env repo has 3 stars. Modest but the paper is well-cited (CVPR 2023 Highlight) and the dataset is widely reused in articulated-object manipulation literature.
Assets only

Google Scanned Objects + mujoco_scanned_objects (kevinzakka)

paper · code

1,030 photogrammetry-scanned household objects from Google Research, pre-converted to MuJoCo MJCF (visual OBJ + 32 V-HACD convex collision submeshes + PNG texture per object) by kevinzakka via obj2mjcf.

Google Scanned Objects: A High-Quality Dataset of 3D Scanned Household Items (ICRA 2022)

Verdict: It is purely an object-asset library: zero tasks, zero robots, zero reward/env code. But as an asset library it is exactly what the mjlab setup needs for scaling object-diversity tasks — 1,030 ready-made MJCF rigid objects that drop into MuJoCo/MJX/mujoco-warp with no conversion work. It cannot by itself take you from 5 to 20+ *distinct* task types (all objects afford the same pick/push/place/stack primitives; no articulation), so it complements, not replaces, an articulated-fixture source (PartNet-Mobility, RoboCasa fixtures, Menagerie cabinets).

Full details
SimulatorNone (asset dataset). Original GSO release preprocessed for Ignition Gazebo and Bullet; the kevinzakka repo targets MuJoCo directly — each object is a standalone model.xml loadable in mujoco simulate, MJX, and mujoco-warp.
# Tasks0 (confirmed — the repo contains only per-object directories with model.obj, model_collision_*.obj, texture.png, model.xml; no environments, robots, rewards, or task definitions)
Example tasksNone shipped. Example generated instances: "lift the 3D-scanned mug", "push the shoe into the goal region", "stack the bowl on the plate", "place the toy in the bin", "reorient the bottle upright" — each parameterized by any of ~1,030 objects.
PrimitivesNone defined. Objects afford: pick, place, push, slide, stack, lift, reorient/rotate-in-place, bin-sort, shelf-place. NOT afforded: open/close, turn, press, insert-with-tolerance (all objects are single rigid bodies; no articulated or multi-part objects, confirmed in repo and paper).
RobotsNone included. You supply the robot (your existing mjlab Franka).
State observationsN/A at the dataset level — you write the envs, so you control the obs. Every task you generate with these objects is trivially state-solvable in mjlab: object 6D pose + velocity come free from mjData (each object is a single free-joint rigid body), plus your existing Franka proprioception. No vision needed anywhere; the photorealistic textures are irrelevant to state-based training (they exist for perception research). This is the strongest property for your use case: 100% of generated tasks are vision-free by construction.
Action spaceN/A (defined by whatever robot/env you attach; your existing mjlab Cartesian/joint action spaces apply unchanged)
AssetsPer object: model.obj (visual mesh), 32 model_collision_*.obj convex submeshes (V-HACD decomposition — NOT CoACD as the prior sweep claimed), texture.png, model.xml (MJCF). Fully self-contained, no external deps. Directly reusable in any MuJoCo-family sim; also usable as raw OBJ in anything else. Early missing-MTL issue was fixed (closed issue #2).
LicenseTwo-tier: 3D geometry + textures CC-BY 4.0 (attribution: cite the GSO ICRA 2022 paper); the MJCF XML files MIT. Both permissive, research-safe. Repo is unaffiliated with Google (community conversion).
CL track recordNot used as a continual-learning benchmark itself (it defines no tasks). GSO objects are widely used as object pools in manipulation-generalization work (25+ papers per Google's blog; e.g. robosuite grasping benchmarks with 24 GSO objects, sim2real perception datasets like Objaverse/GSO mixes). No CL-via-distillation paper builds on this specific MJCF port that I could find. So: proven object diversity source, unproven as a CL task suite — you would be defining the CL protocol yourself.
Port to mjlabNear-zero port: each model.xml drag-and-drops into MuJoCo simulate today; for mjlab you write a small loader that includes/attaches the object MJCF into your scene with a free joint (same pattern as your current lift-cube object). Real effort is per-task env authoring, not asset conversion. Two engineering caveats for GPU scale: (1) 32 convex collision meshes per object is heavy for mujoco-warp with thousands of parallel envs — expect to re-decompose to 4-8 hulls (CoACD or V-HACD with looser params) or use primitive approximations for the bulk of objects; a one-day batch script. (2) obj2mjcf infers inertia from mesh geometry with default density — spot-check masses for very light/heavy objects. Estimated: 1-2 days to a working "spawn any GSO object in mjlab" utility, plus per-task-template work.
Scale potentialHigh along the object axis, capped along the skill axis. Realistic extraction: 5-10 task TEMPLATES (pick-lift, pick-place-to-target, push-to-region, slide-to-edge, stack-A-on-B, bin-place, shelf-place, reorient-upright, object-in-receptacle) x 1,030 objects = hundreds-to-thousands of task instances, all state-solvable. If your CL protocol counts object-variation as distinct tasks (e.g. "pick shoe", "pick mug", "stack bowl on plate"), 50-100 tasks is easy. If you need mechanistically distinct skills (open/turn/press/insert), this contributes ~nothing beyond your existing 5 — pair with PartNet-Mobility or RoboCasa fixtures as the prior sweep suggested. Practical curation note: objects skew heavily toward shoes (~250) and toys; you would hand-pick a graspable, tabletop-scale subset (realistically 200-400 usable objects).
Gotchas(1) Prior sweep error: collision decomposition is V-HACD via obj2mjcf, not CoACD. (2) 32 collision hulls/object will hurt mujoco-warp throughput at high env counts — plan a re-decomposition pass. (3) Inertial properties are geometry-derived defaults, not measured — sanity-check masses. (4) No articulated/multi-part objects at all, so no open/close/turn/press tasks from this source. (5) Heavy category skew (shoes, toys); curation needed. (6) Repo unmaintained since 2022 (acceptable for static assets). (7) Dataset download of full repo is large (~multi-GB, 1,030 dirs with meshes+textures). (8) An old issue reported textures not rendering (missing MTL) — fixed, but verify visuals if you ever do rendering.
Repo activity~283 stars, 21 forks, 1 release (v0.0.1, July 2022). Essentially frozen since 2022 — which is fine for a static asset dump; nothing needs maintaining. 4 open issues, none blocking (dm_control integration question, deformable-tets request, catalog-organization request).
Assets only

gym-aloha (ALOHA sim / ACT sim envs)

paper · code

HuggingFace/LeRobot gym wrapper around the ACT (RSS 2023) MuJoCo bimanual ALOHA sim: 2 tasks, pixel-first wrapper, state accessible only via the underlying dm_control env.

Learning Fine-Grained Bimanual Manipulation with Low-Cost Hardware (ACT/ALOHA), RSS 2023; also ALOHA 2: An Enhanced Low-Cost Hardware for Bimanual Teleoperation (arXiv 2405.02292) for the menagerie model.

Verdict: Only 2 functional tasks (transfer cube, bimanual insertion) — nowhere near the 10-20+ needed, and no procedural generation. The gym-aloha wrapper's obs_type="state" literally raises NotImplementedError, so state-based use requires going to the underlying dm_control env (original ACT repo), which does expose qpos/qvel/object-pose env_state and scripted state-based policies prove state-solvability. The real value is the well-tuned bimanual ViperX/ALOHA MJCF cell — and even that is better obtained from the DeepMind ALOHA 2 model in mujoco_menagerie or mujoco_playground's MJX-native aloha envs (handover, single_peg_insertion), which are already MJX/state-based. Also note it's a bimanual ViperX embodiment, not Franka, so it wouldn't share obs/action spaces with the existing 5-task Franka suite.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo native (dm_control-based, classic MuJoCo, CPU). Not MJX in gym-aloha/ACT; however mujoco_playground provides MJX-native ALOHA envs (handover, single_peg_insertion) and mujoco_menagerie has the ALOHA 2 MJCF with sys-ID'd dynamics.
# Tasks2 functional (TransferCube, Insertion); 2 more registered names (end_effector_* variants) raise NotImplementedError. Original ACT repo: same 2 sim tasks. mujoco_playground adds 2 different ALOHA tasks (handover, single peg insertion). Ecosystem total ~4 distinct tasks.
Example tasksTransferCubeTask: right arm picks red cube from table, places it into left gripper mid-air (reward 4 = success). InsertionTask: left arm grasps socket, right arm grasps peg, mid-air insertion until peg contacts pins inside socket.
Primitivespick, handover (bimanual transfer), insert (bimanual peg-in-socket). That's it.
RobotsBimanual ViperX 300 (ALOHA cell). NOT Franka — different embodiment/obs/action dims from the user's existing 5-task Franka suite.
State observationsgym-aloha wrapper: obs_type in {"state" (NotImplementedError!), "pixels" (default), "pixels_agent_pos" (image + 14-dim joint pos)}. Underlying dm_control env (tonyzhaozh/act sim_env.py) returns qpos (14: 2x6 arm joints + 2 grippers), qvel (14), and env_state = object poses: 7-dim cube pose (TransferCube) or 14-dim peg+socket poses (Insertion). Vision-free solvability is demonstrated: ACT's scripted demo policies compute trajectories directly from ground-truth object poses (that is how all sim demos are generated). So tasks ARE state-solvable, just not through the gym-aloha wrapper as shipped.
Action space14-dim continuous: absolute joint-position targets for 2x 6-DOF ViperX arms + 2 normalized gripper commands.
AssetsPlain MJCF XMLs (bimanual_viperx_transfer_cube.xml, bimanual_viperx_insertion.xml) + STL meshes, vendored in the repo — directly loadable in MuJoCo, trivially reusable. Better-quality alternative: ALOHA 2 MJCF in mujoco_menagerie (sys-ID'd, D405 cameras) and mujoco_playground aloha xmls (MJX-tuned).
Licensegym-aloha: Apache-2.0. tonyzhaozh/act: MIT. mujoco_menagerie ALOHA 2 / mujoco_playground: Apache-2.0. All permissive.
CL track recordNo continual-learning usage found. gym-aloha is used as an imitation-learning eval in LeRobot (ACT, Diffusion Policy, VLA papers). No CL-via-distillation papers use it as a task sequence — unsurprising given only 2 tasks.
Port to mjlabIf wanted anyway: low-moderate. Assets are plain MJCF so loading in MuJoCo is trivial; but for mjlab/GPU-parallel training, skip gym-aloha entirely and start from mujoco_playground's MJX aloha envs (base.py + handover/single_peg_insertion already state-observed, MJX-tuned contacts) — porting those two tasks into mjlab's manager API is days, not weeks. Porting the ACT scenes to MJX yourself would need contact/mesh simplification work that Playground already did. Main cost is not porting but embodiment: a new 14-dim bimanual action space alongside the Franka tasks.
Scale potentialVery low as-is: 2 tasks (4 across the ecosystem). No task generator, no object library. Scaling means hand-authoring new bimanual tasks on the ALOHA 2 scene — the same manual effort the user already spends per Franka task, so it does not solve the scaling problem. Realistic yield toward a 20+ task suite: ~2-4 tasks, all requiring a second embodiment.
Gotchas(1) The prior-sweep claim "solvable from state" is misleading for the wrapper: gym-aloha obs_type="state" raises NotImplementedError — you must use the underlying dm_control env or re-expose env_state yourself. (2) Sparse staged reward (0-4), success = reward==4; RL from state is not demonstrated (only IL from scripted/human demos); scripted policies are open-loop-ish and insertion succeeds only ~50% even for demos. (3) Known issue (#4) that cube-transfer success registration is questionable. (4) end_effector_* task variants are stubs. (5) Bimanual ViperX embodiment breaks obs/action homogeneity with the existing Franka suite, complicating a single distilled policy.
Repo activitygym-aloha: ~215 stars, 54 forks; maintained by HuggingFace as a LeRobot benchmark dependency (low churn, small scope). act repo is research code, effectively frozen.
Assets only

HumanoidBench

paper · code

MuJoCo-native whole-body humanoid benchmark (Unitree H1 + Shadow-derived dexterous hands): 15 base manipulation tasks that even privileged-state SOTA RL mostly cannot solve flat.

HumanoidBench: Simulated Humanoid Benchmark for Whole-Body Locomotion and Manipulation (RSS 2024)

Verdict: Tasks are whole-body humanoid control with dexterous hands (up to 61-DoF action space); the paper's own result is that SOTA RL (DreamerV3 10M steps, TD-MPC2, SAC, PPO) fails to reach success thresholds on nearly all manipulation tasks EVEN from privileged state — only hierarchical policies with pretrained low-level skills partially succeed. Producing teacher policies for distillation would be prohibitively expensive/uncertain, defeating our pipeline. However, the scene assets (cabinet, door, kitchen, bookshelf, window, truck/packages, cube, spoon) are clean MJCF under MIT/BSD/Apache and can be re-rigged for Franka tasks in mjlab with modest effort.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (native MJCF; standard mujoco Python, dm-control-style envs with Gymnasium API). README mentions JAX/MJX-accelerated training variants, but the benchmark is standard-CPU-MuJoCo first, not mujoco-warp.
# Tasks27 base tasks = 12 locomotion + 15 manipulation; 31 registered envs with variants (bookshelf simple/hard, insert normal/small, sit/balance simple/hard). Prior sweep's "17 manipulation / 31" counts variant envs. Same 31 tasks also available for Unitree G1 with three-finger hands, and 20 tasks for hand-less H1.
Example tasksh1hand-push-v0, h1hand-cabinet-v0, h1hand-door-v0, h1hand-truck-v0 (unload packages), h1hand-cube-v0 (bimanual cube rotate), h1hand-bookshelf_simple/hard-v0, h1hand-basketball-v0, h1hand-window-v0 (wipe), h1hand-spoon-v0, h1hand-kitchen-v0, h1hand-package-v0, h1hand-powerlift-v0, h1hand-room-v0 (tidy), h1hand-insert_normal/small-v0.
Primitivespush, pick/carry (package, powerlift), open (cabinet, door), place/rearrange (bookshelf, room, truck unload), insert (peg, two sizes), wipe (window), scoop/stir (spoon), throw (basketball), in-hand/bimanual cube rotation, kitchen multi-step (open microwave, move kettle, switches) — all whole-body, not tabletop primitives.
RobotsUnitree H1 with dexterous (Shadow-derived) hands (primary, 31 envs), H1 hand-less (20 envs), H1 simple-hand, Unitree G1 with three-finger hands (31 envs), plus Digit model files. No tabletop arms — no Franka.
State observationsPrivileged state is the DEFAULT observation: full robot qpos/qvel plus environment/object state (object poses etc.); vision (image) and tactile are optional via obs_wrapper=True. Dims vary per task and are large with dexterous hands (H1+two Shadow-style hands, ~151-dim proprio scale; hand-less H1 ~51). Vision-free operation is standard — but "solvable from state" is NOT demonstrated for most manipulation tasks: baselines fall below success thresholds on almost all of them.
Action spaceJoint position targets; up to ~61 actuators for H1 + two dexterous hands (19-DoF body + hands), ~19 for hand-less H1. High-dimensional action space is a stated reason baselines fail.
AssetsAll MJCF + meshes, MuJoCo-native. Robot models: Unitree H1/G1 (BSD-3), Shadow Hand (Apache-2.0), Robotiq 2F-85 (BSD-2), Digit (MIT). Scene objects (cabinet, door, kitchen, bookshelf, window, packages, cube, spoon, barbell) authored in-repo under MIT; robosuite textures MIT. Directly reusable in mjlab scenes.
LicenseMIT (main repo, Robot Learning Lab @ UC Berkeley 2024); composite file with BSD-3 (Unitree models), Apache-2.0 (Shadow Hand, purejaxrl), BSD-2 (Robotiq), MIT (others). GitHub API reports NOASSERTION only because of the composite file.
CL track recordNo continual-learning papers found using HumanoidBench. CL policy-distillation literature (e.g. arXiv:2601.22475 "Continual Policy Distillation from Distributed RL Teachers", arXiv:2404.04219) uses Meta-World or custom setups instead. HumanoidBench follow-ups are single-task RL/MPC (MuJoCo MPC eval arXiv:2408.00342, TD-M(PC)^2, TDMPBC, SimBa).
Port to mjlabThe robot/tasks are already MuJoCo MJCF, so no simulator port is needed for the benchmark itself — but porting to mjlab as-is is pointless given unsolvable teacher training. Realistic path: extract scene MJCFs (cabinet, door, bookshelf, kitchen appliances, window, packages, cube, spoon) and mount them in our Franka mjlab scenes; each task then needs new reward/termination/randomization code. Estimate ~0.5-1 day per re-rigged Franka task after a 1-2 day asset extraction pass; hinge/slide articulated objects (cabinet, door, kitchen) are the highest-value pulls.
Scale potentialAs a humanoid benchmark: ~15-17 manipulation tasks, but mostly not teacher-solvable. As an asset mine for Franka tasks: roughly 8-12 additional tasks (open/close cabinet drawers+doors, open door variant, bookshelf place, window wipe/slide, kitchen microwave+kettle+switch subtasks, package pick-place, peg insert x2, cube manipulation) — useful but not a route to 50-100 tasks.
Gotchas(1) Headline paper finding: flat SOTA RL fails most tasks from privileged state — teacher policies for distillation are not obtainable at reasonable cost; hierarchical pretraining (walk/reach low-level skills) is required even for push. (2) Prior sweep's task count (17/31) counts variant envs; base is 15 manipulation of 27. (3) Standard MuJoCo (CPU) envs — not mujoco-warp; GPU-parallel teacher training would need a rewrite. (4) GitHub license shows "Other/NOASSERTION" but is actually MIT + permissive component licenses. (5) Whole-body humanoid embodiment mismatches our Franka mjlab stack entirely; only scene assets transfer.
Repo activity777 stars, 125 forks, 26 open issues; last push September 2025 (verified via GitHub API) — maintained but slowing.
Merged duplicate assessments (1)
  • [reject] Wrong embodiment and wrong difficulty regime for a CL-source task set: every task is a whole-body Unitree H1 humanoid (61-dim action, two Shadow Hands) task, not a Franka-arm primitive, so nothing transfers to the mjlab Franka pipeline without redesigning each task from scratch. Worse, the benchmark is intentionally hard — the paper's own result is that DreamerV3/TD-MPC2/SAC/PPO fail the success threshold on most tasks even from privileged state — so per-task teacher-policy cost is prohibitive for distillation. No CL track record. Even its scene assets (truck, kitchen, bookshelf) are humanoid-scale and of marginal use for tabletop Franka tasks.
Assets only

IKEA Furniture Assembly Environment (clvrai/furniture)

paper · code

Legacy MuJoCo furniture-assembly benchmark whose real value today is its 61 MIT-licensed MJCF+STL furniture models with connector-site/weld machinery for generating part-attachment subtasks.

IKEA Furniture Assembly Environment for Long-Horizon Complex Manipulation Tasks (ICRA 2021)

Verdict: The env code is dead legacy (MuJoCo 2.0 / mujoco-py / Python 3.7 / robosuite-fork / Unity renderer, last push 2023-03) and full assemblies are extremely hard (only 2 of 61 models ever solved, needing 200 demos + ~200M steps each). But the 61 MIT-licensed furniture models are native MJCF+STL with explicit connector sites and weld-based attachment — an excellent, trivially-portable asset library for GENERATING 20-60 two-part align/insert/attach subtasks in mjlab. State-solvability of subtasks is proven (T-STAR solved assemblies from a 72-D pure state obs).

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo 2.0 via mujoco-py (physics), built on an old robosuite fork; optional Unity3D renderer. Legacy stack: Python 3.7 pinned, unmaintained since ~2023. Physics XMLs are plain MJCF though, so assets load in modern MuJoCo with minor fixes.
# Tasks61 furniture models (verified); each decomposes into ~1-7 part-pair connect subtasks → ~100-200 potential subtasks.
Example taskstable_lack_0825 (4 legs into tabletop corners), chair_ingolf_0650 (supports + front legs to seat, seat to back), chair_bernhard_0146 (2 legs + seat), swivel_chair_0700 (base/pole/seat), bookcase_billy_0190, cabinet_akurum_0011, desk_mikael_1064, tvunit_lack_0829, bench_bjursta_0210, bed_dalselv_0270, toy_table (simplified 4-leg table used in follow-up papers).
Primitivespick, lift, move/transport, align (6-DoF orientation matching), insert, connect/attach (weld activation when connector sites align within pos+rot threshold), plus screwing motion for some connectors. No push/open/press — purely assembly-centric.
RobotsSawyer, Baxter, Panda, Jaco, Fetch, plus a floating 'Cursor' agent. Franka Panda is nominally supported but published results use Sawyer; for mjlab we would use our own Franka scene and only take the objects.
State observationsConfigurable obs includes agent joint pos/vel + poses (3D pos + quat) of all furniture parts; ground-truth part poses provided. Vision-free solving DEMONSTRATED: T-STAR (arXiv:2111.07999, CoRL 2021) used a 72-D state obs (29-D robot + 35-D object pos/quat for 5 parts + 8-D phase) with Sawyer joint-velocity control, achieving 87% on table_lack and 56% on chair_ingolf — but required 200 demos/task + ~200M steps. Baselines (plain GAIL/PPO policy sequencing) got 59%/0%, i.e. subtask skills are state-learnable but full chains are hard.
Action spaceConfigurable: joint torque/velocity control or task-space (IK) continuous control for arm robots; discrete/continuous movement primitives + part-selection + 'connect' primitive action; Cursor agent has abstract 3-axis move+rotate (no grasping physics).
AssetsMJCF XML per furniture model + STL meshes per part (modeled from real IKEA manuals in Rhino3D); primitive-geom colliders for contact; connector sites named 'PartA-PartB,conn_site' encode attachment pairs; weld equality constraints implement connection. Directly MuJoCo-native and highly reusable for task generation.
LicenseMIT (verified via GitHub API).
CL track recordNo continual-learning usage found. Used for long-horizon RL/IL and skill chaining: T-STAR (Lee et al., CoRL 2021, arXiv:2111.07999) is the flagship result; also IL baselines (BC/GAIL) in the original paper. Not a CL benchmark.
Port to mjlabDo NOT port the env — extract assets. Path: (1) copy per-furniture MJCF + STLs (MIT) into mjlab Franka scene; (2) parse 'PartA-PartB,conn_site' site pairs to auto-generate two-part attach tasks (spawn part A in gripper-reachable pose, part B fixed or free); (3) reimplement the connect logic as a weld equality activated when site pos/rot error < threshold — MuJoCo 3.x supports runtime eq_active toggling, but verify per-env eq_active toggling works in MJX/mujoco-warp (a real gotcha for GPU batching; fallback: distance+orientation success criterion without actual welding); (4) write dense reward (reach/grasp/align stages) like our existing tasks. Estimate: ~1 week for the asset pipeline + first 5 tasks, then ~half-day per additional task family. Old MJCF may need minor syntax updates for MuJoCo 3.x (compiler/actuator defaults).
Scale potentialHigh as a generator: 61 models x 1-7 connector pairs ≈ 100-200 candidate two-part align/insert subtasks; realistically 20-60 curated state-solvable single-primitive tasks (pick-and-align leg-to-tabletop, pole-to-base insertion, shelf-to-frame slide) after filtering for reachability and gripper-graspable part sizes. Full multi-part assemblies are NOT realistic distillation targets (200M steps + demos for 2 tasks in the literature). Diversity caveat: nearly all tasks are variants of pick-align-insert, so they add depth in one primitive family rather than breadth across primitives.
Gotchas(1) Repo is unmaintained legacy (mujoco-py 2.0, Py3.7, Unity) — treat as an asset dump, never as a runtime dependency. (2) Paper's '80+' claim is inflated; 61 real models in repo. (3) Parts are IKEA-scale — many are too large/flat for a Franka gripper; original robots often needed the abstract Cursor agent or two-armed Baxter; expect to rescale meshes. (4) The 'connect' action is a magic weld snap, not physical screwing — decide success criterion carefully for GPU sim (runtime eq_active toggling in MJX/warp needs verification). (5) Insertion sub-skills were the failure mode in follow-up work (grasp/place learnable, precise insert often fails) — expect these to be the hard tail of the curriculum. (6) Primitive-geom colliders are coarse; fine insertion tolerances may need collider rework.
Repo activity564 stars, 63 forks; last push 2023-03-03 — unmaintained for ~3.5 years. Successor FurnitureBench/FurnitureSim moved to Isaac Gym (excluded), confirming this MuJoCo version is abandoned.
Assets only

KitchenShift (Franka Kitchen distribution-shift fork)

paper · code

Dead 19-star fork of adept_envs Franka Kitchen adding 7 XML-worldgen domain shifts for testing image-based imitation robustness; useful only as a source of ~7-9 portable kitchen-object assets/tasks.

KitchenShift: Evaluating Zero-Shot Generalization of Imitation-Based Policy Learning Under Domain Shifts (Xing, Gupta, Powers, Dean — NeurIPS 2021 Workshop on Distribution Shifts, not a main-conference paper)

Verdict: Not a task-scaling benchmark: it is a NeurIPS-2021 *workshop* zero-shot-generalization protocol that evaluates IMAGE-based imitation on only 3 Franka Kitchen tasks under 7 domain shifts (texture, lighting, camera, object instance/layout/state, robot state) — most shifts are visual and irrelevant to state-based CL. The underlying Franka Kitchen scene yields only ~7-9 primitive tasks, several overlapping tasks we already have (open door/drawer). The Apache-2.0 MuJoCo XML+STL kitchen assets (microwave, kettle, 4 stove knobs, light switch, slide + hinge cabinets, plus alternate microwave/kettle instances) are the only useful takeaway, and those are equally available from Gymnasium-Robotics/D4RL.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo via dm_control bindings (2021-era, pre-official-bindings). Plain MJCF; scene XML uses cone="elliptic" impratio=2 and noslip_iterations=20 (noslip unsupported in MJX/MuJoCo-Warp — must be dropped, may affect kettle grasping).
# Tasks~9 element-goal tasks in code (constants.py OBS_ELEMENT_GOALS): microwave, kettle, slide cabinet, hinge cabinet, light switch, 4 burner knobs. Paper evaluates only 3 tasks x 7 shift factors. Not 3x7=21 tasks — shifts are eval perturbations, not tasks.
Example tasksopen microwave door to -0.75 rad; move kettle to target burner position [-0.23, 0.75, 1.62]; rotate bottom-left burner knob to -0.88; flip light switch to [-0.69, -0.05]; slide cabinet door to 0.37; open hinge cabinet to 1.45 rad.
Primitivesopen (microwave/hinge cabinet), slide (cabinet door), rotate (4 stove knobs), press/flip (light switch), pick-and-place (kettle onto burner). No insert, no stack, no dedicated push.
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (primary); xArm7 assets also included (change_robot worldgen shift).
State observationsYes, fully state-solvable: 30-dim qpos-based obs = 9 robot joints (7 arm + 2 gripper) + 21 object DoF (knob/joint angles, kettle pose), plus optional ee pose (get_obs_ee) and ee force sensors; configurable observation noise. Image obs also supported but not required.
Action spaceConfigurable ctrl_mode: 'absvel'/'abspos' 9-dim joint control, or 'relmocapik'/'absmocapik' Cartesian mocap-IK (xyz + euler/quat + gripper). Franka Panda, 7-DoF + 2-finger gripper.
AssetsApache-2.0. MJCF XMLs (kitchen.xml, per-object asset XMLs: microwave, kettle, hingecabinet, slidecabinet, oven, counters, backwall, trashbin) + 66 STL meshes + textures. Borrowed from adept_envs, furniture_sim, sim2real2sim_rad. Fully portable; same assets exist in Gymnasium-Robotics/D4RL.
LicenseApache-2.0 (verified LICENSE file in repo).
CL track recordNone for KitchenShift itself (zero-shot robustness protocol, not CL; negligible citations/adoption). Underlying Franka Kitchen has strong track record in offline/multi-stage RL (D4RL, Relay Policy Learning) and occasional lifelong-skill works, but not as a per-task CL sequence benchmark.
Port to mjlabLow-moderate (~1-2 weeks for ~8 tasks): assets are self-contained XML+STL and directly loadable in modern MuJoCo, but the monolithic kitchen scene must be decomposed into per-task mjlab scenes, noslip_iterations removed and contact params retuned for Warp/MJX, and rewards/success predicates rewritten in the manager API (goals already given as joint-value targets in constants.py — easy).
Scale potentialLow: caps at ~9 base tasks, 3-4 of which duplicate our existing open-door/open-drawer/press-button primitives. Worldgen object variants (2 microwaves, alternate kettles from furniture_sim) give a few instance variations, not new primitives. Cannot get us to 20+, let alone 50-100.
Gotchas(1) Marketed shift factors are eval perturbations, not tasks — easy to overcount. (2) noslip_iterations and 2021 dm_control-era conventions need cleanup for MuJoCo-Warp. (3) Single monolithic scene: all objects share one XML, so per-task envs require scene surgery or accepting a shared heavy scene per env instance (costly for GPU-parallel envs). (4) Knob observation index ordering was fixed vs D4RL (constants.py flips indices) — trajectories/goals from D4RL are not drop-in. (5) Workshop paper only; OpenReview PDF is behind a bot-check.
Repo activity19 stars, 2 forks; initial release Aug 2021, v0.1 Dec 2021, effectively abandoned since (~5 years stale). Minimal community adoption.
Assets only

MimicLabs

paper · code

MuJoCo/robosuite-based BDDL task-description + MimicGen demo-generation pipeline from the ICLR 2025 "What Matters in Learning from Large-Scale Datasets" study; huge dataset variation but few distinct skills.

What Matters in Learning from Large-Scale Datasets for Robot Manipulation (ICLR 2025)

Verdict: The headline scale (3K "task instances", 850K demos, 384 BDDL files) is combinatorial variation in camera pose / spatial arrangement / scene — not distinct skills. Actual skill diversity is ~12 skill-object combos (bin/place 7 objects, open/close microwave, open/close drawer) + 5 multi-stage benchmark tasks, i.e. barely beyond your existing 5 tasks. It is an imitation-learning demo-collection pipeline (teleop + MimicGen) on CPU robosuite: no reward functions, no GPU-parallel envs, no RL support, no CL track record. Its value to you is the MJCF assets (6 cabinets, 7 microwaves, coffee machine, food objects, 7 lab scene XMLs) and the BDDL compositional task-spec idea, both under MIT — but you would write all mjlab task/reward code yourself anyway.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo via robosuite (CPU, classic MuJoCo — NOT MJX/mujoco-warp). Depends on robosuite, robomimic, MimicGen, LIBERO-style BDDL parsing, RoboCasa assets.
# Tasks5 benchmark target tasks (bin carrot, bin bowl, clear table, microwave teapot, make coffee); 384 BDDL config files in mimiclabs_study, but these are ~12 distinct skill-object combos crossed with camera-pose/spatial/receptacle variants (naming: {object}_{scene}+camPose*+objSpat*+recepSpat*.bddl). Paper counts "3K task instances" the same combinatorial way.
Example tasksbinCarrot0, binBagel4, binBowl0, binCorn0, binMug1, binMushroom0 (place object into bin/receptacle); openMicrowave2 / closeMicrowave2; openTopDrawerColorfulCabinet / closeTopDrawerColorfulCabinet; benchmark: bin carrot, bin bowl, clear table, microwave teapot, make coffee.
Primitivespick-and-place into receptacles/bins (dominant), open, close (microwave, drawer/cabinet), plus multi-stage compositions (clear table, make coffee). No insert/turn/press/slide/stack in the released configs (real-world demos show stack/pour/wipe but those are not sim tasks).
Robotsrobosuite robots (Panda etc. — standard robosuite single-arm table-top setup).
State observationsDemos are stored as raw MuJoCo sim states (scripts dataset_states_to_obs.py / add_obs_to_mimiclabs_datasets.py regenerate obs from states), and robosuite exposes standard low-dim obs (robot proprio + object poses). MimicGen itself synthesizes demos purely by pose-based trajectory transforms, so every task is by construction solvable from object poses + proprioception — no vision needed. However, exact low-dim obs dims are robosuite-standard, not documented per task, and the paper's learning experiments are vision-based (camera-pose diversity is a core study axis); no state-only policy results are demonstrated.
Action spacerobosuite OSC end-effector control (teleop via Quest VR / SpaceMouse); standard robosuite arm+gripper actions. Panda-compatible.
AssetsMJCF/XML throughout: 7 lab scene XMLs (lab2-lab8), 6 articulated cabinet variants, 7 microwave models, coffee machine, food/household objects (own assets + robosuite + LIBERO + RoboCasa sources). Downloaded via provided scripts; MuJoCo-native so directly loadable. Caveat: RoboCasa-sourced objects may carry upstream (partly AI-generated/Objaverse) license nuances even though the repo is MIT.
LicenseMIT (repo). Bundled third-party assets inherit robosuite (MIT), LIBERO (MIT), RoboCasa licenses.
CL track recordNone found. Repo is from July 2025; no continual/lifelong-learning paper uses MimicLabs. CL imitation papers (M2Distill, LOTUS, SPREAD) all use LIBERO, its upstream cousin, instead.
Port to mjlabAssets: low effort — scene XMLs, cabinets, microwaves, objects are MJCF and load in vanilla MuJoCo; convexify meshes and check RoboCasa-object mesh counts for MJX/warp throughput. Framework: high effort and low value — task logic lives in robosuite+BDDL classes (bddl_base_domain.py, ~54KB) with no rewards (success predicates only); you would reimplement each task as an mjlab manager env with your own rewards, as you did for your current 5 tasks. Realistic path: cherry-pick assets + BDDL success predicates as success-criteria templates; ~1-2 days per new task in mjlab.
Scale potentialAs distinct CL tasks: ~10-15 genuinely new tasks (bin/place x ~7 objects, open/close x microwave + several cabinet types, coffee-machine interaction, multi-stage clear-table) — several overlap your existing open-drawer/lift. The BDDL compositional spec (object x receptacle x scene) could mechanically emit 50+ configs, but they are variants of the same 2-3 primitives, so for skill-diversity-driven CL it does not get you to 20+ distinct skills. The asset library, however, supports authoring many new mjlab tasks yourself.
Gotchas(1) "850K trajectories / 3K tasks" is variation in camera pose and spatial layout — camera pose is meaningless for state-based policies, so effective diversity collapses. (2) No reward functions anywhere — pure IL pipeline (teleop + MimicGen); RL in mjlab needs rewards written from scratch. (3) robosuite CPU sim; nothing is MJX/warp-ready. (4) Heavy dependency chain (robosuite+robomimic+MimicGen+LIBERO+RoboCasa) if you tried to run it as-is. (5) RoboCasa-derived object assets may have upstream license/mesh-quality issues. (6) Paper's experiments are vision-based; state-only solvability is inferred (MimicGen is pose-based) but never demonstrated.
Repo activity43 stars, 5 forks, 0 open issues; created 2025-07-17, last push 2026-03-13 — young, low-traction, but maintained.
Assets only

MuJoCo Menagerie (robots + hands)

paper · code

DeepMind-curated collection of 70+ high-quality, simulation-ready MJCF robot models (incl. Franka Panda/FR3 and many grippers/hands) — pure asset library, no tasks.

n/a — no paper. Cite via repo CITATION (Zakka et al., "MuJoCo Menagerie: A collection of high-quality simulation models for MuJoCo", 2022) if needed.

Verdict: Confirmed: zero tasks/environments — it is purely a curated library of robot MJCF models (arms, grippers, hands, humanoids, quadrupeds). It contributes nothing to task-count scaling by itself, but it is the canonical, MuJoCo-native, permissively-licensed source of embodiments (Franka Panda, FR3, Robotiq 2F-85, Allegro/Shadow/Leap hands) and is already the de-facto asset source that mjlab's own docs point users to. Keep it as the embodiment layer under whatever task suite (e.g. Meta-World-style tasks, robosuite ports) you adopt; do not evaluate it as a benchmark.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo native (MJCF XML + STL/OBJ meshes); models are curated for out-of-the-box MuJoCo quality and most work with MJX/mujoco-warp (MuJoCo Playground and mjlab both build on Menagerie models).
# Tasks0 (verified against the repo: "no explicit task or RL environments — model collection only"). The prior sweep's tasks=0 claim is correct.
Example tasksNone — no tasks exist. Downstream suites that build tasks on Menagerie models include MuJoCo Playground (e.g. Panda pick/open-cabinet, which this project already uses) — those are the things to evaluate, not Menagerie.
PrimitivesNone provided. Enables authoring: in-hand reorientation, grasp, pick/place, turn/rotate (valve, pen), bimanual handover, catch/throw — i.e. Bi-DexHands/Adroit-style primitives — but all task logic must be written by us.
Robots70+ models: arms (Franka Emika Panda, Franka FR3, UR5e/UR10e, KUKA iiwa14, xArm7, Kinova Gen3, ...), end-effectors (Panda gripper, Robotiq 2F-85, Allegro V3, Shadow E3M5, Shadow DEX-EE, Leap Hand), mobile manipulators (Stretch, TIAGo, Google Robot), humanoids (Unitree H1/G1, TALOS, Apollo), quadrupeds (Go1/Go2, ANYmal B/C, Spot), plus drones/bipeds/biomechanical.
State observationsN/A at the library level — models expose full qpos/qvel/actuator state (Shadow Hand 24 DoF, Allegro 16, LEAP 16, DEX-EE 12), so any task we author is trivially state-observable (joint states + object poses). Precedent: Adroit, dm_control, and MuJoCo Playground hand tasks are all solved from low-level state with these same model classes. But no vision-free solving is "demonstrated" by this repo itself because there are no tasks.
Action spaceNot defined by the repo; each MJCF ships actuators (e.g. Panda: 7 arm joints + gripper tendon, position/torque actuators as modeled) that your env layer wraps. Panda listed as 9 DoF (7 arm + 2 finger), FR3 7 DoF.
AssetsMJCF XML + STL/OBJ meshes per model directory, each with its own README and LICENSE; deliberately curated for clean collision geometry and sane actuator/joint parameters. Directly loadable in MuJoCo/MJX/mujoco-warp with no conversion — maximal reusability for mjlab.
LicenseRepo-level Apache-2.0; per-model licenses vary (Apache-2.0, BSD-3-Clause, MIT, Apache-2.0-Clear) — check each model dir's LICENSE. Franka Panda/FR3 dirs are Apache-2.0 (originals from Franka's BSD/Apache descriptions). All permissive; no CC-BY-NC landmines observed.
CL track recordNot used as a continual-learning benchmark in any paper (it cannot be — no tasks). It appears ubiquitously as the asset source in RL papers (MuJoCo Playground, various loco/manip works), and the current project's mjlab/madrona_mjx stack already vendors it (local copy at /media/cvlab/EXTDRIVE/sudhir/continual_learning/madrona_mjx/external/mujoco_menagerie; mjlab/docs/faq.md explicitly recommends it).
Port to mjlabZero porting for models — MJCF loads directly into MuJoCo/MJX/mujoco-warp and mjlab (mjlab already consumes Menagerie-style assets). The real effort is task authoring: each new hand task in mjlab needs scene XML, reset randomization, reward, termination, and obs spec — realistically 0.5-2 days per task once a first hand-task template exists (the first one, e.g. LEAP or Shadow cube reorientation, is 3-5 days including tuning). Budget extra for MJX/warp contact tuning on 20+ DoF hands (contact count, solver params).
Scale potentialDirectly: 0 tasks. Indirectly: it diversifies embodiments, not tasks — e.g. running your existing 5 tasks across Panda + FR3 + UR5e+Robotiq would multiply task-instances (5 tasks x 3-4 arms = 15-20 embodiment-task pairs), which is a legitimate CL axis but not new skills. For genuine 20-100 task scaling you need an object/task library (e.g. Meta-World port, robosuite, Playground manipulation) layered on Menagerie robots.
Gotchas1) Per-model licenses differ from the top-level Apache-2.0 — audit each model dir. 2) Models are tuned for CPU MuJoCo; some (multi-finger hands, Robotiq's differential tendon) need contact/actuator simplification for stable large-batch MJX/warp rollout. 3) The prior sweep's description was accurate; the only trap is mistaking it for a benchmark — any "Menagerie tasks" you see cited are actually MuJoCo Playground or dm_control tasks. 4) There are no manipulable OBJECTS (no YCB-style prop library) — it covers robots only, so it solves neither the task nor the object side of scaling.
Repo activity~3.7k stars per the GitHub page (GitHub API rate-limited from this host, so exact count unverified), ~480 commits, active maintenance with recent contributions and continuous new-model additions; backed by Google DeepMind, effectively the community-standard MJCF model source.
Merged duplicate assessments (1)
  • [assets-only] It is exactly what the sweep said: a high-quality, actively maintained, MuJoCo-native model library with zero tasks. Verified 3.7k stars, Apache-2.0 top-level with per-model permissive licenses, MJCF format, and hand/gripper models (Shadow Hand E3M5, Allegro V3, LEAP, Shadow DEX-EE, Robotiq 2F-85, Panda gripper, etc.). Every task, reward, reset distribution, and observation spec would have to be authored by us in mjlab. That is the right long-term enabler for dexterous-hand scaling, but it contributes no ready-made state-solvable tasks toward the 10-20 task goal, so it cannot be a top-pick/strong/usable benchmark on its own. Note: the sweep's claim of "TriFinger-class platforms" is WRONG — no trifinger directory exists in Menagerie.
Assets only

Objaverse 1.0 / Objaverse-XL

paper · code

800K (1.0, Sketchfab CC) to 10M+ (XL) raw 3D meshes with a pip download API; the upstream raw-material source that RoboCasa and MolmoSpaces mined and converted for their MuJoCo object banks.

Objaverse-XL: A Universe of 10M+ 3D Objects (NeurIPS 2023 Datasets & Benchmarks); Objaverse 1.0: "Objaverse: A Universe of Annotated 3D Objects" (CVPR 2023)

Verdict: It is a raw 3D-mesh library, not a benchmark: zero tasks, zero robots, zero simulator integration, no rewards, no state definitions. Its only value to us is object diversity, and even that comes with heavy curation cost (watertighting, convex decomposition, scale/mass/orientation canonicalization, license filtering). RoboCasa and MolmoSpaces already did that curation on Objaverse subsets and ship MuJoCo-ready results — consume Objaverse through them, not directly. Direct use only makes sense if the curated banks (GSO ~1K, RoboCasa 2.5K+, MolmoSpaces) run out, which is implausible at our 10-100 task scale.

Full details
SimulatorNone. Raw meshes only (GLB/OBJ/FBX/STL etc., Blender-processable). No physics properties, no MJCF/URDF, no simulator bindings of any kind.
# Tasks0 — verified. The repo provides download/rendering scripts only; there are no task definitions anywhere in Objaverse or Objaverse-XL.
Example tasksNone exist. Downstream examples of what others built from it: RoboCasa's pick-and-place/insertion objects (2,500+ objects, 153 categories, partly Objaverse-sourced) and MolmoSpaces' MuJoCo-compatible household scenes.
PrimitivesNone defined. As a diversity reservoir it could supply objects for pick/place/push/stack/insert-style tasks, but every primitive would have to be authored by us in mjlab.
RobotsNone.
State observationsN/A — no environments exist, so no observation space. Any state obs (object pose, geometry features) would be whatever we define after importing a mesh into mjlab. Note many Objaverse meshes are decorative/non-graspable-scale scans, so per-object curation is needed before a state-solvable task can even be posed.
Action spaceN/A — no environments.
AssetsObjaverse 1.0: ~800K Sketchfab objects, each CC-licensed (mostly CC-BY variants; per-object license metadata included). Objaverse-XL: 10M+ objects from GitHub, Thingiverse, Sketchfab, Polycam, Smithsonian; formats GLB/OBJ/STL/FBX etc. Downloadable via pip API (objaverse-xl 0.1.7, Nov 2023). Highly reusable as raw geometry; NOT simulation-ready (no collision meshes, mass, articulation).
LicenseCode: Apache-2.0. Dataset as a whole: ODC-By v1.0; individual objects carry their own licenses (1.0 = Creative Commons via Sketchfab; XL includes GitHub objects with arbitrary repo licenses and Polycam data requiring separate approval). License filtering per object is required for redistribution.
CL track recordNo continual-learning usage as a benchmark (it has no tasks to sequence). Its primary research uses are 3D generative modeling (Zero123-XL, text-to-3D). Indirect CL relevance only via suites that mined it (e.g., RoboCasa), and I found no CL paper sequencing Objaverse-derived tasks directly.
Port to mjlabConcrete path exists and is proven (it is what RoboCasa/MolmoSpaces did): pip `objaverse`/`objaverse-xl` API to download GLBs -> Manifold for watertight meshes -> V-HACD or CoACD convex decomposition -> obj2mjcf/MJCF wrapping -> manual scale/mass/orientation/license curation. Per-object effort is small once a pipeline is scripted (~hours to build the pipeline), but curation quality is the real cost: expect high rejection rates (bad scale, non-watertight scans, multi-object scenes). Effort: days for a pipeline + curated set of ~50-200 usable objects; effectively already done for free if we instead take RoboCasa's 2,500 converted objects.
Scale potentialUnbounded in principle (10M+ meshes) but it yields OBJECT variation, not TASK variation — swapping the lifted/pushed object gives variants of the same primitive, not new skills. Realistically it could parameterize 20-100 object-variant tasks (lift/push/place with diverse objects), same as GSO/RoboCasa banks would, with none of their curation done for us.
Gotchas(1) Meshes are not sim-ready: no collision geometry, mass, friction, or articulation; scans are often non-watertight, wrongly scaled, or contain whole scenes. (2) License is per-object — XL includes GitHub-sourced objects of unclear licensing and Polycam data needing separate approval; stick to Objaverse 1.0 CC subset if redistribution matters. (3) Object diversity is not task diversity — it will not move us from 5 primitives toward 20 skills; it only widens within-primitive variation. (4) Quality is extremely uneven; curated subsets (Objaverse-LVIS, RoboCasa's picks) exist precisely because raw sampling is unusable. (5) Repo effectively unmaintained since late 2023, though downloads still function.
Repo activity~1.3k stars, 87 forks, ~60 open issues; last release 0.1.7 in Nov 2023 — maintenance is largely dormant but the download API still works and the dataset is hosted by AI2/HuggingFace.
Assets only

RGB-Stacking (google-deepmind/rgb_stacking)

paper · code

DeepMind's single stack-red-on-blue MuJoCo benchmark whose real asset is 152 graded-difficulty geometric object meshes, not task diversity.

Beyond Pick-and-Place: Tackling Robotic Stacking of Diverse Shapes (CoRL 2021)

Verdict: It is a single task (stack red-on-blue within 20s), not a task suite — the 5 "triplets" are object-geometry splits of the same stacking primitive, so it cannot scale our task count. It also runs on legacy MuJoCo 200 + Sawyer + dm_robotics MoMa, none of which we want. The genuinely valuable part is the 152 Apache-2.0 STL object meshes (procedurally graded grasping/stacking difficulty), which port trivially into mjlab as props to create shape-diverse variants of our existing Franka pick/stack/push tasks. State solvability is proven (paper's stage-1 experts trained from full state), but that doesn't rescue the task-count problem.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo 200 (legacy, pre-open-source license-key era) via dm_robotics MoMa/dm_control composer — NOT modern MuJoCo/MJX; would need full re-implementation in mjlab.
# Tasks1 task type (stack red on blue, green distractor). 8 environment variants that are only object-set splits: rgb_test_triplet1-5, rgb_test_random, rgb_train_random, rgb_heldout_random. 152 objects (103 train / 36 held-out / 13 test-triplet).
Example tasksrgb_test_triplet1..5 (stack red on blue for 5 fixed object triplets of graded difficulty), rgb_train_random (random triplet from 103 training objects), rgb_heldout_random. All are the same stacking objective with different geometry.
Primitivesstack (grasp + place); implicitly grasp/reorient of hard geometries. No push/open/close/insert/press/slide — single-primitive benchmark.
RobotsRethink Sawyer 7-DoF arm with parallel Robotiq-style gripper (not Franka); 4D Cartesian control.
State observationsYes, full privileged state confirmed in environment.py STATE_ONLY set: sawyer joints angle/velocity/torque, tcp+pinch pose/velocity, gripper grasp+joint state, wrist force/torque, previous action, and ground-truth abs_pose + to_pinch for all 3 objects. Paper trained state-based MPO experts from this (stage 1), so state-solvability is proven (~75%+ sim stacking success on specialists).
Action space4D Cartesian velocity controller: x, y, z translation + rotation about z, plus gripper — deliberately restricted 4-DoF end-effector control.
Assets152 STL meshes in dm_robotics py/manipulation/props/rgb_objects (Apache-2.0), plus Onshape CAD and parametric generation code; simple convex-ish geometric solids, MJX/Warp-friendly. dm_robotics repo archived April 2026 (read-only but downloadable). Sawyer/basket MJCF tied to MoMa.
LicenseApache-2.0 (code and dm_robotics assets); trained policy weights CC-BY-4.0. Fully permissive.
CL track recordNone as a continual-learning benchmark. Its research lineage is sim-to-real and state-to-vision interactive distillation (MPO experts -> vision policy -> CRR offline RL), which is methodologically adjacent to our distillation pipeline but it has no adoption in CL papers.
Port to mjlabEnv code: high (MuJoCo 200 + MoMa + Sawyer arm; not worth porting). Object assets: low — 152 simple rigid STL meshes, easy to wrap in MJCF with convex decomposition and drop into mjlab Franka scenes as props for stack/pick variants (~days).
