Scale potentialAs a task suite: near zero (1 primitive). As an asset library: moderate — the 152 objects have designed difficulty gradations, so could yield ~5-20 shape-varied stack/lift/push task instances in mjlab, but that is object variation, not primitive variation.
GotchasRequires legacy MuJoCo 200 with license key — does not run on modern mujoco/MJX at all. Robot is Sawyer, not Franka. "Benchmark" framing in press oversells it: task count is 1. dm_robotics (asset source) is archived, so grab STLs now. README does not list per-field obs spec — had to read environment.py to confirm state contents.
Repo activity130 stars, 13 forks; created Oct 2021, last push July 2024, not archived but effectively dormant; asset-hosting dm_robotics repo archived April 2026 (read-only).
Assets only

RoboTwin 2.0

paper · code

SAPIEN-based bimanual benchmark/data-generator: 50 dual-arm tasks, 731-object annotated library, MLLM-generated expert code with heavy domain randomization (arXiv 2506.18088, CVPR 2025 challenge).

RoboTwin 2.0: A Scalable Data Generator and Benchmark with Strong Domain Randomization for Robust Bimanual Robotic Manipulation (2025)

Verdict: Simulator is SAPIEN (verified: `import sapien.core` in envs/_base_task.py) with curobo motion planning — hard-excluded ecosystem — and all 50 tasks are defined/evaluated as DUAL-arm, mismatching our single-Franka mjlab setup. No task logic ports cheaply. But the RoboTwin-OD object library (731 objects: plain GLB/OBJ meshes + JSON grasp/functional-point annotations + 44 PartNet-Mobility URDFs) IS portable to MuJoCo, and its expert-code-generation recipe (annotated grasp points + planner + LLM-synthesized task code) is directly imitable in mjlab. Use its assets and recipe, not the benchmark.

Full details
SimulatorSAPIEN 3 (ManiSkill ecosystem) — confirmed by `import sapien.core as sapien` in envs/_base_task.py; expert trajectories planned with NVIDIA curobo. NOT MuJoCo; no MuJoCo port exists (recent repo news adds IsaacLab-Arena and RLinf integrations, still not MuJoCo).
# Tasks50 (verified full list on docs site; ~60 env files in envs/). All are bimanual by construction, though a substantial fraction are functionally single-arm skills done with one of the two arms.
Example tasksstack_blocks_two/three, stack_bowls_two/three, place_empty_cup, place_shoe, place_object_basket, handover_block, handover_mic, open_laptop, open_microwave, put_object_cabinet, press_stapler, click_bell, click_alarmclock, turn_switch, rotate_qrcode, hanging_mug, lift_pot, shake_bottle, dump_bin_bigbin, beat_block_hammer, pick_dual_bottles, blocks_ranking_rgb
Primitivespick, place (A-to-B, into basket/bin/cabinet/skillet), stack (blocks/bowls), handover, press (stapler, bell, alarm clock), turn/rotate (switch, QR code), open (laptop, microwave, cabinet), shake, hang (mug), lift (pot), scan, stamp, dump/pour, sort/rank blocks
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (7 DoF), UR5, AgileX Piper, ARX-X5, Aloha-AgileX — used in dual-arm pairings; Franka is natively supported, which helps when copying grasp annotations to our setup.
State observationsget_obs() returns joint qpos + per-arm end-effector pos+quat + gripper state, plus RGB/depth/seg/point clouds per camera (modalities selectable via data_type config). Object poses are not in the default obs dict but are fully available as privileged sim state (SAPIEN actor.get_pose()). Crucially, the ENTIRE expert pipeline is vision-free: LLM-generated task code executes using privileged object poses + annotated grasp/functional points + curobo planning, so every task is state-solvable by construction. The benchmark's own baselines (DP, ACT, DP3, RDT, Pi0) are vision/point-cloud policies — no state-only RL/BC results are reported.
Action spaceDual-arm joint position (2× 6-7 DoF + grippers) or end-effector pose targets executed via curobo planner; embodiment-agnostic across 5 arms (Franka Panda 7-DoF, UR5, AgileX Piper, ARX-X5, Aloha-AgileX) in arbitrary dual-arm pairings.
AssetsRoboTwin-OD: 731 objects / 147 categories = 534 Rodin RGB-to-3D generated meshes, 153 Objaverse meshes, 44 articulated PartNet-Mobility instances (URDF). Meshes are GLB/OBJ with convex collision + JSON annotations of semantic labels, grasp poses, functional/placement axes and points. Downloadable from HuggingFace (TianxingChen/RoboTwin2.0, objects.zip). These annotations are the key reusable asset for auto-generating tasks. 100k+ pre-collected trajectories (HDF5, vision-centric) also available but less useful for us.
LicenseRepo: MIT. Trajectory dataset: Apache 2.0. Object library: mixed — Rodin-generated objects under project license, Objaverse subset carries per-object licenses (mostly CC-BY family), PartNet-Mobility subset is under SAPIEN's non-commercial academic terms of use. Fine for research use; check per-object licenses before redistribution.
CL track recordNo continual-learning paper found that uses RoboTwin as its task sequence (searched; CL manipulation work overwhelmingly uses LIBERO, Meta-World, dm_control instead). RoboTwin is used for VLA/imitation robustness and sim-to-real, not CL. We would be first, minus any very recent unindexed work.
Port to mjlabNo trivial port. Task envs are SAPIEN Python classes with curobo planning and VLM-in-the-loop code generation — porting the framework is out of scope. Realistic path: (1) download RoboTwin-OD objects.zip; (2) convert GLB→OBJ and build MJCF with obj2mjcf/trimesh + convex decomposition (coacd), URDF articulated objects via MuJoCo's URDF importer (~1-2 days to script the pipeline); (3) reuse the JSON grasp/functional-point annotations to define reach/grasp/place targets and success predicates in mjlab; (4) re-implement chosen tasks as single-arm Franka mjlab tasks (~0.5-1 day each once the pipeline exists, since our reward/manager scaffolding already exists). Total: ~1-2 weeks for a first batch of 10 tasks.
Scale potentialHigh for asset-driven generation, zero for direct reuse. The 50 dual-arm tasks themselves don't transfer, but ~25-30 of them are single-arm-reducible recipes (press, turn, open, stack, place-A-in-B, hang, lift), and the 731-object annotated library supports combinatorial task generation: place-{object}-{container}, press/turn articulated instances, open {microwave, laptop, cabinet} from 44 PartNet-Mobility items. Realistically 20-50 distinct state-solvable single-arm mjlab tasks extractable, more with object-instance variation.
Gotchas(1) Prior sweep was right: SAPIEN-only, violates simulator requirement. (2) Bimanual framing everywhere — success checkers and layouts assume two arms; single-arm reduction is per-task manual work. (3) Default recorded obs lacks object poses (privileged state must be read from sim / re-derived in mjlab). (4) The 100k trajectory dataset is vision+qpos, tied to SAPIEN cameras — not directly useful for state-based distillation teachers; we'd retrain teachers in mjlab anyway. (5) PartNet-Mobility subset has non-commercial academic license; Objaverse items have per-object licenses. (6) Expert code generation relies on MLLM API calls + simulation-in-loop verification — the recipe is imitable but not a turnkey artifact. (7) Rodin-generated meshes are visual-quality; check collision-mesh quality after coacd decomposition.
Repo activity~2.6k stars, ~420 forks; very active — RoboTwin 2.0 release Aug 2025, IsaacLab-Arena/RLinf support Jan 2026, RMBench release Mar 2026; CVPR 2025 challenge platform with community-contributed policies (TinyVLA, DexVLA, LLaVA-VLA).
Assets only

SceneSmith

paper · code

LLM/VLM-agent pipeline that generates whole simulation-ready indoor scenes (rooms/houses) from text, with text-to-3D static objects + retrieved articulated objects; native Drake, experimental MuJoCo/USD export. A scene generator, not a task benchmark.

SceneSmith: Agentic Generation of Simulation-Ready Indoor Scenes (2026)

Verdict: It is a scene GENERATOR, not a task suite: zero task definitions, zero rewards, zero env/obs code, no RL interface. Everything you'd need for a CL benchmark (task semantics, success criteria, resets, state obs) must be built by you. Its value is as a source of simulation-ready cluttered scenes / manipulable objects with physics params and (experimental) MJCF export. For scaling a Franka tabletop CL suite, directly mining PartNet-Mobility/ArtVIP (its own articulated-object sources) is a far shorter path than running its heavy Drake+Blender+VLM pipeline; the pre-generated HF scenes (Apache-2.0, MJCF included) are the only low-effort useful artifact.

Full details
SimulatorNative: Drake (model directives .dmd.yaml). Export: MuJoCo MJCF and USD/Isaac Sim, both explicitly flagged "experimental" in the README. The HF example-scenes dataset ships MJCF + USD + Drake + Blender files per scene, so MJCF export is real but unproven for contact-rich manipulation fidelity.
# Tasks0. The prior-sweep claim of "0 tasks; open-ended scene generation" is CONFIRMED. There is a "robot evaluation pipeline" that converts a human task description (e.g. "Pick a fruit from the fruit bowl and place it on a plate") into diverse scene prompts for policy evaluation, but no reward functions, success metrics code, or RL environments are provided.
Example tasksNone shipped. Paper's evaluation example: "Pick a fruit from the fruit bowl and place it on a plate" (used only as a scene-generation prompt, not an implemented task). HF dataset: 1,284 scenes (179 room-level, 31 house-level in main subsets, plus ablation variants) — scenes, not tasks.
PrimitivesNone defined by the framework. Scenes contain affordance-relevant content: small manipulable objects (pick/place), articulated furniture from PartNet-Mobility/ArtVIP (open/close doors, drawers; turn knobs), wall/ceiling fixtures. All primitives would have to be authored by us on top of the generated scenes.
RobotsNone. Robot-agnostic scene generation; the robot evaluation pipeline is described but no robot embodiment/controller is part of the released tasks (there are none). We would drop our Franka into exported MJCF scenes ourselves.
State observationsNo observation space exists — there are no environments. Positively, every object is a separable (non-composite) body with VLM-estimated mass/friction and its own pose, so any scene is in principle fully state-observable (object poses + articulation joint angles) once loaded into MuJoCo; nothing is baked into monolithic scene meshes. But vision-free solvability is never demonstrated because no policies/tasks ship with it.
Action spaceN/A — no environments or controllers defined.
AssetsPer-scene tarballs with: Drake .dmd.yaml, MJCF, USD, .blend, glTF visual meshes, OBJ collision meshes, SDF model descriptions, floor plans, PBR materials (AmbientCG), VLM-estimated physical params. Static objects generated via SAM3D/Hunyuan3D-2 text-to-3D; articulated objects retrieved from PartNet-Mobility and ArtVIP (converted to SDFormat). Reusable at object level since objects are separable bodies. Full HF dataset is 1.13 TB.
LicenseCode: MIT (confirmed via GitHub API). Example-scenes dataset: mostly Apache-2.0; the "NotGenerated" ablation subset uses HSSD assets under CC BY-NC 4.0 (non-commercial). Caveat: articulated objects derive from PartNet-Mobility, which has its own restrictive terms-of-use upstream.
CL track recordNone found. Paper is ~5 months old; no continual-learning paper uses it. Its own paper only mentions an end-to-end pipeline for automatic robot policy evaluation, no CL or even RL training results.
Port to mjlabTwo paths. (a) Low effort, low value: download pre-generated MJCF scenes from HF (Apache-2.0 subsets), load in MuJoCo, hand-author tasks/rewards in mjlab — but MJCF export is self-declared experimental, scenes are room-scale (heavy geom counts, likely poor MJX/warp throughput), and collision meshes are text-to-3D OBJs of unverified contact quality. (b) High effort: run the full generator — requires Drake + Blender + CUDA + VLM API calls (recurring cost), then per-scene MJCF export + validation. Either way, 100% of the RL task layer (rewards, resets, success, obs) is on us. Estimate: days to load a few scenes; weeks to get a robust multi-task pipeline; the articulated-object sources (PartNet-Mobility/ArtVIP) can be mined directly with less work.
Scale potentialScene diversity is effectively unbounded (prompt-driven; 1,284 scenes already released), so in principle 20+ or 100+ task INSTANCES could be procedurally derived (open drawer X in kitchen Y, pick object Z). But each task family still needs hand-written rewards/success logic, and room-scale scenes are a poor fit for GPU-parallel tabletop Franka training in mjlab. Realistic near-term yield as a task suite: ~0 without substantial engineering; as an object/articulation source it adds little over using PartNet-Mobility/ArtVIP directly.
Gotchas1) MuJoCo export explicitly labeled experimental by the authors. 2) Native format is Drake, not MuJoCo — the sweep's "simulator-agnostic" is generous. 3) Full dataset is 1.13 TB. 4) Generation requires paid VLM API calls plus Drake/Blender/CUDA stack. 5) Text-to-3D collision meshes may be unreliable for contact-rich manipulation. 6) Articulated assets inherit PartNet-Mobility upstream license terms; HSSD-based subset is CC BY-NC. 7) Room/house-scale scenes will be slow under MJX/mujoco-warp with thousands of parallel envs. 8) Very new (Feb 2026), zero adoption in RL/CL literature so far.
Repo activity506 stars, 44 forks, 5 open issues; created 2026-02-08, last push 2026-06-20 — young but actively maintained, decent traction for a 5-month-old repo.
Assets only

TCDM / PGDM (facebookresearch/TCDM)

paper · code

Archived FAIR benchmark of 50 dexterous-hand tasks where an Adroit hand must reproduce exemplar object trajectories (from GRAB mocap/animators), trained per-task with state-based PPO in modern MuJoCo.

Learning Dexterous Manipulation from Exemplar Object Trajectories and Pre-Grasps (Dasari, Gupta, Kumar; ICRA 2023)

Verdict: All 50 tasks are object-trajectory-mimicry with a floating-base Adroit dexterous hand, not Franka primitive tasks — wrong embodiment and wrong reward paradigm for our Franka policy-distillation CL suite, and the repo is archived. The genuinely valuable part is the bundled, clean MJCF object library (approx. 60 GRAB/YCB object XMLs + meshes) which is trivially portable to mjlab for building our own pick/push/place/insert task variants.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (python bindings mujoco==2.1.5 + dm_control==1.0.1, no mujoco-py). Pure MJCF assets, so mechanically compatible with MuJoCo-Warp/mjlab, though 30-DoF hand-object mesh contact is heavy for GPU-parallel sim.
# Tasks50 (TCDM-50; TCDM-30 curated subset). Verified by counting ~50 .npz reference trajectories bundled in trajectories/ (48 obj_mimic entries in task_trajs.yaml plus dhand/dmanus extras).
Example taskshammer_use1, hammer_strike, cup_pour1, cup_drink1, knife_chop1, scissors_use1, wineglass_toast1, waterbottle_shake1, alarmclock_lift, duck_inspect1, banana_pass1, spheremedium_relocate, door_open, fryingpan_cook2, dmanus_crackerbox (sim2real)
PrimitivesNominally lift, relocate, pass, pour, chop, hammer, stamp, inspect, door-open — but ALL are implemented as trajectory tracking (ObjectMimic reward = follow the exemplar object motion), not primitive-semantic goals. Not push/pull/press/slide/insert style primitives with a gripper.
RobotsAdroit hand (Shadow-Hand-like, ~30 DoF, floating 6-DoF base) for most tasks; DHand and DManus variants for a handful; Franka MJCF assets exist in the repo but the 50 benchmark tasks do not use a Franka arm.
State observationsYes, fully state-based, no vision: robot qpos/qvel, hand body poses + 6D velocities, object COM position + quaternion + 6D velocity, future reference-motion goal frames, sinusoidal time encoding (control.py). PGDM solves 74.5% of tasks from this state with per-task PPO (stable-baselines3).
Action spaceJoint-space actuation of the dexterous hand plus floating wrist base (position actuators); per-task RL policies. No Cartesian/gripper primitive interface.
AssetsBundled in-repo: MJCF XMLs for ~60 objects (GRAB objects: airplane, banana, wineglass, alarmclock, etc.), YCB_sim meshes, adroit/dhand/dmanus/franka robot models, scenes, textures; 50 reference-trajectory .npz files; task_trajs.yaml + robot_config.yaml. Depends on object_sim (Apache-2.0).
LicenseCode MIT; object_sim dependency Apache-2.0. Caveat: GRAB-derived object meshes and mocap trajectories nominally inherit GRAB's research-only/non-commercial terms even though redistributed here — fine for research use.
CL track recordNone found. Used for per-task RL and dexterous-manipulation follow-ups; no continual-learning or policy-distillation papers use TCDM as a CL benchmark.
Port to mjlabAs tasks: HIGH — port Adroit hand + ObjectMimic reference-motion machinery (reference.py/control.py/rewards.py) into mjlab's manager API, tune dense mesh contacts for MuJoCo-Warp, and accept a non-Franka embodiment incompatible with our distillation target. As assets: LOW — object MJCFs/meshes drop into mjlab scenes almost directly to define new Franka primitive tasks.
Scale potentialIf we adopted the mimicry paradigm and Adroit hand, the recipe auto-generates a task per object trajectory (50 now, unbounded from GRAB/animators) — real scaling, but on the wrong embodiment. For our Franka suite the realistic yield is 0 direct tasks; instead ~60 portable object assets could seed 10-30 self-authored pick/place/push/pour-style task variants.
Gotchas1) Repo archived, deps pinned old (gym 0.18, SB3 1.1, mujoco 2.1.5). 2) Tasks are time-indexed trajectory tracking — obs includes phase/time encoding, awkward for phase-free distillation and for our primitive-task formulation. 3) Dexterous hand contact richness is expensive and finicky in GPU-parallel warp sim. 4) GRAB data license technically non-commercial. 5) README undersells details — task list lives in trajectories/TASKS.md and task_trajs.yaml, and 'Franka support' in assets does not mean Franka tasks exist.
Repo activity71 stars, 12 forks; last push 2023-07-12; archived July 2025 — effectively unmaintained/dead.
Assets only

UniDoorManip

paper · code

Large combinatorial door dataset (328 bodies x 204 handles across 6 categories with functional lever/round/latch mechanisms) plus IsaacGym door-opening envs; useful to us purely as a URDF asset generator for many open-door variants.

UniDoorManip: Learning Universal Door Manipulation Policy Over Large-scale and Diverse Door Manipulation Environments (Li, Zhang, Wu, Zhang, Geng, Dong, He; 2024, ICRA 2026)

Verdict: Environments are IsaacGym-only (excluded), and it is a single task family (unlatch-and-open a door) rather than a diverse primitive suite — but the asset library (328 door bodies + 204 handles, plain URDF+OBJ, combinatorially composable) is genuinely valuable for deepening/scaling our existing open-door and open-drawer tasks with realistic latch/handle mechanics. Main risks: NO license file in the repo, and parts derive from PartNet-Mobility (non-commercial terms) and 3D Warehouse (restrictive SketchUp terms), so redistribution/licensing is murky.

Full details
SimulatorIsaacGym (confirmed in repo install docs and paper; NOT SAPIEN/MuJoCo). PartNet-Mobility is only the source of parts, not the runtime sim.
# Tasks1 task family (open door) instantiated as 6 category environments: franka_slider_door (lever + round-knob interior doors), _cabinet (StorageFurniture), _fridge, _window, _safe, _car. Thousands of door instances via body x handle composition; paper reports 4 mechanism types (lever rotate, round knob rotate, push/pull, latch).
Example tasksOpen lever-handle interior door (rotate lever then pull); open round-knob door (rotate knob then pull); open safe (rotate handle, heavy door); open car door (latch handle, pull); slide/pull open window; open cabinet (StorageFurniture); open fridge — each across hundreds of body+handle combinations.
Primitivesgrasp handle, rotate handle (lever/knob), unlatch, pull open, push open, slide (window). All within the open-door family; no pick/place/insert/stack.
RobotsFranka on a slider/mobile base (env files are franka_slider_*.py; paper uses a mobile agent). Trivially swappable to our fixed Franka for interior-door-scale assets.
State observationsPaper policy uses partial/occluded point clouds (4096 pts) + robot joint state — vision-first. BUT the env code exposes full privileged state tensors: door joint qpos (door_dof_tensor), handle DOF (door_handle_dof_tensor), handle rigid-body pose quaternion (door_handle_rigid_body_tensor), hand pose/velocity, Jacobian. Success criteria are computed from these state tensors (e.g. handle DOF >= 0.65 of range, door displacement > 0.01 rad), so the tasks are fully state-solvable (proprio + handle pose + door/handle joint angles ~ 20-30 dims). Vision-free solving is not demonstrated in the paper but is clearly feasible — their staged heuristics already operate on these tensors.
Action spaceMultiple drive modes in env: IK end-effector pose (pos + quat, scaled to workspace), joint-position deltas, or OSC torque control; plus gripper.
AssetsURDF + OBJ mesh parts (door bodies and handles as separate parts with composition scripts); pre-composed example doors on Google Drive (link in DatasetsGeneration README). Sourced from PartNet-Mobility and 3D Warehouse. Highly reusable format-wise; licensing unclear.
LicenseNONE — repo has no LICENSE file (confirmed via GitHub API: license=null). Upstream parts inherit PartNet-Mobility (non-commercial research terms) and 3D Warehouse (SketchUp ToU) restrictions. Fine for internal research use, problematic for redistribution.
CL track recordNone found. No continual/lifelong-learning paper appears to use UniDoorManip; searches surface only generic CL benchmarks (Continual World, LIBERO/LOTUS, etc.). We would be first.
Port to mjlabAssets are plain URDF + OBJ meshes (DatasetsGeneration composes body+handle IDs into URDFs via per-category scripts, e.g. generate_round_datasets.sh). MuJoCo compiles URDF directly, so per-door import is easy; the real work is reimplementing the latch mechanism (handle-rotation-gates-door-DOF logic lives in IsaacGym env Python, not the URDF) in MJCF — doable with joint equality constraints / dynamically-set joint limits, as we already do for our door task. Estimate: ~1-2 weeks for a conversion pipeline + one manager-based mjlab env parameterized by category and door instance; reward/staging logic can be adapted from their env code.
Scale potentialAs DISTINCT tasks: ~6-10 (one per category/mechanism: lever door, round-knob door, safe, car door, window, cabinet, fridge, push-vs-pull variants). As instance-level diversity: effectively unlimited (328 bodies x 204 handles, thousands of combos) — great for in-task randomization/generalization, weak for growing the task COUNT to 20+. Best used to turn our 1 open-door task into 6-10 mechanically distinct door tasks with heavy asset randomization.
Gotchas1) No license anywhere + non-commercial upstream sources (PartNet-Mobility, 3D Warehouse) — do not redistribute converted assets. 2) The latch/lock mechanism is implemented in IsaacGym env Python logic, not in the URDF, so a naive URDF-to-MJCF import gives doors WITHOUT functional latches; must reimplement gating in MuJoCo. 3) Prior sweep called it 'hundreds of door instances' — actual figure is 328 bodies + 204 handles = thousands of composable instances, but still only ONE task family. 4) Paper's headline pipeline is point-cloud/affordance-based; none of their trained policies or training code transfer usefully to our state-based distillation setup — only assets and reward/staging design do. 5) Mesh quality varies (3D Warehouse car doors); expect convex-decomposition work for MuJoCo collision geoms.
Repo activity106 stars, 7 forks, 43 commits; created 2024-03; last push 2026-05-24 (active — repo notes ICRA 2026 acceptance). Small but maintained.
Assets only

VLABench

paper · code

MuJoCo/dm_control VLA-evaluation benchmark whose "100 tasks" are mostly language-conditioned variants of pick-and-place; valuable mainly as a large MJCF asset library.

VLABench: A Large-Scale Benchmark for Language-Conditioned Robotics Manipulation with Long-Horizon Reasoning Tasks

Verdict: Its scale (claimed 100 tasks, 96 registered) comes almost entirely from language/world-knowledge variation over the SAME pick-and-place primitive (select_X / _semantic / _spatial / _common_sense series) — exactly the axis we exclude. Only ~10-15 physically distinct behaviors exist. Default obs are vision-centric (RGB/depth/seg/pcd + robot qpos/ee); no object-state vector is provided, and for most tasks the goal is specified by the instruction, not recoverable from state. It runs classic CPU dm_control MuJoCo with heuristic-skill demos and success conditions only (no rewards), so porting to GPU-parallel mjlab means rewriting tasks and rewards from scratch. However, its MIT-licensed MuJoCo-native asset library (2000+ MJCF objects, articulated fixtures: fridge, microwave, coffee machine, drawers, flower vases, condiment bottles, hammers/nails, billiards) is directly loadable and could seed 10-20 new mjlab primitive tasks.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (classic CPU) via dm_control composer — NOT MJX/Warp; no GPU-parallel env support
# Tasks96 registered task names (README claims 100 categories); collapses to ~10-15 physically distinct behaviors once semantic/spatial/common-sense variants of the same pick-place are merged. Split: ~60 "primitive" (mostly select-and-place series + physical-QA series) and ~30 composite long-horizon tasks.
Example tasksselect_fruit(_common_sense/_semantic/_spatial), select_book, select_billiards, select_chemistry_tube, insert_flower, add_condiment, assemble_hammer, hammer_loose_nail, get_coffee(_with_milk/_with_sugar), heat_food (microwave), store_food (fridge), cool_drink, make_juice (insert power cord + juicer), set_dining_table, play_snooker, density_qa/friction_qa/weight_qa (physical-reasoning pick tasks)
Primitivespick/place (dominant, in many semantic disguises), insert (flower, power cord), pour/add condiment, hammer nail, open/close door-drawer (fridge, microwave, coffee machine in composite tasks), press (coffee machine), strike (cuestick), place-on-seesaw. No dedicated push/slide/rotate/stack primitives.
RobotsFranka Panda (primary, single 7-DOF arm); WidowX and XArm classes present; dual-arm and humanoid only as base-class stubs (README says tested but unreleased)
State observationsDefault get_observation returns multi-view RGB/depth/segmentation/point-cloud + robot q_state/q_velocity/q_acceleration/ee_state/grasped_obj_name — NO object poses. Privileged object poses ARE accessible via dm_control physics (entity.get_xpos), so a state vector can be built, but most task goals (which object to pick) are defined by the language instruction/world knowledge, not inferable from state; state-only training requires fixing targets, which collapses the task count.
Action spaceSkill/waypoint-level oracle (SkillLib: moveto/pick/pull/push with EE pose targets + gripper) generating joint trajectories; env steps low-level qpos control via dm_control. No RL-ready normalized action wrapper.
Assets2000+ objects as MuJoCo MJCF + meshes (sourced from Objaverse and RoboCasa), plus articulated scene fixtures (fridge, microwave, coffee machine, drawers, tables). Downloaded as zips from Google Drive via scripts/download_assets.py. Directly loadable in MuJoCo hence nominally mjlab-compatible, but meshes are high-poly Objaverse-style — contact/collision cost for GPU-parallel Warp sim unverified; per-object asset licenses (Objaverse is per-object CC) not documented.
LicenseCode: MIT (LICENSE.txt, copyright oddly says "simpler-env"). Asset licenses not specified per object (Objaverse/RoboCasa provenance).
CL track recordNone. It is a VLA/VLM evaluation benchmark; no continual-learning papers or task-sequence protocol use it. No RL training track record either — demos come from scripted heuristic skills (SkillLib) with motion planning, and tasks define success conditions but no dense rewards.
Port to mjlabHigh for tasks: dm_control composer Entity/Task classes, language-defined goals, no rewards — porting to mjlab means rewriting task logic, randomization, and dense rewards from scratch, and heavy Objaverse meshes may need collision simplification for MuJoCo-Warp GPU throughput. Low-to-moderate for assets: MJCF objects and articulated fixtures load in MuJoCo directly and can be dropped into mjlab scenes after collision-mesh cleanup.
Scale potentialAs-is for state-based CL: ~10-15 distinct tasks at best (after collapsing semantic variants), below our 50-100 goal. As an asset mine: the articulated fixtures + 2000 objects could support authoring 10-20 new mjlab primitive tasks (open fridge/microwave, insert cord/flower, pour, hammer, press) on top of our existing 5.
Gotchas(1) "100 tasks" is marketing for our purposes — variation axis is language/world-knowledge, not motor skill. (2) No state observation of objects out of the box; must be built from physics, and goals aren't state-observable. (3) No rewards — success Conditions only; demos via scripted skills, so no RL baseline exists. (4) Classic CPU MuJoCo, no MJX/Warp path; GPU batch-sim performance of its mesh-heavy scenes untested. (5) Assets ship via Google Drive zips with undocumented per-object licenses. (6) Repo is a "preview"; composite-task datasets and infra still unreleased; LICENSE copyright line copied from simpler-env suggests loose housekeeping.
Repo activity~440 stars; last commit Nov 2025; self-described "preview version" with incomplete roadmap (composite-task datasets, leaderboard, full infra still pending); active-ish issue tracker (Fudan/OpenMOSS group)
Assets only

YCB objects (MuJoCo-ready conversions)

paper · code

The canonical ~77-object real+3D-model set for manipulation benchmarking; multiple community repos ship MuJoCo-ready MJCF conversions with convex decompositions, but it is objects only — no tasks.

Benchmarking in Manipulation Research: Using the Yale-CMU-Berkeley Object and Model Set (IEEE Robotics & Automation Magazine, 2015); companion: The YCB Object and Model Set: Towards Common Benchmarks for Manipulation Research (ICAR 2015)

Verdict: Verified: zero tasks in either repo — both are pure object-model libraries (meshes + MJCF/URDF), no environments, rewards, or observation definitions. The canonical ~77-object set is valuable as a drop-in MJCF object bank for generating graded pick/place/push variants in mjlab, but it provides no task logic and object-swap variants add object diversity, not skill/primitive diversity. Redundant with GSO if GSO is adopted, though YCB is smaller, canonical, and already CoACD-decomposed for MuJoCo.

Full details
SimulatorNone (asset dataset). MuJoCo-ready conversions exist: elpis-lab/YCB_Dataset (MJCF + URDF, tested in MuJoCo, PyBullet, Genesis; CoACD convex decomposition, max 5 hulls, 32-face decimation, masses from official YCB docs, diagonal inertia, 1024px textures) and vikashplus/YCB_sim (MJCF + meshes + textures, MuJoCo-specific).
# Tasks0 tasks. Confirmed by inspecting both repos: no environments, no reward functions, no task definitions. The original YCB paper defines physical benchmarking protocols for real robots, not sim tasks.
Example tasksNone shipped. Realistic tasks you would author: pick-and-place mustard bottle / power drill / mug; stack graded boxes; nest measuring cups; push soup can to target; insert peg into peg board; pour from pitcher; in-hand rotate baseball. Graded-size object families (spheres, boxes, cups) give natural difficulty curricula for a single primitive.
PrimitivesNone built in. Enables user-authored pick, place, push, stack, in-hand rotate, pour, insert (peg/cup nesting), and tool-use tasks — the object set was curated to span these use cases (graded sphere/cup/box sizes, clamps, peg board, cups that nest, tools like drill/hammer/scissors).
RobotsNone included — objects only. You supply the Franka.
State observationsN/A — no tasks means no observation spec. But any task built on these assets is trivially state-solvable in mjlab: object free-joint poses (7D per object) + proprioception come directly from MuJoCo qpos/qvel; geometry is simple rigid bodies with known bounding boxes. No articulated objects (all single rigid bodies), so state is just poses. Vision-free solving with YCB objects in MuJoCo is demonstrated in many works (e.g. dexterous grasping papers using privileged object pose).
Action spaceN/A (no tasks; whatever robot/controller you attach in mjlab).
Assets~61-77 objects. elpis-lab: Google-16K scans converted to per-object URDF + MJCF with CoACD convex hulls (physics-ready, mass-calibrated, OBB-aligned) — covers most of the set minus 16 unusable models (~61 objects). vikashplus/YCB_sim: MJCF ycb_objects.xml + meshes/textures. Original source meshes (16K/64K laser scans + RGBD) on ycbbenchmarks.com / AWS Open Data. Highly reusable; the elpis-lab MJCFs should load in mjlab with essentially no work.
LicenseConversions: MIT (elpis-lab), Apache-2.0 (vikashplus). Underlying YCB models/data: CC BY 4.0 (attribution required). All permissive — no problem for research or redistribution.
CL track recordNot as a CL benchmark itself (it defines none). YCB objects appear ubiquitously inside other benchmarks (ManiSkill, RLBench variants, dexterous grasping suites, MuBlE) and in some lifelong-grasping papers as the object pool, but there is no established YCB-based continual-learning task sequence — CL manipulation work standardizes on Meta-World/Continual World instead.
Port to mjlabMinimal — arguably the lowest-effort asset source available. elpis-lab MJCFs are already MuJoCo-native with CoACD convex decompositions (max 5 hulls/object), so they should attach into an mjlab scene via include/spawn with minor edits (asset path, free joint, contact params). Estimate: hours to a day to get a handful of objects into an existing pick task; a few days to build a small spawning utility over the whole set. Caveat: verify contact stability of the auto-decomposed hulls under MJX/mujoco-warp (thin objects like scissors/fork may need hand-tuned collision geoms), and diagonal 1e-3 inertias are simplified — fine for pick/push, check for in-hand tasks.
Scale potentialAs tasks: ~0 for free; everything must be authored. Combinatorially you could generate 20-100 "tasks" as (primitive x object-family) pairs — e.g. 5 primitives x 10 object groups — all state-solvable. But that scaling axis is object variation within existing primitives, not new skills, so it complements rather than replaces a task suite; best used to diversify/harden your existing 5 tasks and add pick/place/stack/pour variants.
Gotchas(1) It is not a benchmark suite despite the name — the 2015 'benchmarks' are physical-robot protocols, not sim tasks; prior sweep's '0 tasks' is confirmed correct. (2) elpis-lab repo is very new (Jul 2025, 21 stars) — spot-check a few MJCFs before committing; 16 YCB models are excluded (~61 usable). (3) Aggressive convex decomposition (max 5 hulls) will misrepresent concave objects (mugs may not accept insertions, cups may not nest) — regenerate with CoACD at higher hull counts if you need concavity. (4) Simplified diagonal inertias. (5) CC BY 4.0 requires attribution. (6) Largely redundant with GSO (~1000 objects) if adopted, though YCB is canonical and physics-tuned.
Repo activityelpis-lab/YCB_Dataset: 21 stars, created 2025-07, last push 2025-08 (young, low-activity but recent and purpose-built for MuJoCo/Genesis). vikashplus/YCB_sim: 37 stars, 8 commits, last push 2025-02, 2 open issues (small, semi-dormant, from Vikash Kumar of RoboHive lineage).

Reject (20)

Does not fit (wrong simulator without a porting path, wrong embodiment, vision-locked, or impractical).

Reject

ARNOLD

paper · code

Vision-first, language-conditioned Isaac Sim benchmark of 8 Franka manipulation tasks whose distinguishing feature is continuous goal states (e.g., open drawer to a target fraction, pour water to a level).

ARNOLD: A Benchmark for Language-Grounded Task Learning with Continuous States in Realistic 3D Scenes (ICCV 2023)

Verdict: Only 8 task types (7 "splits" are eval splits, not tasks), of which 2 are fluid tasks (PBD particle water) that MuJoCo cannot simulate, and the remaining 6 (pickup, reorient, open/close drawer, open/close cabinet) overlap almost entirely with our existing 5 mjlab tasks. Runtime is Isaac Sim/PhysX with USD assets (non-trivial to convert to MJCF); the articulated objects come from SAPIEN/PartNet-Mobility, which we could fetch as URDFs directly from the source rather than via ARNOLD. The one genuinely useful contribution — continuous goal states (open drawer to 50%, etc.) — is a task-formulation idea we can implement natively in mjlab in a day, not a reason to port the benchmark. No continual-learning track record found.

Full details
SimulatorNVIDIA Isaac Sim (PhysX 5.0); fluids via GPU position-based dynamics; ray-traced rendering. Excluded runtime per our requirements.
# Tasks8 task types (verified: the "x7" in the sweep note refers to evaluation splits per task — Normal train/val/test, Novel Object, Novel Scene, Novel State, Any State — not extra tasks). ~10k demos from ~2k human-annotated goal configurations, 40 objects, 20 scenes.
Example tasksPickupObject (lift bottle to height h), ReorientObject (rotate bottle to target orientation), OpenDrawer / CloseDrawer (prismatic joint to target fraction), OpenCabinet / CloseCabinet (revolute joint to target angle), PourWater / TransferWater (pour to target volume, <10% spill allowed)
Primitivespick, reorient (rotate), open, close (drawers + cabinets, prismatic + revolute joints), pour/transfer liquid. All with continuous target values rather than binary success.
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (7-DoF + parallel gripper); mobile base pose given as observation but manipulation is fixed-arm keyframe control via Isaac Sim's built-in motion planner (end-effector position + quaternion actions).
State observationsBenchmark is vision-first: default obs = 5 RGB-D cameras at 128x128 plus camera params, robot base pose, part-level semantic masks; demos store EE pose, gripper status, joint positions per keyframe. There is NO state-based track and no paper demonstrates vision-free solving. However, ground-truth continuous object state (joint fraction, object pose, water level) is computed internally for the success metric, so 6 of 8 tasks (all but the two water tasks, whose state is a particle set) are in-principle solvable from privileged low-level state — they are ordinary articulated-object and rigid-body tasks under the vision wrapper.
Action spaceKeyframe end-effector actions: 3D translation + quaternion + gripper open/close; 4-6 keyframes per demo; joint-space execution delegated to Isaac Sim motion planner. Not a per-step torque/velocity RL action space — would need re-formulation for our RL-teacher pipeline anyway.
AssetsUSD only (Omniverse-native). 20 house scenes from 3D-FRONT converted via their "USDify" pipeline; 40 objects sourced from Isaac Sim, AI2-THOR, and SAPIEN. No URDF/MJCF distribution. The articulated objects' upstream source (SAPIEN PartNet-Mobility) ships URDFs that convert to MJCF with existing tools — but that argues for going to PartNet-Mobility directly, not through ARNOLD. Fluid assets are PhysX PBD particles, unportable.
LicenseMIT (per GitHub repo page).
CL track recordNo continual-learning usage found. Web search for CL/policy-distillation papers using ARNOLD returned nothing; CL distillation work in this space uses Meta-World, LIBERO, Continual World instead.
Port to mjlabNo direct port path: Isaac Sim runtime is excluded and USD-to-MJCF conversion of 3D-FRONT scenes and Omniverse assets is high-effort (weeks) with little payoff. Realistic path is re-implementation: (a) take the continuous-goal-state task formulation (target joint fraction / target pose sampled per episode, success = state held within tolerance for N steps) and add it to our existing mjlab drawer/door/cabinet/lift tasks — roughly 1-3 days; (b) if more articulated objects are wanted, pull PartNet-Mobility URDFs directly (SAPIEN's source, same objects ARNOLD used) and convert with urdf2mjcf-style tooling — days per object category. Water tasks: skip, MuJoCo has no fluid simulation.
Scale potentialWeak as a task source: 6 portable task types, ~4 of which we already have. The continuous-goal formulation multiplies our existing tasks into goal-conditioned families (open-to-25/50/75% etc.), but that is goal variation, not new skills — likely worth ~5-10 additional distinct task variants at most. It will not get us to 20+ new tasks; PartNet-Mobility itself (2,300+ articulated objects) is the scalable resource hiding behind ARNOLD.
Gotchas(1) "8 tasks x 7 splits" in sweep notes overstates scale — splits are train/test generalization splits, not tasks. (2) Two of eight tasks depend on PhysX particle fluids and are fundamentally unportable to MuJoCo. (3) The demo dataset is keyframe-based (4-6 keyframes/demo) and useless for our per-step RL/distillation pipeline. (4) Benchmark requires Isaac Sim + RTX GPU for ray tracing even to reproduce baselines. (5) Rendering is stochastic (ray-tracing sampling), an eval-noise caveat they document themselves. (6) Docs README lacks explicit license text in-repo beyond the GitHub MIT badge — verify LICENSE file before reusing assets, and note 3D-FRONT scenes carry their own non-commercial-style upstream terms.
Repo activity~186 stars, 16 forks. ICCV 2023 release; ARNOLD Challenge at CVPR 2024 EAI workshop was the last major activity signal; low maintenance since.
Reject

BEHAVIOR-1K / OmniGibson

paper · code

1,000 long-horizon household activities in NVIDIA OmniGibson (Isaac Sim/PhysX) with 50 scenes and 9k+ encrypted proprietary objects — impressive embodied-AI benchmark, unusable for MuJoCo state-based RL primitive extraction.

BEHAVIOR-1K: A Human-Centered, Embodied AI Benchmark with 1,000 Everyday Activities and Realistic Simulation (CoRL 2022; extended arXiv version 2024)

Verdict: Triple disqualification, all verified: (1) Runtime is NVIDIA Omniverse/Isaac Sim (PhysX) — explicitly excluded; heavy install (RTX GPU, 32GB RAM, Isaac Sim EULA). (2) Assets are proprietary: encrypted 3D models under a click-through BEHAVIOR Dataset License that forbids redistribution and restricts use to the provided simulator stack (originally "models are encrypted and allowed only to be used within iGibson 2.0"; same access-form + license-key model persists for OmniGibson) — no legal or practical MuJoCo porting path. (3) Task character mismatch: the 1,016 activities are long-horizon household chores ("baking_cookies_for_the_PTA_bake_sale", "buying_groceries") defined by BDDL semantic predicates (Cooked, Saturated, Inside) that depend on fluid/particle/deformable/temperature simulation, not short-horizon state-solvable manipulation primitives; the paper itself reports SOTA RL cannot solve even single activities without heavy domain knowledge. No continual-learning track record found. Nothing usable for mjlab, not even assets-only.

Full details
SimulatorOmniGibson, built on NVIDIA Omniverse / Isaac Sim (PhysX + RTX rendering). Requires Ubuntu 22.04+/Win10+, 32GB RAM, RTX 2070+ GPU, NVIDIA Isaac Sim EULA. Excluded runtime per our requirements. No MJX/MuJoCo path.
# Tasks1,016 activity definitions in the current knowledge base (paper claims 1,000; task instances counted per scene instantiation can exceed this). But these are long-horizon BDDL activities, not manipulation-primitive tasks.
Example tasksadding_chemicals_to_hot_tub, assembling_furniture, baking_cookies_for_the_PTA_bake_sale, boil_water, bringing_in_mail, buying_groceries, clean_a_baseball_glove, clean_a_kitchen_sink, changing_sheets, brewing_coffee (from behavior.stanford.edu knowledge base, 1,016 total)
PrimitivesUnderlying primitives inside activities: pick, place, open, close, pour, wipe/soak, toggle-on/off, heat/cook, slice, spray, fold. However tasks are defined at the semantic-goal level (BDDL first-order-logic predicates like Inside/OnTop/Cooked/Saturated), each activity chaining many primitives over minutes-long horizons with navigation between rooms. Framework ships "action primitives" (scripted pick/place/navigate controllers) as a crutch because flat RL fails.
RobotsFetch, Tiago (dual-arm), Stretch, Turtlebot, Locobot, Franka (arm-only), Husky, plus R1 and others in recent releases; robot USD/URDF assets mirrored at huggingface.co/datasets/behavior-1k/omnigibson-robot-assets (robot assets are the one openly reusable part).
State observationsLow-level state IS technically exposed: OmniGibson supports "low-dimensional" observations = proprioception (configurable via proprio_obs, robot.default_proprio_obs) plus "task-relevant" object state read from the simulator, alongside vision/scan modalities. So privileged state exists. BUT vision-free solving is NOT demonstrated: goals are semantic object-state predicates (temperature, saturation, cooked, particle coverage) over dozens of objects across a whole scene, with fluids/deformables — no fixed low-dim state vector per task, and the CoRL paper reports RL baselines cannot solve even one activity without major domain knowledge injection. Effectively not state-solvable in our sense.
Action spaceContinuous control of mobile manipulators: differential/omnidirectional base + arm joint-position/velocity or IK delta-pose + gripper; configurable controllers per robot. Also high-level scripted action primitives (navigate_to, grasp, place_on_top, etc.).
Assets50 interactive scenes + 9,000+ object models with rich physical/semantic annotations, native USD format. Objects sourced from commercial 3D marketplaces, therefore ENCRYPTED on disk and decrypted only inside the simulator with a license key obtained via access form. Not redistributable, not legally convertible to MJCF. Only the robot models (standard URDF/meshes on HuggingFace) and the BDDL activity-definition language (github.com/StanfordVL/bddl, logic-level task specs that are simulator-independent) are reusable.
LicenseCode (OmniGibson/BDDL): MIT. Dataset/assets: proprietary "BEHAVIOR Dataset License" — click-through agreement, non-commercial research use, no redistribution, assets encrypted to protect 3D artists' copyright, usable only within the sanctioned simulator; plus NVIDIA Isaac Sim EULA for the runtime. This alone disqualifies porting.
CL track recordNo continual-learning usage found. Searches for CL/lifelong-learning papers using BEHAVIOR-1K return nothing; the benchmark is used for long-horizon planning, VLA, and mobile-manipulation challenges, not sequential-task CL. (Unrelated CL benchmarks like Continual World use MuJoCo/Meta-World instead.)
Port to mjlabNo practical path. Assets: encrypted + license forbids extraction/redistribution — legally dead for MJCF conversion. Runtime: Omniverse-only, deep PhysX/USD coupling plus a semantic object-state system (fluids, particles, temperature, cloth) with no MuJoCo equivalent. The only portable artifact is BDDL (MIT, simulator-independent logic task definitions) — one could hand-reimplement a few simple BDDL activities over our own MuJoCo assets, but that is authoring tasks from scratch (weeks per handful), not porting. Effort: effectively infinite for the benchmark proper.
Scale potentialZero tasks directly extractable. Even ignoring license, <5% of the 1,016 activities reduce to short-horizon state-solvable primitives, and those (pick X, put X on Y) are trivially re-authorable without BEHAVIOR. BDDL as a task-specification formalism is the only idea worth borrowing if we later want programmatic goal predicates over our own mjlab scenes.
GotchasPrior sweep was essentially correct. Gotchas: (1) repo README hides the license issue — encryption/license terms only surface at install (--accept-dataset-tos) and in older BDDL dataset docs; (2) "low-dimensional observations" in docs sound promising but task success is semantic-predicate-based, so state obs does not equal state-solvable; (3) GitHub releases page redirects StanfordVL/OmniGibson -> BEHAVIOR-1K monorepo (repos were merged); (4) the 2024 arXiv is a re-post of the CoRL 2022 paper, don't cite as new work; (5) robot assets on HuggingFace are open — the one salvageable piece if we ever need Fetch/Tiago models.
Repo activity~1.6k stars, 213 forks, very active: latest release v3.9.0 (July 2026), 16k+ commits, active Discord, annual BEHAVIOR Challenge (2025 challenge demo dataset on HuggingFace).
Reject

CausalWorld / TriFinger

paper · code

PyBullet TriFinger benchmark whose 9 parametric task-generator families (push/pick/place/stack) each yield unbounded instances via causal do-interventions on scene variables; state-solvable by design but wrong robot, wrong simulator, no portable assets.

CausalWorld: A Robotic Manipulation Benchmark for Causal Structure and Transfer Learning (ICLR 2021)

Verdict: Wrong embodiment (TriFinger 3-finger platform, not an arm — incompatible with your Franka-based mjlab suite), PyBullet-only with zero MJCF/mesh assets to port (objects are programmatic primitive cuboids), unmaintained since 2022, and never used as a continual-learning benchmark. Its only real value is as a DESIGN TEMPLATE: the parametric task-generator + do-intervention-on-scene-variables pattern is exactly how you could generate 50-100 task variants natively in mjlab, but you would copy the idea, not a single line of code or asset.

Full details
SimulatorPyBullet only (CausalWorld and trifinger_simulation are both Bullet-based). No official MuJoCo version. Robot URDF (trifinger_edu, MIT) loads in MuJoCo via URDF compilation; one abandoned community MJCF exists (vikashplus/trifinger_sim, Apache-2.0, 3 commits).
# Tasks9 task-generator families (prior sweep said 8; docs list 9): reaching, pushing, picking, pick_and_place, stacking2, towers, stacked_blocks, creative_stacked_blocks, general. Each family yields unbounded parametric instances via interventions (block size/mass/pose, goal shape, friction, gravity, colors), so 'task count' is effectively infinite instances within ~9 skill families — but only ~4-5 genuinely distinct skills.
Example taskspushing (push cuboid to goal pose on floor), picking (lift cuboid to goal height), pick_and_place (move block over a fixed obstacle to target), stacking2 (stack 2 blocks), towers (build block tower matching goal shape), stacked_blocks / creative_stacked_blocks (restore/build arbitrary target silhouettes from blocks), general (restore an arbitrary scene configuration), reaching (3 fingertips to 3 goal points).
Primitivespush, reach, pick, pick-and-place, stack (2-block and multi-block towers), shape restoration / creative building from blocks. No open/close/insert/turn/press/slide — narrower primitive coverage than your existing 5-task set; it is depth-within-family, not breadth-across-skills.
RobotsTriFinger only (open-source 3-fingered manipulation platform from MPI). Not an arm; skills do not transfer conceptually to Franka gripper manipulation without full task redesign.
State observationsStructured (non-vision) mode is the DEFAULT and fully specified: time-left (1) + joint_positions (9) + joint_velocities (9) + end_effector_positions (9) = 28 base dims, plus 17 dims per tool object (type 1, size 3, cartesian pos 3, quaternion 4, lin vel 3, ang vel 3) and 11 dims per goal subshape (type, size, pos, orientation). Vision-free solving demonstrated in the paper (PPO/SAC/TD3 on structured obs), with the caveat that vanilla RL only partially solved pushing/picking and largely failed on the stacking families — dense per-generator reward functions exist but tasks are hard.
Action spaceJoint positions (default), joint torques, or delta joint positions for the 9-DoF TriFinger (3 fingers x 3 joints). No end-effector/Cartesian controller — another mismatch with your Franka Cartesian setup.
AssetsNo reusable assets. Objects are primitive cuboids/spheres instantiated programmatically in PyBullet (no mesh library). Robot is the TriFinger URDF (from MPI robot_properties_fingers, BSD-licensed, meshes available) — loadable into MuJoCo with moderate URDF-to-MJCF work, but it is not in MuJoCo Menagerie and is useless for a Franka-based suite.
LicenseCausalWorld: MIT. trifinger_edu URDF: MIT. trifinger_simulation: BSD-3-Clause. All permissive.
CL track recordNone found. The Continual RL review (arXiv:2012.13490) explicitly states CausalWorld 'does not directly consider continual learning.' Recent continual-policy-distillation work (e.g. arXiv:2601.22475) uses Meta-World, not CausalWorld. It has been used for transfer/curriculum learning (e.g. arXiv:2403.17266) and sim2real on the real TriFinger, but no CL benchmark lineage.
Port to mjlabThere is nothing to port directly: no MJCF, no mesh assets, tasks are PyBullet-programmatic, and the TriFinger embodiment does not match your Franka mjlab stack. The realistic path is re-implementation of the IDEA: write parametric task-generator classes in mjlab (randomized block size/mass/pose/goal-shape with intervention hooks) on your existing Franka scene — roughly 1-2 weeks to build a generator framework, after which each new task family is cheap. Porting CausalWorld itself (TriFinger URDF to MJCF + reward reimplementation) would be 2-4 weeks for a robot you do not want.
Scale potentialAs a codebase: 0 tasks for you (wrong robot/simulator). As a template: high — its intervention-space design (mass, friction, size, pose, goal shape as first-class controllable variables with train/test distribution splits A/B/C) is a proven recipe for generating dozens-to-hundreds of state-solvable task instances; applying that pattern to your 5 Franka tasks could plausibly yield 20-50 parametric variants, though variants within a family are weaker CL 'tasks' than distinct skills.
Gotchas1) Even in the original paper, vanilla model-free RL on structured obs failed to solve the stacking/tower families — teacher policies for distillation may be hard to obtain for the harder families. 2) 'Unbounded tasks' is marketing for parametric variation within ~5 skills; skill diversity is much lower than Meta-World. 3) Dormant repo, old gym API, Python/dependency rot likely on modern stacks. 4) 9-DoF trifinger action space and 3-fingertip kinematics make its reward/curriculum code non-transferable to a gripper arm.
Repo activity245 stars, 27 forks, last push 2022-11-01, last release Oct 2020 — dormant/unmaintained for ~4 years. Pinned to old gym API (pre-gymnasium) and old stable-baselines examples.
Merged duplicate assessments (1)
  • [usable] Structured-state-first procedural task engine with MIT license and portable robot URDF, but: (a) PyBullet-only — every task generator/reward must be re-implemented in mjlab; (b) only ~8 task FAMILIES (variants are parametric goal/property changes, not new skills), so it does not straightforwardly yield 20+ distinct skills; (c) TriFinger is a different embodiment than your Franka suite — 9-DoF 3-finger dexterous manipulation whose harder tasks (stacking, towers) were NOT solved even by the paper's own PPO/SAC/TD3 state-based baselines, risky for a pipeline needing reliable teacher policies; (d) prior-sweep claim of a Menagerie trifinger_edu MJCF is FALSE (404 — only a URDF in robot_descriptions and a dead 0-star community MJCF exist); (e) zero continual-learning track record. Usable only if you deliberately want a second embodiment plus parametric-variant scaling; otherwise close to reject for your Franka-centric scaling goal.
Reject

Continual Learning from Demonstration (CLfD / HN-NODE, HelloWorld + RoboTasks9)

paper · code

Continual learning-from-demonstration benchmark: sequences of end-effector trajectory-following skills (2D letters, 6D pose tasks) recorded on a real Franka and learned with hypernetwork-generated (stable) neural-ODE dynamics models; pure kinematic trajectory data, no simulator.

Continual Learning from Demonstration of Robotics Skills (RAS 2023) + Scalable and Efficient Continual Learning from Demonstration via a Hypernetwork-generated Stable Dynamics Model

Verdict: Wrong artifact type for our pipeline: it is a set of kinesthetic end-effector trajectory datasets (.npy pose trajectories) learned as neural-ODE motion models, not simulated environments. No simulator, no physics/contact, no objects, no rewards, no assets to port. Turning any of its 9 real manipulation motions into an mjlab task means building the environment from scratch, so it offers no savings and no scale. Only residual value: related-work/CL-baseline literature (hypernetwork-based continual LfD).

Full details
SimulatorNone. Data is real-robot kinesthetic demonstrations (Franka Emika Panda); evaluation is trajectory-reproduction error plus qualitative real-robot playback. No physics engine, no MuJoCo/PyBullet anywhere in the pipeline.
# TasksHelloWorld: 7 (2D letter trajectories h,e,l,o,w,r,d; 8 demos each, 1000 points). RoboTasks9: 9 (9 demos each, 1000 timesteps, 7D pos+quat). High-dim LASA: synthetic 2/8/16/32-D trajectory sets forming sequences up to 26 'tasks'. So 9 real manipulation-flavored tasks max; the 26 figure is synthetic letter-drawing, not manipulation.
Example tasksRoboTasks9: box opening, bottle shelving, plate stacking, pouring, mat folding, navigating, pan on stove, scooping, glass upright. HelloWorld: writing letters h, e, l, o, w, r, d.
PrimitivesTrajectory/pose-following motions only: box opening, bottle shelving, plate stacking, pouring, mat folding, navigating, pan-on-stove, scooping, glass upright, letter writing. Open-loop motion patterns with no object feedback or contact logic — not closed-loop push/pick/insert primitives.
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (real robot, kinesthetic teaching; Cartesian playback).
State observationsThere is no environment observation at all. Each 'task' is a dataset of demonstrated end-effector trajectories: HelloWorld is 2D (x,y); RoboTasks9 is 7D per timestep (px,py,pz + quaternion), learned as 6D (3D pos + 3D tangent-space rotation). No proprioception vector, no object poses, no reward. 'Solvable from state' is vacuous — the model reproduces a time-indexed pose trajectory from an initial pose.
Action spaceNot an RL benchmark; learned models output an end-effector velocity field / next pose (neural ODE over 2D or 6D pose) tracked by the Franka's Cartesian controller. No gripper action in the datasets.
Assets.npy trajectory arrays (HelloWorld 7x8x1000x2; RoboTasks9 9x9x1000x7 pos+quat), high-dim LASA .npy variants, mp4 demo videos, PyTorch hypernetwork/NODE code. No 3D assets, meshes, MJCF, or URDF. Only the hypernetwork/chunked-hypernetwork CL implementations are conceptually reusable (as CL baselines), not the data.
LicenseMIT (both repos).
CL track recordYes — this IS a continual-learning line of work: Auddy et al., 'Continual Learning from Demonstration of Robotics Skills' (RAS 2023, arXiv 2202.06843); 'Scalable and Efficient CL from Demonstration via a Hypernetwork-generated Stable Dynamics Model' (arXiv 2311.03600); follow-up arXiv 2401.00524 on hypernetwork optimizer/architecture effects. Compares HN/chunked-HN vs SI, MAS, replay, finetuning — but benchmarks CL of trajectory regression, not of control policies.
Port to mjlabNo porting path in the useful sense. Repos contain no MJCF/URDF, meshes, or scene descriptions — only .npy pose trajectories, mp4 videos, and PyTorch code. To make e.g. 'pouring' an mjlab task you would model scene, objects, rewards, and success criteria yourself; the dataset contributes only a nominal reference trajectory. Effort = building 9 tasks from scratch, i.e., no advantage.
Scale potentialEffectively zero: 9 real manipulation motions, extensible only by recording more real-robot demos. The 26-task sequences are synthetic high-dim LASA letters. Cannot generate state-solvable simulated manipulation tasks.
Gotchas(1) 'Tasks' means demonstrated motion patterns, not environments — success is trajectory error (DTW/Frechet/quaternion), no task-success criterion. (2) The headline '26 tasks' is synthetic 32-D LASA letter data, easily mistaken for manipulation scale. (3) Despite manipulation-sounding names there is no simulator; videos are open-loop replay on the real robot. (4) Prior-sweep github attribution was incorrect. (5) Worth citing as related work — their O(N) chunked-hypernetwork approach is conceptually adjacent to policy distillation — but it is not a task source.
Repo activityVery low traction: clfd-snode ~2 stars, 1 fork, 1 open issue; clfd similarly small. Research code, essentially frozen since publication.
Reject

CORA / CHORES

paper · code

Continual-RL platform (benchmarks + baselines + metrics); its robotics track CHORES is 4 short task sequences in AI2-THOR built from ALFRED trajectories, purely visual with discrete high-level actions.

CORA: Benchmarks, Baselines, and Metrics as a Platform for Continual Reinforcement Learning Agents (Powers et al., CoLLAs 2022, PMLR v199)

Verdict: Fails all three hard requirements: CHORES is vision-only (64x64x6 egocentric RGB + goal image, no privileged state vector exposed), runs in AI2-THOR (Unity, no physics-level manipulation — object interactions are scripted discrete actions like PutObject that auto-act on task-relevant objects), and assets are Unity scenes with no URDF/MJCF porting path. Task count is tiny (~12 task instances in 4 sequences). Only residual value: MIT-licensed implementations of CL metrics (Continual Evaluation, Isolated Forgetting, Zero-Shot Forward Transfer) and baselines (CLEAR, EWC, P&C, etc.), which could inform our evaluation code — but that is not a task source.

Full details
SimulatorAI2-THOR (Unity-based home simulator) for CHORES; Atari/Procgen/MiniHack(NetHack) for the game tracks. No MuJoCo anywhere.
# Tasks4 CHORES sequences, each with 3 task variants (~12 task instances total): Mem-VaryRoom (same task, 3 bathrooms), Mem-VaryTask (3 toilet-paper placements), Mem-VaryObject (clean fork/knife/spoon), Gen-MultiTraj (cool-and-place cup/potato slice/lettuce slice across kitchens). Plus non-robotic game benchmarks.
Example tasksPut hand towel in bathtub (rooms 402/419/423); hang toilet paper / put in cabinet / put on counter; clean fork/knife/spoon in kitchen sink; cool cup / sliced potato / sliced lettuce and place in sink.
PrimitivesNominally pick/place/clean/cool/slice/open, but executed as symbolic discrete actions (12 actions: MoveAhead, LookDown, PutObject, SliceObject, ...) where the simulator auto-selects the target object — not motor-level manipulation primitives.
RobotsNone in the manipulation sense — AI2-THOR embodied agent (floating camera/body); no Franka or any articulated arm.
State observationsObservation is (64,64,6): current egocentric RGB + RGB goal image. No low-level state observation mode is provided or demonstrated; there is no object-pose/proprioception vector because there is no articulated robot arm — the agent is a navigating camera with scripted interactions. Vision-free solving is not applicable/possible as shipped.
Action space12 discrete high-level actions (navigation: MoveAhead/RotateLeft/LookDown etc.; interaction: PutObject, SliceObject, PickupObject etc., auto-targeted to task-relevant object). No continuous joint or Cartesian control.
AssetsAI2-THOR Unity scene binaries + ALFRED task/trajectory JSON definitions. Not exportable to MuJoCo (no URDF/MJCF/mesh library exposed). Reusable parts are pure-Python: CL metrics and baseline implementations (IMPALA, CLEAR, EWC, Progress&Compress, Sane).
LicenseMIT (repo LICENSE); AI2-THOR itself is Apache-2.0, ALFRED MIT.
CL track recordYes — it IS a continual-RL platform (CoLLAs 2022). Used by the authors' follow-up SANE (Self-Activating Neural Ensembles, Powers et al.) and cited as a CL benchmark/metrics reference in later continual-RL papers; the Atari/Procgen tracks see more reuse than CHORES itself, whose baseline returns were near-floor (authors note advances in exploration/sample efficiency required).
Port to mjlabNo viable port. CHORES tasks are defined by ALFRED trajectory JSONs bound to AI2-THOR Unity scenes with scripted interaction semantics; recreating them in MuJoCo means rebuilding scenes, objects, and task logic from scratch — i.e., not a port at all. Effort to extract anything task-shaped: weeks-to-months for a handful of tasks, with no asset reuse. The only cheap lift (~1-2 days) is adapting their MIT-licensed CL metric code (Continual Evaluation / Isolated Forgetting / Zero-Shot Forward Transfer) to our mjlab distillation pipeline.
Scale potentialEffectively zero state-solvable manipulation tasks extractable. Even generously, the 4 sequences yield ~12 vision-only symbolic tasks; ALFRED's larger task pool could generate more sequences, but all inherit the same Unity/vision/discrete-action limitations.
GotchasPrior sweep info was accurate. Key gotchas: (1) "robotics" track is symbolic embodied AI, not physics manipulation — interaction actions auto-target objects, so there are no manipulation dynamics to learn; (2) goal specification is a goal IMAGE, so even task identity is visual; (3) repo is stale (last substantive activity ~2022-23) and pins old AI2-THOR versions; (4) baselines score near zero on CHORES, so it is not even a healthy vision benchmark. Do not spend further time on it beyond possibly borrowing metric definitions.
Repo activity~137 stars, 15 forks, ~1500 commits on develop; largely dormant since ~2022-2023.
Reject

CRIL task set (Meta-World subset)

paper · code

IROS 2021 continual-imitation method (GAN replay + dynamics prediction) evaluated on 10 Meta-World tasks with 64x64 RGB obs; the task set is a Meta-World subset, not a standalone benchmark.

CRIL: Continual Robot Imitation Learning via Generative and Prediction Model (IROS 2021), Gao, Gao, Guo, Zhang, Chen

Verdict: CRIL contributes NO environments or assets of its own — its "task set" is just 10 stock Meta-World v2 tasks wrapped with custom scripted experts, 64x64 top-view RGB observations, and a crude discrete 7-action space (fixed-magnitude XYZ moves + stop). Everything useful here (the 10 tasks, plus 40 more) is obtained by using Meta-World directly, which is MuJoCo-native and state-solvable. The repo is a 21-star, unmaintained (last push March 2021) method-code dump, not a benchmark. Prior-sweep info was wrong on both counts: simulator is MuJoCo (via Meta-World), not PyBullet, and the tasks are not original push/pick variants.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (via Meta-World / Sawyer envs) — NOT PyBullet as the prior sweep claimed. Real-robot experiments used a Kinova Jaco2, no sim assets involved.
# Tasks10 simulation tasks (all Meta-World v2) + 4 real-robot tasks (push, place, pick-over, stack; hardware only, no sim envs).
Example tasksreach-v2, button-press-v2, drawer-open-v2, door-open-v2, sweep-v2, push-v2, sweep-into-v2, coffee-button-v2, faucet-open-v2, window-open-v2 (verified in data_collect.py and the paper).
Primitivesreach, press (button, coffee-button), open (door, drawer, faucet, window), push, sweep, sweep-into; real robot adds place/pick/stack.
RobotsSim: Sawyer (Meta-World default). Real: 6-DoF Kinova Jaco2.
State observationsCRIL itself uses 3x64x64 top-view RGB images with a discrete 7-action space (6 fixed-step Cartesian moves + stop) — it is deliberately vision-based because its generative-replay method replays images. However, the underlying Meta-World tasks are fully state-solvable: Meta-World's native obs is a 39-dim state vector (proprioception + object/goal poses), and CRIL's own scripted experts operate on parsed hand/object/goal positions, proving vision-free solvability. So the state-solvability credit belongs to Meta-World, not CRIL.
Action spaceDiscrete, 7 actions: +/-fixed-step in X/Y/Z plus stop (CRIL's wrapper); underlying Meta-World is continuous 4D (xyz delta + gripper).
AssetsNo original assets. All scenes/objects come from Meta-World (MuJoCo MJCF). Repo contains method code (WGAN-GP, dynamics predictor, BC) plus custom scripted policies; MIT licensed but nothing asset-wise worth extracting.
LicenseMIT (repo). Meta-World itself is also MIT.
CL track recordCRIL is itself a continual-imitation-learning paper (sequential 10-task Meta-World stream with generative replay). Its specific 10-task ordering is occasionally cited as an early continual-IL baseline, but it never became a reused benchmark; the community standardized on Continual World / CW10-CW20 (also Meta-World-based) instead.
Port to mjlabNothing to port. The environments ARE Meta-World (already MuJoCo). Porting Meta-World's Sawyer tasks to mjlab's Franka + manager API is a separate effort that should target Meta-World directly (its MJCF scenes/objects are plain MuJoCo XML, reusable per-object; retarget robot + rewrite rewards, roughly 0.5-1 day per task after the first). CRIL's repo adds only scripted policies hard-coded to its discrete action space — not reusable.
Scale potentialZero incremental scale beyond Meta-World: CRIL selects 10 of Meta-World's 50 tasks. If you want 20-50 state-solvable MuJoCo tasks, evaluate Meta-World (ML1/MT50) as the candidate; CRIL is subsumed by it.
Gotchas1) Prior sweep info was doubly wrong (PyBullet, "~10 simple tabletop push/pick tasks") — it is Meta-World/MuJoCo with standard Meta-World tasks. 2) The GitHub-README framing hides the Meta-World dependency; you only see it in imports (from tests.metaworld.envs.mujoco.sawyer_xyz...), which target an old Meta-World commit — code likely broken against current Meta-World. 3) CRIL's pipeline is image-based by design; do not confuse the method's obs with task solvability. 4) Real-robot tasks (place/pick-over/stack) have no sim counterparts in the repo.
Repo activity21 stars, 1 fork, 0 open issues; created Feb 2021, last push Mar 2021 — effectively abandoned method code. Authors note it "cannot run with only one click."
Reject

DexJoCo

paper · code

MuJoCo IL/VLA benchmark of 11 dexterous Allegro-hand tasks (6 single-arm, 5 bimanual) with 1.1K teleop demos and a pi0.5 training pipeline — demo-driven, no rewards, no RL, no GPU-parallel sim.

DexJoCo: A Benchmark and Toolkit for Task-Oriented Dexterous Manipulation on MuJoCo

Verdict: Wrong tool for our pipeline on three counts: (1) it is an imitation-learning/VLA benchmark with NO reward functions and no RL training support — every task would need a hand-written dense reward before our RL-teacher + distillation pipeline could use it; (2) the embodiment is Franka + 16-DoF Allegro dexterous hand (single and bimanual), not a parallel gripper — action space is 22D/44D, and the tasks (Hanoi, bimanual assembly, fold glasses) were explicitly chosen to be things grippers CANNOT do, so state-based RL solvability is unproven and likely very hard; (3) only 11 tasks (6 single-arm), CPU MuJoCo only, no MJX/mujoco-warp, so no scaling story. Its MJCF scenes (microwave, mouse, tongs, glasses, plant, hammer/nail) are technically scavengeable, but each object is tied to a hand-centric task design, so even assets-only value is marginal versus dedicated object libraries.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (plain CPU MuJoCo; no MJX or mujoco-warp support found in repo or paper)
# Tasks11 total (verified on project page): 6 single-arm + 5 bimanual. Domain-randomization variants exist for evaluation but are not distinct tasks.
Example tasksSingle-arm: Water Plant, Hammer Nail, Click Mouse, Pick Bucket, Pinch Tongs, Fold Glasses. Bimanual: Microwave (cook), Unlock iPad, Hanoi, Assembly, Photograph.
PrimitivesTool-use (hammer, tongs, watering can), press/click, pick, fold, insert/assemble, open (microwave), unlock/swipe, long-horizon stacking (Hanoi), bimanual coordination. Deliberately chosen to require finger dexterity beyond parallel-gripper primitives.
RobotsFranka Emika Panda arm(s) + Wonik Allegro 16-DoF hand; bimanual dual-arm setup for 5 tasks. No parallel-gripper variant.
State observationsProprioceptive state: TCP pose (7D) + Allegro hand joint angles (16D) = 23D single-arm, 46D bimanual; actions are absolute EE pose + hand joints (22D/44D rot-vec). Privileged environment state (object poses, table height) is stored for demo replay but explicitly FILTERED OUT during policy training — policies are trained vision+proprio. No paper result demonstrates vision-free solving, and with no reward functions there is no state-based RL path out of the box.
Action spaceAbsolute end-effector pose (rotation-vector) + 16 hand joint targets: 22D single-arm, 44D bimanual.
AssetsMJCF XML scenes and models (Franka Apache-2.0, Allegro BSD-2-Clause, presumably from mujoco_menagerie); demo data in Zarr (video, depth, state) plus LeRobot-format converter; 1.1K human teleop trajectories.
LicenseCore code MIT; third-party robot models Apache-2.0 (Franka) and BSD-2-Clause (Allegro). Demo-data license not stated.
CL track recordNone. Paper evaluates VLA/IL policies (pi0.5, multi-task training, action-head adaptation, domain randomization); no continual-learning usage found — the benchmark is under two months old.
Port to mjlabAssets are MJCF already, so scene XMLs load in MuJoCo/mjlab trivially. But making tasks usable for us means: swapping Allegro hand for our Franka gripper (breaks most task designs), writing dense rewards + success/termination for each task from scratch, and rewrapping in mjlab's manager API. Effort roughly equals authoring new tasks ourselves; est. 1-2 days per task with uncertain solvability for the dexterity-dependent ones.
Scale potentialVery low: 11 tasks ceiling, only ~6 plausibly adaptable to a single arm, no procedural task generation, no object library beyond the 11 scenes. Cannot get us to 20+ tasks.
GotchasPrior sweep's claim of 'RL support' is wrong — the arXiv abstract's 'benchmarks modern models' refers to VLA/IL models, and the repo contains no reward functions or RL code (verified via repo tree: configs/, dexjoco/, openpi/, teleoperation/ — nothing RL). 'Privileged state available' is true only for demo replay; it is filtered during training. Also note the hand-centric task designs mean tasks mostly stop making sense with a parallel gripper.
Repo activity~149 stars, 14 forks, ~22 commits on main — very new (paper May 2026), shallow commit history, single release cycle; immature.
Reject

GemBench (robot-3dlotus)

paper · code

RLBench-derived vision-language generalization benchmark: 16 train tasks (31 vars) + 44 test tasks (92 vars) over 7 primitives, running on CoppeliaSim via a custom RLBench fork.

Towards Generalizable Vision-Language Robotic Manipulation: A Benchmark and LLM-guided 3D Policy (GemBench + 3D-LOTUS/3D-LOTUS++), ICRA 2025

Verdict: CoppeliaSim/PyRep-only with proprietary binary .ttm assets that have no automated path to MJCF; porting means re-authoring every task by hand, at which point GemBench contributes nothing beyond a curated task list. Add the restrictive Imperial College non-commercial RLBench licence, no MuJoCo story, and zero continual-learning track record. Its 60-task taxonomy (7 primitives x rigid/articulated objects) is worth copying as a DESIGN TEMPLATE for new mjlab tasks, but the software/assets themselves fail hard requirement 2.

Full details
SimulatorCoppeliaSim 4.1 via PyRep, using a modified RLBench fork (github.com/rjgpinel/RLBench) that adds the new GemBench tasks. No MuJoCo support of any kind.
# Tasks60 distinct tasks / 123 variations total: 16 train (31 vars) + 44 test (92 vars), test split into L1 novel placements (16/31), L2 novel rigid objects (15/28), L3 novel articulated objects (18/21), L4 long-horizon (6/12). Verified against assets/taskvars_*.json in the repo and the paper.
Example tasksTrain: push_button, open_drawer, open_door, close_fridge, close_laptop_lid, close_microwave, pick_and_lift, pick_up_cup, stack_blocks, put_groceries_in_cupboard, put_money_in_safe, slide_block_to_color_target, reach_and_drag, close_jar, light_bulb_in. Test L3: open_fridge, open_microwave, open_laptop_lid, close_door, close_box, close_drawer, close_grill, toilet_seat_up, open_drawer_long.
Primitivespress, pick, push, screw, close, open, put (paper's 7 primitives); in practice also slide, drag, stack, lift, insert (light bulb, money-in-safe).
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (7-DoF + parallel gripper), single arm, tabletop.
State observationsGemBench's own policies use multi-view RGB-D point clouds + proprioception (joint and gripper poses) — it is vision-first by design. However, the underlying RLBench engine exposes privileged low-dim state (task_low_dim_state with object poses, joint positions, gripper state), and every demo is generated by scripted waypoint motion planning with no vision, so essentially all tasks are solvable from state. No state-only RL baselines are reported in the paper, though; state-solvability is inherited from RLBench, not demonstrated by GemBench.
Action spaceKeystep/end-effector pose actions (position + quaternion + gripper open) executed via RLBench motion planner; RLBench also supports joint-space control modes.
AssetsCoppeliaSim .ttm binary scene/model files + Python task scripts (PyRep API) in the rjgpinel/RLBench fork; generated datasets on HuggingFace (rjgpinel/GEMBench) are processed point clouds + CLIP instruction embeddings, useless for state-based RL. No URDF/MJCF/plain-mesh assets. Not reusable in MuJoCo without manual mesh extraction.
Licenserobot-3dlotus repo: no LICENSE file (all rights reserved by default). rjgpinel/RLBench fork: Imperial College London RLBench licence — non-commercial/internal academic research only, restrictions on derivative distribution. Verified from the LICENSE file text.
CL track recordNone found. GemBench is a train-once/test-on-unseen generalization benchmark, not a task-sequence benchmark; web search surfaced no continual-learning papers using it (LIBERO and Continual World remain the standard CL suites).
Port to mjlabNo automated path. Task scenes are CoppeliaSim .ttm binary models plus Python task scripts calling PyRep APIs. Porting requires manually exporting meshes from each .ttm via the CoppeliaSim GUI, re-authoring MJCF (joints, contacts, sensors), and rewriting success conditions/rewards in mjlab — realistically 1-3 days per task, i.e., re-implementing from scratch with the GemBench task list as a spec. Nothing GemBench-specific transfers; you would be porting RLBench itself.
Scale potentialAs a resource for mjlab: near zero directly. As a template: the 60-task taxonomy (esp. L3's articulated-object family — fridge, microwave, laptop lid, grill, toilet seat, drawers, doors, boxes) maps well onto our existing open-door/open-drawer style tasks and could guide authoring 20-40 mjlab tasks, but every one must be built by hand. The full parent RLBench library (~100 tasks) has the same portability wall.
Gotchas1) Sweep's GitHub link is stale (moved to vlc-robot org). 2) 'State-solvable' is inherited from RLBench's privileged state + scripted demos, not demonstrated in the paper — GemBench itself never trains from state. 3) Requires CoppeliaSim 4.1 + custom PyRep + custom RLBench forks pinned to Ubuntu 20.04-era binaries; headless eval is finicky. 4) Non-commercial licence on the task assets. 5) taskvars_train.json actually lists 32 variation instances vs the paper's 31 — trivial off-by-one, counts otherwise match.
Repo activityvlc-robot/robot-3dlotus: 130 stars, 17 forks, last push 2026-05-26 (active, maintained). The rjgpinel/robot-3dlotus URL from the sweep now 301-redirects.
Reject

Genesis / Genesis World 1.0

paper · code

Universal multi-physics simulator (rigid+FEM+MPM+PBD/SPH+IPC) with photorealistic rendering; impressive engine but essentially zero curated manipulation tasks in the open-source release.
