Genesis: A Universal and Generative Physics Engine for Robotics and Beyond (2024 project release; no standalone arXiv benchmark paper). Genesis World 1.0 announced via blog, May 2026.

Verdict: It is a simulator, not a task benchmark: the open-source repo ships only ~4 manipulation example files (a single Franka grasp env + behavior cloning script), and Genesis World 1.0's advertised "hundreds of tasks" evaluation suite is internal/proprietary and not released. Using it would mean writing every task ourselves in a non-MuJoCo engine, which is strictly worse than staying in mjlab. No usable asset library either — it just parses standard formats.

Full details
SimulatorGenesis's own unified multi-physics engine (built on the Quadrants Python-to-GPU compiler, formerly Taichi-derived) — NOT MuJoCo and not MuJoCo-compatible at the physics level. GPU-parallel like MJX/warp.
# Tasks~1-4 open-source manipulation examples (grasp env, grasp train/eval, behavior_cloning) plus locomotion/drone examples. The "hundreds of tasks" cited for Genesis World 1.0 evaluation are internal to Genesis AI and NOT publicly released as of July 2026. No public curated benchmark exists — prior sweep info confirmed correct.
Example tasksgrasp (Franka cube grasping via grasp_env.py/grasp_train.py), behavior cloning on grasping, differentiable push demo, cloth manipulation demo, Go2 locomotion, drone hovering, smoke/liquid demos.
PrimitivesOpen-source examples: grasp/pick only (plus a differentiable push demo). Internal (unreleased) suite reportedly spans soft-body and rigid manipulation (cloth, string, deformables) across dexterous hands, but none of this is available to use.
RobotsExamples: Franka (grasping, cloth), Unitree Go2, drones. Genesis AI internally: Wuji, Sharpa, Genesis hand, Pika gripper, Tianji arm, Unitree G1 humanoid (none released as task suites).
State observationsThe example grasp_env exposes full sim state (it is a from-scratch env; you query any body pose/joint state via the scene API), so state-based solving is possible in principle — but there is no defined benchmark observation spec. Genesis World 1.0's own evaluation framing is vision-first (camera frames + proprioception; RGB/depth/tactile/lidar sensors emphasized). No demonstrated vision-free task suite.
Action spaceEnv-defined by the user; example grasp env uses joint/EE control of a Franka. No standardized action-space convention across a benchmark (because there is no benchmark).
AssetsParses URDF, MJCF, OBJ, GLB, USD, terrain, etc., but ships no object/asset library of its own beyond a few demo meshes (dragon, cloth, Franka). Nothing reusable for task generation in mjlab.
LicenseApache 2.0 (main Genesis repo; Nyx renderer and Quadrants compiler also open-sourced).
CL track recordNo continual-learning papers found that use Genesis as their task suite. CL policy-distillation literature (DisCoRL 2019, soft-robot CPD 2024, distributed-teacher CPD 2026) uses other simulators. Genesis appears in RL-speed papers (e.g. FlashSAC) as a fast engine, not as a CL benchmark.
Port to mjlabNothing to port: there are no task assets or scene definitions worth extracting. Genesis loads MJCF/URDF (so the format direction is Genesis←MuJoCo, useful for them not us). Effort to get 20+ tasks from Genesis = writing 20+ envs from scratch in a different engine, then they'd still be Genesis-native. Not a viable path to mjlab.
Scale potentialNear zero from released code. Their blog describes an automated programmatic environment-generation pipeline (scene layout, asset selection, task spec, success metrics), but it is internal to Genesis AI and unreleased. We cannot realistically extract even 5 state-solvable tasks today.
Gotchas(1) "Genesis World 1.0" marketing implies a large task/eval suite; the actual open release is only the engine + renderer + compiler — the task suite and programmatic env-generation pipeline are proprietary to Genesis AI's commercial evaluation service. (2) Physics is not MuJoCo — sim-behavior differences would make any teacher policies non-transferable to mjlab. (3) Early Genesis speed claims (430,000x) were widely disputed; treat performance claims skeptically. (4) The company pivot (genesis.ai) means open-source priorities may drift toward their paid platform.
Repo activity~29.5k stars, ~2.8k forks, very active: latest release v1.2.1 (July 3, 2026), 30 releases, ~1.5k commits on main.
Reject

Gymnasium-Robotics Shadow Dexterous Hand

paper · code

MuJoCo-native goal-conditioned in-hand reorientation of block/egg/pen with a Shadow Hand, from OpenAI's 2018 multi-goal robotics envs, now maintained by Farama.

Multi-Goal Reinforcement Learning: Challenging Robotics Environments and Request for Research (Plappert et al., 2018)

Verdict: Wrong embodiment and wrong skill axis: all tasks are in-hand reorientation with a 24-DoF Shadow Hand, not Franka-arm manipulation primitives. Only ~9 truly distinct tasks (the ~30-50 env IDs are sensor/reward permutations of the same tasks), no continual-learning track record, and the hard ones (Pen, Full position+rotation) are notoriously difficult even for dedicated RL. Cannot contribute to a 20-100 task Franka primitive suite; even the assets (hand MJCF, block/egg/pen) are useless for an arm-based benchmark.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (native MJCF, modern DeepMind `mujoco` bindings; no mujoco-py). Actively maintained by Farama-Foundation.
# Tasks~9 distinct tasks: HandReach + Block x {RotateZ, RotateParallel, RotateXYZ, Full} + Egg x {Rotate, Full} + Pen x {Rotate, Full}. Env-ID count inflates to ~30-50 via BooleanTouchSensors/ContinuousTouchSensors and Dense-reward variants, but those are obs/reward permutations, not new skills.
Example tasksHandReach-v2, HandManipulateBlockRotateZ-v1, HandManipulateBlockRotateParallel-v1, HandManipulateBlockRotateXYZ-v1, HandManipulateBlockFull-v1, HandManipulateEggFull-v1, HandManipulatePenRotate-v1, plus _BooleanTouchSensors/_ContinuousTouchSensors and Dense reward variants of each.
PrimitivesSingle skill family: in-hand reorientation (rotate object to goal orientation, optionally goal position) + fingertip reaching. No push/pick/place/open/insert/press — none of the arm-manipulation primitives the project needs.
RobotsShadow Dexterous Hand (24 DoF, 20 actuated joints) — hand only, no arm. Incompatible embodiment with the project's Franka Panda.
State observationsFully state-based and vision-free by design: observation is 61-dim (24 joint angles + 24 joint velocities + 6 object linear/angular velocities + 7 object pose pos+quat), plus achieved_goal/desired_goal 7-dim pose each. Original OpenAI results (HER/PPO) were trained purely from this state, so state-solvability is demonstrated — though HandManipulatePen and Full (pos+rot) variants remain hard to solve reliably even from state.
Action spaceBox(-1,1)^20: absolute target angular positions for the 20 actuated joints (wrist + fingers + thumb).
AssetsMJCF XML (hand model + block/egg/pen objects), MIT-licensed, directly loadable in MuJoCo/MJX. Reusable in principle, but only useful if you want a dexterous hand embodiment; objects themselves are trivial primitives.
LicenseMIT
CL track recordEssentially none. Widely used for goal-conditioned RL / HER research (the 2018 OpenAI multi-goal paper), but no notable continual-learning paper builds its task sequence on the Shadow Hand suite; CL manipulation work standardized on Meta-World/Continual World instead.
Port to mjlabTechnically trivial physics-wise: MJCF assets load directly in MuJoCo/MJX. But porting to mjlab means adding a Shadow Hand embodiment (24 DoF, 20 actuators) alongside the Franka — a whole new robot model, action space, and controller stack — for tasks that don't share primitives with the existing suite. Effort is moderate but pointless for this project.
Scale potentialPoor. The goal distribution is a dense goal generator within each task, but for continual learning across tasks it yields at most ~9 distinct tasks, all the same skill on 3 objects. Swapping in more object meshes could generate more reorientation variants, but never the push/open/insert diversity required; realistically 0 usable tasks for a Franka primitive suite.
GotchasSparse reward (-1/0) by default with tight success thresholds (0.1 rad, 1 cm) — needs HER or dense variants. Prior-sweep task count of 12-18 conflates env-ID permutations with distinct tasks; real skill count is ~9. Pen task has low success rates even in the original paper. Docs pages omit some spec details; per-env pages (e.g. manipulate_block) have the full obs breakdown.
Repo activity~939 stars, 136 forks; actively maintained — v1.4.2 released Jan 2026, 15 releases total.
Reject

LEGION lifelong task sequence (Meta-World-derived, Ghiara/LEGION)

paper · code

Nature MI 2025 lifelong-RL paper's task suite: a 10-task Meta-World-v1 sequence ported to a KUKA iiwa R800 (sim + real), trained one-task-at-a-time with a Dirichlet-process (DPMM) knowledge space plus language embeddings.

Preserving and combining knowledge in robotic lifelong reinforcement learning (Nature Machine Intelligence, 2025)

Verdict: Adds zero tasks beyond Meta-World: it is a 10-task Meta-World v1 sequence re-rigged onto a KUKA iiwa R800 (NOT Franka — prior sweep was wrong), running on a dead stack (mujoco-py 2.0.2.8 / mujoco200 / gym 0.20, old-Metaworld API). If we want these tasks we should port them from upstream Farama Metaworld (modern MuJoCo 3, 50 tasks, actively maintained) instead of this fork. LEGION's value is purely as a reference point: a 2025 Nature MI lifelong-RL flagship evaluated on exactly a 10-task state-based Meta-World sequence, which validates that our 10-20-task Franka plan is at/above the published state of the art.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo 2.0 via mujoco-py==2.0.2.8 (requires mujoco200 binary), gym==0.20, custom Meta-World fork "Metaworld-KUKA-IIWA-R800". Legacy old-Metaworld codebase (HARD_MODE/EASY_MODE env dicts), pre-dates the modern Farama Metaworld rewrite.
# Tasks10 in the lifelong sequence (verified in mtrl/logger.py and config/env/metaworld-mt10.yaml): reach, push, pick-place, door-open, faucet-open, drawer-close, button-press-topdown, peg-unplug-side, window-open, window-close. Exactly 11 KUKA env classes exist (the 10 + door base). MT50 configs exist but only for the default Sawyer arm.
Example tasksreach-v1, push-v1, pick-place-v1, door-open-v1, faucet-open-v1, drawer-close-v1, button-press-topdown-v1, peg-unplug-side-v1, window-open-v1, window-close-v1; deployment demo: long-horizon "clean the table" composed from learned skills on the real KUKA.
Primitivesreach, push, pick-place, open (door/window/faucet/drawer via MT50-Sawyer), close (drawer/window), press (button), unplug/insert (peg). Long-horizon "clean the table" demo is composed from the 10 learned skills at deployment (skill sequencing, not a trained task).
RobotsKUKA iiwa 14 R800 with gripper (LEGION experiments, sim + real); the fork also retains original Sawyer envs (config flag use_kuka_env, default False). No Franka — the prior sweep's "Franka tabletop" claim is incorrect.
State observationsPure low-level state, vision-free (verified in metaworld/envs/mujoco/sawyer_xyz/base.py _get_obs): 12-dim = end-effector xyz (3) + object position padded to 6 + goal xyz (3); goal zeroed if partially observable. LEGION additionally feeds a frozen-LLM language embedding of the task description into the DPMM task-inference module (not needed for control). All 10 tasks were solved from this state obs with off-policy SAC-style RL — strong evidence of state-solvability, but that was already known from Meta-World itself.
Action spaceMeta-World-style 4-D end-effector delta control: xyz displacement + gripper open/close (inherited from Meta-World v1 base envs).
AssetsMJCF XML throughout (279 XMLs in the Metaworld-KUKA fork; kuka_xyz/ and sawyer_xyz/ asset trees) — but these are just Meta-World's own table-top objects (drawer, door, window, faucet, button, peg, puck) with a KUKA arm swapped in; nothing new over upstream Meta-World. Reusable in principle, redundant in practice.
LicenseMIT (both Ghiara/LEGION and Ghiara/Metaworld-KUKA-IIWA-R800; verified LICENSE badge/file). Codebase is a fork of facebookresearch/mtrl (also MIT).
CL track recordYes — this IS a continual/lifelong-learning paper (Nature MI 2025): one-task-at-a-time training on the 10-task stream with DPMM-based knowledge preservation, forward/backward transfer analysis (t-SNE snapshots at 2/4/6/8/10 tasks), and real-robot skill recombination. Its baselines are standard mtrl multi-task/CL methods. No evidence other CL papers have adopted this specific KUKA suite; the community uses plain Meta-World (e.g. Continual World) instead.
Port to mjlabAssets are plain MJCF XML (279 XML files, e.g. metaworld/envs/assets/kuka_xyz/kuka_drawer.xml), so mechanically portable. But the sensible path is NOT via this repo: port task logic/objects from upstream Farama Metaworld (already MuJoCo 3 / gymnasium, Sawyer arm) and re-mount on our mjlab Franka + manager API. Effort per task roughly comparable to what we already did for open-drawer/push-button (days per task for reward shaping + success criteria); LEGION's KUKA-specific XMLs and mujoco-py-era Python classes would need full rewrites anyway, so they save nothing.
Scale potentialLow as a direct source: only 10 KUKA tasks exist; the MT50 configs fall back to stock Sawyer Meta-World, i.e. the real scaling resource is Meta-World itself (50 tasks), which should be evaluated on its own merits, not through this fork. LEGION contributes no additional tasks, objects, or generation machinery.
Gotchas1) Robot is KUKA iiwa R800, not Franka, contradicting the prior sweep. 2) Built on Meta-World v1 envs (12-dim obs) with mujoco-py 2.0 / mujoco200 — deprecated, painful to even install today. 3) The "long-horizon composability" is a deployment-time skill-sequencing demo, not extra trainable tasks. 4) Nature link redirect-loops for anonymous fetchers (idp.nature.com cookie wall) — use the Research Square preprint. 5) LEGION's language embeddings are for task inference in the DPMM, not required for solving tasks; do not mistake it for a language benchmark.
Repo activityModest research-code repo (official Nature MI code release, Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.14265088); env fork has ~747 commits but is a frozen fork of old Meta-World. No sign of ongoing maintenance beyond the paper release.
Reject

MuBlE (SHOP-VRB2 benchmark)

paper · code

MuJoCo(robosuite)-physics + Blender-photoreal-rendering environment with the SHOP-VRB2 benchmark: 10 classes of multi-step language/reasoning scenarios (weigh, move, stack household objects) for a Franka, solved via scripted motion primitives + symbolic planners, not learned control.

MuBlE: MuJoCo and Blender simulation Environment and Benchmark for Task Planning in Robot Manipulation (2025)

Verdict: It is a task-PLANNING/reasoning benchmark, not a skill benchmark: the 10 task classes differ in language/reasoning (weigh, count, order-by-weight) while all motor behavior is executed by a fixed scripted primitive controller (move/approach/grip/lift/weigh/squeeze). Underlying manipulation skills reduce to pick, place, stack, weigh — ~3-4 primitives we already cover. No RL interface, no CL usage, tiny/inactive repo (4 commits). Blender rendering is irrelevant to state-based distillation. Household-object MuJoCo assets are the only marginally reusable part, but YCB-style objects are easier to get elsewhere.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo physics via robosuite (MuJoCo-native), with a separate Blender module for photorealistic rendering. Two-level loop: control-physics (MuJoCo) and visual-action (Blender renders after each primitive).
# Tasks10 task classes (SHOP-VRB2) instantiated into 12,000 procedurally generated scenes (10k/1k/1k train/val/test), 4-5 objects per scene; plus 30 YCB scenes for sim-to-real. Some sources cite "24 tasks" but the paper's Table II lists 10 multi-step reasoning classes. Distinct MOTOR skills: ~4.
Example tasks"Measure the weight of the mug"; "Place the bowl on the left part of the table"; "Pick up the heaviest of all glasses"; "Stack the plate on top of the bowl on top of the container"; "Stack all mugs from heaviest to lightest".
PrimitivesReasoning-level: weigh (single/multiple), pick-by-weight, move single/multiple, move-by-weight, stack 2/3 objects, stack-by-weight, order-by-weight. Motor-level primitives (fixed controller): move, approach, close_gripper, open_gripper, lift, lower, weigh, squeeze. Net manipulation skills: pick, place, stack, weigh/squeeze.
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (7-DoF) with 2-finger parallel gripper; sim-to-real validated on a real Panda with YCB objects.
State observationsYes — symbolic scene graphs with positions, orientations, and physical measurements (weight, stiffness) for all objects, plus gripper status and end-effector pose; ground-truth segmentation/depth also available. However, "state-solvable" here means solvable by a symbolic PLANNER over scene graphs (best baseline PDDLController hits 82.9%); no state-based RL policy training is demonstrated or supported out of the box (no reward functions, no gym-style RL loop).
Action spaceDiscrete symbolic primitive actions (move/approach/grip/lift/lower/weigh/squeeze) executed by a built-in trajectory controller with obstacle avoidance; not joint/Cartesian continuous control for learning.
AssetsHousehold objects (bowls, plates, glasses, mugs, containers) as paired MuJoCo models (physics) + .blend models (procedural photoreal materials); YCB objects for real-world scenes; scene/instruction/sequence generators included. MuJoCo-side assets are reusable in mjlab; Blender assets are useless for state-based work.
LicenseMIT (repo); paper arXiv 2503.02834.
CL track recordNone found. Paper (Mar 2025) benchmarks 9 planning/embodied-reasoning baselines (best: PDDLController, 82.9%); no continual-learning or policy-distillation papers use it as of Jul 2026.
Port to mjlabPhysics side is already MuJoCo (robosuite MJCF scenes + Franka), so object MJCF/mesh assets could be dropped into mjlab scenes with low effort (days). But there are no reward functions, no RL env wrappers, and tasks are defined as instruction+planner-primitive sequences — turning them into mjlab RL tasks means writing every reward/termination/reset from scratch, i.e. you'd only be importing objects, not tasks.
Scale potentialSuperficially 12,000 scenes, but they are combinatorial variations of ~4 motor skills over household objects. Realistically extractable distinct state-solvable RL tasks for our purposes: ~3-5 (pick, place-on-region, stack-2, stack-3), most of which overlap with our existing 5 tasks. Does not get us to 20+.
Gotchas(1) Prior sweep's "low tens of tasks" is misleading — 10 reasoning classes share ~4 motor skills, so skill diversity is near-zero for our purposes. (2) No RL training support: primitives are scripted, no rewards/gym API. (3) Half the tasks hinge on weight/stiffness measurement via a "weigh"/"squeeze" primitive — a sensing gimmick, not a manipulation skill. (4) Built on robosuite, so an extra dependency layer between the MJCF assets and raw MuJoCo. (5) Blender rendering pipeline is heavyweight and entirely orthogonal to state-based training.
Repo activity~50 stars, 4 forks, only 4 commits on main — essentially a one-shot code release with minimal maintenance activity.
Reject

MuJoCo Manipulus

paper · code: NONE. Claimed site https://mujoco-manipulus.github.io returns 404; github user/org 'mujoco-manipulus' does not exist; GitHub API search for 'manipulus' finds no robotics repo. Prior sweep's 'see OpenReview for code link' does not resolve to any working code.

Anonymous OpenReview submission claiming 16 MuJoCo tool-manipulation tasks (pour/scoop/scrape/stack/gather/hammer/golf/ping-pong) with state+vision obs — but the claimed code release is a dead link and no repository exists anywhere.

MuJoCo Manipulus: A Robot Learning Benchmark for Generalizable Tool Manipulation (anonymous OpenReview submission; mentions ICLR Code of Ethics, likely ICLR 2026; decision status unverifiable)

Verdict: Code does not exist publicly. The paper's stated project site (mujoco-manipulus.github.io) is a 404, the GitHub org does not exist, no snapshot was ever archived, and a GitHub-wide search for 'manipulus' returns nothing robotics-related. The paper is an anonymous OpenReview submission (ICLR, decision unverifiable behind Cloudflare) with no arXiv version, zero citations, and zero adoption. There is nothing to use: no repo, no assets, no license, no verifiable state-obs spec. Additionally, its signature tasks (pouring granular media, ping-pong, mini-golf) are dynamic/particle-heavy and would be poor fits for MJX/mujoco-warp GPU parallelization even if code appeared. Revisit only if a public release materializes.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (classic CPU MuJoCo via Gymnasium API, per abstract). No evidence of MJX/mujoco-warp support.
# Tasks16 claimed (variants over 8 task families, using 14 tools). Unverifiable — task list beyond family names could not be inspected.
Example tasksPouring (from cup/pot), Scooping, Scraping (spatula), Stacking, Gathering (brush), Hammering, Mini-Golf (golf club), Ping-Pong (paddle) — 16 variants across these 8 families per the abstract; exact per-variant list not accessible.
PrimitivesTool-use primitives: pour, scoop, scrape, stack, gather, hammer/strike, putt (mini-golf), hit (ping-pong). Genuinely rare primitives, but mostly dynamic/contact-rich rather than quasi-static.
RobotsUnverified; robot arm not identifiable from accessible sources (abstract snippets do not name the arm).
State observationsAbstract claims both state-based and vision-based observation spaces are supported, implying tasks are intended to be state-solvable. However, exact contents/dimensions and whether RL from state actually solves each task are unverifiable: the PDF is Cloudflare-gated on OpenReview, there is no arXiv version, and no code exists to read. Treat 'state-solvable' as an unvalidated claim.
Action spaceUnverified (paper inaccessible); presumably arm joint/EE control via Gymnasium continuous actions.
Assets14 tool models claimed (kitchen: bowl, mug, knife, pan, pot, plate, spatula, ladle, cup; home: brush, hammer, scooper; sports: ping-pong paddle, golf club). Format presumably MJCF+meshes, but none are downloadable — assets are not reusable because nothing is released.
LicenseUnknown — no repository, no license file, no statement accessible.
CL track recordNone. No citations found, no continual-learning papers use it, no third-party usage of any kind detected.
Port to mjlabNo porting path exists — there is no code or asset release to port. If it were released as claimed (MuJoCo MJCF + Gymnasium), env-logic port to mjlab would be moderate, but pouring (particles/liquid proxy), ping-pong, and mini-golf involve many-body dynamics that scale badly on MJX/warp GPU-parallel sims. Effort: N/A today.
Scale potentialOn paper: 8 task families x 14 tools could combinatorially yield 20-50 tasks. In practice: zero, because nothing is released. Do not plan around this resource.
Gotchas1) The 'open-source' claim is currently false in practice: the project website is dead and no code repo exists anywhere. 2) OpenReview itself is Cloudflare-gated, so even the paper PDF cannot be programmatically retrieved; decision status unknown. 3) Dynamic tool tasks (pouring particles, ping-pong rallies, golf putts) are ill-suited to MJX/mujoco-warp large-batch training and to quasi-static distillation pipelines. 4) Do not confuse with DexJoCo (arXiv 2605.16257), a separate dexterous-hand MuJoCo benchmark that surfaced in the same searches and may itself be worth a look.
Repo activityN/A — no repository exists.

MyoSuite

paper · code

MuJoCo-native musculoskeletal (muscle-actuated) motor-control suite; state-solvable but action space and assets do not fit torque/position-controlled Franka manipulation.

MyoSuite: A contact-rich simulation suite for musculoskeletal motor control (arXiv:2205.13600, 2022)

Verdict: State-solvable and MuJoCo-native, but the entire suite is built on MUSCLE-ACTUATED musculoskeletal models (Hill-type muscles, action = per-muscle activation in [0,1], 39 muscles for the hand, muscles pull-only). This is a fundamentally different action space and physics from a torque/position-controlled Franka arm. Neither the policies, the actuation, nor the anatomical assets (bones/tendons/muscle wrapping) transfer to our Franka-centric mjlab distillation setup. It is a dexterous-hand motor-control suite, not a manipulation-primitive suite for robot arms. Skill diversity exists (twirl, hold, key-turn, reorient, baoding) but is inseparable from the muscle-model odd fit. Assets are not reusable objects either — they are myoHand/myoArm/myoLeg biomechanical models. Small manipulanda (pen, die, key, baoding balls) are the only trivially portable items, insufficient to justify inclusion.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (native, gym API wrapper). Not MJX/warp GPU-parallel out of the box; envs are single-instance MuJoCo. No manager-based (Isaac-Lab-style) API.
# TasksPaper claims 204 total = 9 task-families x 2 difficulty (easy/hard) x 3 reset (fixed/random/none) x non-stationarity variations (sarcopenia/fatigue/tendon-transfer). Docs enumerate ~48 base registered env IDs, expandable to ~192 with the 3 physiological variations. Distinct SKILLS are far fewer: ~9 families.
Example tasksmyoHandKeyTurnFixed/Random-v0, myoHandObjHoldFixed/Random-v0, myoHandPenTwirlFixed/Random-v0, myoHandReorient8/100/ID/OOD-v0, myoHandPoseFixed/Random-v0, myoHandReachFixed/Random-v0, myoChallengeDieReorientP1/P2-v1, myoChallengeBaodingP1/P2-v1, myoChallengeRelocateP1-v0, myoFingerPose/Reach-v0, myoElbowPose1D6MRandom-v0, myoArmReach-v0, myoLegWalk-v0.
Primitivesreach, pose, in-hand rotate/twirl, in-hand reorient, object-hold, key-turn, baoding rotation, relocate/pick-place. Dexterous in-hand focus, not arm primitives (push/pull/open/close/insert/slide/stack).
RobotsMusculoskeletal models: myoFinger, myoElbow, myoHand (29 bones, 23 joints, 39 muscle-tendon units), myoArm (32 muscles), myoLeg (80 muscles), MyoSkeleton full-body. No standard robot arm (no Franka/UR/etc.). Torque/position robots are explicitly not the modeling target.
State observationsFully state/proprioceptive; NO vision required. Obs contains joint angles (all joints, [-pi,pi]), muscle-tendon length/velocity, muscle activations, fingertip/end-effector positions, and object pose/orientation for manipulation tasks. Reported obs dims ~83-115, action dim 39 (per-muscle activation [0,1]) for myoHand. Vision-free solving is the default and demonstrated throughout (RL baselines trained on state).
Action spacePer-muscle activation / alpha-motoneuron signals, continuous [0,1], pull-only (muscles cannot push). Hand = 39-dim. This is the core incompatibility with position/torque-controlled robot arms.
AssetsMJCF/XML models under Apache-2.0. Anatomical models (bones, tendons, muscle wrapping geometry) are specialized and non-reusable for robots. Only the small manipulanda (pen, die, key, baoding spheres) are trivially portable plain-geometry meshes. Reusability for a Franka suite is low.
LicenseApache License 2.0 (permissive; assets and code reusable).
CL track recordYes, used in continual/lifelong and scalable-RL literature as a hard high-DoF motor-control benchmark: e.g. 'Hyperspherical Normalization for Scalable Deep RL' (arXiv 2502.15280) and 'Lifelong RL with Modulating Masks' (arXiv 2212.11110) reference/use MyoSuite tasks (Key Turn, Object Hold, Pen Twirl, Pose, Reach; fixed vs randomized-goal hard settings). So it has a CL track record, but in the muscle-control regime, not arm manipulation.
Port to mjlabHigh to prohibitive for our use. (1) Actuation mismatch is the blocker: policies control 39 muscle activations with third-order Hill-type muscle dynamics; our pipeline distills torque/position-controlled Franka policies. No path to reuse the trained policies or teachers. (2) Rebuilding the tasks with a Franka gripper would mean discarding the myoHand entirely and re-authoring reward/goal logic — at which point almost nothing of MyoSuite remains except the small manipulanda MJCF (pen, die, key, baoding balls) and task goal definitions. (3) MJCF is available (Apache-2.0) so the object meshes ARE trivially portable, but they are toy objects we could source anywhere. Estimated effort to get even 2-3 usable Franka tasks: weeks of re-authoring, with the suite's value (muscle control) thrown away.
Scale potentialFor our Franka mjlab pipeline: does not scale — effectively 0 directly usable tasks, and re-authoring on a Franka discards the suite's muscle-control value; at most 3-5 objects harvestable as assets. Rich as a hand-motor-control CL benchmark, but wrong actuation and wrong robot for us.
GotchasThird-order Hill-type muscle dynamics make tasks harder and non-transferable to torque/position robots. Envs are single-instance MuJoCo (not GPU-parallel MJX/warp) so they do not slot into mjlab's parallel manager API without a rewrite. 'Task count' of ~200 is inflated by difficulty x reset x physiological-variation multipliers over only ~9 skill families. myoChallenge task IDs change yearly (versioned -v1). Distinct skills << advertised env count.
Repo activityActively maintained by MyoHub; hosts yearly MyoChallenge (NeurIPS) with new tasks (DieReorient, Baoding, Relocate, ChaseTag). Ongoing releases (docs at v2.11). Healthy, active repo.
Reject

panda-gym

paper · code

Six goal-conditioned Franka tasks (reach/push/slide/pick-place/stack/flip) in PyBullet with pure state observations; clean but tiny and largely redundant with your current task set.

panda-gym: Open-source goal-conditioned environments for robotic learning (NeurIPS 2021 Workshop on Robot Learning; paper lists 5 tasks, repo now has 6)

Verdict: Only 6 distinct tasks, 4 of which overlap your existing 5 (push, pick/lift, reach-like, stack~pick); simulator is PyBullet with no reusable assets (primitive shapes built in code). The only extractable value is 2-3 task RECIPES (slide, flip, stack) that you would reimplement from scratch in mjlab in a day or two — no need to depend on the repo at all. Cannot scale toward 20+ tasks.

Full details
SimulatorPyBullet (gymnasium API). Not MuJoCo; a community MuJoCo/MJCF reimplementation exists (zichunxx/panda_mujoco_gym, MIT, ~161 stars) covering push/slide/pick-and-place using the Menagerie Panda.
# Tasks6 distinct tasks (prior sweep said 5; Flip was added post-paper). 24 registered envs = 6 tasks x {sparse,dense} x {end-effector,joint} control — variants, not new skills.
Example tasksPandaReach-v3, PandaPush-v3, PandaSlide-v3, PandaPickAndPlace-v3, PandaStack-v3, PandaFlip-v3 (+Dense/Joints variants, e.g. PandaPickAndPlaceJointsDense-v3)
Primitivesreach, push, slide (hit puck to out-of-reach goal), pick-and-place, stack (2 cubes), flip (rotate cube to target orientation). Genuinely new vs your set: slide, flip, and stack.
RobotsFranka Emika Panda only (URDF in PyBullet; Menagerie MJCF in the MuJoCo fork)
State observations100% state-based, no vision at all (only modality offered). Goal-conditioned dict obs: 'observation' = ee position (3) + ee velocity (3) + fingers width (grasping tasks) + per-object position (3), rotation (3), linear vel (3), angular vel (3); plus achieved_goal/desired_goal (3 each, 6/7 for stack/flip). E.g. Push/Slide obs ~18-19D, Reach 6D. All tasks demonstrably solved from state with SAC/TQC/DDPG+HER (baselines in repo/rl-baselines3-zoo).
Action spaceEnd-effector displacement control (3D delta + gripper) or 7-DoF joint control; each task registered in both modes.
AssetsEssentially none: scenes are primitive geoms (table, cubes, puck) created procedurally via PyBullet calls; robot is the standard Panda URDF. Nothing mesh/asset-wise worth porting — task logic is the only IP.
LicenseMIT (both panda-gym and panda_mujoco_gym)
CL track recordMinor/recent: used as a compact 3-task continual-RL sequence (ReachDense→PushDense→PickAndPlaceDense) in recent continual-RL papers, e.g. plasticity-preservation CRL (arXiv 2604.15414) and TSN-Affinity continual offline RL (arXiv 2604.25898). Not an established CL benchmark like Continual World.
Port to mjlabTrivial but manual: no assets to port; reimplement slide/flip/stack reward+scene logic (~100 lines each of primitive-geom MJCF + reward code) directly in mjlab against your existing Franka scene. panda_mujoco_gym already provides MJCF/Menagerie reference implementations for push/slide/pick-and-place to crib from. Estimate: 1-2 days for all 3 new tasks.
Scale potential~3 genuinely new state-solvable tasks (slide, flip, stack); dense/sparse and ee/joint variants don't count as skill diversity. No object library, no procedural generation — hard ceiling far below your 10-20 task target.
GotchasPrior sweep's '5 tasks' is stale (6 in repo); PyBullet contact params differ from MuJoCo so slide/flip dynamics need retuning; goal-conditioned HER-style dict obs and sparse rewards differ from your PPO-style setup (use Dense variants' reward shaping as reference); repo unmaintained since 2024; panda_mujoco_gym is a 4-commit hobby port — treat as reference code, not a dependency.
Repo activity762 stars; effectively unmaintained (last activity ~June 2024). panda_mujoco_gym: ~161 stars, small (4 commits), also quiet.

PerAct2 / RLBench2 bimanual

paper · code

Bimanual extension of RLBench (CoppeliaSim): 13 language-conditioned dual-Franka coordination tasks with 23 variations, built for vision-based (voxel) behavioral cloning.

PerAct2: Benchmarking and Learning for Robotic Bimanual Manipulation Tasks (arXiv 2024, also on OpenReview)

Verdict: Hard simulator exclusion: CoppeliaSim/PyRep with .ttm scene assets that are not portable to MuJoCo (proprietary binary scene format; success criteria and variations live in PyRep-coupled Python). Only 13 tasks / 23 variations (prior sweep's 18/41 is wrong), all bimanual dual-arm while mjlab tasks are single-Franka, and no continual-learning track record. The only value is the task LIST as a design reference if you ever build dual-Franka mjlab tasks — nothing digital is reusable, so not even assets-only.

Full details
SimulatorCoppeliaSim via PyRep (custom bimanual forks of RLBench, PyRep, and YARR required). EXCLUDED engine per requirements; no MJX/MuJoCo path exists.
# Tasks13 tasks / 23 unique variations (verified on arXiv abstract and project page; the "18 tasks / 41 variations" figure from the prior sweep is incorrect)
Example tasksbimanual_push_box, bimanual_lift_ball, bimanual_dual_push_buttons, bimanual_pick_plate, put item in drawer, put bottle in fridge, handover item (+easy variant), pick up notebook, straighten rope, sweep dustpan, lift tray, take tray out of oven
PrimitivesBimanual coordination primitives: coordinated lift (ball, tray, plate), push (box, dual buttons), handover, pick-and-place (item in drawer, bottle in fridge), deformable straighten (rope), sweep (dustpan), take-out (tray from oven)
RobotsTwo Franka Panda arms (dual-arm setup in RLBench2); method is "robot-agnostic" 6-DoF keyframe pose prediction, with real-world transfer shown incl. a humanoid
State observationsRLBench-style low-dim obs exist (joint positions/velocities, gripper poses, task_low_dim_state with object poses), so most rigid-body tasks (push box, lift ball/tray, buttons, handover) are in principle state-solvable. However PerAct2 itself is trained purely from multi-camera RGB-D voxel grids + proprioception + language; no state-only baseline is demonstrated, and the RLBench fork's own README warns the benchmark "was designed to be challenging, putting emphasis on vision rather than toy-based low dimensional inputs." Rope-straighten and sweep involve deformables/particles that are not cleanly state-representable.
Action spaceNext-best keyframe prediction: 6-DoF end-effector pose + gripper open/close per arm (voxel-grid Q-function, PerAct-style); underlying RLBench also supports joint/EE velocity and position modes
AssetsCoppeliaSim .ttm scene/model files + Python task definitions coupled to PyRep; pre-generated demo datasets as RGB-D image archives (128x128 and 256x256). None of it is MuJoCo-reusable.
LicenseApache 2.0 (repo), incorporating MIT-licensed components (PerceiverIO, ViT, LAMB, CLIP); RLBench fork carries its own RLBench license
CL track recordNone found. No continual-learning paper uses PerAct2/RLBench2-bimanual; it is a language-conditioned multi-task imitation benchmark. (Unimanual RLBench appears in some CL work, but not this bimanual extension.)
Port to mjlabNo asset-level porting path: task scenes are CoppeliaSim .ttm binary model files wired to PyRep-based Python task logic; meshes are not distributed as plain URDF/MJCF/OBJ. Porting means re-implementing each task from scratch in mjlab against a dual-Franka MJCF scene (Menagerie has franka_emika_panda; duplicate + mount). Estimate ~1-2 days per task for the simple rigid ones (push box, lift ball, lift tray, dual buttons, handover) purely as reimplementation inspired by the task list — that is the same effort as designing your own tasks, so the benchmark buys you almost nothing.
Scale potentialLow. 13 tasks total, all bimanual (a different action space from your current single-arm setup). Even fully re-implemented it adds at most ~8-10 state-solvable rigid-body tasks, and each is a from-scratch build. Cannot yield 20+ tasks.
Gotchas(1) Prior sweep task count was wrong: 13/23, not 18/41. (2) Requires exact custom forks of RLBench+PyRep+YARR and a specific CoppeliaSim version — brittle stack. (3) All tasks are bimanual: adopting them means doubling your action/obs space and rewriting your Franka manager setup. (4) Benchmark explicitly designed vision-first; two tasks (rope, sweep) involve deformables that MuJoCo would handle very differently. (5) .ttm is a binary CoppeliaSim format — no mesh extraction path short of manually exporting from the CoppeliaSim GUI.
Repo activity~127 stars, 9 forks; last substantive update around Feb 2025 — low activity since
Reject

roboverse (Berkeley, avisingh599)

paper · code

Small dormant PyBullet WidowX-250 tabletop suite (grasp / pick-place / drawer / button) built for the COG offline-RL paper; 68 registered variants of 4 skills, image-centric but with privileged state available.

COG: Connecting New Skills to Past Experience with Offline Reinforcement Learning (CoRL 2020)

Verdict: Only ~4 primitive families (grasp, pick-place, drawer, button) despite 68 registered env IDs — the diversity is object/container swaps, not skill diversity, and we already cover drawer/button/pick/push in mjlab. PyBullet-native (URDF world-building in Python code, no scene files), dormant since ~2021, Python 3.6-era, never used for continual learning. Its only unique value is the ShapeNet-derived object URDF set, which is more easily obtained from ShapeNet directly or from better-maintained object libraries (Objaverse, Google Scanned Objects) — so not even a strong assets-only case.

Full details
SimulatorPyBullet only. No MuJoCo/MJX support of any kind.
# Tasks68 registered gym env IDs, but they reduce to ~4 primitive families: grasping (~9 IDs), pick-and-place (~45 IDs, mostly container/object variants), drawer open/grasp incl. double-drawer (~12 IDs), button press (3 IDs). Prior-sweep estimate of 10-15 undercounted IDs but overcounted distinct skills.
Example tasksWidow250Grasp-v0, Widow250MultiObjectGraspTrain-v0, Widow250PickPlace-v0, Widow250PutInBowl-v0, Widow250PutOnPlate-v0, Widow250DrawerOpen-v0, Widow250DoubleDrawerOpenGraspNeutral-v0, Widow250ButtonPress-v0, RandPosButtonPressTwoObjGrasp-v0
Primitivesgrasp, pick, place (into bowl/tray/box/basket/pan, onto plate/cube/etc.), drawer open (single + double, with blocking object), button press. No insert, turn, rotate, stack, slide, door.
RobotsWidowX-250 (Interbotix) only in this repo; Sawyer appears in related Berkeley repos (bullet-manipulation), not here.
State observationsObservation dict contains: 'state' = 10-D proprio (EE pos 3 + EE quat 4 + gripper joint pos 2 + gripper binary 1), 'object_position' (3), 'object_orientation' (4), plus 'image'. Rewards are computed from simulator state (height thresholds, in-container checks, drawer joint angle), and the repo ships scripted state-based policies (e.g. drawer_open ~90% success) — so all tasks are solvable from low-level state. However, the COG paper and most downstream users trained from 48x48 images; no published state-only RL results, though scripted policies demonstrate feasibility.
Action space8-D continuous in [-1,1]: 3 EE position deltas, 3 EE orientation deltas (degrees), 1 gripper, 1 'neutral pose' trigger. Discrete-gripper control mode used in most registered envs.
AssetsURDFs: WidowX-250 (interbotix_descriptions) + 'bullet-objects' directory of ShapeNet-derived object URDFs/OBJ meshes + containers (trays, bowls, boxes, drawer). Scenes are constructed programmatically in Python, not as scene description files. ShapeNet objects inherit ShapeNet's non-commercial license despite repo MIT license.
LicenseMIT (repo code); embedded ShapeNet-derived meshes are subject to ShapeNet terms (non-commercial, attribution).
CL track recordNone found. Used in offline-RL / skill-chaining work (COG CoRL 2020) and multi-task language+demo work (DeL-TaCo ICLR 2023 via fork), but no continual/lifelong-learning paper uses it; CL community uses Continual World (Meta-World) and LIBERO instead.
Port to mjlabNo MuJoCo path exists in-repo. Porting = converting WidowX-250 URDF to MJCF (a WidowX MJCF exists in mujoco_menagerie, so robot is free) and converting ShapeNet object URDFs/OBJs to MJCF bodies (scriptable, but mesh quality/collision hulls need work + license issue). Scenes must be re-authored from Python construction code. Effort: days per task family, but since the task types (pick, place, drawer, button) already exist in mjlab, you would be re-implementing tasks you have, gaining only object variety.
Scale potentialNominally high in env-ID count (68+, and the multi-object machinery can generate more pick-place variants from ShapeNet lists), but low in skill diversity: everything reduces to 4 primitives. For our goal (distinct manipulation skills for continual distillation), it contributes at most 2-3 genuinely new task types (put-in-container variants, double-drawer with obstruction) beyond our existing 5.
Gotchas(1) Name collision: this is NOT RoboVerseOrg/RoboVerse (the 2025 unified-platform benchmark) — cite carefully. (2) 68 env IDs sounds big but is ~4 skills. (3) ShapeNet meshes are non-commercial-licensed inside an MIT repo. (4) Downstream papers (COG) are image-based; state-solvability is via scripted policies, not published state-RL baselines. (5) Python 3.6 / old gym API — code needs modernization even for PyBullet use.
Repo activity29 stars, 12 forks, 272 commits; effectively dormant since ~2021, targets Python 3.6. Fork roboverse-deltaco (ICLR 2023) is the most recent derivative.
Reject

SERL franka_sim (rail-berkeley/serl)

paper · code

Minimal MuJoCo Franka+Robotiq lift-cube sim environment serving as the sim on-ramp for the SERL/HIL-SERL real-robot RL software suite; deprecated.

SERL: A Software Suite for Sample-Efficient Robotic Reinforcement Learning (ICRA 2024)

Verdict: Only one sim task (PandaPickCube, plus a vision variant of the same task) — the four flagship SERL tasks (peg insert, PCB insert, cable route, relocate) are real-robot only. Repo is officially deprecated in favor of HIL-SERL. Assets are Menagerie-derived Panda + Robotiq, already available directly from MuJoCo Menagerie, so no unique asset value. Zero contribution toward scaling to 10-100 tasks.

Full details
SimulatorMuJoCo (native Python bindings, CPU, single-env; not MJX/warp, not GPU-parallel)
# Tasks1 (PandaPickCube; the vision env is the same task with cameras). Real-robot SERL tasks (4) have no sim counterparts.
Example tasksPandaPickCube-v0 (lift cube ~0.2 m; reward = 0.3*reach + 0.7*lift), PandaPickCubeVision-v0 (same task, front+wrist cameras)
Primitivespick/lift only
RobotsFranka Panda + Robotiq 2F-85 gripper (Menagerie-derived MJCF)
State observationsFully state-solvable and demonstrated: dict obs with panda/tcp_pos (3), panda/tcp_vel (3), panda/gripper_pos (1), block_pos (3) — ~10-D total. The state-based variant is the documented default training mode (RLPD/DRQ examples train from state in minutes). No vision needed.
Action space4-D continuous [-1,1]: Cartesian delta x/y/z + gripper grasp command
AssetsPlain MJCF (Panda arm, Robotiq gripper, cube arena) derived from MuJoCo Menagerie — trivially MuJoCo-native but redundant since Menagerie already provides them; you already have Panda tasks in mjlab/playground.
LicenseApache-2.0
CL track recordNone for continual learning. Used as a sim sanity-check for sample-efficient online RL (RLPD, DRQ) in the SERL/HIL-SERL papers; no CL papers build on franka_sim.
Port to mjlabTrivial (hours) — plain MJCF already MuJoCo-native and its Cartesian-delta lift-cube design mirrors your existing mjlab lift-cube task, so porting yields nothing new. Would still need rewrite from single-env gym to GPU-parallel manager API.
Scale potentialEssentially zero: one task, no object/task generation machinery, no asset library beyond what Menagerie already gives you.
GotchasRepo deprecated (successor HIL-SERL is real-robot focused, adds no sim tasks); CPU single-env MuJoCo, not MJX/warp; the well-known SERL task suite is hardware-only, which can make the project look richer in sim than it is.
Repo activity~841 stars, 116 forks, 207 commits; DEPRECATED — README redirects to HIL-SERL; sim side never expanded
Reject

The COLOSSEUM

paper · code

RLBench fork adding 14 systematic perturbation factors (color/texture/size/lighting/distractors/friction/mass/camera) to 20 curated RLBench tasks for evaluating visual generalization of manipulation policies.

THE COLOSSEUM: A Benchmark for Evaluating Generalization for Robotic Manipulation (RSS 2024)

Verdict: CoppeliaSim/PyRep-only (explicitly excluded simulator class), assets are binary .ttm CoppeliaSim models with no MuJoCo porting path short of manual per-task reconstruction, and its unique contribution (14 visual/physical perturbation axes) targets vision-generalization evaluation, which is orthogonal to our need. Only 20 tasks, all of which are just RLBench tasks — if RLBench-style tasks were wanted, the upstream RLBench (100 tasks) dominates it, and it fails the same simulator requirement anyway.

Full details
SimulatorCoppeliaSim via PyRep (fork of RLBench). No MuJoCo/MJX support of any kind.
# Tasks20 (verified by file listing: 20 .ttm task assets + 20 task python files). Each task has 14 perturbation variants, but those are eval axes, not distinct skills.
Example tasksbasketball_in_hoop, close_box, close_laptop_lid, empty_dishwasher, get_ice_from_fridge, hockey, insert_onto_square_peg, meat_on_grill, move_hanger, open_drawer, place_wine_at_rack_location, put_money_in_safe, reach_and_drag, scoop_with_spatula, setup_chess, slide_block_to_target, stack_cups, straighten_rope, turn_oven_on, wipe_desk
Primitivespick-place (basketball_in_hoop, meat_on_grill, stack_cups, setup_chess), slide/push (slide_block_to_target, hockey), open/close (open_drawer, close_box, close_laptop_lid), insert (insert_onto_square_peg), turn (turn_oven_on), drag (reach_and_drag), wipe (wipe_desk), articulated-object interaction (empty_dishwasher, get_ice_from_fridge, put_money_in_safe), deformable (straighten_rope), tool use (scoop_with_spatula)
RobotsFranka Emika Panda (RLBench default)
State observationsInherits RLBench Observation: joint positions/velocities/forces, gripper pose+state, and task_low_dim_state (task-specific object state vector, dims vary per task). Demos are generated from privileged waypoint scripting, so tasks are in-principle state-solvable, but neither the paper nor the repo demonstrates state-only policy learning — all 5 evaluated baselines (RVT, PerAct, etc.) are vision-based, and the benchmark's raison d'etre (color/texture/lighting perturbations) is meaningless for state-based policies.
Action spaceRLBench action modes: joint position/velocity/torque or end-effector pose (via motion planner), plus gripper open/close
AssetsBinary CoppeliaSim .ttm task models (colosseum/rlbench/task_ttms/, 20 files) plus texture/color assets for perturbations; also 3D-print files for real-world replication. Not reusable in MuJoCo without manual mesh extraction and scene reconstruction.
LicenseMIT per setup.cfg metadata, but NO LICENSE file exists in the repo (GitHub API reports license: null) — minor legal ambiguity, though intent is clearly MIT/open
CL track recordNo continual-learning papers found using COLOSSEUM; it is cited in generalization/VLA evaluation work (e.g. GenIMA, BridgeVLA, GemBench challenge). Parent RLBench has occasional CL use, but COLOSSEUM itself is a generalization-eval benchmark.
Port to mjlabVery high. Assets are binary CoppeliaSim .ttm scene models (meshes+joints+dynamics embedded); porting requires opening each in CoppeliaSim, exporting meshes to OBJ, hand-rebuilding kinematics/joints/scenes as MJCF, and reimplementing task success logic and waypoint scripts — roughly days of work per task with no automation. No URDF/MJCF anywhere in the repo.
Scale potentialNear zero for us: 20 tasks max, none portable. The 14 perturbation axes could multiply eval conditions (20x14=280 eval settings) but not distinct skills, and most axes (color, texture, lighting, camera pose) are invisible to state-based policies; only friction/mass/size/distractor axes are a transferable idea we could reimplement natively in mjlab.
GotchasPrior sweep info was accurate. Key gotchas: (1) requires CoppeliaSim 4.1 + PyRep, headless rendering is notoriously finicky (repo ships two Dockerfiles for a reason); (2) the "20 tasks x 14 factors" scale is eval variants, not 280 tasks; (3) mostly-visual perturbation axes are irrelevant for state-based CL; (4) no LICENSE file despite MIT claim in setup.cfg; (5) the one reusable idea — systematic friction/mass/size/distractor perturbation taxonomy — can be reimplemented in mjlab in a day without touching this repo.
Repo activity150 stars, 9 forks, ~60 commits; last push 2025-03-03 — effectively dormant, 4 open issues
Reject

VIMA-Bench

paper · code

Ravens-extended PyBullet UR5 suction-cup benchmark whose 17 "tasks" are multimodal-prompt templates over a handful of top-down pick/place/sweep primitives, with thousands of texture/object instantiations and 650K oracle demos.

VIMA: General Robot Manipulation with Multimodal Prompts (ICML 2023)

Verdict: Its task diversity lives in multimodal-prompt variation (novel words, video imitation, texture matching), not motor-skill diversity: stripped of prompts, the 17 templates collapse to ~4-5 tabletop primitives (pick-place, rotate, sweep, stack) that Ravens already provides. Action space is high-level 2D pick/place pose primitives (no joint/Cartesian control), simulator is PyBullet, state obs is not exposed by default (only privileged oracles), the repo is unmaintained since Sept 2023, and it has no continual-learning track record. Porting cost buys almost nothing we don't already have in mjlab.

Full details
SimulatorPyBullet (built on Google Ravens); UR5 arm with suction cup or spatula end-effector; top-down tabletop workspace. Not MuJoCo.
# Tasks17 task templates (verified in README), each procedurally instantiable into thousands of instances via texture/object combinations — but instances vary appearance/prompt, not skill.
Example tasksVisual Manipulation (put X into Y), Rotate (rotate object by given angle), Rearrange / Rearrange then Restore (match a goal-scene image), Sweep without Exceeding/Touching (spatula-push blocks into zone under constraints), Pick in Order then Restore, Same Texture / Same Shape (pick objects matching a probe), Novel Adjective/Noun (grounding invented words), Follow Motion / Follow Order (one-shot video imitation), Twist, Manipulate Old Neighbor, Scene Understanding.
PrimitivesEffective motor primitives after removing prompt semantics: pick-and-place, rotate (yaw of held object), sweep/push with spatula, rearrange/restore (sequenced pick-place), stack. No insert, no articulated objects (no doors/drawers/buttons), no in-hand or 6-DoF manipulation — everything is top-down 2D pose pairs.
RobotsUR5 with suction cup / spatula (no gripper, no Franka).
State observationsDefault env observation is RGB (front + top views) + segmentation + end-effector-type indicator — NO object poses in the standard obs. Ground-truth state exists only through the built-in scripted oracles, which read privileged PyBullet state to solve all 17 tasks (proving state-solvability in principle). However, task identity for most templates (Novel Adjective/Noun, Same Texture, Scene Understanding, Follow Motion/Order) is defined by the prompt's images/words, so a state-only agent cannot even know the goal without hand-crafting a goal encoding per instance. Vision-free policy learning is not demonstrated anywhere; also the action space is discrete-primitive pick/place poses, not continuous low-level control, so 'solving from state' here means predicting two 2D poses, not a control policy.
Action spacePrimitive actions: pose0 (pick) and pose1 (place), each a 2D position + quaternion rotation, executed by scripted motion; suction-cup attach or spatula sweep. No joint-space or Cartesian-velocity control — fundamentally incompatible with mjlab's low-level Franka control without rebuilding tasks from scratch.
AssetsRavens-style assets: URDF models with OBJ meshes and PNG textures — folders: block, bowl, box, container, google (Google Scanned Objects subset), hanoi, kitting, pallet, plane, rectangle, square, stacking, zone, ur5, textures. URDF+OBJ is mechanically portable to MJCF, but the objects are mostly trivial primitives and flat kitting shapes with little value beyond what mjlab/MuJoCo Menagerie already offers.
LicenseCode: MIT. Dataset (650K trajectories): CC BY 4.0.
CL track recordNo continual-learning usage found. CL manipulation papers (e.g., M2Distill, lifelong imitation work) standardize on LIBERO; searches for VIMA-Bench + continual/lifelong learning return nothing using VIMA-Bench as the CL testbed.
Port to mjlabHigh effort, low payoff. There is no MuJoCo port of VIMA-Bench or Ravens. A port would require: converting URDF/OBJ assets to MJCF (easy, days), reimplementing suction-cup attachment (weld-constraint hack in MuJoCo, moderate), reimplementing the 17 task-generation/goal-checking pipelines (they are deeply tied to Ravens' top-down pose abstraction and prompt assembly — weeks), and inventing a low-level control formulation since the benchmark has none (you would be designing new RL tasks, not porting them). Realistically 4-8 weeks to get a fraction of it, and the result would be generic pick/place/sweep tasks you could write directly in mjlab in days.
Scale potentialPoor for our axis of scale. Of 17 templates, only ~6-8 are meaningful without a multimodal prompt (Visual Manipulation, Rotate, Rearrange, Sweep x2, Pick-in-Order, Stack-like kitting), and those reduce to ~4-5 distinct motor skills. The 'thousands of instances' are texture/object recolorings of the same skill — useless for skill-diversity scaling. Would not get us to 20+ genuinely distinct state-solvable tasks.
GotchasPrior sweep claim '17 task types, thousands of instances' is technically accurate but misleading for our use: instance diversity is visual/linguistic, not behavioral. Default env exposes no object poses (RGB+segmentation only); state access requires hooking oracle/privileged internals. Action space is two 2D poses per step, so 'episodes' are 1-3 macro-steps — a policy-distillation setup on this looks nothing like our low-level Franka control. Repo unmaintained; pins old PyBullet/Python. No Franka, no articulated objects (our existing door/drawer/button tasks have no counterpart here).
Repo activity327 stars, 38 forks, 8 open issues; last push 2023-09-26 — unmaintained for ~3 years.
